# More adjectives: B–C

> Adjectives B–C beyond the grammar groups — the everyday adjectives that a triage of WordNet’s adjective lemmas showed a learner needs.

[← The grammar of adjectives](../README.md) &middot; [All groups](README.md)

Most English adjectives behave: they go before a noun or after *be*, and they compare with *-er* or *more*. These are those — but each still says how it compares, where it can stand, and what it takes. WordNet lists about twenty thousand adjectives; most of the rest are technical, archaic or British-only, and a triage pass left them out on purpose.

---
### baby-faced

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbeɪbiˌfeɪst/ &middot; *BAY-bee-fayst*
**Русский:** с детским лицом
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *baby* + *face* + *-ed*

Having a young-looking, round face like a child's.

1. The *baby-faced* quarterback is actually thirty.
2. He looks *baby-faced*, but he's the oldest guy on the team.
3. A *baby-faced* actor played the young president.

---

### Babylonian

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbæbəˈloʊniən/ &middot; *bab-uh-LOH-nee-uhn*
**Русский:** вавилонский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ian from *Babylon*

From or relating to ancient Babylon.

1. The *Babylonian* king Hammurabi wrote one of the first law codes.
2. *Babylonian* astronomers tracked the movements of the planets.
3. Experts believe the clay tablet is *Babylonian*.

---

### back

**Pronunciation:** /bæk/ &middot; *BAK*
**Русский:** задний; прошлый; просроченный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *the back door*; after a verb use *at the back* or *in the back*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *front*
**Contrast:** The adjective only goes before a noun: ✗ *The door is back* → *The door is in the back*. *Back* is much more often an adverb (*come back*), a noun (*my back hurts*) or a verb (*back up*).

Back has 3 distinct senses.

**1. At or near the rear of something.** (задний)

1. Let's use the *back* door.
2. The kids sat in the *back* seat.
3. We had coffee on the *back* porch.

**2. From an earlier time; owed or published before now.** (прошлый, просроченный)

4. He owes three years of *back* taxes.
5. The library keeps *back* issues of the magazine.

**3. Away from main roads and busy places.** (глухой, окольный)

6. We took the *back* roads to avoid traffic.
7. They live on a quiet *back* street.

---

### back-channel

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbækˌtʃænəl/ &middot; *BAK-chan-uhl*
**Русский:** неофициальный; закулисный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — *back-channel talks*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *back* + *channel*

Secret and unofficial, outside the normal channels.

1. The two governments held *back-channel* talks for months.
2. She used her *back-channel* contacts to get the information.
3. The deal came out of *back-channel* negotiations.

---

### back-to-back

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbæktəˈbæk/ &middot; *bak-tuh-BAK*
**Русский:** идущие подряд
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — *back-to-back wins*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *back* + *to* + *back*
**Contrast:** As an adverb, written without hyphens, it means *with backs touching* (*stand back to back*) or *one after another* (*two games back to back*).

Happening one right after the other.

1. The Warriors won *back-to-back* championships.
2. I have *back-to-back* meetings all afternoon.
3. We had *back-to-back* snowstorms last week.

---

### backbreaking

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbækˌbreɪkɪŋ/ &middot; *BAK-bray-king*
**Русский:** изнурительный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more backbreaking*, *most backbreaking*
**Form:** compound — *back* + *breaking*

Very hard and physically tiring.

1. Picking cotton was *backbreaking* work.
2. The job was more *backbreaking* than I expected.
3. They spent a *backbreaking* summer building the barn.

---

### backhanded

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbækˌhændɪd/ &middot; *BAK-han-did*
**Русский:** сомнительный; двусмысленный; наотмашь
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only — *a backhanded compliment*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *back* + *hand* + *-ed*

Backhanded has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Seeming to praise but actually criticizing.** (сомнительный, двусмысленный)

1. 'You look great for your age' is a *backhanded* compliment.
2. She's an expert at *backhanded* praise.

**2. Done with the back of the hand facing forward.** (наотмашь; (в теннисе) слева)

3. He hit a *backhanded* shot down the line.
4. She gave him a *backhanded* slap.

---

### backstage

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbækˌsteɪdʒ/ &middot; *BAK-stayj*
**Русский:** закулисный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a backstage pass*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** More common as an adverb, stressed on the second syllable: *We went backstage after the show*.

Behind the stage, or private and out of public view.

1. I got a *backstage* pass for the concert.
2. The documentary shows the *backstage* drama of a Broadway show.
3. There was a lot of *backstage* arguing before the deal was signed.

---

### backward

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbækwərd/ &middot; *BAK-wurd*
**Русский:** обратный; отсталый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more backward*, *most backward*
**Opposite:** *forward*
**Contrast:** In American English the adjective is *backward*; *backwards* is only an adverb (*walk backwards*), and *backward* can be the adverb too. Calling people or places *backward* sounds insulting.

Backward has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Directed toward the back or the starting point.** (обратный, направленный назад)

1. She took a *backward* step.
2. He left without a *backward* glance.

**2. Less developed or modern than others.** (отсталый)

3. The region was once one of the most *backward* in the state.
4. Some people still hold *backward* ideas about women.

---

### bacterial

**Pronunciation:** /bækˈtɪriəl/ &middot; *bak-TEER-ee-uhl*
**Русский:** бактериальный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *bacteria*

Caused by or relating to bacteria.

1. Antibiotics only work on *bacterial* infections.
2. Strep throat is *bacterial*, not viral.
3. The lab tests water for *bacterial* growth.

---

### bad-mannered

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbædˈmænərd/ &middot; *bad-MAN-urd*
**Русский:** невоспитанный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bad-mannered*, *most bad-mannered*
**Form:** compound — *bad* + *manner* + *-ed*
**Opposite:** *well-mannered*

Behaving rudely; not polite.

1. The *bad-mannered* guests left without saying goodbye.
2. It's *bad-mannered* to check your phone at dinner.
3. He was the most *bad-mannered* kid in the class.

---

### bad-tempered

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbædˈtɛmpərd/ &middot; *bad-TEM-purd*
**Русский:** раздражительный; сердитый
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bad-tempered*, *most bad-tempered*
**Form:** compound — *bad* + *temper* + *-ed*
**Opposite:** *good-natured*

Easily annoyed or angry.

1. Our neighbor is a *bad-tempered* old man.
2. She gets *bad-tempered* when she's hungry.
3. The meeting got more *bad-tempered* as it went on.

---

### baffled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbæfəld/ &middot; *BAF-uhld*
**Русский:** озадаченный; сбитый с толку
**Type:** participial; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more baffled*, *completely baffled*
**Takes:** *by* + noun
**Form:** -ed — how someone feels
**Contrast:** *baffled* is the person who does not understand; *baffling* is the thing that causes it: *I was baffled by the baffling instructions*.

Completely unable to understand something.

1. Doctors were *baffled* by her symptoms.
2. He gave me a *baffled* look.
3. I'm even more *baffled* now than before.

---

### baffling

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbæflɪŋ/ &middot; *BAF-ling*
**Русский:** непонятный; загадочный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more baffling*, *most baffling*
**Form:** -ing — what causes the feeling
**Contrast:** *baffling* is the thing; *baffled* is the person who does not understand it.

Impossible to understand or explain.

1. The instructions were completely *baffling*.
2. Police called it a *baffling* case.
3. The most *baffling* part is that nobody saw anything.

---

### baggy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbæɡi/ &middot; *BAG-ee*
**Русский:** мешковатый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *baggier*, *baggiest*
**Form:** -y from *bag*
**Opposite:** *tight*

Hanging loosely, because it is too big.

1. He wore *baggy* jeans and a hoodie.
2. My pants got *baggier* after I lost weight.
3. The sweater was a little *baggy* on him.

---

### Bahamian

**Pronunciation:** /bəˈheɪmiən/ &middot; *buh-HAY-mee-uhn*
**Русский:** багамский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ian from *the Bahamas*

From or relating to the Bahamas.

1. We tried *Bahamian* conch fritters.
2. The *Bahamian* dollar is worth the same as the US dollar.
3. Her mother is *Bahamian*.

---

### Bahraini

**Pronunciation:** /bɑːˈreɪni/ &middot; *bah-RAY-nee*
**Русский:** бахрейнский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -i from *Bahrain*

From or relating to Bahrain.

1. The *Bahraini* government announced new visa rules.
2. He's *Bahraini* but lives in Houston.
3. *Bahraini* pearls were famous for centuries.

---

### baked

**Pronunciation:** /beɪkt/ &middot; *BAYKT*
**Русский:** печёный; запечённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *bake*

Cooked in an oven with dry heat.

1. I'll have a *baked* potato with sour cream.
2. Is the chicken fried or *baked*?
3. The church sells *baked* goods every Sunday.

---

### baking hot

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbeɪkɪŋ ˈhɑːt/ &middot; *bay-king HAHT*
**Русский:** раскалённый; очень жаркий
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** *baking* makes *hot* stronger
**Contrast:** An extreme adjective, so not *very baking hot*. Americans also just say *baking*: *It's baking out there!*

Extremely hot.

1. It was *baking hot* inside the car.
2. We spent a *baking hot* afternoon at the ballpark.
3. Don't leave the dog outside; it's *baking hot*.

---

### balanced

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbælənst/ &middot; *BAL-uhnst*
**Русский:** сбалансированный; взвешенный; уравновешенный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more balanced*, *most balanced*
**Opposite:** *unbalanced*

Balanced has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Having the right amounts of different things.** (сбалансированный)

1. Try to eat a *balanced* diet.
2. The team has a more *balanced* offense this year.

**2. Fair, giving all sides equal attention.** (взвешенный, объективный)

3. The article gives a *balanced* view of the issue.
4. Viewers want *balanced* news coverage.

**3. Calm and sensible.** (уравновешенный)

5. She's a very *balanced* person.
6. He seems *well-balanced* despite everything he's been through.

---

### bald

**Pronunciation:** /bɔːld/ &middot; *BAWLD*
**Русский:** лысый; стёртый; неприкрытый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *balder*, *baldest*

Bald has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Having little or no hair on the head.** (лысый)

1. My dad started going *bald* in his thirties.
2. He's *balder* than his older brother.
3. A *bald* man in a suit opened the door.

**2. Worn smooth or without the usual covering.** (стёртый, голый)

4. You need new tires; these are *bald*.
5. The lawn has *bald* spots after the drought.

**3. Plain and direct, with nothing added to soften it.** (голый, неприкрытый)

6. The report was a *bald* statement of the facts.
7. That's a *bald* lie.

---

### bald-faced

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɔːldˌfeɪst/ &middot; *BAWLD-fayst*
**Русский:** наглый; неприкрытый
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — *a bald-faced lie*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *bald* + *face* + *-ed*
**Contrast:** Almost always *a bald-faced lie*. *Barefaced* means the same.

Open and shameless, with no attempt to hide it.

1. That's a *bald-faced* lie.
2. It was a *bald-faced* attempt to buy votes.
3. He told a *bald-faced* lie to the judge.

---

### balding

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɔːldɪŋ/ &middot; *BAWL-ding*
**Русский:** лысеющий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *bald*

Starting to lose the hair on the head.

1. A *balding* man in his fifties sat next to me.
2. He's *balding* and a little overweight.
3. My uncle was already *balding* at twenty-five.

---

### ballistic

**Pronunciation:** /bəˈlɪstɪk/ &middot; *buh-LIS-tik*
**Русский:** баллистический; взбешённый
**Type:** classifying; also emotion
**Position:** both — before a noun in the missile sense; after *go* in the angry sense
**Comparison:** not gradable

Ballistic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to missiles or bullets and how they fly.** (баллистический)

1. The country tested a *ballistic* missile.
2. Police sent the bullets for *ballistic* testing.

**2. Extremely angry, in *go ballistic* (informal).** (взбешённый)

3. My mom went *ballistic* when she saw the phone bill.
4. The coach went *ballistic* at the referee.

---

### balmy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɑːmi/ &middot; *BAH-mee*
**Русский:** тёплый; мягкий (о погоде)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *balmier*, *balmiest*
**Contrast:** The *l* is silent: BAH-mee.

Pleasantly warm, of weather or air.

1. It was a *balmy* evening in Miami.
2. The weather gets *balmier* as you drive south.
3. We had the *balmiest* February on record.

---

### balsamic

**Pronunciation:** /bɔːlˈsæmɪk/ &middot; *bawl-SAM-ik*
**Русский:** бальзамический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *balsamic vinegar*
**Comparison:** not gradable

Made with dark, sweet Italian vinegar.

1. Drizzle the salad with *balsamic* vinegar.
2. The tomatoes come with a *balsamic* glaze.
3. She served the chicken with a *balsamic* reduction.

---

### Baltic

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɔːltɪk/ &middot; *BAWL-tik*
**Русский:** балтийский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** attributive only — *the Baltic states*
**Comparison:** not gradable

Relating to the Baltic Sea or the countries around it.

1. Estonia, Latvia and Lithuania are the *Baltic* states.
2. The *Baltic* Sea freezes in some winters.
3. We took a cruise along the *Baltic* coast.

---

### banal

**Pronunciation:** /bəˈnɑːl/ &middot; *buh-NAHL*
**Русский:** банальный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more banal*, *most banal*
**Opposite:** *original*

Boring and ordinary, with nothing new.

1. The lyrics are pretty *banal*.
2. We had a *banal* conversation about the weather.
3. His second book was even more *banal* than the first.

---

### bandaged

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbændɪdʒd/ &middot; *BAN-dijd*
**Русский:** перевязанный; забинтованный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *bandage*

Wrapped in a bandage.

1. He came home with a *bandaged* hand.
2. Her knee was still *bandaged* a week after the fall.
3. A nurse checked the *bandaged* wound.

---

### bang-up

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbæŋˌʌp/ &middot; *BANG-uhp*
**Русский:** отличный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** attributive only — usually *a bang-up job*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** American informal. Not the same as *banged-up*, which means damaged or bruised.

Excellent (informal).

1. You did a *bang-up* job on the kitchen.
2. The team had a *bang-up* season.
3. They threw a *bang-up* party for his retirement.

---

### Bangladeshi

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbɑːŋɡləˈdɛʃi/ &middot; *bahng-gluh-DESH-ee*
**Русский:** бангладешский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -i from *Bangladesh*

From or relating to Bangladesh.

1. The *Bangladeshi* restaurants in Queens serve great curry.
2. He grew up in a *Bangladeshi* family in New York.
3. My coworker is *Bangladeshi*.

---

### bankable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbæŋkəbəl/ &middot; *BANG-kuh-buhl*
**Русский:** прибыльный; кассовый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bankable*, *most bankable*
**Form:** -able from *bank*

Sure to make money.

1. She's one of the most *bankable* stars in Hollywood.
2. Superhero movies are still *bankable*.
3. The investors didn't think the idea was *bankable*.

---

### bankrupt

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbæŋkrʌpt/ &middot; *BANGK-ruhpt*
**Русский:** обанкротившийся; лишённый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *of* + noun, in the sense 'completely lacking'
**Contrast:** Say *go bankrupt*. *Bankrupt* is also a verb (*The lawsuit bankrupted them*) and a noun (*a bankrupt*).

Bankrupt has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Officially unable to pay one's debts.** (обанкротившийся)

1. The company went *bankrupt* in 2009.
2. A *bankrupt* airline left thousands of passengers stuck.

**2. Completely without a good quality.** (лишённый (чего-то), несостоятельный)

3. Critics called the government morally *bankrupt*.
4. The campaign is *bankrupt* of new ideas.

---

### banned

**Pronunciation:** /bænd/ &middot; *BAND*
**Русский:** запрещённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *from* + noun or *-ing*
**Form:** -ed from *ban*

Officially not allowed.

1. The athlete tested positive for a *banned* substance.
2. Fireworks are *banned* in this county.
3. He's *banned* from the gym for a month.

---

### banner

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbænər/ &middot; *BAN-ur*
**Русский:** рекордный; выдающийся
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** attributive only — *a banner year*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Only before a noun, mostly *a banner year* or *a banner season*. The noun *banner* is a flag or a long sign.

Unusually good or successful.

1. It was a *banner* year for the company.
2. Farmers had a *banner* harvest.
3. The Braves are coming off a *banner* season.

---

### baptized

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbæptaɪzd/ &middot; *BAP-tyzd*
**Русский:** крещёный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed participle of *baptize*

Having gone through the Christian ceremony of baptism.

1. She's a *baptized* Catholic.
2. Only *baptized* members can take communion.
3. He was raised in the church but was never *baptized*.

---

### Barbadian

**Pronunciation:** /bɑːrˈbeɪdiən/ &middot; *bar-BAY-dee-uhn*
**Русский:** барбадосский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ian from *Barbados*
**Contrast:** The informal word is *Bajan*.

From or relating to Barbados.

1. Rihanna is *Barbadian*.
2. *Barbadian* rum is famous around the world.
3. They flew the *Barbadian* flag at the festival.

---

### barbaric

**Pronunciation:** /bɑːrˈbærɪk/ &middot; *bar-BAIR-ik*
**Русский:** варварский; жестокий
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more barbaric*, *most barbaric*
**Form:** -ic from *barbarian*
**Opposite:** *civilized*

Extremely cruel and violent.

1. Many people think the death penalty is *barbaric*.
2. It was a *barbaric* attack on civilians.
3. Conditions in the prison were even more *barbaric* than reported.

---

### barbecued

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɑːrbɪkjuːd/ &middot; *BAR-bi-kyood*
**Русский:** жаренный на гриле; барбекю
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *barbecue*

Cooked slowly over a fire or on a grill, often with a sauce.

1. We had *barbecued* ribs for dinner.
2. The chicken is *barbecued* over charcoal.
3. They sell *barbecued* corn at the fair.

---

### barbed

**Pronunciation:** /bɑːrbd/ &middot; *BARBD*
**Русский:** колючий; язвительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more barbed*, *most barbed*
**Form:** -ed from *barb* 'a sharp point'

Barbed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having sharp points that stick out backward.** (колючий, с шипами)

1. The farm is surrounded by a *barbed* wire fence.
2. The hook is *barbed* so the fish can't get off.

**2. Meant to hurt someone's feelings.** (язвительный, колкий)

3. She made a *barbed* comment about his weight.
4. His jokes got more *barbed* as the night went on.

---

### bare

**Pronunciation:** /bɛr/ &middot; *BAIR*
**Русский:** голый; пустой; минимальный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — before a noun only in the sense 'the smallest amount'
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *barer*, *barest*
**Contrast:** Sounds exactly like *bear*. For 'naked' about a whole person Americans usually say *naked*; *bare* is for parts of the body and surfaces.

Bare has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Not covered by clothes or anything else.** (голый, босой, непокрытый)

1. Don't walk on the hot sand in *bare* feet.
2. The trees were *bare* by November.
3. He slept on the *bare* floor.

**2. Empty; without the usual things.** (пустой)

4. Store shelves were *bare* before the hurricane.
5. The apartment still had *bare* walls.

**3. Only the most basic; the smallest amount needed.** (самый необходимый, минимальный)

6. We packed only the *bare* necessities.
7. He did the *bare* minimum to pass.
8. There wasn't even the *barest* hint of a smile.

---

### bare-knuckle

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɛrˌnʌkəl/ &middot; *BAIR-nuhk-uhl*
**Русский:** кулачный; беспощадный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — *a bare-knuckle fight*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *bare* + *knuckle*

Bare-knuckle has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Fought with fists and no boxing gloves.** (кулачный, без перчаток)

1. *Bare-knuckle* boxing is legal in some states.
2. The two men got into a *bare-knuckle* fight outside the bar.

**2. Aggressive and without any rules or restraint.** (беспощадный, жёсткий)

3. It was a *bare-knuckle* political campaign.
4. The firm is known for *bare-knuckle* business tactics.

---

### bareback

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɛrˌbæk/ &middot; *BAIR-bak*
**Русский:** без седла
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — *a bareback rider*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *bare* + *back*
**Contrast:** More often an adverb: *She rode bareback*.

Riding a horse without a saddle.

1. *Bareback* riding is one of the toughest rodeo events.
2. The circus had a *bareback* rider in a sparkly costume.
3. The ranch offers *bareback* lessons for experienced riders.

---

### barefoot

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɛrfʊt/ &middot; *BAIR-fuut*
**Русский:** босой
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *bare* + *foot*
**Contrast:** Also an adverb (*walk barefoot*), with the same form. *Barefooted* exists but is less common.

Not wearing shoes or socks.

1. She was *barefoot* when she answered the door.
2. Two *barefoot* kids were playing in the yard.
3. Is it OK to go *barefoot* in here?

---

### barometric

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbærəˈmɛtrɪk/ &middot; *bair-uh-MET-rik*
**Русский:** барометрический; атмосферный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *barometric pressure*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ic from *barometer*

Relating to air pressure or a barometer.

1. *Barometric* pressure drops before a storm.
2. Changes in *barometric* pressure give me headaches.
3. The *barometric* reading was unusually low.

---

### baroque

**Pronunciation:** /bəˈroʊk/ &middot; *buh-ROHK*
**Русский:** барочный; вычурный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more baroque*, *most baroque*
**Contrast:** For the historical style it is often written with a capital: *Baroque music*.

Baroque has 2 distinct senses.

**1. In the style of European art, buildings and music of about 1600 to 1750.** (барочный, в стиле барокко)

1. We went to a concert of *baroque* music.
2. The church has a painted *baroque* ceiling.

**2. Too complicated or full of decoration.** (вычурный, запутанный)

3. The tax rules are *baroque*.
4. His writing is even more *baroque* than his speeches.

---

### barred

**Pronunciation:** /bɑːrd/ &middot; *BARD*
**Русский:** зарешёченный; не допущенный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *from* + noun or *-ing*, in the sense 'not allowed'
**Form:** -ed from *bar*

Barred has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having bars across it.** (зарешёченный, запертый на засов)

1. The house has *barred* windows.
2. The door was *barred* from the inside.

**2. Not allowed to do something or go somewhere.** (не допущенный, лишённый права)

3. He is *barred* from entering the country.
4. Reporters were *barred* from the meeting.

---

### barren

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbærən/ &middot; *BAIR-uhn*
**Русский:** бесплодный; пустынный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more barren*, *most barren*
**Takes:** *of* + noun, in the sense 'without'
**Opposite:** *fertile*
**Contrast:** *Barren* for a woman who cannot have children is old-fashioned and now sounds offensive.

Barren has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Too dry or poor for plants to grow.** (бесплодный, голый, пустынный)

1. The land around the town is flat and *barren*.
2. They drove through miles of *barren* desert.
3. The hills looked even more *barren* in winter.

**2. Producing no results or useful ideas.** (бесплодный, безрезультатный)

4. It was a *barren* period in her career.
5. The talks were *barren* of progress.

---

### barricaded

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbærəkeɪdɪd/ &middot; *BAIR-uh-kay-did*
**Русский:** забаррикадированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *barricade*

Blocked or shut in behind a barrier.

1. Police surrounded the *barricaded* house.
2. The streets were *barricaded* for the parade.
3. A *barricaded* suspect refused to come out.

---

### base

**Pronunciation:** /beɪs/ &middot; *BAYS*
**Русский:** низменный; неблагородный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *baser*, *basest*
**Contrast:** Formal. *Base* is far more often a noun (*a military base*) or a verb (*based on*).

Base has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Morally low; without honor.** (низменный, подлый)

1. The ad appeals to people's *basest* instincts.
2. Greed is one of the *baser* human emotions.
3. He was driven by *base* motives.

**2. Of a metal: common and not valuable.** (неблагородный (о металле))

4. Alchemists tried to turn *base* metals into gold.
5. The ring is a *base* metal with gold plating.

---

### based

**Pronunciation:** /beɪst/ &middot; *BAYST*
**Русский:** базирующийся; основанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — before a noun only in compounds: *a Denver-based company*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *in* or *at* + place; *on* + source
**Preposition patterns:** [based on](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#based-on)
**Form:** -ed from *base*
**Contrast:** ✗ *basing on the numbers* → *based on the numbers*.

Based has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having its main office or home in a place.** (базирующийся, находящийся)

1. The company is *based* in Seattle.
2. She works for a *Denver-based* startup.
3. Where are you *based*?

**2. Using something as the starting point or source.** (основанный)

4. The movie is *based* on a true story.
5. Her decision was *based* on the facts.

---

### baseless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbeɪsləs/ &middot; *BAYS-luhs*
**Русский:** беспочвенный; необоснованный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *base*
**Opposite:** *well-founded*

Not supported by any facts.

1. The accusations are completely *baseless*.
2. He spread *baseless* rumors about his rival.
3. The judge threw out the *baseless* lawsuit.

---

### bashful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbæʃfəl/ &middot; *BASH-fuhl*
**Русский:** застенчивый
**Type:** quality; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bashful*, *most bashful*
**Form:** -ful from *bash*, short for *abash* 'to embarrass'
**Opposite:** *outgoing*

Shy and easily embarrassed.

1. He gave her a *bashful* smile.
2. Don't be *bashful*; come say hi.
3. She was more *bashful* than her sisters.

---

### basic

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbeɪsɪk/ &middot; *BAY-sik*
**Русский:** основной; простой; заурядный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more basic*, *most basic*
**Form:** -ic from *base*
**Opposite:** *advanced*
**Contrast:** Russian *основной* often means *main* (*the main idea*), not *basic*. *Basic* is 'the simplest, necessary level'.

Basic has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Forming the main or most necessary part.** (основной, базовый)

1. Food and shelter are *basic* human needs.
2. First learn the *basic* rules of the game.

**2. Simple, with nothing extra.** (простой, элементарный)

3. The hotel room was pretty *basic*.
4. I only know some *basic* Spanish.
5. This is the most *basic* model they sell.

**3. Ordinary and unoriginal, following popular trends (slang).** (банальный, заурядный)

6. Pumpkin spice lattes? That's so *basic*.
7. She thinks his taste in music is kind of *basic*.

---

### bastardized

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbæstərdaɪzd/ &middot; *BAS-tur-dyzd*
**Русский:** искажённый; испорченный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed participle of *bastardize*

Changed in a way that makes it worse than the original.

1. The restaurant serves a *bastardized* version of Italian food.
2. The movie is a *bastardized* version of the novel.
3. Over the years the song became more and more *bastardized*.

---

### battered

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbætərd/ &middot; *BAT-urd*
**Русский:** потрёпанный; избитый; в кляре
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more battered*, *most battered*
**Form:** -ed from *batter*

Battered has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Old and damaged from a lot of use.** (потрёпанный, побитый)

1. He drives a *battered* old pickup.
2. My copy of the book is more *battered* than yours.

**2. Hurt by repeated violence.** (избитый)

3. The shelter helps *battered* women and their children.
4. The *battered* boxer could barely stand.

**3. Covered in batter and fried.** (в кляре)

5. We ordered *battered* shrimp.
6. The fish is *battered* and deep-fried.

---

### battery-powered

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbætəriˌpaʊərd/ &middot; *BAT-uh-ree-pow-urd*
**Русский:** на батарейках; аккумуляторный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun *battery* + participle *powered*

Getting its power from a battery.

1. I bought a *battery-powered* lawn mower.
2. The lamp is *battery-powered*, so you can use it outside.
3. *Battery-powered* cars are getting cheaper.

---

### battle-scarred

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbætəlˌskɑːrd/ &middot; *BAT-uhl-skard*
**Русский:** израненный в боях; потрёпанный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *battle* + *scarred*

Damaged or marked by fighting or long struggle.

1. A *battle-scarred* veteran spoke at the ceremony.
2. The *battle-scarred* city is slowly rebuilding.
3. After the long campaign, the senator looked tired and *battle-scarred*.

---

### batty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbæti/ &middot; *BAT-ee*
**Русский:** чокнутый, чудаковатый
**Type:** quality — informal
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *battier*, *battiest*
**Contrast:** Informal and mild, often affectionate. *Drive someone batty* means 'annoy someone a lot'.

Slightly crazy or strange in a harmless way.

1. My *batty* old aunt still talks to her plants.
2. That constant beeping is driving me *batty*.
3. His ideas got *battier* every year.

---

### Bavarian

**Pronunciation:** /bəˈvɛriən/ &middot; *buh-VAIR-ee-uhn*
**Русский:** баварский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ian from *Bavaria*

Of or from Bavaria, a state in southern Germany.

1. We ate at a *Bavarian* restaurant in Chicago.
2. The *Bavarian* Alps are beautiful in winter.
3. Her grandparents were *Bavarian*.

---

### bawdy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɔːdi/ &middot; *BAW-dee*
**Русский:** непристойный, похабный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *bawdier*, *bawdiest*

Funny in a rude way about sex.

1. The comedian told a few *bawdy* jokes.
2. The play is pretty *bawdy* for a high school audience.
3. The sailors sang the *bawdiest* songs they knew.

---

### beaded

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbiːdɪd/ &middot; *BEE-did*
**Русский:** расшитый бисером; покрытый каплями
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun (drops of liquid)
**Form:** -ed from the noun *bead*

Beaded has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Decorated with small beads.** (расшитый бисером, украшенный бусинами)

1. She wore a *beaded* dress to the wedding.
2. Her moccasins are *beaded* and fringed.

**2. Covered with small drops of liquid.** (покрытый каплями)

3. His forehead was *beaded* with sweat.
4. The cold glass was *beaded* with water.

---

### beady

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbiːdi/ &middot; *BEE-dee*
**Русский:** маленький и блестящий (о глазах)
**Type:** shape — of eyes
**Position:** attributive only — almost always *beady eyes*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *beadier*, *beadiest*

(Of eyes) small, round and shiny, often in a way that seems unfriendly or greedy.

1. The crow watched us with its *beady* black eyes.
2. I don't trust that guy with the *beady* little eyes.
3. The villain had the *beadiest* eyes in the whole cartoon.

---

### beaming

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbiːmɪŋ/ &middot; *BEE-ming*
**Русский:** сияющий (от радости)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — after a verb it is the verb *beam*: *She was beaming*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *beam* (to smile widely)

Smiling widely because very happy.

1. The *beaming* parents posed with their son at graduation.
2. She walked in with a *beaming* smile.
3. The *beaming* winner held up the trophy.

---

### bearable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɛrəbəl/ &middot; *BAIR-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** терпимый, сносный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bearable*, *most bearable*
**Form:** -able from *bear* (to put up with)
**Opposite:** *unbearable*

Possible to accept or deal with, though unpleasant.

1. The heat was *bearable* once the sun went down.
2. A good book makes a long flight more *bearable*.
3. The pain was barely *bearable*.

---

### bearded

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɪrdɪd/ &middot; *BEER-did*
**Русский:** бородатый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from the noun *beard*
**Opposite:** *clean-shaven*

Having a beard.

1. A *bearded* man in a flannel shirt answered the door.
2. Half the guys in the band are *bearded*.
3. He's been *bearded* since college.

---

### bearish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɛrɪʃ/ &middot; *BAIR-ish*
**Русский:** медвежий (о рынке); медвежеватый
**Type:** classifying — finance; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bearish*, *most bearish*
**Takes:** *on* or *about* + noun: *bearish on tech stocks*
**Form:** -ish from *bear* (a trader who expects prices to fall)
**Opposite:** *bullish*

Bearish has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Expecting prices on a financial market to fall.** (медвежий, ожидающий падения цен)

1. Many analysts are *bearish* on oil right now.
2. The market has been *bearish* for months.
3. Investors grew more *bearish* after the jobs report.

**2. Big, heavy and a little rough, like a bear.** (медвежеватый, грузный)

4. He gave me a *bearish* hug.
5. Her dad is a *bearish* guy with a surprisingly soft voice.

---

### beat

**Pronunciation:** /biːt/ &middot; *BEET*
**Русский:** вымотанный, выдохшийся
**Type:** quality — informal
**Position:** predicative only — never before a noun: *a tired worker*, not *a beat worker*
**Comparison:** not gradable — an extreme word like *exhausted*: *totally beat*
**Contrast:** *I'm beat* is not the past tense of the verb; it means 'I'm exhausted'. The participle of the verb is *beaten*: *a beaten team*.

Very tired.

1. I'm *beat* — I'm going to bed.
2. After the double shift she was totally *beat*.
3. You look *beat*. Sit down for a minute.

---

### beat-up

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbiːtˈʌp/ &middot; *beet-UP*
**Русский:** потрёпанный, побитый
**Type:** compound — informal
**Position:** both — hyphenated before a noun; *beat up* or *beat-up* after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more beat-up*, *most beat-up*
**Form:** compound — *beat* + *up*
**Opposite:** *brand-new*
**Contrast:** *Beat someone up* is the phrasal verb 'hit someone many times'. The adjective is about things worn out by use.

Old and in bad condition from a lot of use.

1. He drives a *beat-up* old pickup.
2. My sneakers are pretty *beat-up*.
3. This couch is even more *beat-up* than the last one.

---

### beaten

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbiːtən/ &middot; *BEE-tuhn*
**Русский:** побеждённый; проторённый; взбитый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — *a beaten team*, *He looked beaten*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** past participle of *beat*
**Contrast:** *Off the beaten path* is the usual American form; British English says *off the beaten track*.

Beaten has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Defeated, and feeling that you have lost.** (побеждённый, сломленный)

1. The *beaten* team walked slowly off the field.
2. After the third rejection he felt *beaten*.

**2. (Of a path) worn flat by many people walking on it.** (проторённый)

3. We followed a *beaten* path through the woods.
4. The best restaurants are off the *beaten* path.

**3. (Of eggs or cream) mixed quickly until smooth.** (взбитый)

5. Add two *beaten* eggs to the flour.
6. Brush the dough with *beaten* egg before baking.

---

### beautiful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbjuːtəfəl/ &middot; *BYOO-tuh-fuhl*
**Русский:** красивый; прекрасный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more beautiful*, *most beautiful*
**Form:** -ful from *beauty*
**Opposite:** *ugly*
**Contrast:** Russian *красивый* works for men too, but English usually calls a man *handsome* or *good-looking*; *a beautiful man* sounds poetic. Never *beautifuller*.

Beautiful has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Very attractive to look at.** (красивый)

1. She has a *beautiful* smile.
2. The view from the top is *beautiful*.
3. It's the most *beautiful* beach I've ever seen.

**2. Very pleasant or good; done very well.** (прекрасный, чудесный)

4. What a *beautiful* day!
5. That was a *beautiful* pass.
6. She's a *beautiful* person, inside and out.

---

### becoming

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈkʌmɪŋ/ &middot; *bih-KUM-ing*
**Русский:** идущий (к лицу), подобающий
**Type:** quality — formal
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more becoming*, *most becoming*
**Form:** -ing from *become* (to suit)
**Opposite:** *unbecoming*
**Contrast:** Old-fashioned. Everyday English says *That color looks good on you* or *suits you*. The negative is more common, as in *conduct unbecoming an officer*.

(Of clothes or a hairstyle) making someone look attractive.

1. She wore a *becoming* shade of blue.
2. The short haircut is very *becoming*.
3. I think the green dress is more *becoming* than the red one.

---

### bedraggled

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈdræɡəld/ &middot; *bih-DRAG-uhld*
**Русский:** промокший и растрёпанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bedraggled*, *most bedraggled*

Wet, dirty and messy.

1. A *bedraggled* cat showed up at our door in the rain.
2. The hikers looked *bedraggled* after two days in the storm.
3. By morning the flowers looked even more *bedraggled*.

---

### bedridden

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɛdrɪdən/ &middot; *BED-rid-uhn*
**Русский:** прикованный к постели, лежачий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *bed* + *ridden* (old participle of *ride*)

Too sick or weak to get out of bed.

1. Her grandmother has been *bedridden* for a year.
2. He takes care of his *bedridden* father.
3. The flu left me *bedridden* for a week.

---

### beefed-up

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbiːftˈʌp/ &middot; *beeft-UP*
**Русский:** усиленный
**Type:** participial — informal
**Position:** attributive only — after a verb it is the phrasal verb: *Security was beefed up*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — from the phrasal verb *beef up* (make stronger)

Made bigger, stronger or more effective.

1. There's *beefed-up* security at the airport this week.
2. The new model has a *beefed-up* engine.
3. The *beefed-up* defense held the other team to one goal.

---

### beefy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbiːfi/ &middot; *BEE-fee*
**Русский:** здоровенный, мускулистый; мощный
**Type:** quality — informal
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *beefier*, *beefiest*

Beefy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. (Of a person) big, strong and heavy.** (здоровенный, мускулистый)

1. Two *beefy* guys carried the piano upstairs.
2. He got a lot *beefier* after a year at the gym.

**2. Bigger or more powerful than usual.** (мощный, солидный)

3. The laptop has a *beefy* processor.
4. You'll need a *beefier* battery for that drill.

---

### befitting

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈfɪtɪŋ/ &middot; *bih-FIT-ing*
**Русский:** подобающий, достойный
**Type:** quality — formal
**Position:** after the noun — with its own object: *a welcome befitting a hero*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** a noun directly, with no preposition: *befitting a queen*
**Contrast:** Everyday English prefers *fitting* or *suitable* (*a fitting tribute*). The verb is common too: *as befits a champion*.

Suitable for a particular person or occasion.

1. They gave him a funeral *befitting* a president.
2. She dressed in a style *befitting* her new job.
3. The team celebrated in a manner *befitting* champions.

---

### befuddled

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈfʌdəld/ &middot; *bih-FUD-uhld*
**Русский:** сбитый с толку, озадаченный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more befuddled*, *most befuddled*
**Takes:** *by* + noun

Confused and unable to think clearly.

1. A *befuddled* tourist asked us for directions.
2. I was completely *befuddled* by the new tax forms.
3. The more he explained, the more *befuddled* we got.

---

### beginning

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈɡɪnɪŋ/ &middot; *bih-GIN-ing*
**Русский:** начальный; для начинающих
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *A beginning student* is American; British English prefers *a beginner* or *a beginners' class*. *The beginning* on its own is the noun.

At the first level; for people just starting to learn something.

1. She teaches *beginning* Spanish at the community college.
2. This series is for *beginning* readers.
3. The class is full of *beginning* guitar players.

---

### beguiling

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈɡaɪlɪŋ/ &middot; *bih-GY-ling*
**Русский:** пленительный, обольстительный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more beguiling*, *most beguiling*
**Form:** -ing from *beguile* (to charm or trick)

Charming and attractive, sometimes in a way that misleads.

1. She had a *beguiling* smile.
2. The idea is *beguiling*, but it won't work in practice.
3. It's one of the most *beguiling* novels of the year.

---

### behavioral

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈheɪvjərəl/ &middot; *bih-HAYV-yur-uhl*
**Русский:** поведенческий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *behavior*
**Contrast:** American spelling; British English writes *behavioural*.

Connected with how people or animals behave.

1. The school hired a *behavioral* specialist.
2. *Behavioral* therapy helped him with his anxiety.
3. Scientists studied *behavioral* changes in the mice.

---

### behind

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈhaɪnd/ &middot; *bih-HYND*
**Русский:** отстающий; имеющий задолженность
**Type:** quality
**Position:** predicative only — *we're behind*, never *a behind student*
**Comparison:** not gradable — but *far behind*, *further behind*
**Takes:** *on*, *with* or *in* + noun; *by* + amount
**Contrast:** *Behind* is much more often a preposition (*behind the house*) or an adverb (*stay behind*). Only the 'late' and 'losing' uses after *be* or *get* are adjectival.

Behind has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Late with work, payments or a schedule.** (отстающий, запаздывающий, с задолженностью)

1. We're two weeks *behind* on the project.
2. They fell *behind* on the rent after he lost his job.
3. The flight put us *behind* schedule.

**2. Losing in a game or a contest.** (проигрывающий, отстающий по счёту)

4. The Cubs were *behind* by three runs in the seventh.
5. She's still *behind* in the polls, but the gap is closing.

---

### behind-the-scenes

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˌhaɪnd ðə ˈsiːnz/ &middot; *bih-HYND thuh SEENZ*
**Русский:** закулисный
**Type:** compound — a prepositional phrase
**Position:** attributive only — after a verb it has no hyphens: *work behind the scenes*
**Comparison:** not gradable

Happening in private, out of public view.

1. The DVD includes a *behind-the-scenes* look at the movie.
2. There was a lot of *behind-the-scenes* negotiating before the deal.
3. Her *behind-the-scenes* work kept the campaign running.

---

### beholden

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈhoʊldən/ &middot; *bih-HOHL-duhn*
**Русский:** обязанный, в долгу
**Type:** quality — formal
**Position:** predicative only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + noun
**Preposition patterns:** [beholden to](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#beholden-to)

Owing something to someone, so that you must do what they want.

1. The senator is *beholden* to big donors.
2. I don't want to be *beholden* to anyone.
3. Local papers were *beholden* to their advertisers.

---

### Belarusian

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbɛləˈruːsiən/ &middot; *bel-uh-ROO-see-uhn*
**Русский:** белорусский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ian from *Belarus*
**Contrast:** The older spelling *Byelorussian* is dated. Also a noun for the person and the language.

Of or from Belarus, its people or its language.

1. She's a *Belarusian* tennis player.
2. *Belarusian* and Russian are both official languages there.
3. His parents are *Belarusian*.

---

### belated

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈleɪtɪd/ &middot; *bih-LAY-tid*
**Русский:** запоздалый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** be- + *late* + -ed

Coming or happening later than it should.

1. Happy *belated* birthday!
2. I sent her a *belated* thank-you note.
3. His apology was sincere but *belated*.

---

### Belgian

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɛldʒən/ &middot; *BEL-juhn*
**Русский:** бельгийский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ian from *Belgium*

Of or from Belgium.

1. We had *Belgian* waffles for breakfast.
2. Is this chocolate *Belgian*?
3. The *Belgian* rider won the race.

---

### believable

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈliːvəbəl/ &middot; *bih-LEE-vuh-buhl*
**Русский:** правдоподобный, убедительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more believable*, *most believable*
**Form:** -able from *believe*
**Opposite:** *unbelievable*
**Contrast:** In everyday talk *unbelievable* usually means 'amazing' (*The game was unbelievable!*); *believable* only means 'realistic, easy to believe'.

Easy to believe; seeming real.

1. The characters in the show are very *believable*.
2. He needed a *believable* excuse for being late.
3. Her second version of the story was more *believable*.

---

### belittling

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈlɪtlɪŋ/ &middot; *bih-LIT-ling*
**Русский:** унизительный, пренебрежительный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more belittling*, *most belittling*
**Form:** -ing from *belittle*

Making someone or something seem unimportant.

1. Her boss made *belittling* comments about her work.
2. His tone was *belittling*.
3. It was the most *belittling* thing anyone had ever said to me.

---

### bell-bottom

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɛlˌbɑːtəm/ &middot; *BEL-bah-tuhm*
**Русский:** расклёшенный (о брюках)
**Type:** shape; also compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *bell* + *bottom*; the pants themselves are *bell-bottoms*

(Of pants) very wide at the bottom of the legs.

1. She found a pair of *bell-bottom* jeans at a thrift store.
2. *Bell-bottom* pants were everywhere in the 1970s.
3. The sailors wore white *bell-bottom* trousers.

---

### bell-shaped

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɛlʃeɪpt/ &middot; *BEL-shaypt*
**Русский:** колоколообразный
**Type:** shape
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *bell* + *shaped*

Shaped like a bell, narrow at the top and wide at the bottom.

1. Test scores usually fall on a *bell-shaped* curve.
2. The plant has small *bell-shaped* flowers.
3. The lampshade is *bell-shaped*.

---

### bellicose

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɛlɪkoʊs/ &middot; *BEL-ih-kohs*
**Русский:** воинственный
**Type:** quality — formal
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bellicose*, *most bellicose*

Eager to fight or start a war.

1. The general's *bellicose* speech worried the neighboring countries.
2. The president's tone has become more *bellicose*.
3. He gets *bellicose* when he drinks.

---

### belligerent

**Pronunciation:** /bəˈlɪdʒərənt/ &middot; *buh-LIJ-ur-uhnt*
**Русский:** агрессивный, враждебный; воюющий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more belligerent*, *most belligerent*

Belligerent has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Angry and ready to argue or fight.** (агрессивный, враждебный)

1. The *belligerent* customer was asked to leave.
2. He got *belligerent* when the police arrived.
3. Her emails grew more *belligerent* every week.

**2. (Formal, of a country or group) fighting in a war.** (воюющий)

4. The *belligerent* nations agreed to a ceasefire.
5. Neither of the *belligerent* sides would give up the city.

---

### belly-up

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbɛliˈʌp/ &middot; *bel-ee-UP*
**Русский:** обанкротившийся, прогоревший
**Type:** compound — informal
**Position:** predicative only — almost always *go belly-up*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *belly* + *up*, like a dead fish floating upside down

(Of a business) failed or bankrupt.

1. The restaurant went *belly-up* after a year.
2. Half the startups in that building have gone *belly-up*.
3. If sales don't improve, we'll be *belly-up* by summer.

---

### beloved

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈlʌvɪd/ &middot; *bih-LUV-id*
**Русский:** любимый, горячо любимый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *most beloved* is common
**Takes:** *by* + noun

Loved very much.

1. He was a *beloved* teacher at the school for thirty years.
2. The park is *beloved* by locals.
3. She's one of the most *beloved* singers in the country.

---

### below the belt

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˌloʊ ðə ˈbɛlt/ &middot; *bih-LOH thuh BELT*
**Русский:** нечестный, подлый («ниже пояса»)
**Type:** quality — idiom
**Position:** both — *That was below the belt*; hyphenated before a noun: *a below-the-belt joke*
**Comparison:** not gradable — though *a little below the belt* is common
**Form:** idiom — from boxing, where hitting below the belt is against the rules

Unfair and hurtful.

1. That comment about his divorce was *below the belt*.
2. Bringing up her weight was a little *below the belt*.
3. The ad was a *below-the-belt* attack on his family.

---

### bemused

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈmjuːzd/ &middot; *bih-MYOOZD*
**Русский:** озадаченный; слегка насмешливый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bemused*, *most bemused*
**Contrast:** A trap: *bemused* traditionally means 'puzzled', not 'amused'. Many Americans now use it for 'mildly amused', so context decides; careful writers keep it for 'puzzled'.

Bemused has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Puzzled and a little confused.** (озадаченный, недоумевающий)

1. He looked *bemused* by all the questions.
2. She gave me a *bemused* look when I answered in French.

**2. Mildly amused, often because something seems odd.** (снисходительно-насмешливый)

3. He watched the kids' argument with a *bemused* smile.
4. She read the angry comments with *bemused* detachment.

---

### bendable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɛndəbəl/ &middot; *BEN-duh-buhl*
**Русский:** гибкий, сгибаемый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bendable*, *most bendable*
**Form:** -able from *bend*
**Opposite:** *rigid*

Easy to bend without breaking.

1. The toy has *bendable* arms and legs.
2. Give the kids *bendable* straws.
3. This wire is more *bendable* than the old kind.

---

### beneficial

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbɛnəˈfɪʃəl/ &middot; *ben-uh-FISH-uhl*
**Русский:** полезный, благотворный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more beneficial*, *most beneficial*
**Takes:** *to* or *for* + noun
**Form:** -ial from *benefit*
**Opposite:** *harmful*

Having a good effect; helpful.

1. Regular exercise is *beneficial* to your health.
2. The deal was *beneficial* for both companies.
3. Some bacteria are actually *beneficial*.

---

### benevolent

**Pronunciation:** /bəˈnɛvələnt/ &middot; *buh-NEV-uh-luhnt*
**Русский:** доброжелательный; благотворительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more benevolent*, *most benevolent*
**Opposite:** *malevolent*

Benevolent has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Kind and wanting to help others.** (доброжелательный, великодушный)

1. He ran the company like a *benevolent* grandfather.
2. The old king was seen as *benevolent*.

**2. (Of an organization) set up to help people in need.** (благотворительный)

3. The *benevolent* society paid for the funeral.
4. He left his money to a police *benevolent* association.

---

### Bengali

**Pronunciation:** /bɛŋˈɡɔːli/ &middot; *beng-GAW-lee*
**Русский:** бенгальский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Covers both Bangladesh and the Indian state of West Bengal. Also the name of the language and a person: *She speaks Bengali*.

Of or from Bengal, its people or its language.

1. We ordered *Bengali* fish curry.
2. *Bengali* literature has a long history.
3. Her family is *Bengali*.

---

### benign

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈnaɪn/ &middot; *bih-NYN*
**Русский:** доброкачественный; безвредный; добрый
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — not gradable in the medical sense
**Opposite:** *malignant* (medical); *harmful*
**Contrast:** The *g* is silent: it rhymes with *nine*.

Benign has 3 distinct senses.

**1. (Of a tumor or growth) not cancerous.** (доброкачественный)

1. Luckily, the tumor was *benign*.
2. The doctor removed a small *benign* growth.

**2. Not harmful or dangerous.** (безвредный, безобидный)

3. Most of these apps are fairly *benign*.
4. The side effects are usually *benign*.

**3. Kind, gentle or mild.** (добрый, мягкий)

5. He had a *benign* smile.
6. The weather was *benign* all week.

---

### bent

**Pronunciation:** /bɛnt/ &middot; *BENT*
**Русский:** согнутый, погнутый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — usually *slightly bent* or *badly bent*
**Form:** past participle of *bend*
**Opposite:** *straight*
**Contrast:** *Bent on* something means 'determined to do it' — see *bent on*. British slang *bent* 'dishonest' is not used in American English.

Bent has 3 distinct senses.

**1. No longer straight; curved or folded.** (согнутый, погнутый)

1. The nail was *bent*, so I pulled it out.
2. Keep your knees *bent* when you lift the box.
3. The front wheel of my bike is *bent*.

**2. (Of a person) bending forward.** (сгорбленный, согнувшийся)

4. An old man, *bent* with age, shuffled past.
5. She sat *bent* over her desk all afternoon.

**3. (bent out of shape, informal) Upset or angry.** (расстроенный, взвинченный)

6. Don't get all *bent* out of shape about it.
7. He got *bent* out of shape over a parking ticket.

---

### bent on

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɛnt ɑːn/ &middot; *BENT ahn*
**Русский:** твёрдо намеренный, одержимый (чем-л.)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** predicative only — also directly after a noun: *a man bent on revenge*
**Comparison:** not gradable — *hell-bent on* is the strong form
**Takes:** *on* + noun or *-ing*

Determined to do something, often something bad.

1. He seems *bent on* ruining my weekend.
2. The team came out *bent on* revenge.
3. She's *bent on* becoming a pilot.

---

### bereaved

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈriːvd/ &middot; *bih-REEVD*
**Русский:** понёсший утрату, скорбящий
**Type:** participial — formal
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *The bereaved* is used as a noun for the people whose relative has died. Compare *bereft*, which is about a feeling of loss or lack.

Having lost a close relative or friend who has died.

1. The *bereaved* family asked for privacy.
2. Counselors are available for *bereaved* parents.
3. She was recently *bereaved* and still in shock.

---

### bereft

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈrɛft/ &middot; *bih-REFT*
**Русский:** лишённый; опустошённый (горем)
**Type:** quality — formal
**Position:** predicative only — *a bereft mother* is rare
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *of* + noun

Bereft has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Completely without something.** (лишённый (чего-л.))

1. The team seemed *bereft* of ideas.
2. The town was *bereft* of jobs after the plant closed.

**2. Very sad and lonely because someone has died or left.** (опустошённый, безутешный)

3. She was *bereft* after her husband died.
4. The kids felt *bereft* when their best friend moved away.

---

### berserk

**Pronunciation:** /bərˈzɜːrk/ &middot; *bur-ZURK*
**Русский:** взбесившийся, неистовый
**Type:** quality — informal
**Position:** predicative only — almost always *go berserk*
**Comparison:** not gradable

Wildly out of control, with anger or excitement.

1. The crowd went *berserk* when the band came on.
2. The dog goes *berserk* whenever the mail carrier comes.
3. My phone went *berserk* and started calling people.

---

### besieged

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈsiːdʒd/ &middot; *bih-SEEJD*
**Русский:** осаждённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *by* or *with* + noun
**Form:** past participle of *besiege*

Besieged has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Surrounded by an army that is trying to capture it.** (осаждённый)

1. Food was running out in the *besieged* city.
2. The *besieged* soldiers held out for six months.

**2. Overwhelmed by many people, questions or requests.** (осаждаемый, заваленный (просьбами))

3. The *besieged* star hid from photographers.
4. Teachers say they feel *besieged* by new rules.

---

### besotted

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈsɑːtɪd/ &middot; *bih-SAH-tid*
**Русский:** без памяти влюблённый
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more besotted*, *most besotted*
**Takes:** *with* + noun

So in love with someone or something that you cannot think clearly.

1. He's completely *besotted* with his new girlfriend.
2. The *besotted* grandparents took hundreds of photos.
3. She's *besotted* with that puppy.

---

### bespectacled

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈspɛktəkəld/ &middot; *bih-SPEK-tuh-kuhld*
**Русский:** в очках
**Type:** quality — formal
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** be- + *spectacles* (glasses) + -ed

Wearing glasses.

1. A *bespectacled* librarian helped us find the book.
2. The suspect was described as tall and *bespectacled*.
3. The *bespectacled* professor peered over his notes.

---

### bespoke

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈspoʊk/ &middot; *bih-SPOHK*
**Русский:** сделанный на заказ, индивидуальный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Originally British. Americans usually say *custom* or *custom-made*; *bespoke* sounds upscale and shows up in marketing (*bespoke software*, *bespoke cocktails*).

Made specially for one customer.

1. He paid $5,000 for a *bespoke* suit.
2. The agency builds *bespoke* software for banks.
3. Every piece of furniture in the hotel is *bespoke*.

---

### best-known

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbɛstˈnoʊn/ &middot; *best-NOHN*
**Русский:** самый известный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — hyphenated before a noun; *best known* after a verb
**Comparison:** irregular — the superlative of *well-known*: *well-known*, *better-known*, *best-known*
**Takes:** *for* + noun or *-ing*
**Form:** compound — *best* (superlative of *well*) + *known*

Known by the most people.

1. It's her *best-known* novel.
2. He's *best known* for his role in a TV comedy.
3. The *best-known* landmark in the city is the bridge.

---

### best-loved

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbɛstˈlʌvd/ &middot; *best-LUVD*
**Русский:** самый любимый
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — hyphenated before a noun; *best loved* after a verb
**Comparison:** irregular — the superlative of *well-loved*: *well-loved*, *best-loved*
**Form:** compound — *best* + *loved*

Loved by the most people.

1. It's one of America's *best-loved* songs.
2. The bakery's *best-loved* item is the cinnamon roll.
3. Of all his books, this one is *best loved* by kids.

---

### best-selling

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbɛstˈsɛlɪŋ/ &middot; *best-SEL-ing*
**Русский:** самый продаваемый; популярный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable — *best* is already built in
**Form:** compound — *best* + *selling*; also written *bestselling*
**Contrast:** *A best-selling author* usually just means 'very popular, on the bestseller list', not literally number one.

Selling in very large numbers.

1. She's a *best-selling* author with over twenty novels.
2. It was the *best-selling* car in America last year.
3. The album became their *best-selling* record.

---

### better off

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbɛtər ˈɔːf/ &middot; *bet-ur AWF*
**Русский:** в лучшем положении; более обеспеченный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb; hyphenated before a noun: *better-off families*
**Comparison:** irregular — the comparative of *well off*: *well off*, *better off*, *best off*
**Takes:** *-ing*; *without* + noun
**Opposite:** *worse off*
**Contrast:** *You'd be better off* + *-ing* is a common way to give advice: *You'd be better off calling first*.

Better off has 2 distinct senses.

**1. In a better situation.** (в лучшем положении)

1. You'd be *better off* taking the train.
2. She's *better off* without him.
3. We'd have been *best off* staying home.

**2. Having more money.** (более обеспеченный, зажиточный)

4. Most families are *better off* than they were ten years ago.
5. The *better-off* neighborhoods are on the north side.

---

### better-known

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbɛtərˈnoʊn/ &middot; *bet-ur-NOHN*
**Русский:** более известный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — hyphenated before a noun; *better known* after a verb
**Comparison:** irregular — the comparative of *well-known*: *well-known*, *better-known*, *best-known*
**Takes:** *as* or *for* + noun
**Form:** compound — *better* (comparative of *well*) + *known*
**Contrast:** *Better known as* introduces the more familiar name: *Samuel Clemens, better known as Mark Twain*.

Known by more people.

1. He's *better known* as a singer than as an actor.
2. The *better-known* brands cost twice as much.
3. Samuel Clemens, *better known* as Mark Twain, grew up in Missouri.

---

### better-looking

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbɛtərˈlʊkɪŋ/ &middot; *bet-ur-LUUK-ing*
**Русский:** более привлекательный, симпатичнее
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** irregular — the comparative of *good-looking*: *good-looking*, *better-looking*, *best-looking*
**Form:** compound — *better* + *looking*

More attractive to look at.

1. He's much *better-looking* in person.
2. The new phone is *better-looking* than the old one.
3. She married the *best-looking* guy in her class.

---

### bewildered

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈwɪldərd/ &middot; *bih-WIL-durd*
**Русский:** сбитый с толку, растерянный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bewildered*, *most bewildered*
**Takes:** *by* or *at* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *bewilder*
**Contrast:** *Bewildered* is how you feel; *bewildering* is what causes the feeling: *a bewildering choice of cereals*.

Very confused and not sure what to do.

1. A *bewildered* tourist stood in the middle of the station.
2. I was *bewildered* by all the forms.
3. She looked even more *bewildered* after his explanation.

---

### bewitched

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈwɪtʃt/ &middot; *bih-WICHT*
**Русский:** заколдованный; очарованный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — not gradable in the magic sense
**Takes:** *by* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *bewitch*

Bewitched has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Under a magic spell.** (заколдованный)

1. In the story, the prince lives in a *bewitched* castle.
2. The villagers believed the well was *bewitched*.

**2. Strongly attracted or charmed.** (очарованный, околдованный)

3. He was completely *bewitched* by her voice.
4. *Bewitched* audiences stayed until the last song.

---

### bewitching

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈwɪtʃɪŋ/ &middot; *bih-WICH-ing*
**Русский:** чарующий, обворожительный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bewitching*, *most bewitching*
**Form:** -ing from *bewitch*

So beautiful or charming that you cannot stop looking or listening.

1. She had *bewitching* green eyes.
2. The music was strange and *bewitching*.
3. It's the most *bewitching* place I've ever visited.

---

### beyond doubt

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˌjɑːnd ˈdaʊt/ &middot; *bih-yahnd DOWT*
**Русский:** несомненный, бесспорный
**Type:** quality — a prepositional phrase
**Position:** predicative only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also an adverb phrase after verbs: *proved beyond doubt*. The American legal standard is *beyond a reasonable doubt*.

Certain; impossible to question.

1. Her talent is *beyond doubt*.
2. The facts of the case are now *beyond doubt*.
3. His loyalty to the team was never *beyond doubt*.

---

### Bhutanese

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbuːtəˈniːz/ &middot; *boo-tuh-NEEZ*
**Русский:** бутанский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ese from *Bhutan*

Of or from Bhutan.

1. *Bhutanese* food is famous for its chili peppers.
2. The *Bhutanese* government measures 'Gross National Happiness'.
3. Many families in the neighborhood are *Bhutanese*.

---

### biannual

**Pronunciation:** /baɪˈænjuəl/ &middot; *by-AN-yoo-uhl*
**Русский:** проходящий два раза в год
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** bi- (two) + *annual*
**Contrast:** Easy to confuse with *biennial* (every two years). *Biannual* means twice a year; *twice-yearly* is clearer.

Happening twice a year.

1. The store's *biannual* sale is in January and July.
2. Don't forget your *biannual* dental checkup.
3. The board holds *biannual* meetings in spring and fall.

---

### biased

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbaɪəst/ &middot; *BY-uhst*
**Русский:** предвзятый, необъективный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more biased*, *most biased*
**Takes:** *against*, *toward* or *in favor of* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *bias*
**Opposite:** *unbiased*; *impartial*

Unfairly favoring one side.

1. The referee was clearly *biased* against our team.
2. Many viewers think the coverage is *biased*.
3. The survey questions were *biased* toward younger people.

---

### biblical

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɪblɪkəl/ &middot; *BIB-lih-kuhl*
**Русский:** библейский; грандиозный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *Bible*

Biblical has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to or found in the Bible.** (библейский)

1. The movie tells a *biblical* story.
2. The name Noah is *biblical*.

**2. Very large or extreme, like events in the Bible.** (грандиозный, катастрофический)

3. The city faced a traffic jam of *biblical* proportions.
4. The flooding this spring was *biblical*.

---

### bibliographical

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbɪbliəˈɡræfɪkəl/ &middot; *bib-lee-uh-GRAF-ih-kuhl*
**Русский:** библиографический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *bibliography*; also *bibliographic*

Relating to lists of books and articles.

1. The book ends with a long *bibliographical* note.
2. Check the *bibliographical* details before you submit the paper.
3. The library keeps *bibliographical* records for every item.

---

### bicameral

**Pronunciation:** /baɪˈkæmərəl/ &middot; *by-KAM-ur-uhl*
**Русский:** двухпалатный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** bi- (two) + Latin *camera* (chamber)

(Of a lawmaking body) having two parts, or houses.

1. The US Congress is a *bicameral* legislature.
2. Nebraska is the only state whose legislature is not *bicameral*.
3. Most large democracies have *bicameral* parliaments.

---

### bicentennial

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbaɪsɛnˈtɛniəl/ &middot; *by-sen-TEN-ee-uhl*
**Русский:** двухсотлетний (юбилейный)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** bi- + *centennial*
**Contrast:** American English; British English prefers *bicentenary*. Also a noun: *the nation's bicentennial in 1976*.

Marking two hundred years since something happened.

1. The town held a *bicentennial* parade.
2. My parents still have a *bicentennial* quarter from 1976.
3. The university is planning its *bicentennial* celebration.

---

### biennial

**Pronunciation:** /baɪˈɛniəl/ &middot; *by-EN-ee-uhl*
**Русский:** проходящий раз в два года; двулетний
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** bi- (two) + Latin *annus* (year)
**Contrast:** *Biennial* is every two years; *biannual* is twice a year.

Biennial has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Happening every two years.** (проходящий раз в два года)

1. The art show is a *biennial* event.
2. The *biennial* conference will meet again in 2028.

**2. (Of a plant) living for two years.** (двулетний)

3. Parsley is a *biennial* plant.
4. *Biennial* flowers bloom only in their second year.

---

### big-boned

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbɪɡˈboʊnd/ &middot; *big-BOHND*
**Русский:** ширококостный, крупный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *big* + *bone* + -ed
**Contrast:** Often a gentle or joking way to avoid saying 'overweight'.

Having a large, heavy body frame.

1. He's a *big-boned* farm kid from Iowa.
2. I'm not fat, I'm just *big-boned*!
3. The breed is tall and *big-boned*.

---

### big-ticket

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbɪɡˈtɪkɪt/ &middot; *big-TIK-it*
**Русский:** дорогостоящий
**Type:** compound — informal
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *big* + *ticket* (price tag)

Expensive.

1. Families are putting off *big-ticket* purchases like cars.
2. Refrigerators and TVs are *big-ticket* items.
3. The *big-ticket* projects were cut from the budget.

---

### bigoted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɪɡətɪd/ &middot; *BIG-uh-tid*
**Русский:** нетерпимый, предубеждённый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bigoted*, *most bigoted*
**Form:** -ed from *bigot*

Having strong, unfair opinions about people of another race, religion or group.

1. He made a *bigoted* comment about immigrants.
2. Her uncle's views are openly *bigoted*.
3. It was the most *bigoted* speech I've ever heard.

---

### bilateral

**Pronunciation:** /baɪˈlætərəl/ &middot; *by-LAT-ur-uhl*
**Русский:** двусторонний
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** bi- (two) + *lateral* (of a side)

Bilateral has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Between two countries or groups.** (двусторонний)

1. The two countries signed a *bilateral* trade agreement.
2. *Bilateral* talks will continue next week.

**2. (Medical) on both sides of the body.** (двусторонний (с обеих сторон тела))

3. She had *bilateral* knee surgery.
4. The hearing loss is *bilateral*.

---

### bilingual

**Pronunciation:** /baɪˈlɪŋɡwəl/ &middot; *by-LING-gwuhl*
**Русский:** двуязычный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** bi- (two) + Latin *lingua* (tongue)

Speaking two languages well, or using two languages.

1. Their kids are *bilingual* in English and Spanish.
2. The school offers *bilingual* classes.
3. Road signs in the area are *bilingual*.

---

### billowing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɪloʊɪŋ/ &middot; *BIL-oh-ing*
**Русский:** клубящийся, вздымающийся
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — after a verb it is the verb *billow*: *smoke was billowing*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *billow*

Rising or spreading out in large waves or clouds.

1. *Billowing* smoke rose from the warehouse.
2. She wore a white dress with *billowing* sleeves.
3. We watched the *billowing* storm clouds roll in.

---

### bimonthly

**Pronunciation:** /baɪˈmʌnθli/ &middot; *by-MUNTH-lee*
**Русский:** выходящий раз в два месяца; дважды в месяц
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** bi- + *monthly*
**Contrast:** Ambiguous: usually 'every two months', but sometimes 'twice a month'. *Every other month* or *twice a month* (*semimonthly*) is clearer. Also an adverb: *The magazine comes out bimonthly*.

Bimonthly has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Happening every two months.** (выходящий раз в два месяца)

1. The newsletter is *bimonthly*.
2. We get a *bimonthly* water bill.

**2. Happening twice a month.** (проходящий дважды в месяц)

3. The club holds *bimonthly* meetings on the first and third Tuesdays.
4. The *bimonthly* farmers market runs on the 1st and the 15th.

---

### binary

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbaɪnəri/ &middot; *BY-nuh-ree*
**Русский:** двоичный; бинарный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Binary has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Using only the digits 0 and 1.** (двоичный)

1. Computers store data in *binary* code.
2. Write the number 5 in *binary* form.

**2. Having only two parts or two options.** (бинарный, двойственный)

3. Life isn't a *binary* choice between success and failure.
4. Many people reject a *binary* view of gender.

---

### binding

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbaɪndɪŋ/ &middot; *BYN-ding*
**Русский:** обязывающий, имеющий обязательную силу
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *on* or *upon* + noun
**Preposition patterns:** [binding on](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#binding-on-binding-upon) &middot; [binding upon](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#binding-on-binding-upon)
**Form:** -ing from *bind* (to tie; to oblige)
**Contrast:** *The binding* (noun) is the cover and spine of a book, or the part of a ski that holds the boot.

(Of an agreement or decision) that must be obeyed by law.

1. The contract is legally *binding*.
2. The decision is *binding* on both parties.
3. They signed a *binding* agreement.

---

### biochemical

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbaɪoʊˈkɛmɪkəl/ &middot; *by-oh-KEM-ih-kuhl*
**Русский:** биохимический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** bio- + *chemical*

Relating to the chemical processes in living things.

1. Stress causes *biochemical* changes in the brain.
2. She works in a *biochemical* research lab.
3. The test measures *biochemical* markers in the blood.

---

### biodegradable

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbaɪoʊdɪˈɡreɪdəbəl/ &middot; *by-oh-dih-GRAY-duh-buhl*
**Русский:** биоразлагаемый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** bio- + *degrade* + -able
**Opposite:** *nonbiodegradable*

Able to break down naturally and not harm the environment.

1. The cups are made of *biodegradable* plastic.
2. Is this packaging *biodegradable*?
3. Paper bags are *biodegradable*, but they still take energy to make.

---

### biographical

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbaɪəˈɡræfɪkəl/ &middot; *by-uh-GRAF-ih-kuhl*
**Русский:** биографический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *biography*

About the facts of a person's life.

1. The movie is a *biographical* drama about a jazz singer.
2. The website has a short *biographical* note on each author.
3. Please include some *biographical* information with your application.

---

### biological

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbaɪəˈlɑːdʒɪkəl/ &middot; *by-uh-LAH-jih-kuhl*
**Русский:** биологический; родной (по крови); бактериологический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *biology*
**Contrast:** For parents, *biological* is the neutral word; *real mother* can hurt adoptive families. Russian *родной* is wider: *родной язык* is *native language*, not *biological*.

Biological has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to living things or to biology.** (биологический)

1. Sleep is a basic *biological* need.
2. The causes of the disease are partly *biological*.

**2. Related by birth, not by adoption or marriage.** (родной, биологический (по крови))

3. She found her *biological* mother at age thirty.
4. He's not my *biological* dad, but he raised me.

**3. (Of weapons) using germs to cause disease.** (бактериологический, биологический)

5. The treaty bans *biological* weapons.
6. Officials trained for a possible *biological* attack.

---

### biomedical

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbaɪoʊˈmɛdɪkəl/ &middot; *by-oh-MED-ih-kuhl*
**Русский:** биомедицинский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** bio- + *medical*

Relating to both biology and medicine.

1. He studied *biomedical* engineering.
2. The company makes *biomedical* devices like pacemakers.
3. The grant funds *biomedical* research on cancer.

---

### bionic

**Pronunciation:** /baɪˈɑːnɪk/ &middot; *by-AH-nik*
**Русский:** бионический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Bionic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having or being electronic body parts.** (бионический)

1. The veteran walks on a *bionic* leg.
2. Researchers are testing a *bionic* eye.

**2. (Informal) Stronger or better than a normal person.** (сверхчеловеческий)

3. Our goalie has *bionic* reflexes.
4. She must have *bionic* hearing — she heard us from upstairs.

---

### bipartisan

**Pronunciation:** /baɪˈpɑːrtɪzən/ &middot; *by-PAHR-tih-zuhn*
**Русский:** двухпартийный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bipartisan*
**Form:** bi- + *partisan* (of a party)
**Contrast:** A key word in US politics: supported by both Democrats and Republicans.

Supported by or involving both main political parties.

1. The bill passed with *bipartisan* support.
2. A *bipartisan* group of senators wrote the plan.
3. Voters want a more *bipartisan* approach.

---

### bipolar

**Pronunciation:** /baɪˈpoʊlər/ &middot; *by-POH-lur*
**Русский:** биполярный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** bi- + *polar*
