**Contrast:** Using *bipolar* casually for 'moody' (*the weather is so bipolar*) is common but offends many people.

Bipolar has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having bipolar disorder, a mental illness with extreme changes of mood.** (страдающий биполярным расстройством)

1. Her brother was diagnosed with *bipolar* disorder.
2. He's *bipolar* and takes medication for it.

**2. Having two opposite poles or two main powers.** (биполярный, двухполюсный)

3. The *bipolar* world of the Cold War ended in 1991.
4. Scientists studied the star's *bipolar* jets of gas.

---

### biracial

**Pronunciation:** /baɪˈreɪʃəl/ &middot; *by-RAY-shuhl*
**Русский:** с родителями разных рас
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** bi- + *racial*

Having parents of two different races.

1. She's *biracial* — her mom is Korean and her dad is Black.
2. The show follows a *biracial* family in Atlanta.
3. Many *biracial* Americans check more than one box on the census.

---

### bisexual

**Pronunciation:** /baɪˈsɛkʃuəl/ &middot; *by-SEK-shoo-uhl*
**Русский:** бисексуальный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** bi- + *sexual*
**Contrast:** Also a noun (*a bisexual*), but the adjective is more usual; *bi* is the informal short form.

Sexually attracted to more than one gender.

1. He came out as *bisexual* in college.
2. The novel has a *bisexual* main character.
3. The group supports gay, lesbian and *bisexual* teens.

---

### bitchy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɪtʃi/ &middot; *BICH-ee*
**Русский:** стервозный, язвительный
**Type:** quality — offensive slang
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *bitchier*, *bitchiest*
**Contrast:** Rude slang, mostly about women; avoid it in polite or professional speech.

Mean and unkind in what you say about people.

1. She made a *bitchy* remark about my hair.
2. Sorry I was so *bitchy* this morning.
3. The comments got *bitchier* as the night went on.

---

### biting

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbaɪtɪŋ/ &middot; *BY-ting*
**Русский:** пронизывающий; язвительный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more biting*, *most biting*
**Form:** -ing from *bite*

Biting has 2 distinct senses.

**1. (Of wind or cold) so cold that it hurts.** (пронизывающий, резкий)

1. We walked home in the *biting* wind.
2. The cold that morning was *biting*.

**2. (Of words) sharp and cruel.** (язвительный, едкий)

3. The critic wrote a *biting* review.
4. Her humor is more *biting* than her sister's.

---

### bitter

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɪtər/ &middot; *BIT-ur*
**Русский:** горький; озлобленный; ожесточённый; лютый
**Type:** quality; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *bitterer* or *more bitter*, *bitterest*
**Takes:** *about* or *at* + noun (feeling resentment)
**Opposite:** *sweet* (taste)
**Contrast:** Russian *горький перец* is *hot pepper* in English: *bitter* is the taste of coffee, dark chocolate or medicine, never spicy heat.

Bitter has 5 distinct senses.

**1. Having a strong, sharp taste that is not sweet.** (горький)

1. This coffee is too *bitter* for me.
2. The longer the tea steeps, the *more bitter* it gets.

**2. Angry and upset because you think you were treated unfairly.** (озлобленный, обиженный)

3. He's still *bitter* about losing his job.
4. She gave a short, *bitter* laugh.

**3. Making you very unhappy; hard to accept.** (горький, тяжёлый)

5. The loss was a *bitter* disappointment.
6. She learned from *bitter* experience not to trust him.

**4. Full of hate; very intense.** (ожесточённый, заклятый)

7. The two brothers became *bitter* enemies.
8. It was the *bitterest* election fight in years.

**5. (Of weather) extremely cold.** (лютый, пронизывающий)

9. A *bitter* wind blew off the lake.
10. Chicago had its *bitterest* winter in decades.

---

### bittersweet

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɪtərˌswiːt/ &middot; *BIT-ur-sweet*
**Русский:** горько-сладкий; радостный и грустный одновременно
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bittersweet*, *most bittersweet*
**Contrast:** In American recipes *bittersweet chocolate* is dark chocolate with only a little sugar.

Bittersweet has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Happy and sad at the same time.** (радостный и грустный одновременно)

1. Graduation was a *bittersweet* day for her.
2. The ending of the movie is *bittersweet*.

**2. (Of chocolate) dark and only slightly sweet.** (горький (о шоколаде))

3. Melt six ounces of *bittersweet* chocolate.
4. I prefer *bittersweet* chocolate to milk chocolate.

---

### biweekly

**Pronunciation:** /baɪˈwiːkli/ &middot; *by-WEEK-lee*
**Русский:** раз в две недели; дважды в неделю
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** bi- + *weekly*
**Contrast:** Ambiguous: in the US it usually means 'every two weeks' (*a biweekly paycheck*), but it can mean 'twice a week'. *Every other week* or *twice a week* is clearer. Also an adverb: *We get paid biweekly*.

Biweekly has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Happening every two weeks.** (проходящий раз в две недели)

1. Most of us get a *biweekly* paycheck.
2. The team has *biweekly* planning meetings.

**2. Happening twice a week.** (проходящий дважды в неделю)

3. She has *biweekly* physical therapy, on Tuesdays and Fridays.
4. The paper went from weekly to *biweekly* delivery, Mondays and Thursdays.

---

### bizarre

**Pronunciation:** /bɪˈzɑːr/ &middot; *bih-ZAHR*
**Русский:** причудливый, очень странный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bizarre*, *most bizarre*
**Contrast:** Sounds almost like *bazaar* (a market), but means 'very strange'.

Very strange and unusual.

1. I had a *bizarre* dream last night.
2. His behavior at the party was *bizarre*.
3. It's the most *bizarre* story I've heard all year.

---

### black-and-blue

**Pronunciation:** /ˌblæk ən ˈbluː/ &middot; *blak-uhn-BLOO*
**Русский:** в синяках
**Type:** color; also compound
**Position:** both — hyphenated before a noun; *black and blue* after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable

Covered in bruises.

1. His arm was *black and blue* after the fall.
2. She showed me her *black-and-blue* knee.
3. The older kids beat him *black and blue*.

---

### black-and-white

**Pronunciation:** /ˌblæk ən ˈwaɪt/ &middot; *blak-uhn-WYT*
**Русский:** чёрно-белый; однозначный
**Type:** color; also compound
**Position:** both — hyphenated before a noun; *black and white* after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable — but in the second sense *as*, *so*, *very* are common: *not as black and white as it looks*
**Contrast:** *In black and white* (a noun phrase) means 'in writing': *It's right there in black and white*.

Black-and-white has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Showing only black, white and gray, with no other colors.** (чёрно-белый)

1. Grandpa watched *black-and-white* TV as a kid.
2. The old photos are all *black and white*.

**2. Seeing things as simply right or wrong, with nothing in between.** (однозначный, упрощённый (чёрно-белый))

3. Life isn't as *black and white* as you think.
4. He has a very *black-and-white* view of the world.

---

### black-market

**Pronunciation:** /ˌblæk ˈmɑːrkɪt/ &middot; *blak-MAHR-kit*
**Русский:** нелегальный, с чёрного рынка
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *black* + *market*
**Contrast:** The noun is two separate words: *goods sold on the black market*. Only the adjective before a noun takes the hyphen.

Bought or sold illegally, outside the legal market.

1. Agents seized thousands of *black-market* cigarettes.
2. *Black-market* prices for the drug tripled last year.
3. He paid a *black-market* dealer twice what the ticket was worth.

---

### black-tie

**Pronunciation:** /ˌblækˈtaɪ/ &middot; *blak-TY*
**Русский:** официальный (форма одежды — смокинг)
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *black* + *tie* (a bow tie worn with a tuxedo)
**Contrast:** *Black-tie* means men wear a tuxedo; *white-tie* is even more formal. On invitations the dress code is written without a hyphen: *Black tie optional*.

Requiring formal evening clothes.

1. They're going to a *black-tie* dinner at the museum.
2. The gala is *black-tie*, so rent a tux.
3. Is the wedding *black-tie* or casual?

---

### blackened

**Pronunciation:** /ˈblækənd/ &middot; *BLAK-uhnd*
**Русский:** почерневший, закопчённый; (кулин.) обжаренный в специях
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — the cooking sense only before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *blacken* (*black* + -en)

Blackened has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Made black by smoke, fire or dirt.** (почерневший, закопчённый)

1. Firefighters walked through the *blackened* remains of the house.
2. The walls of the old kitchen were *blackened* with soot.
3. He pulled a *blackened* pan out of the oven.

**2. Of food, coated in spices and cooked quickly in a very hot pan until dark, in the Cajun style.** (обжаренный в специях до тёмной корочки)

4. I'll have the *blackened* catfish with rice.
5. The restaurant is famous for its *blackened* chicken sandwich.

---

### blameless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbleɪmləs/ &middot; *BLAYM-luhs*
**Русский:** невиновный, безупречный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — *completely blameless*
**Form:** -less from *blame*
**Opposite:** *to blame*; *guilty*

Not responsible for anything bad; having done nothing wrong.

1. The report found that the driver was *blameless*.
2. Neither side in the divorce was completely *blameless*.
3. She had lived a quiet, *blameless* life.

---

### bland

**Pronunciation:** /blænd/ &middot; *BLAND*
**Русский:** пресный, безвкусный; скучный, невыразительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *blander*, *blandest*

Bland has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of food, having very little flavor.** (пресный, безвкусный)

1. The soup was a little *bland*, so I added some salt.
2. After the surgery he had to stick to a *bland* diet.
3. This is the *blandest* salsa I've ever tasted.

**2. Dull and uninteresting; without strong features or opinions.** (скучный, невыразительный)

4. The office was painted a *bland* beige.
5. The senator gave a *bland* statement that said almost nothing.

---

### blank

**Pronunciation:** /blæŋk/ &middot; *BLANGK*
**Русский:** пустой, чистый; безучастный, непонимающий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *filled in*; *full*
**Contrast:** The noun is common too: *fill in the blanks*, *my mind drew a blank*. *A blank check* is a signed check with no amount, and figuratively unlimited freedom.

Blank has 2 distinct senses.

**1. With nothing written, printed or recorded on it.** (пустой, чистый, незаполненный)

1. Write your answer on a *blank* sheet of paper.
2. Please don't leave any lines *blank*.
3. The screen suddenly went *blank*.

**2. Showing no expression, understanding or memory.** (безучастный, непонимающий)

4. When I asked about the money, he just gave me a *blank* look.
5. As soon as the test started, my mind went *blank*.

---

### blanket

**Pronunciation:** /ˈblæŋkɪt/ &middot; *BLANG-kit*
**Русский:** общий, огульный, всеобъемлющий
**Type:** classifying — covering every case
**Position:** attributive only — *a blanket ban*, never *the ban was blanket*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Much more often a noun (*одеяло*). As an adjective it only comes before a noun and means *applying to everything, with no exceptions* — often with disapproval: *a blanket statement*.

Applying to all cases or people, without exceptions.

1. The city imposed a *blanket* ban on fireworks.
2. That's a *blanket* statement, and it isn't fair to everyone.
3. The policy offers *blanket* coverage for all employees.

---

### blaring

**Pronunciation:** /ˈblɛrɪŋ/ &middot; *BLAIR-ing*
**Русский:** оглушительный, орущий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *blare*

Making a very loud, harsh noise.

1. We could hear *blaring* sirens all night.
2. He drove past with his music *blaring*.
3. The TV in the waiting room was *blaring*.

---

### blase

**Pronunciation:** /blɑːˈzeɪ/ &middot; *blah-ZAY*
**Русский:** равнодушный, пресыщенный
**Type:** quality; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more blase*, *most blase*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*
**Form:** from French; also written *blasé*

Not impressed or worried by something, often because you have seen it many times before.

1. New Yorkers are pretty *blase* about celebrities on the subway.
2. She sounded *blase*, but I could tell she was nervous.
3. The second time around, I was a lot more *blase* about flying.

---

### blasphemous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈblæsfəməs/ &middot; *BLAS-fuh-muhs*
**Русский:** богохульный, кощунственный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more blasphemous*, *most blasphemous*
**Form:** from *blasphemy*
**Contrast:** The stress moves: the adjective and the noun are BLAS-fuh-, but the verb *blaspheme* is blas-FEEM.

Showing disrespect for God or for something religious; humorously, disrespecting something people treat as sacred.

1. The film was banned in several countries as *blasphemous*.
2. Some viewers found the painting *blasphemous*.
3. In Chicago, putting ketchup on a hot dog is about the most *blasphemous* thing you can do.

---

### blatant

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbleɪtənt/ &middot; *BLAY-tuhnt*
**Русский:** явный, вопиющий, неприкрытый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more blatant*, *most blatant*
**Contrast:** *Blatant* is about something bad done openly, without trying to hide it: *a blatant lie*. For something simply easy to see, use *obvious*.

Very obvious and done openly, without shame.

1. That was a *blatant* lie, and everyone in the room knew it.
2. The referee missed a *blatant* foul.
3. It was the most *blatant* case of discrimination the lawyer had ever seen.

---

### blazing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbleɪzɪŋ/ &middot; *BLAY-zing*
**Русский:** пылающий, палящий; стремительный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *blaze*
**Contrast:** *The fire was blazing* is usually the verb *blaze*. Before another adjective, *blazing* works like an adverb: *blazing hot*, *blazing fast*.

Blazing has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Burning strongly and brightly; of the sun or weather, extremely hot.** (пылающий, палящий)

1. We sat around a *blazing* campfire.
2. The players were exhausted after two hours in the *blazing* sun.
3. It was a *blazing* July afternoon in Phoenix.

**2. Extremely fast.** (стремительный, молниеносный)

4. The new phone runs at *blazing* speed.
5. He ran the forty-yard dash in a *blazing* 4.3 seconds.

---

### bleached

**Pronunciation:** /bliːtʃt/ &middot; *BLEECHT*
**Русский:** обесцвеченный, выбеленный, выгоревший
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *bleach*

Made white or lighter in color by chemicals or by the sun.

1. She showed up with *bleached* blond hair.
2. The old fence was *bleached* by years of desert sun.
3. Some bakers prefer unbleached flour to *bleached* flour.

---

### bleak

**Pronunciation:** /bliːk/ &middot; *BLEEK*
**Русский:** мрачный, безрадостный; унылый, голый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *bleaker*, *bleakest*
**Opposite:** *bright*; *promising*

Bleak has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Without hope or encouragement.** (мрачный, безрадостный)

1. The future looks *bleak* for the town's last factory.
2. Doctors gave him a *bleak* prognosis.
3. Things got even *bleaker* when the funding ran out.

**2. Of a place or weather, cold, bare and unwelcoming.** (унылый, голый, холодный)

4. The prairie looks *bleak* in the middle of winter.
5. It was a gray, *bleak* February morning.

---

### bleary

**Pronunciation:** /ˈblɪri/ &middot; *BLIR-ee*
**Русский:** мутный, затуманенный (о глазах)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *blearier*, *bleariest*

Of eyes, tired and unable to see clearly, especially from lack of sleep.

1. He looked at me with *bleary* eyes and asked what time it was.
2. My eyes were *bleary* after staring at the screen all night.
3. By the end of the flight, the passengers looked even *blearier* than the crew.

---

### bleary-eyed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌblɪriˈaɪd/ &middot; *blir-ee-YD*
**Русский:** с мутными (сонными) глазами
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *bleary* + *eyed*, like *blue-eyed*

Having tired, red eyes that cannot see clearly, usually after too little sleep.

1. A few *bleary-eyed* students showed up for the 8 a.m. class.
2. She came downstairs *bleary-eyed* and went straight for the coffee.
3. We were all *bleary-eyed* after the overnight drive.

---

### blended

**Pronunciation:** /ˈblɛndɪd/ &middot; *BLEN-did*
**Русский:** смешанный, купажированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *blend*

Blended has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Made by mixing different kinds or sources together.** (смешанный, купажированный)

1. This is a *blended* whiskey, not a single malt.
2. The school uses *blended* learning, with some classes online.
3. Pour in the *blended* fruit and stir.

**2. Of a family, made up of a couple and the children from their earlier relationships.** ((о семье) смешанная, с детьми от прежних браков)

4. They're a *blended* family with five kids between them.
5. Holidays can be complicated in *blended* families.

---

### blessed

**Pronunciation:** /blɛst; ˈblɛsɪd/ &middot; *BLEST; BLES-id*
**Русский:** счастливый, одарённый; святой; благословенный
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both — *blessed with* after a verb; *a blessed relief* before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more blessed*, *most blessed*
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Contrast:** The pronunciation follows the meaning. After a verb, *lucky* is one syllable: *We're blessed* (BLEST). Before a noun, *holy* and *welcome* are two: *the Blessed Virgin*, *a blessed relief* (BLES-id).

Blessed has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Lucky to have something good; given a talent or advantage. Pronounced BLEST.** (счастливый, одарённый)

1. We've been *blessed* with three healthy kids.
2. She's *blessed* with a great singing voice.
3. I feel very *blessed* to have friends like you.

**2. Holy; connected with God. Pronounced BLES-id.** (святой, блаженный)

4. The church is dedicated to the *Blessed* Virgin Mary.
5. The pastor quoted the Bible: "It is *more blessed* to give than to receive."

**3. Bringing welcome relief or happiness. Pronounced BLES-id.** (благословенный, долгожданный)

6. After a week of storms, the sunshine was a *blessed* relief.
7. The kids finally fell asleep, and there was *blessed* silence.

---

### blighted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈblaɪtɪd/ &middot; *BLY-tid*
**Русский:** запущенный, пришедший в упадок; поражённый, загубленный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more blighted*, *most blighted*
**Form:** -ed from *blight*

Blighted has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a city area, run-down, neglected and poor.** (запущенный, пришедший в упадок)

1. The program helps rebuild *blighted* neighborhoods.
2. The old mill district was one of the *most blighted* parts of the city.
3. Rows of *blighted* houses stood empty along the street.

**2. Damaged by disease, or spoiled by something bad.** (поражённый болезнью, загубленный)

4. Farmers had to pull up the *blighted* potato plants.
5. His career was *blighted* by injuries.

---

### blindfolded

**Pronunciation:** /ˈblaɪndˌfoʊldɪd/ &middot; *BLYND-fohl-did*
**Русский:** с завязанными глазами
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *blindfold*

With a piece of cloth tied over the eyes so that you cannot see.

1. The *blindfolded* kids took turns swinging at the piñata.
2. The hostages were kept *blindfolded* for days.
3. I've made this drive so many times I could do it *blindfolded*.

---

### blinding

**Pronunciation:** /ˈblaɪndɪŋ/ &middot; *BLYN-ding*
**Русский:** ослепительный, слепящий; сильнейший
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *blind*

Blinding has 2 distinct senses.

**1. So bright that you cannot see.** (ослепительный, слепящий)

1. There was a *blinding* flash, and then the power went out.
2. Drivers were stuck in *blinding* snow on the interstate.
3. The sun off the water was *blinding*.

**2. Very strong or intense.** (сильнейший, невыносимый)

4. She woke up with a *blinding* headache.
5. The car took off at *blinding* speed.

---

### blissful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈblɪsfəl/ &middot; *BLIS-fuhl*
**Русский:** блаженный, безмятежно счастливый
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more blissful*, *most blissful*
**Form:** -ful from *bliss*

Extremely happy and peaceful.

1. We spent a *blissful* week on the beach with no phones.
2. The first months of their marriage were *blissful*.
3. It was the *most blissful* nap of my life.

---

### blistering

**Pronunciation:** /ˈblɪstərɪŋ/ &middot; *BLIS-tur-ing*
**Русский:** палящий; стремительный; резкий, уничтожающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *blister*, as if hot enough to raise blisters

Blistering has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Extremely hot.** (палящий, изнуряющий (о жаре))

1. The marathon was run in *blistering* heat.
2. By noon the sidewalk was *blistering*.

**2. Very fast.** (стремительный, бешеный (о темпе))

3. She set a *blistering* pace from the start.
4. The band played a *blistering* guitar solo.

**3. Very angry and critical.** (резкий, уничтожающий (о критике))

5. The paper published a *blistering* review of the new restaurant.
6. The senator launched a *blistering* attack on the plan.

---

### blithe

**Pronunciation:** /blaɪð/ &middot; *BLYTH*
**Русский:** беспечный, беззаботный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more blithe*, *most blithe*

Blithe has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Casual and unconcerned, in a way that seems careless.** (беспечный, легкомысленный)

1. The company showed a *blithe* disregard for safety rules.
2. He made the *blithe* assumption that someone else would pay.
3. She was even *more blithe* about the deadline than her boss.

**2. Happy and carefree; mostly in literary writing.** (беззаботный, жизнерадостный)

4. She had a *blithe* spirit that everyone loved.
5. The song has a *blithe*, sunny melody.

---

### blocked

**Pronunciation:** /blɑːkt/ &middot; *BLAHKT*
**Русский:** заблокированный, перекрытый, забитый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *block*
**Opposite:** *clear*; *open*
**Contrast:** For a drain or a nose, Americans more often say *clogged* and *stuffy* (*a clogged drain*, *a stuffy nose*); *blocked* there sounds more British.

With something in the way, so that nothing can get through.

1. The road was *blocked* by a fallen tree.
2. He had surgery to open two *blocked* arteries.
3. Your number is *blocked*, so my calls won't go through.

---

### blood-red

**Pronunciation:** /ˌblʌdˈrɛd/ &middot; *bluhd-RED*
**Русский:** кроваво-красный
**Type:** color
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *blood* + *red*, like *sky-blue*

Deep red, like the color of blood.

1. The sky turned *blood-red* at sunset.
2. She wore *blood-red* lipstick to the premiere.
3. The moon looked *blood-red* during the eclipse.

---

### bloodcurdling

**Pronunciation:** /ˈblʌdˌkɜːrdlɪŋ/ &middot; *BLUHD-kurd-ling*
**Русский:** леденящий кровь, жуткий
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *blood* + *curdling*: so frightening it makes your blood go thick

Extremely frightening, especially of a sound.

1. We heard a *bloodcurdling* scream from the woods.
2. The movie has some truly *bloodcurdling* moments.
3. The noise from the basement was *bloodcurdling*.

---

### bloodless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈblʌdləs/ &middot; *BLUHD-luhs*
**Русский:** бескровный; бледный, безжизненный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both — the violence sense mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *blood*

Bloodless has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Done without violence or killing.** (бескровный)

1. The government fell in a *bloodless* coup.
2. It was a rare *bloodless* revolution.

**2. Very pale; also without energy or emotion.** (бледный; безжизненный, вялый)

3. His lips were thin and *bloodless*.
4. Critics called the remake a *bloodless* copy of the original.
5. Her face went *bloodless* when she heard the news.

---

### bloodshot

**Pronunciation:** /ˈblʌdʃɑːt/ &middot; *BLUHD-shaht*
**Русский:** налитый кровью, покрасневший (о глазах)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *blood* + *shot* (streaked)

Of eyes, red because the small blood vessels in them are swollen.

1. His eyes were *bloodshot* from lack of sleep.
2. She stared at me with *bloodshot* eyes.
3. Allergies make my eyes itchy and *bloodshot*.

---

### bloodstained

**Pronunciation:** /ˈblʌdˌsteɪnd/ &middot; *BLUHD-staynd*
**Русский:** запачканный кровью, окровавленный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *blood* + *stained*

Marked or covered with blood.

1. Police found a *bloodstained* shirt in the trunk.
2. The bandage was *bloodstained* by morning.
3. The museum displays the general's *bloodstained* coat.

---

### bloodthirsty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈblʌdˌθɜːrsti/ &middot; *BLUHD-thur-stee*
**Русский:** кровожадный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bloodthirsty*, *most bloodthirsty*
**Form:** compound — *blood* + *thirsty*

Eager to kill or hurt people, or enjoying violence.

1. The story is about a *bloodthirsty* pirate captain.
2. The crowd grew *bloodthirsty* as the fight went on.
3. It's one of the *most bloodthirsty* movies of the year.

---

### bloody

**Pronunciation:** /ˈblʌdi/ &middot; *BLUH-dee*
**Русский:** окровавленный; кровопролитный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *bloodier*, *bloodiest*
**Contrast:** In British English *bloody* is also a mild swear word used for emphasis (*bloody hell*). Americans recognize it from British TV but don't use it that way; in American English it means *with blood*.

Bloody has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Covered in blood or bleeding.** (окровавленный, в крови)

1. He came home from practice with a *bloody* nose.
2. Her knees were scraped and *bloody*.
3. Someone left a *bloody* towel on the floor.

**2. Involving a lot of violence and killing.** (кровопролитный, кровавый)

4. Antietam was the *bloodiest* single day of the Civil War.
5. The strike ended in a *bloody* clash with the police.

---

### blotchy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈblɑːtʃi/ &middot; *BLAH-chee*
**Русский:** пятнистый, покрытый пятнами
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *blotchier*, *blotchiest*
**Form:** -y from *blotch*

Covered with uneven patches of color, especially red marks on the skin.

1. Her face gets red and *blotchy* when she cries.
2. The cheap paint dried *blotchy*.
3. The rash was *blotchier* the next morning.

---

### blow-by-blow

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbloʊbaɪˈbloʊ/ &middot; *bloh-by-BLOH*
**Русский:** подробный, во всех деталях
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only — *a blow-by-blow account*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — from a boxing commentary that describes every punch
**Contrast:** After a verb it is an adverb phrase, usually without hyphens: *She told us about the fight blow by blow.*

Describing every detail of an event in the order it happened.

1. He gave us a *blow-by-blow* account of the meeting.
2. The book offers a *blow-by-blow* history of the campaign.
3. I don't need a *blow-by-blow* description, just tell me who won.

---

### blue-chip

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbluːˌtʃɪp/ &middot; *BLOO-chip*
**Русский:** надёжный, первоклассный
**Type:** compound; also evaluative
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — from the highest-value blue chip in poker

Blue-chip has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a company or stock, large, well established and reliable.** (надёжный, первоклассный (о компании, акциях))

1. Her portfolio is mostly *blue-chip* stocks.
2. Several *blue-chip* companies reported strong earnings.

**2. Of the highest quality, especially of a sports recruit or a work of art.** (лучший, самый ценный)

3. The college landed three *blue-chip* recruits this year.
4. The gallery sells *blue-chip* contemporary art.

---

### blue-collar

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbluːˈkɑːlər/ &middot; *bloo-KAH-lur*
**Русский:** рабочий, связанный с физическим трудом
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *blue* + *collar*, from work shirts
**Opposite:** *white-collar*

Doing or connected with physical work in factories, construction and similar jobs.

1. He grew up in a *blue-collar* town in Ohio.
2. Many *blue-collar* jobs now require technical training.
3. Her family was proudly *blue-collar*.

---

### blue-eyed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbluːˈaɪd/ &middot; *bloo-YD*
**Русский:** голубоглазый
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *blue* + *eyed*

Having blue eyes.

1. They have a *blue-eyed* baby girl.
2. Both of her sons are blond and *blue-eyed*.
3. The poster showed a smiling, *blue-eyed* soldier.

---

### blue-green

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbluːˈɡriːn/ &middot; *bloo-GREEN*
**Русский:** сине-зелёный, бирюзовый
**Type:** color
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — two colors mixed

Of a color between blue and green.

1. The water in the bay is a clear *blue-green*.
2. She bought a *blue-green* sweater to match her eyes.
3. The lake turns *blue-green* in late summer.

---

### blue-ribbon

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbluːˈrɪbən/ &middot; *bloo-RIB-uhn*
**Русский:** авторитетный; первоклассный, призовой
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — from the blue ribbon given for first prize

Blue-ribbon has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a group, made up of especially qualified or respected people.** (авторитетный, из лучших специалистов)

1. The governor appointed a *blue-ribbon* panel to study the problem.
2. A *blue-ribbon* commission released its report on Monday.

**2. Of the best quality; having won first prize.** (первоклассный, призовой)

3. Grandma's *blue-ribbon* apple pie won at the county fair.
4. They raise *blue-ribbon* cattle.

---

### bluish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbluːɪʃ/ &middot; *BLOO-ish*
**Русский:** голубоватый, синеватый
**Type:** color
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** *blue* + -ish, with the *e* dropped

Slightly blue.

1. The screen gives off a *bluish* light.
2. His lips were turning *bluish* from the cold.
3. The mountains look *bluish* in the distance.

---

### blunt

**Pronunciation:** /blʌnt/ &middot; *BLUHNT*
**Русский:** прямой, резкий; тупой
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *blunter*, *bluntest*
**Opposite:** *tactful* (sense 1); *sharp* (sense 2)
**Contrast:** For an everyday knife, Americans usually say *dull*; *blunt* is more common in *a blunt object* and *blunt-force trauma*.

Blunt has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Saying exactly what you think, without trying to be polite.** (прямой, резкий, прямолинейный)

1. To be *blunt*, your business plan won't work.
2. She gave a *blunt* answer to a simple question.
3. His feedback was even *blunter* than I expected.

**2. Not sharp; having a rounded or flat edge or point.** (тупой, затупленный)

4. The victim was hit with a *blunt* object.
5. Kids' scissors have *blunt* tips.

---

### blurred

**Pronunciation:** /blɜːrd/ &middot; *BLURD*
**Русский:** размытый, нечёткий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more blurred*, *most blurred*
**Form:** -ed from *blur*, with the *r* doubled

Blurred has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not clear in shape or outline.** (размытый, нечёткий)

1. Most of the vacation photos came out *blurred*.
2. He complained of *blurred* vision after the fall.

**2. Of a difference or line between things, no longer clear.** (размытый, стёршийся (о границе, различии))

3. The line between work and home has become *blurred*.
4. With remote jobs, those boundaries are *more blurred* than ever.

---

### blurry

**Pronunciation:** /ˈblɜːri/ &middot; *BLUR-ee*
**Русский:** размытый, расплывчатый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *blurrier*, *blurriest*
**Form:** -y from *blur*, with the *r* doubled
**Contrast:** *Blurry* and *blurred* mean nearly the same for pictures and eyesight; *blurry* is the more casual everyday word. Only *blurred* is used for lines between ideas: *blurred boundaries*.

Not clear; out of focus.

1. The security footage is too *blurry* to identify anyone.
2. Everything looks *blurry* without my glasses.
3. This picture is even *blurrier* than the last one.

---

### blustery

**Pronunciation:** /ˈblʌstəri/ &middot; *BLUHS-tuh-ree*
**Русский:** ветреный, порывистый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more blustery*, *most blustery*
**Form:** -y from *bluster*

Of weather, with strong, sudden gusts of wind.

1. It was a cold, *blustery* day in Chicago.
2. The weekend will be *blustery*, with gusts up to forty miles an hour.
3. March was *more blustery* than usual this year.

---

### boastful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈboʊstfəl/ &middot; *BOHST-fuhl*
**Русский:** хвастливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more boastful*, *most boastful*
**Form:** -ful from *boast*
**Opposite:** *modest*

Talking too proudly about yourself and what you have done.

1. He's talented but never *boastful*.
2. The coach was annoyed by the team's *boastful* posts online.
3. The more he won, the *more boastful* he became.

---

### bodily

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɑːdəli/ &middot; *BAH-duh-lee*
**Русский:** телесный, физический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *bodily harm*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** *body* + -ly, with *y* changed to *i*
**Contrast:** An *-ly* word that is an adjective, not an adverb. There is also a separate adverb: *They carried him bodily out of the bar* (by lifting his whole body).

Relating to the human body.

1. He was charged with assault causing *bodily* harm.
2. Doctors monitored all of her *bodily* functions.
3. Gloves protect nurses from contact with *bodily* fluids.

---

### bogus

**Pronunciation:** /ˈboʊɡəs/ &middot; *BOH-guhs*
**Русский:** фальшивый, поддельный; липовый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — *totally bogus*
**Opposite:** *genuine*

Fake or false, though pretending to be real; informally, unfair or wrong.

1. The website was selling *bogus* concert tickets.
2. Most of his insurance claims turned out to be *bogus*.
3. They kicked him out for being late? That's totally *bogus*.

---

### bohemian

**Pronunciation:** /boʊˈhiːmiən/ &middot; *boh-HEE-mee-uhn*
**Русский:** богемный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bohemian*, *most bohemian*
**Contrast:** With a capital letter, *Bohemian* means *from Bohemia*, the region of the Czech Republic. Lowercase *bohemian* (often shortened to *boho* for fashion) describes an artistic, unconventional lifestyle.

Living in an artistic, unconventional way, without caring about ordinary social rules.

1. Greenwich Village was once the center of *bohemian* life in New York.
2. Her apartment has a *bohemian* look, with rugs and plants everywhere.
3. The neighborhood was a lot *more bohemian* before the rents went up.

---

### boiled

**Pronunciation:** /bɔɪld/ &middot; *BOYLD*
**Русский:** варёный, отварной
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *boil*

Cooked in boiling water.

1. I had two *boiled* eggs for breakfast.
2. Serve the fish with *boiled* potatoes.
3. Are the peanuts roasted or *boiled*?

---

### boisterous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɔɪstərəs/ &middot; *BOY-stuh-ruhs*
**Русский:** шумный, буйный, неугомонный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more boisterous*, *most boisterous*
**Opposite:** *quiet*; *subdued*

Noisy, energetic and lively.

1. A *boisterous* crowd filled the stadium.
2. The kids got *boisterous* after all that cake.
3. Our table was the *most boisterous* one in the restaurant.

---

### bold

**Pronunciation:** /boʊld/ &middot; *BOHLD*
**Русский:** смелый, дерзкий; яркий, насыщенный; жирный (о шрифте)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *bolder*, *boldest*
**Opposite:** *timid* (sense 1); *subtle* (sense 2)

Bold has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Brave and confident, willing to take risks; sometimes too confident to be polite.** (смелый, решительный, дерзкий)

1. The mayor has a *bold* plan to rebuild downtown.
2. It was one of the *boldest* moves in company history.
3. If I may be so *bold*, I think you're making a mistake.

**2. Strong, bright and easy to notice.** (яркий, насыщенный, выразительный)

4. She likes *bold* colors like orange and purple.
5. This coffee has a *bold* flavor.

**3. Of printed letters, thick and dark.** (жирный, полужирный (о шрифте))

6. The key terms are in *bold* type.
7. Put the headings in *bold*.

---

### Bolivian

**Pronunciation:** /bəˈlɪviən/ &middot; *buh-LIV-ee-uhn*
**Русский:** боливийский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Always capitalized. *A Bolivian*, *Bolivians* are nouns for the people.

From or connected with Bolivia.

1. La Paz is the seat of the *Bolivian* government.
2. My roommate is *Bolivian*.
3. We tried *Bolivian* salteñas at a café in Virginia.

---

### bombastic

**Pronunciation:** /bɑːmˈbæstɪk/ &middot; *bahm-BAS-tik*
**Русский:** напыщенный, высокопарный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bombastic*, *most bombastic*

Using loud, grand, impressive-sounding language that has little real meaning.

1. The candidate is known for his *bombastic* speeches.
2. Critics found the movie's score loud and *bombastic*.
3. Her tweets got *more bombastic* as the election got closer.

---

### bona fide

**Pronunciation:** /ˈboʊnə ˌfaɪd/ &middot; *BOH-nuh fyd*
**Русский:** подлинный, настоящий; добросовестный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** Latin for *in good faith*; two words, no hyphen
**Contrast:** Also said boh-nuh-FY-dee. Do not confuse it with the noun *bona fides* (proof that someone is qualified or trustworthy): *She has the academic bona fides for the job.*

Bona fide has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Real and genuine, not fake or pretending.** (подлинный, настоящий)

1. After one hit song, she became a *bona fide* star.
2. Is he a *bona fide* expert, or does he just play one on TV?
3. Only *bona fide* residents can get a parking permit.

**2. Made honestly and sincerely, without intending to deceive.** (добросовестный, честный)

4. The company says it made a *bona fide* offer to the union.
5. The court decided the sale was *bona fide*.

---

### bone-dry

**Pronunciation:** /ˌboʊnˈdraɪ/ &middot; *bohn-DRY*
**Русский:** совершенно сухой, сухой как кость
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *bone* + *dry*: as dry as a bone

Completely dry, with no water at all.

1. After three months without rain, the creek was *bone-dry*.
2. Check that the wood is *bone-dry* before you paint it.
3. The turkey came out *bone-dry*, so we covered it in gravy.

---

### boneheaded

**Pronunciation:** /ˈboʊnˌhɛdɪd/ &middot; *BOHN-hed-id*
**Русский:** тупой, дурацкий
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more boneheaded*, *most boneheaded*
**Form:** from the noun *bonehead* (a stupid person) + -ed

Informal: very stupid.

1. Leaving the keys in the car was a *boneheaded* mistake.
2. The coach called the penalty *boneheaded*.
3. That was the *most boneheaded* play of the whole season.

---

### boneless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈboʊnləs/ &middot; *BOHN-luhs*
**Русский:** без костей, филейный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *bone*

Of meat or fish, with the bones removed.

1. Buy two pounds of *boneless*, skinless chicken breasts.
2. We ordered a dozen *boneless* wings.
3. Is the ham *boneless*?

---

### bonkers

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɑːŋkərz/ &middot; *BAHNG-kurz*
**Русский:** чокнутый, спятивший; безумный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** predicative only — *he's bonkers*, *go bonkers*
**Comparison:** not gradable — *totally bonkers*
**Contrast:** Informal. It ends in *-s* but is not a plural: *She's bonkers*, never ✗ *a bonker*.

Informal: crazy, or wildly excited or angry.

1. You want to drive to Florida tonight? You're *bonkers*.
2. The fans went *bonkers* when the home team scored.
3. The price of eggs this year is just *bonkers*.

---

### bony

**Pronunciation:** /ˈboʊni/ &middot; *BOH-nee*
**Русский:** костлявый, худой; костистый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *bonier*, *boniest*
**Form:** *bone* + -y, with the *e* dropped

Bony has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Very thin, so that the bones show.** (костлявый, худой)

1. The stray dog was *bony* and scared.
2. He had long, *bony* fingers.
3. She was the *boniest* kid in the class.

**2. Of fish, full of small bones.** (костистый, с множеством костей)

4. Carp is tasty but very *bony*.
5. I don't like *bony* fish.

---

### booked

**Pronunciation:** /bʊkt/ &middot; *BUUKT*
**Русский:** забронированный, занятый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — *the hotel is booked*
**Comparison:** not gradable — *fully booked*, *booked solid*
**Takes:** often *up*: *booked up*
**Form:** -ed from *book* (reserve)
**Contrast:** *He was booked* (by the police) is the passive of the verb *book*, *to charge with a crime*, not this adjective.

Of a place, event or person, with all the places or time already reserved.

1. Every hotel in town is *booked* for the game.
2. The restaurant is *booked* solid through December.
3. Sorry, I'm *booked* all week, can we meet Monday?

---

### bookish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbʊkɪʃ/ &middot; *BUU-kish*
**Русский:** любящий книги, книжный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bookish*, *most bookish*
**Form:** -ish from *book*

Liking reading and study more than sports or social activities.

1. He was a quiet, *bookish* kid who loved the library.
2. She's too *bookish* to enjoy loud parties.
3. Of the two sisters, Emma was the *more bookish* one.

---

### booming

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbuːmɪŋ/ &middot; *BOO-ming*
**Русский:** процветающий, бурно растущий; громкий, раскатистый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *boom*

Booming has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Growing quickly and very successful.** (процветающий, бурно растущий)

1. Austin has a *booming* tech industry.
2. Business is *booming* this summer.
3. They opened a shop in the town's *booming* downtown.

**2. Of a voice or sound, loud and deep.** (громкий, раскатистый, гулкий)

4. The announcer has a *booming* voice.
5. We heard *booming* thunder in the distance.

---

### boorish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbʊrɪʃ/ &middot; *BUUR-ish*
**Русский:** грубый, невоспитанный, хамский
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more boorish*, *most boorish*
**Form:** -ish from *boor* (a rude person)
**Opposite:** *polite*; *courteous*

Rude and rough in behavior; bad-mannered.

1. His *boorish* behavior at the wedding embarrassed everyone.
2. The fans in the upper deck were loud and *boorish*.
3. He got *more boorish* with every drink.

---

### bootleg

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbuːtˌlɛɡ/ &middot; *BOOT-leg*
**Русский:** контрабандный, пиратский, самопальный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *boot* + *leg*, from hiding bottles in a boot
**Contrast:** Also a noun (*a bootleg of the concert*) and a verb (*to bootleg liquor*). For software, movies and music, Americans more often say *pirated*.

Made, copied or sold illegally.

1. During Prohibition, *bootleg* whiskey was sold in secret bars.
2. Vendors outside the stadium sell *bootleg* T-shirts.
3. Fans traded *bootleg* recordings of the band's live shows.

---

### boozy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbuːzi/ &middot; *BOO-zee*
**Русский:** с большим количеством алкоголя, пьяный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *boozier*, *booziest*
**Form:** -y from *booze* (informal for alcohol)

Informal: involving a lot of alcohol, or containing a lot of it.

1. We had a long, *boozy* brunch on Sunday.
2. This eggnog is pretty *boozy*.
3. The office party was *boozier* than anyone expected.

---

### borderline

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɔːrdərˌlaɪn/ &middot; *BOR-dur-lyn*
**Русский:** пограничный, спорный, на грани
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *border* + *line*
**Contrast:** Before another adjective it works like an adverb: *borderline rude*, *borderline illegal*. As a noun, *the borderline* is the dividing line itself.

Very close to the line between two categories, so hard to decide; just barely acceptable or just barely a condition.

1. It was a *borderline* call, but the umpire said he was safe.
2. Her test scores are *borderline*, so she might not get in.
3. The doctor said I have *borderline* diabetes.

---

### born

**Pronunciation:** /bɔːrn/ &middot; *BORN*
**Русский:** прирождённый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a born leader*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *Was born* is the passive of the verb *bear*, not this adjective. Russian *я родился* needs *was*: ✗ *I born in 1990* → *I was born in 1990*. Do not confuse *born* with *borne* (*carried*): *airborne*, *borne by the taxpayer*.

Having a natural ability for something, as if from birth.

1. She's a *born* leader.
2. My grandfather was a *born* storyteller.
3. You can tell he's a *born* salesman.

---

### born-again

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbɔːrnəˈɡɛn/ &middot; *born-uh-GEN*
**Русский:** заново обращённый (в христианство); новообращённый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *born* + *again*

Born-again has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having become an evangelical Christian through a strong personal religious experience.** (заново обращённый (в христианство))

1. He became a *born-again* Christian in his thirties.
2. Her parents are *born-again* and very involved in their church.

**2. Newly and very enthusiastic about a belief or activity.** (новообращённый, ярый (сторонник))

3. My brother is a *born-again* vegan.
4. Like many *born-again* runners, she talks about marathons constantly.

---

### Bosnian

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɑːzniən/ &middot; *BAHZ-nee-uhn*
**Русский:** боснийский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Always capitalized. *A Bosnian*, *Bosnians* are nouns for the people, and *Bosnian* is also the name of the language.

From or connected with Bosnia and Herzegovina.

1. St. Louis has one of the largest *Bosnian* communities in the country.
2. Her grandparents are *Bosnian*.
3. We had *Bosnian* coffee after dinner.

---

### bossy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɔːsi/ &middot; *BAW-see*
**Русский:** властный, любящий командовать
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *bossier*, *bossiest*
**Form:** -y from *boss*

Always telling other people what to do.

1. My older sister was really *bossy* when we were kids.
2. Nobody wants to work with a *bossy* manager.
3. He's the *bossiest* person on the committee.

---

### botanical

**Pronunciation:** /bəˈtænɪkəl/ &middot; *buh-TAN-i-kuhl*
**Русский:** ботанический; растительный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from *botany*

Connected with plants or the science of plants; made from plants.

1. We spent the afternoon at the *botanical* garden.
2. The book has beautiful *botanical* drawings.
3. The shampoo contains *botanical* extracts.

---

### botched

**Pronunciation:** /bɑːtʃt/ &middot; *BAHCHT*
**Русский:** проваленный, испорченный, неудачный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *botch* (to do badly)

Done badly or clumsily, so that it fails or causes damage.

1. Two men were arrested after a *botched* robbery.
2. She sued the clinic over a *botched* surgery.
3. The website launch was completely *botched*.

---

### bothered

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɑːðərd/ &middot; *BAH-thurd*
**Русский:** обеспокоенный, раздражённый
**Type:** participial; also emotion
**Position:** predicative only — *I'm not bothered*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bothered*, *most bothered*
**Takes:** *by* or *about* + noun; *that*-clause; *to*-infinitive in *can't be bothered to*
**Form:** -ed from *bother* — how you feel
**Contrast:** *Bothered* is how you feel; *bothersome* is what causes it. *Can't be bothered (to do something)*, meaning *too lazy to make the effort*, is more British; Americans often say *I don't feel like it*. *Hot and bothered* means *upset* or *sexually excited*.

Bothered has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Worried, annoyed or upset by something.** (обеспокоенный, раздражённый)

1. I'm not *bothered* by the noise anymore.
2. Were you *bothered* that he didn't call?
3. She seemed *more bothered* about the car than about her injury.

**2. Willing to make the effort; almost always in the negative.** ((не) желающий утруждать себя)

4. He couldn't be *bothered* to reply to my email.
5. Some people just can't be *bothered* to recycle.

---

### bothersome

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɑːðərsəm/ &middot; *BAH-thur-suhm*
**Русский:** докучливый, надоедливый, беспокоящий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bothersome*, *most bothersome*
**Form:** -some from *bother*

Causing annoyance or small problems.

1. The cough was *bothersome* but not serious.
2. Mosquitoes are the most *bothersome* part of camping here.
3. The pop-up ads are getting *more bothersome* every day.

---

### Botswanan

**Pronunciation:** /bɑːtˈswɑːnən/ &middot; *baht-SWAH-nuhn*
**Русский:** ботсванский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Always capitalized. In Botswana itself the people are *Batswana* (one person is a *Motswana*), and *Botswana* is often used before a noun: *the Botswana government*. *Botswanan* is the form many American writers use.

From or connected with Botswana.

1. The *Botswanan* team won the relay.
2. Diamonds are a large part of the *Botswanan* economy.
3. Our guide on the safari was *Botswanan*.

---

### bottom

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɑːtəm/ &middot; *BAH-tuhm*
**Русский:** нижний; последний, самый низкий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *the bottom shelf*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *top*
**Contrast:** Much more common as a noun: *at the bottom of the page*. As an adjective it only comes before a noun. *The bottom line* is the final result or the most important fact.

Bottom has 2 distinct senses.

**1. In the lowest position.** (нижний)

1. The towels are in the *bottom* drawer.
2. Sign your name on the *bottom* line.
3. The cereal is on the *bottom* shelf.

**2. Lowest in rank, amount or quality.** (последний, самый низкий)

4. Students in the *bottom* half of the class got extra tutoring.
5. Families in the *bottom* 20 percent pay almost no income tax.

---

### bottom-up

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbɑːtəmˈʌp/ &middot; *bah-tuhm-UHP*
**Русский:** восходящий, идущий снизу
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a bottom-up approach*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *bottom* + *up*
**Opposite:** *top-down*
**Contrast:** After a verb it is an adverb phrase, usually with *from* and no hyphen: *We rebuilt the program from the bottom up.*

Starting with details or ordinary people and working toward the whole or the leadership.

1. The company takes a *bottom-up* approach to new ideas.
2. It was a *bottom-up* movement led by local volunteers.
3. Economists built a *bottom-up* estimate from store-level sales.

---

### bottomless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɑːtəmləs/ &middot; *BAH-tuhm-luhs*
**Русский:** бездонный; с бесплатной добавкой
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *bottom*

Bottomless has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Extremely deep, or seeming to have no limit.** (бездонный, безграничный)

1. The canyon looked almost *bottomless*.
2. Teenage boys seem to have *bottomless* stomachs.
3. The project became a *bottomless* pit for taxpayer money.

**2. Of a drink in a restaurant, refilled as many times as you want for one price.** (с бесплатной добавкой, безлимитный (о напитках))

4. The diner has *bottomless* coffee.
5. They offer *bottomless* mimosas at weekend brunch.

---

### bouncy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbaʊnsi/ &middot; *BOWN-see*
**Русский:** упругий, прыгучий; бодрый, жизнерадостный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *bouncier*, *bounciest*
**Form:** -y from *bounce*, with the *e* dropped
**Contrast:** The inflatable playhouse that British English calls a *bouncy castle* is usually a *bounce house* in American English.

Bouncy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Springing back up easily; soft and springy.** (упругий, прыгучий, пружинистый)

1. The kids love the *bouncy* ball from the dentist.
2. This mattress is too *bouncy* for me.
3. Her hair looked *bouncier* after the haircut.

**2. Lively, cheerful and full of energy.** (бодрый, жизнерадостный)

4. The song has a *bouncy* beat.
5. She's always so *bouncy* in the morning.

---

### bound up

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbaʊnd ˈʌp/ &middot; *bownd UHP*
**Русский:** тесно связанный; поглощённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — *be bound up with*
**Comparison:** not gradable — *closely bound up*
**Takes:** *with* — connected; *in* — very involved

Bound up has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Closely connected with something, so that one depends on the other.** (тесно связанный)

1. Her identity is *bound up* with her work.
2. The town's history is closely *bound up* with the railroad.
3. Money problems are often *bound up* with health problems.

**2. So busy or involved with something that you have no time for anything else.** (поглощённый, всецело занятый)

4. He was too *bound up* in his own problems to notice.
5. She's completely *bound up* in the new project.

---

### boundless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbaʊndləs/ &middot; *BOWND-luhs*
**Русский:** безграничный, бескрайний
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *bound* (a limit)

Without any limits; seeming to have no end.

1. The puppy has *boundless* energy.
2. Her curiosity seems *boundless*.
3. Settlers crossed the *boundless* prairie in wagons.

---

### bountiful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbaʊntɪfəl/ &middot; *BOWN-ti-fuhl*
**Русский:** обильный; щедрый
**Type:** quantity; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bountiful*, *most bountiful*
**Form:** -ful from *bounty*, with *y* changed to *i*

Bountiful has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Existing in large amounts; plentiful.** (обильный, изобильный)

1. Farmers are hoping for a *bountiful* harvest.
2. This year's crop was *more bountiful* than last year's.
3. Thanksgiving dinner was *bountiful*, as always.

**2. Generous in giving; mostly in formal or religious writing.** (щедрый)

4. They thanked God for his *bountiful* blessings.
5. The festival was funded by a *bountiful* patron.

---

### bourgeois

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbʊrʒwɑː/ &middot; *BUURZH-wah*
**Русский:** буржуазный; мещанский
**Type:** evaluative; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bourgeois*, *most bourgeois*
**Form:** from French *bourgeois*, a town dweller
**Contrast:** Also stressed at the end, boorzh-WAH. In everyday American speech it is usually a joke or a criticism: *Avocado toast is so bourgeois.* The noun for the class is *the bourgeoisie*.

Bourgeois has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Belonging to or typical of the middle class, especially in political writing.** (буржуазный)

1. Marx wrote about the struggle against *bourgeois* society.
2. She came from a comfortable *bourgeois* family.

**2. Too concerned with comfort, money and respectability; conventional.** (мещанский, обывательский)

3. His artist friends think a house in the suburbs is hopelessly *bourgeois*.
4. Their tastes got *more bourgeois* as they got richer.
5. Is a twelve-dollar latte a little *bourgeois*?

---

### boxed in

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbɑːkst ˈɪn/ &middot; *bahkst IN*
**Русский:** загнанный в угол; зажатый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — *feel boxed in*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from the phrasal verb *box in*

Boxed in has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Feeling trapped, with no freedom or no choices.** (загнанный в угол, скованный)

1. She felt *boxed in* by her parents' expectations.
2. The governor is *boxed in* by the budget.
3. Small apartments make me feel *boxed in*.

**2. Physically surrounded so that you cannot move out.** (зажатый, запертый со всех сторон)

4. My car was *boxed in* by two delivery trucks.
5. The runner got *boxed in* on the final lap.

---

### boxy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɑːksi/ &middot; *BAHK-see*
**Русский:** угловатый, квадратный, мешковатый
**Type:** shape
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *boxier*, *boxiest*
**Form:** -y from *box*

Shaped like a box, square and without curves.

1. The old Volvo was famous for its *boxy* shape.
2. That jacket looks a little *boxy* on you.
3. The new model is even *boxier* than the old one.

---

### boyish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɔɪɪʃ/ &middot; *BOY-ish*
**Русский:** мальчишеский, юношеский
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more boyish*, *most boyish*
**Form:** -ish from *boy*

Like a boy, especially looking young and attractive; of a girl or woman, looking like a boy.

1. At fifty he still has a *boyish* grin.
2. She wore her hair in a short, *boyish* cut.
3. He looked *more boyish* without the beard.

---

### bracing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbreɪsɪŋ/ &middot; *BRAY-sing*
**Русский:** бодрящий, свежий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bracing*, *most bracing*
**Form:** -ing from *brace*

Cold and fresh in a way that makes you feel awake; also, strong and energizing.

1. We went for a *bracing* walk along the lake.
2. The water was *bracing*, but we jumped in anyway.
3. The morning air was even *more bracing* up in the mountains.

---

### brackish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbrækɪʃ/ &middot; *BRAK-ish*
**Русский:** солоноватый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more brackish*, *most brackish*

Of water, slightly salty, as where a river meets the sea.

1. Crabs thrive in the *brackish* water of the bay.
2. The well water tasted *brackish*.
3. The river gets *more brackish* closer to the ocean.

---

### braided

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbreɪdɪd/ &middot; *BRAY-did*
**Русский:** заплетённый, плетёный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *braid*
**Contrast:** American English says *braid* and *braided* for hair; British English often uses *plait* and *plaited*.

Twisted together in three or more strands to form one length.

1. The girl's hair was *braided* for the recital.
2. He wore a *braided* leather bracelet.
3. She bought a loaf of *braided* bread for the holiday.

---

### brain dead

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbreɪn ˌdɛd/ &middot; *BRAYN ded*
**Русский:** с мёртвым мозгом; отупевший
**Type:** classifying; also evaluative
**Position:** both — hyphenated before a noun: *a brain-dead patient*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *brain* + *dead*

Brain dead has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having no brain activity, though the heart may be kept beating by machines.** (в состоянии смерти мозга)

1. Doctors declared him *brain dead* after the accident.
2. The family agreed to donate the *brain-dead* patient's organs.

**2. Informal: very stupid, or too tired to think.** (отупевший, безмозглый)

3. After eight hours of exams, I'm completely *brain dead*.
4. That was a *brain-dead* decision by management.

---

### brainless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbreɪnləs/ &middot; *BRAYN-luhs*
**Русский:** безмозглый, глупый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more brainless*, *most brainless*
**Form:** -less from *brain*

Stupid; showing no thought.

1. It's a fun, *brainless* action movie.
2. Don't be so *brainless*, check the address first.
3. Each season of the show gets *more brainless*.

---

### brainwashed

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbreɪnˌwɑːʃt/ &middot; *BRAYN-wahsht*
**Русский:** с промытыми мозгами, зомбированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — *completely brainwashed*
**Form:** -ed from *brainwash* (*brain* + *wash*)
**Contrast:** *He was brainwashed into joining* is the passive of the verb; the adjective describes the state: *brainwashed followers*.

Made to believe something completely by repeated pressure, so that you no longer think for yourself.

1. The cult's *brainwashed* members gave away all their money.
2. Her family thinks she's been *brainwashed*.
3. You sound totally *brainwashed* by those ads.

---

### brainy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbreɪni/ &middot; *BRAY-nee*
**Русский:** умный, башковитый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *brainier*, *brainiest*
**Form:** -y from *brain*

Informal: very intelligent.

1. She was the *brainy* kid who always finished first.
2. You don't have to be *brainy* to enjoy the show.
3. Her little brother turned out to be even *brainier*.

---

### braised

**Pronunciation:** /breɪzd/ &middot; *BRAYZD*
**Русский:** тушёный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *braise*

Browned in fat and then cooked slowly in a little liquid in a covered pot.

1. The special tonight is *braised* short ribs.
2. Serve the *braised* cabbage with pork chops.
3. The lamb is *braised* for four hours.

---

### branded

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbrændɪd/ &middot; *BRAN-did*
**Русский:** фирменный, брендовый; клеймёный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *brand*
**Contrast:** *He was branded a traitor* is the passive of the verb *brand*, *to label someone as bad*, not this adjective.

Branded has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Made or sold under a known company name.** (фирменный, брендовый)

1. The team store sells *branded* hats and jerseys.
2. *Branded* drugs cost more than generic ones.
3. The company gave everyone *branded* water bottles.

**2. Of an animal, marked with a hot iron to show who owns it.** (клеймёный)

4. The rancher counted the *branded* cattle.
5. *Branded* horses are easier to identify if they're stolen.

---

### brash

**Pronunciation:** /bræʃ/ &middot; *BRASH*
**Русский:** самоуверенный, нахальный, бесцеремонный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *brasher*, *brashest*

Too confident, loud and aggressive, often in a way that is rude.

1. The *brash* young CEO promised to beat every competitor.
2. His style was *brash*, but he got results.
3. She was the *brashest* player on the team.

---

### bratty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbræti/ &middot; *BRAT-ee*
**Русский:** избалованный, капризный, противный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *brattier*, *brattiest*
**Form:** -y from *brat*, with the *t* doubled

Informal: behaving like a spoiled, badly behaved child.

1. Stop being so *bratty* and share your toys.
2. The movie is about a *bratty* rich kid who learns a lesson.
3. He's the *brattiest* kid on the block.

---

### brave

**Pronunciation:** /breɪv/ &middot; *BRAYV*
**Русский:** храбрый, смелый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *braver*, *bravest*
**Opposite:** *cowardly*

Willing to do things that are dangerous or difficult without showing fear.

1. The *brave* firefighters ran back into the building.
2. It was *brave* of you to tell the truth.
3. She's the *bravest* person I know.

---

### brawny

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbrɔːni/ &middot; *BRAW-nee*
**Русский:** мускулистый, крепкий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *brawnier*, *brawniest*
**Form:** -y from *brawn* (muscle)

Big, strong and muscular.

1. Two *brawny* guys carried the piano up the stairs.
2. The lumberjack had *brawny* arms.
3. The linemen are *brawnier* than they were a decade ago.

---

### brazen

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbreɪzən/ &middot; *BRAY-zuhn*
**Русский:** наглый, бесстыдный, дерзкий
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more brazen*, *most brazen*

Open and without shame, about something that should cause shame.

1. Thieves stole the car in a *brazen* daylight robbery.
2. His lies were so *brazen* that people almost believed them.
3. It was one of the *most brazen* art thefts in history.

---

### breakable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbreɪkəbəl/ &middot; *BRAY-kuh-buhl*
**Русский:** хрупкий, бьющийся
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** *break* + -able
**Opposite:** *unbreakable*

Easy to break.

1. Put anything *breakable* on the top shelf, away from the kids.
2. Mark the box "*breakable*" before you ship it.
3. We packed the *breakable* dishes in towels.

---

### breakaway

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbreɪkəˌweɪ/ &middot; *BRAY-kuh-way*
**Русский:** отколовшийся, сепаратистский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a breakaway region*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — from the phrasal verb *break away*
**Contrast:** Also a noun, especially in hockey and soccer: *He scored on a breakaway.*

Having left a larger group, country or organization to form a separate one.

1. Fighting broke out in the *breakaway* region.
2. A *breakaway* group of members started a new church.
3. The *breakaway* league signed several star players.

---

### breakneck

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbreɪkˌnɛk/ &middot; *BRAYK-nek*
**Русский:** бешеный, головокружительный (о скорости)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only — *at breakneck speed*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *break* + *neck*

Extremely and dangerously fast.

1. They drove at *breakneck* speed down the highway.
2. The company has grown at a *breakneck* pace.
3. The movie was shot on a *breakneck* six-week schedule.

---

### breathless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbrɛθləs/ &middot; *BRETH-luhs*
**Русский:** запыхавшийся; затаивший дыхание
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more breathless*, *most breathless*
**Form:** -less from *breath*

Breathless has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Breathing hard and fast, especially after effort.** (запыхавшийся, задыхающийся)

1. She arrived *breathless* after running up the stairs.
2. The climb left us *breathless*.
3. He felt *more breathless* than usual after a short walk, so he saw a doctor.

**2. Very excited or eager, as if holding your breath.** (затаивший дыхание; восторженный)

4. We waited in *breathless* silence for the results.
5. The news was full of *breathless* praise for the new phone.

---

### breathtaking

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbrɛθˌteɪkɪŋ/ &middot; *BRETH-tay-king*
**Русский:** захватывающий дух; поразительный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more breathtaking*, *most breathtaking*
**Form:** compound — *breath* + *taking*: it takes your breath away

Breathtaking has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Extremely beautiful or impressive.** (захватывающий дух, потрясающий)

1. The view from the top is *breathtaking*.
2. We drove through some *breathtaking* scenery in Utah.
3. It was the *most breathtaking* sunset I've ever seen.

**2. Surprisingly great in degree, often of something bad.** (поразительный, невероятный)

4. The senator showed *breathtaking* arrogance.
5. The project went over budget by a *breathtaking* amount.

---

### breezy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbriːzi/ &middot; *BREE-zee*
**Русский:** ветреный, со свежим ветерком; непринужденный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *breezier*, *breeziest*
**Form:** -y from *breeze*

Breezy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. With a light, pleasant wind blowing.** (ветреный, со свежим ветерком)

1. It was a sunny, *breezy* afternoon.
2. The porch is *breezier* than the living room.
3. Bring a jacket — it gets *breezy* by the lake.

**2. Cheerful, relaxed and casual in manner or style.** (беззаботный, непринужденный)

4. She writes in a *breezy*, friendly style.
5. His *breezy* confidence annoyed some of the older staff.

---

### bridal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbraɪdl/ &middot; *BRY-duhl*
**Русский:** свадебный (невесты)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a bridal gown*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *bride*
**Contrast:** Sounds exactly like *bridle* (уздечка).

Of a bride or a wedding.

1. The *bridal* party arrived at the church at noon.
2. She works in a *bridal* shop in Dallas.
3. Her friends threw her a *bridal* shower.

---

### brief

**Pronunciation:** /briːf/ &middot; *BREEF*
**Русский:** краткий, короткий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *briefer*, *briefest*
**Contrast:** *Brief* is about time or the length of what is said, not physical length: *a short rope*, never *a brief rope*. The noun *brief* (a legal document) and *briefs* (underwear) are separate words.

Brief has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Lasting only a short time.** (короткий, недолгий)

1. We had a *brief* conversation in the hallway.
2. The meeting was *brief*.
3. This year's visit was even *briefer* than last year's.

**2. Using few words.** (краткий, сжатый)

4. Please keep your answers *brief*.
5. She gave a *brief* description of the suspect.

---

### brightly-colored

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbraɪtliˈkʌlərd/ &middot; *bryt-lee-KUHL-urd*
**Русский:** яркий, ярко окрашенный
**Type:** compound — adverb + participle; also color
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more brightly colored*, *most brightly colored*
**Form:** compound — *brightly* + *colored*
**Contrast:** American style guides write it open, with no hyphen after an *-ly* adverb: *brightly colored birds*. The hyphenated spelling is common but counts as an error in edited writing. American *colored*, not British *coloured*.

Having strong, vivid colors.

1. The kids wore *brightly-colored* raincoats.
2. Tropical fish are often *brightly colored*.
3. The males are *more brightly colored* than the females.

---

### brilliant

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbrɪljənt/ &middot; *BRIL-yuhnt*
**Русский:** блестящий, гениальный; яркий
**Type:** evaluative; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more brilliant*, *most brilliant*
**Contrast:** In American English *brilliant* means very clever, excellent or very bright. The British use as a general *great!* (*That's brilliant, thanks!*) sounds British to American ears.

Brilliant has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Extremely intelligent or talented.** (блестящий, гениальный)

1. She's a *brilliant* scientist.
2. That was a *brilliant* idea.
3. He was one of the *most brilliant* students I ever taught.

**2. Very bright or vivid.** (яркий, сияющий)

4. The sky was a *brilliant* blue.
5. We woke up to *brilliant* sunshine.

**3. Excellent; extremely successful.** (великолепный, блестящий)

6. The team had a *brilliant* season.
7. Her performance in the final act was *brilliant*.

---

### brimming

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbrɪmɪŋ/ &middot; *BRIM-ing*
**Русский:** полный до краев; переполненный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb or a noun with *with*: *eyes brimming with tears*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Form:** -ing from *brim*, the top edge of a cup

Brimming has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Full to the top with liquid.** (полный до краев)

1. She handed me a cup *brimming* with coffee.
2. His eyes were *brimming* with tears.
3. After a week of rain, the pond was *brimming*.

**2. Full of a feeling or quality.** (переполненный (чувством), полный)

4. The team is *brimming* with confidence.
5. The new coach is *brimming* with ideas.

---

### brisk

**Pronunciation:** /brɪsk/ &middot; *BRISK*
**Русский:** быстрый, энергичный; свежий; оживленный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *brisker*, *briskest*

Brisk has 4 distinct senses.

**1. Quick and energetic.** (быстрый, энергичный)

1. We took a *brisk* walk after dinner.
2. She set off at a *brisk* pace.
3. The music got *brisker* toward the end.

**2. Of weather: cold and fresh.** (свежий, бодрящий)

4. It was a *brisk* fall morning.
5. The wind off the lake was *brisk*.

**3. Of business or sales: busy and active.** (оживленный, бойкий)

6. Business at the store has been *brisk* all week.
7. Ticket sales were *brisk*.

**4. Of a manner: quick and practical, a little unfriendly.** (деловитый, резковатый)

8. The nurse was *brisk* but kind.
9. He gave me a *brisk* nod and walked on.

---

### brittle

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbrɪtl/ &middot; *BRIT-uhl*
**Русский:** хрупкий, ломкий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *brittler*, *brittlest*, *more brittle*, *most brittle*

Brittle has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Hard but easily broken.** (хрупкий, ломкий)

1. Old plastic becomes *brittle* in the sun.
2. Calcium keeps your bones from getting *brittle*.
3. Cast iron is *more brittle* than steel.

**2. Easily damaged or upset; tense and not warm.** (неустойчивый; напряженный)

4. The peace agreement is still *brittle*.
5. She gave a *brittle* laugh.

---

### broad

**Pronunciation:** /brɔːd/ &middot; *BRAWD*
**Русский:** широкий; общий
**Type:** size; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *broader*, *broadest*
**Contrast:** *Broad* and *wide* overlap for size (*a broad/wide river*), but *broad* is the word for *shoulders*, *smile*, *terms*, *daylight* and *accent*, and *wide* is the word for measurements: *ten feet wide*.

Broad has 4 distinct senses.

**1. Wide from side to side.** (широкий)

1. He has *broad* shoulders.
2. The river is *broader* here.
3. She gave me a *broad* smile.

**2. Including many different things or people.** (широкий, разнообразный)

4. The store sells a *broad* range of products.
5. The plan has *broad* support in Congress.
6. This is the *broadest* definition of the term.

**3. General, without details.** (общий, в общих чертах)

7. In *broad* terms, the plan is simple.
8. Can you give me a *broad* outline of the project?

**4. Clear and obvious; of an accent, strong and noticeable.** (явный, откровенный; сильный (об акценте))

9. He was robbed in *broad* daylight.
10. She dropped a *broad* hint that she wanted a ring.
11. He speaks with a *broad* Texas accent.

---

### broad-minded

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbrɔːdˈmaɪndɪd/ &middot; *brawd-MYN-did*
**Русский:** широких взглядов, терпимый
**Type:** compound — adjective + noun + -ed; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more broad-minded*, *most broad-minded*
**Form:** compound — *broad* + *mind* + -ed
**Opposite:** *narrow-minded*

Willing to accept opinions and ways of life different from your own.

1. My grandparents were surprisingly *broad-minded*.
2. She's a *broad-minded* teacher who listens to every point of view.
3. Young voters tend to be *more broad-minded* on social issues.

---

### broad-shouldered

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbrɔːdˈʃoʊldərd/ &middot; *brawd-SHOHL-durd*
**Русский:** широкоплечий
**Type:** compound — adjective + noun + -ed
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more broad-shouldered*, *most broad-shouldered*
**Form:** compound — *broad* + *shoulder* + -ed

Having wide shoulders.

1. He's *broad-shouldered* and over six feet tall.
2. A *broad-shouldered* man blocked the doorway.
3. Swimmers are usually *more broad-shouldered* than runners.

---

### broadband

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbrɔːdbænd/ &middot; *BRAWD-band*
**Русский:** широкополосный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *broad* + *band*
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *We finally got broadband out here*.

Of an internet connection: high-speed, carrying a lot of data at once.

1. Half the county still has no *broadband* service.
2. The town is paying to run *broadband* cable to every farm.
3. *Broadband* speeds here are slower than the ad promised.

---

### broiled

**Pronunciation:** /brɔɪld/ &middot; *BROYLD*
**Русский:** запеченный под грилем
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** past participle of *broil*
**Contrast:** *Broil* is American: to cook under strong direct heat from above, usually in the oven. British English calls this *grill*, while American *grilled* food is cooked over heat on a grill.

Cooked under very strong direct heat from above.

1. I'll have the *broiled* salmon, please.
2. The chicken is better *broiled* than fried.
3. Top the *broiled* steak with butter and herbs.

---

### broke

**Pronunciation:** /broʊk/ &middot; *BROHK*
**Русский:** без денег, на мели
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb: *I'm broke*; informally also *broke college kids*
**Comparison:** not gradable — but *flat broke*, *totally broke*
**Contrast:** Informal adjective, not the past tense of *break*: *I'm broke* (no money), but *My phone is broken* (damaged); ✗ *My phone is broke* is nonstandard. *Go broke* means to lose all your money or go bankrupt.

Having no money.

1. I can't go out this weekend — I'm *broke*.
2. The company went *broke* in 2009.
3. We were flat *broke* after paying the rent.

---

### broken

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbroʊkən/ &middot; *BROH-kuhn*
**Русский:** сломанный, разбитый; неработающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** past participle of *break*
**Opposite:** *unbroken*; *working*

Broken has 5 distinct senses.

**1. Damaged, cracked or in pieces.** (сломанный, разбитый)

1. Watch out for the *broken* glass.
2. She has a *broken* arm.
3. The back window was *broken*.

**2. Not working.** (неисправный, неработающий)

4. The elevator is *broken* again.
5. We called a plumber about the *broken* dishwasher.

**3. Of a promise, rule or agreement: not kept.** (нарушенный)

6. Voters are tired of *broken* promises.
7. The *broken* treaty led to war.
