**4. Of a person, family or relationship: badly hurt or ruined.** (сломленный; распавшийся)

8. He grew up in a *broken* home.
9. After losing his business, he was a *broken* man.
10. She never got over her *broken* engagement.

**5. Of a language: spoken badly and with many mistakes.** (ломаный)

11. He asked for directions in *broken* English.
12. We got by with *broken* Spanish and hand signals.

---

### broken-down

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbroʊkənˈdaʊn/ &middot; *broh-kuhn-DOWN*
**Русский:** сломавшийся; ветхий, развалившийся
**Type:** compound — participle + adverb
**Position:** both — hyphenated before a noun, usually *broken down* after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — from the verb *break down*

Broken-down has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a vehicle or machine: no longer working.** (сломавшийся)

1. We pushed the *broken-down* truck to the side of the road.
2. The bus was *broken down* on the highway for three hours.

**2. In very bad condition because of age, use or neglect.** (ветхий, развалившийся; изношенный)

3. They live in a *broken-down* farmhouse.
4. He played a *broken-down* old boxer in the film.

---

### bronchial

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbrɑːŋkiəl/ &middot; *BRAHNG-kee-uhl*
**Русский:** бронхиальный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *bronchial tubes*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *bronchi*, the air tubes of the lungs

Of the tubes that carry air into the lungs.

1. She has a *bronchial* infection.
2. Smoking damages the *bronchial* tubes.
3. The doctor gave him an inhaler for his *bronchial* asthma.

---

### bronze

**Pronunciation:** /brɑːnz/ &middot; *BRAHNZ*
**Русский:** бронзовый (о цвете)
**Type:** color
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** In *a bronze statue* or *a bronze medal* the word is the noun (the metal) used as a modifier; the adjective is the color. Russian *бронзовый* covers both.

Having the reddish-brown color of bronze.

1. His arms were *bronze* from a summer of working outside.
2. The leaves turn *bronze* in late fall.
3. She wore a *bronze* dress to the gala.

---

### brooding

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbruːdɪŋ/ &middot; *BROO-ding*
**Русский:** мрачный, задумчивый; зловещий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more brooding*, *most brooding*
**Form:** -ing from *brood*, to think for a long time about something sad

Brooding has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Seeming sad, serious and deep in thought.** (мрачный, погруженный в раздумья)

1. He played a *brooding* detective with a dark past.
2. She's quieter and *more brooding* than her sister.
3. His silence felt *brooding*, not calm.

**2. Dark and threatening.** (зловещий, гнетущий)

4. *Brooding* storm clouds gathered over the hills.
5. The old house has a *brooding* atmosphere.

---

### brownish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbraʊnɪʃ/ &middot; *BROWN-ish*
**Русский:** коричневатый, буроватый
**Type:** color
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ish from *brown*: somewhat brown

Slightly brown.

1. The tap water came out *brownish*.
2. The grass turned *brownish* in the heat.
3. She has *brownish* hair with red highlights.

---

### bruising

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbruːzɪŋ/ &middot; *BROO-zing*
**Русский:** тяжелый, изнурительный, жесткий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bruising*, *most bruising*
**Form:** -ing from *bruise*

Very hard and tiring, leaving the people involved hurt or weakened.

1. It was a *bruising* election campaign.
2. The team lost after a *bruising* overtime game.
3. The second round of talks was even *more bruising* than the first.

---

### brunette

**Pronunciation:** /bruːˈnɛt/ &middot; *broo-NET*
**Русский:** темноволосая (шатенка или брюнетка)
**Type:** color
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Often a noun (*a brunette*), and used mostly of women; the rare masculine spelling is *brunet*. English *brunette* covers dark brown to black hair, so it matches both Russian *шатенка* and *брюнетка*.

Of hair: dark brown; of a person: having dark brown hair.

1. She dyed her hair *brunette* for the role.
2. Both sisters are *brunette*.
3. The ad showed a tall *brunette* woman in a red coat.

---

### brusque

**Pronunciation:** /brʌsk/ &middot; *BRUHSK*
**Русский:** резкий, отрывистый, бесцеремонный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more brusque*, *most brusque*

Using few words in a way that seems rude.

1. The receptionist was *brusque* with us.
2. He gave a *brusque* reply and hung up.
3. Her emails got *more brusque* as the deadline got closer.

---

### brutal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbruːtl/ &middot; *BROO-tuhl*
**Русский:** жестокий; беспощадный, суровый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more brutal*, *most brutal*
**Contrast:** False friend: Russian *брутальный* is praise for a rugged, very masculine man. English *brutal* is always negative. For the Russian sense say *rugged* or *macho*.

Brutal has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Very cruel and violent.** (жестокий, зверский)

1. It was a *brutal* attack.
2. The dictator ran a *brutal* regime.
3. The war was *more brutal* than anyone expected.

**2. Very harsh, unpleasant or difficult.** (суровый, тяжелейший)

4. We had a *brutal* winter this year.
5. That workout was *brutal*.

**3. Direct and honest in a way that ignores people's feelings.** (беспощадный, нелицеприятный)

6. Let me be *brutal*: your essay isn't ready.
7. She was known for her *brutal* honesty.

---

### brute

**Pronunciation:** /bruːt/ &middot; *BROOT*
**Русский:** грубый, чисто физический (о силе)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only — *brute force*, *brute strength*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Usually a noun: *He's a brute* (грубый, жестокий человек). The adjective lives mainly in *brute force* and *brute strength*.

Using only physical strength or power, without thought or skill.

1. The thieves opened the safe by *brute* force.
2. He lifted the car off her with *brute* strength.
3. The hackers guessed the password with a *brute*-force attack.

---

### brutish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbruːtɪʃ/ &middot; *BROO-tish*
**Русский:** грубый, скотский, животный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more brutish*, *most brutish*
**Form:** -ish from *brute*

Rough, cruel and without thought or feeling, like an animal.

1. He was a *brutish* man with a violent temper.
2. Life in the prison camp was short and *brutish*.
3. The fighting grew *more brutish* as the war went on.

---

### bucolic

**Pronunciation:** /bjuːˈkɑːlɪk/ &middot; *byoo-KAH-lik*
**Русский:** сельский, пасторальный, идиллический
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bucolic*, *most bucolic*

Of the countryside, in a pleasant, peaceful way.

1. They moved to a *bucolic* town in Vermont.
2. The farm looks *bucolic*, but the work is hard.
3. The setting for the wedding couldn't have been *more bucolic*.

---

### budding

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbʌdɪŋ/ &middot; *BUHD-ing*
**Русский:** начинающий, подающий надежды; зарождающийся
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *a budding artist*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *bud*

Beginning to develop, often with promise.

1. She's a *budding* artist.
2. Their *budding* romance was the talk of the office.
3. The town has a *budding* tech industry.

---

### budgetary

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbʌdʒɪˌtɛri/ &middot; *BUHJ-i-tair-ee*
**Русский:** бюджетный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *budgetary problems*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ary from *budget*

Relating to a budget.

1. The school district faces serious *budgetary* problems.
2. The project was canceled for *budgetary* reasons.
3. Congress must approve all *budgetary* changes.

---

### buff

**Pronunciation:** /bʌf/ &middot; *BUHF*
**Русский:** накачанный, мускулистый; желтовато-бежевый
**Type:** quality; also color
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more buff*, *most buff*
**Contrast:** Informal American in the sense 'muscular'. The noun *buff* means a fan: *a movie buff*, *a history buff*.

Buff has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Informal: having strong, well-developed muscles.** (накачанный, мускулистый)

1. He got really *buff* over the summer.
2. The actor has to stay *buff* for the role.
3. He's *more buff* now than he was in college.

**2. Pale yellowish brown.** (желтовато-бежевый)

4. The documents came in a *buff* envelope.
5. The old barn was painted *buff* with white trim.

---

### buggy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbʌɡi/ &middot; *BUHG-ee*
**Русский:** глючный; кишащий насекомыми
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *buggier*, *buggiest*
**Form:** -y from *bug*
**Contrast:** The noun *buggy* is a light carriage, a golf cart, or in American English a baby carriage.

Buggy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of software: full of errors.** (глючный, с ошибками)

1. The first version of the app was really *buggy*.
2. The update made the game even *buggier*.

**2. Full of insects.** (кишащий насекомыми)

3. The campsite was hot and *buggy*.
4. Summer evenings by the lake get *buggy*.

---

### built-in

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbɪltˈɪn/ &middot; *bilt-IN*
**Русский:** встроенный
**Type:** compound — participle + adverb
**Position:** both — hyphenated before a noun, usually *built in* after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *built* + *in*

Built-in has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Made as a fixed part of something, not separate.** (встроенный)

1. The kitchen has *built-in* cabinets.
2. Most laptops have a *built-in* camera.
3. Is the microphone *built in*, or do I need to buy one?

**2. Being a natural or permanent part of something.** (заложенный, изначально присущий)

4. The current rules give incumbents a *built-in* advantage.
5. Kids seem to have a *built-in* sense of fairness.

---

### bulbous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbʌlbəs/ &middot; *BUHL-buhs*
**Русский:** выпуклый, шарообразный, луковицеобразный
**Type:** shape
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bulbous*, *most bulbous*
**Form:** -ous from *bulb*

Round and fat, often in an unattractive way.

1. The clown had a big *bulbous* nose.
2. The lamp has a *bulbous* glass base.
3. The new model looks *more bulbous* than the old one.

---

### Bulgarian

**Pronunciation:** /bʌlˈɡɛriən/ &middot; *buhl-GAIR-ee-uhn*
**Русский:** болгарский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ian from *Bulgaria*

Of or from Bulgaria, its people or its language.

1. She grew up in a *Bulgarian* village near the Black Sea.
2. Is this wine *Bulgarian*?
3. *Bulgarian* grammar has no noun cases, unlike Russian.

---

### bulging

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbʌldʒɪŋ/ &middot; *BUHL-jing*
**Русский:** выпирающий, набитый; выпученный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *bulge*

Sticking out in a round shape, often because of being very full.

1. He carried a *bulging* backpack.
2. The cartoon cat had huge *bulging* eyes.
3. Her suitcase was *bulging* with souvenirs.

---

### bulimic

**Pronunciation:** /buːˈlɪmɪk/ &middot; *boo-LIM-ik*
**Русский:** страдающий булимией; булимический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ic from *bulimia*

Having bulimia, an eating disorder in which a person eats a lot and then forces themselves to vomit.

1. She was *bulimic* all through high school.
2. The clinic treats *bulimic* and anorexic patients.
3. He had no idea his roommate was *bulimic*.

---

### bulky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbʌlki/ &middot; *BUHL-kee*
**Русский:** громоздкий, объемный; грузный
**Type:** size
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *bulkier*, *bulkiest*
**Form:** -y from *bulk*

Bulky has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Large and hard to carry or store.** (громоздкий, объемный)

1. The old TVs were heavy and *bulky*.
2. You can ship *bulky* furniture for an extra fee.
3. The winter coat is *bulkier* than it looks online.

**2. Of a person: big and heavy.** (крупный, грузный)

4. A *bulky* linebacker knocked him to the ground.
5. He's gotten *bulky* from lifting weights.

---

### bulletproof

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbʊlɪtˌpruːf/ &middot; *BUUL-it-proof*
**Русский:** пуленепробиваемый; неуязвимый
**Type:** compound — noun + adjective
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *bullet* + *-proof*

Bulletproof has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Made so that bullets cannot go through it.** (пуленепробиваемый)

1. The president's car has *bulletproof* glass.
2. Officers wear *bulletproof* vests.
3. The bank teller's window is *bulletproof*.

**2. Informal: impossible to fail, defeat or criticize.** (неуязвимый, безупречный)

4. There's no such thing as a *bulletproof* business plan.
5. The team looked *bulletproof* until the playoffs.

---

### bullheaded

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbʊlˈhɛdɪd/ &middot; *buul-HED-id*
**Русский:** упрямый, твердолобый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bullheaded*, *most bullheaded*
**Form:** compound — *bull* + *head* + -ed

Stubborn in a stupid or unreasonable way.

1. He's too *bullheaded* to admit he was wrong.
2. Her *bullheaded* refusal to compromise ended the talks.
3. My dad got even *more bullheaded* as he got older.

---

### bullish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbʊlɪʃ/ &middot; *BUUL-ish*
**Русский:** ожидающий роста (о рынке); оптимистичный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bullish*, *most bullish*
**Takes:** *on* or *about* + noun
**Form:** -ish from *bull*, an investor who expects prices to rise
**Opposite:** *bearish*

Bullish has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of investors or markets: expecting prices to rise.** (ожидающий роста, «бычий»)

1. Investors are *bullish* on tech stocks.
2. Analysts turned *more bullish* after the earnings report.

**2. Confident and optimistic about the future.** (оптимистичный, уверенный)

3. The coach is *bullish* about the team's chances.
4. She sounded *bullish* about the future of the company.

---

### bumbling

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbʌmblɪŋ/ &middot; *BUHM-bling*
**Русский:** неуклюжий, бестолковый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bumbling*, *most bumbling*
**Form:** -ing from *bumble*

Clumsy and confused, making silly mistakes.

1. He played a *bumbling* police officer in the movie.
2. The new manager seemed *bumbling* but well-meaning.
3. The villain in the sequel is even *more bumbling* than the first one.

---

### bumper-to-bumper

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbʌmpərtəˈbʌmpər/ &middot; *buhm-pur-tuh-BUHM-pur*
**Русский:** плотный (о пробке), бампер к бамперу
**Type:** compound — noun + preposition + noun
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *bumper* + *to* + *bumper*

Of traffic: moving very slowly, with cars very close together.

1. We sat in *bumper-to-bumper* traffic for two hours.
2. The freeway was *bumper-to-bumper* all the way to LA.
3. It's always *bumper-to-bumper* on the bridge at five.

---

### bumpy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbʌmpi/ &middot; *BUHM-pee*
**Русский:** ухабистый; тряский; непростой
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *bumpier*, *bumpiest*
**Form:** -y from *bump*
**Opposite:** *smooth*

Bumpy has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Having an uneven surface.** (ухабистый, неровный)

1. The road to the cabin is *bumpy*.
2. The rash made his skin red and *bumpy*.

**2. Of a trip: shaking up and down.** (тряский)

3. It was a *bumpy* flight.
4. The ride got *bumpier* as we climbed.

**3. Full of problems or difficulties.** (трудный, непростой)

5. The company had a *bumpy* first year.
6. The switch to the new system has been *bumpy*.

---

### buoyant

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɔɪənt/ &middot; *BOY-uhnt*
**Русский:** плавучий; жизнерадостный; устойчиво растущий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more buoyant*, *most buoyant*

Buoyant has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Able to float.** (плавучий)

1. Salt water makes you *more buoyant*.
2. Cork is light and *buoyant*.

**2. Cheerful and confident.** (жизнерадостный, бодрый)

3. She was in a *buoyant* mood after the interview.
4. The crowd was *buoyant* after the win.

**3. Of prices, sales or the economy: rising or staying strong.** (устойчивый, растущий)

5. Sales stayed *buoyant* through the holidays.
6. The housing market is still *buoyant*.

---

### burdened

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɜːrdnd/ &middot; *BUR-duhnd*
**Русский:** обремененный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — often *heavily burdened*
**Takes:** *with* or *by* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *burden*

Carrying a heavy load of work, worry, debt or responsibility.

1. Many graduates are *burdened* with student loans.
2. She felt *burdened* by her family's expectations.
3. The program helps heavily *burdened* homeowners.

---

### burdensome

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɜːrdnsəm/ &middot; *BUR-duhn-suhm*
**Русский:** обременительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more burdensome*, *most burdensome*
**Form:** -some from *burden*

Causing a lot of work, worry or expense.

1. Small businesses complain about *burdensome* regulations.
2. The paperwork is *burdensome*.
3. The new rules are even *more burdensome* than the old ones.

---

### bureaucratic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbjʊrəˈkrætɪk/ &middot; *byuur-uh-KRAT-ik*
**Русский:** бюрократический
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bureaucratic*, *most bureaucratic*
**Form:** -ic from *bureaucrat*

Using too many complicated official rules and procedures.

1. Getting a building permit is a slow, *bureaucratic* process.
2. The company became *more bureaucratic* as it grew.
3. The agency is hopelessly *bureaucratic*.

---

### burly

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɜːrli/ &middot; *BUR-lee*
**Русский:** здоровенный, дюжий
**Type:** size
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *burlier*, *burliest*

Of a man: big, strong and heavy.

1. A *burly* security guard stood at the door.
2. Her brothers are all tall and *burly*.
3. He was the *burliest* player on the team.

---

### Burmese

**Pronunciation:** /bərˈmiːz/ &middot; *bur-MEEZ*
**Русский:** бирманский (мьянманский)
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ese from *Burma*
**Contrast:** The country is officially *Myanmar*, and news writing often uses *Myanmar* as a modifier (*the Myanmar military*). *Burmese* is still the usual word for the language, the food and the cat breed.

Of or from Burma (Myanmar), its people or its language.

1. We had dinner at a *Burmese* restaurant in San Francisco.
2. The *Burmese* alphabet has round letters.
3. Her grandmother is *Burmese*.

---

### burned

**Pronunciation:** /bɜːrnd/ &middot; *BURND*
**Русский:** обгоревший, сгоревший, обожженный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** past participle of *burn*
**Contrast:** Americans use *burned* for the past tense and usually for the adjective too; *burnt* is also common as an adjective, especially for food and colors (*burnt toast*, *burnt orange*).

Damaged, hurt or blackened by fire or heat.

1. The *burned* building was torn down.
2. His hand was badly *burned*.
3. Throw out the *burned* cookies.

---

### burned-out

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbɜːrndˈaʊt/ &middot; *burnd-OWT*
**Русский:** выгоревший; измотанный; перегоревший
**Type:** compound — participle + adverb
**Position:** both — hyphenated before a noun, usually *burned out* after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more burned out*, *most burned out*
**Form:** compound — from the verb *burn out*

Burned-out has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Exhausted and without energy or interest because of too much work or stress.** (эмоционально выгоревший, измотанный)

1. Many nurses feel *burned out*.
2. *Burned-out* teachers are leaving the profession.
3. I was *more burned out* after that job than I realized.

**2. Destroyed by fire, with only the outside left.** (выгоревший, сгоревший)

4. We drove past *burned-out* cars.
5. The *burned-out* factory has been empty for years.

**3. Of a light bulb: no longer working.** (перегоревший)

6. Replace the *burned-out* bulb in the hallway.
7. Two of the streetlights on our block are *burned out*.

---

### burning

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɜːrnɪŋ/ &middot; *BUR-ning*
**Русский:** горящий; жгучий; насущный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — attributive only in the sense 'strong, urgent'
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *burn*

Burning has 3 distinct senses.

**1. On fire.** (горящий)

1. Firefighters rushed into the *burning* building.
2. We could smell *burning* rubber.

**2. Very hot, or causing a hot, painful feeling.** (обжигающий, жгучий)

3. The sand was *burning* under our feet.
4. I felt a *burning* pain in my chest.

**3. Very strong or urgent.** (страстный; насущный)

5. She has a *burning* desire to succeed.
6. The *burning* question is who will pay for it.

---

### burnt

**Pronunciation:** /bɜːrnt/ &middot; *BURNT*
**Русский:** подгоревший, горелый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** past participle of *burn*
**Contrast:** As a verb form Americans say *burned* (*I burned the toast*); *burnt* is mostly the adjective: *burnt toast*, *burnt orange*.

Burnt has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Damaged or blackened by fire or heat.** (подгоревший, горелый)

1. The kitchen smelled of *burnt* toast.
2. The rice is *burnt* on the bottom.
3. Scrape off the *burnt* bits before serving.

**2. Of a color: deep and brownish.** (темный, жженый (о цвете))

4. She wore a *burnt* orange sweater.
5. The walls are painted a *burnt* red.

---

### Burundian

**Pronunciation:** /bʊˈrʊndiən/ &middot; *buu-RUUN-dee-uhn*
**Русский:** бурундийский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -an from *Burundi*

Of or from Burundi, its people or its culture.

1. Our church sponsors a *Burundian* refugee family.
2. *Burundian* coffee is known for its fruity flavor.
3. The *Burundian* runner finished second.

---

### bush-league

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbʊʃˌliːɡ/ &middot; *BUUSH-leeg*
**Русский:** любительский, второсортный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bush-league*
**Form:** compound — *bush* + *league*, a small-town minor baseball league
**Contrast:** Informal American, from baseball.

Amateurish and unprofessional; of poor quality.

1. That was a *bush-league* move by the referee.
2. The whole event felt a little *bush-league*.
3. Their website looks even *more bush-league* than their ads.

---

### bushy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbʊʃi/ &middot; *BUUSH-ee*
**Русский:** густой, кустистый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *bushier*, *bushiest*
**Form:** -y from *bush*

Of hair, fur or plants: growing thickly.

1. He has thick, *bushy* eyebrows.
2. The fox has a long *bushy* tail.
3. His beard is *bushier* than it was last year.

---

### businesslike

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɪznəsˌlaɪk/ &middot; *BIZ-nuhs-lyk*
**Русский:** деловой, деловитый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more businesslike*, *most businesslike*
**Form:** -like from *business*
**Contrast:** Russian *деловой* is usually the noun *business* as a modifier: *a business meeting*, *a business suit*. *Businesslike* describes a manner: efficient, serious and not emotional.

Serious, practical and efficient.

1. She has a calm, *businesslike* manner.
2. The meeting was short and *businesslike*.
3. His tone got *more businesslike* once we started talking about money.

---

### busted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbʌstɪd/ &middot; *BUHS-tid*
**Русский:** сломанный, разбитый; пойманный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** past participle of *bust*
**Contrast:** Informal. In careful writing say *broken* or *caught*.

Busted has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Informal: broken or damaged; of a body part, injured.** (сломанный, разбитый)

1. I've got a *busted* phone screen.
2. The AC is *busted* again.
3. He came home with a *busted* lip.

**2. Informal: caught doing something wrong.** (пойманный, попавшийся)

4. Mom found the candy wrappers, and I knew I was *busted*.
5. You're so *busted*!

---

### bustling

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbʌslɪŋ/ &middot; *BUHS-ling*
**Русский:** оживленный, людный, шумный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more bustling*, *most bustling*
**Form:** -ing from *bustle*

Full of people and busy activity.

1. We walked through a *bustling* market.
2. The downtown area is *bustling* with tourists in summer.
3. The port was once the *most bustling* city on the coast.

---

### busty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbʌsti/ &middot; *BUHS-tee*
**Русский:** с пышной грудью
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most
**Form:** -y from *bust*
**Contrast:** Informal and often heard as sexist; avoid it in polite conversation.

Of a woman: having large breasts.

1. The magazine was criticized for its *busty* cartoon characters.
2. She was tired of being cast in *busty* blonde roles.
3. The dress comes in a cut for *busty* women.

---

### butch

**Pronunciation:** /bʊtʃ/ &middot; *BUUCH*
**Русский:** мужеподобный; подчеркнуто мужественный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more butch*, *most butch*
**Contrast:** Informal. Calling a woman *butch* can be offensive, though some lesbian women use it proudly about themselves.

Butch has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a woman: looking or behaving in a traditionally masculine way.** (мужеподобная, с мужскими манерами)

1. She describes her style as *butch*.
2. She dressed *more butch* in college.

**2. Of a man: very masculine and tough-looking.** (подчеркнуто мужественный, брутальный)

3. He tried to look *butch* in a leather jacket.
4. The truck ads always feature some *butch* cowboy.

---

### buttery

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbʌtəri/ &middot; *BUHT-ur-ee*
**Русский:** маслянистый, сливочный; мягкий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more buttery*, *most buttery*
**Form:** -y from *butter*

Buttery has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Containing a lot of butter, or tasting or smelling like butter.** (маслянистый, сливочный)

1. These croissants are soft and *buttery*.
2. The popcorn is too *buttery* for me.
3. This chardonnay is *more buttery* than the last one.

**2. Very soft and smooth.** (мягкий, нежный, бархатистый)

4. The seats are covered in *buttery* leather.
5. She has a smooth, *buttery* voice.

---

### button-down

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbʌtnˌdaʊn/ &middot; *BUHT-uhn-down*
**Русский:** с воротником на пуговицах; консервативный
**Type:** compound — noun + adverb
**Position:** attributive only — *a button-down shirt*
**Comparison:** not gradable — but *very button-down* in the sense 'conservative'
**Form:** compound — *button* + *down*
**Contrast:** *A button-down* (noun) is the shirt itself. In the sense 'conservative', *button-down* and *buttoned-down* mean the same.

Button-down has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a shirt collar: fastened to the shirt with small buttons.** (с воротником на пуговицах)

1. He wore a blue *button-down* shirt and khakis.
2. The school requires a white *button-down* shirt.
3. *Button-down* collars look fine without a tie.

**2. Conservative and conventional.** (консервативный, строгий)

4. It's a very *button-down* law firm.
5. He left his *button-down* office job to become a chef.

---

### buttoned

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbʌtnd/ &middot; *BUHT-uhnd*
**Русский:** застегнутый (на пуговицы)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** past participle of *button*
**Opposite:** *unbuttoned*

Fastened with buttons.

1. Keep your coat *buttoned* — it's freezing.
2. He wore a *buttoned* cardigan over his shirt.
3. Her shirt was *buttoned* all the way up.

---

### buttoned-down

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbʌtndˈdaʊn/ &middot; *buht-uhnd-DOWN*
**Русский:** консервативный, строгий
**Type:** compound — participle + adverb
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more buttoned-down*, *most buttoned-down*
**Form:** compound — *buttoned* + *down*

Conservative, formal and conventional in style or behavior.

1. He's a *buttoned-down* accountant from Ohio.
2. Silicon Valley is less *buttoned-down* than Wall Street.
3. The bank's culture is *more buttoned-down* than a startup's.

---

### buttoned-up

**Pronunciation:** /ˌbʌtndˈʌp/ &middot; *buht-uhnd-UHP*
**Русский:** сдержанный, чопорный; четко организованный
**Type:** compound — participle + adverb
**Position:** both — hyphenated before a noun, often *buttoned up* after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more buttoned-up*, *most buttoned-up*
**Form:** compound — *buttoned* + *up*

Buttoned-up has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Reserved and formal; not showing feelings.** (сдержанный, чопорный)

1. My father was a *buttoned-up* man who rarely laughed.
2. She's *more buttoned-up* at work than at home.

**2. Carefully organized and under control.** (четко организованный, продуманный)

3. The campaign runs a very *buttoned-up* operation.
4. By Friday every detail of the deal was *buttoned up*.

---

### buxom

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbʌksəm/ &middot; *BUHK-suhm*
**Русский:** пышногрудая, полногрудая
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most
**Contrast:** Somewhat old-fashioned and literary; like *busty*, it can sound sexist.

Of a woman: with large breasts and a full, healthy-looking figure.

1. The painting shows a *buxom* barmaid pouring beer.
2. Old sailors' tales describe *buxom* mermaids.
3. The novel's heroine is a *buxom* farm girl.

---

### buzzing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbʌzɪŋ/ &middot; *BUHZ-ing*
**Русский:** жужжащий, гудящий; оживленный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — after a verb it is usually the verb *buzz*: *The room was buzzing*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *buzz*

Buzzing has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Making a low, continuous humming sound.** (жужжащий, гудящий)

1. There's a *buzzing* noise coming from the fridge.
2. I kept hearing a *buzzing* sound in my ears.

**2. Full of activity and excitement.** (оживленный, бурлящий)

3. Austin has a *buzzing* music scene.
4. The café has a *buzzing* atmosphere on weekends.

---

### bygone

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbaɪˌɡɔːn/ &middot; *BY-gawn*
**Русский:** минувший, былой
**Type:** age
**Position:** attributive only — *a bygone era*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *by* + *gone*
**Contrast:** In *let bygones be bygones* (кто старое помянет) the word is a noun.

From a time long past.

1. The hotel reminds me of a *bygone* era.
2. These photos show the farms of *bygone* days.
3. The shop sells toys from a *bygone* age.

---

### Byzantine

**Pronunciation:** /ˈbɪzənˌtiːn/ &middot; *BIZ-uhn-teen*
**Русский:** византийский; запутанный
**Type:** origin; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — but *more byzantine* in the sense 'complicated'
**Contrast:** In the sense 'too complicated' it is often written with a small *b*: *byzantine tax rules*.

Byzantine has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of the Byzantine Empire, its art or its church.** (византийский)

1. The museum has a room full of *Byzantine* mosaics.
2. Constantinople was the capital of the *Byzantine* Empire.

**2. Very complicated and hard to understand, often secretive.** (запутанный, крайне сложный)

3. The tax code is *byzantine*.
4. Getting a visa involves *Byzantine* rules and endless paperwork.

---

### cagey

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkeɪdʒi/ &middot; *KAY-jee*
**Русский:** скрытный, уклончивый; хитрый, осторожный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *cagier*, *cagiest*
**Takes:** *about* + noun (what someone will not talk about)
**Contrast:** Also spelled *cagy*.

Cagey has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Unwilling to give information.** (скрытный, уклончивый)

1. He was *cagey* about his plans.
2. The company has been *cagey* about the sales numbers.
3. She got even *cagier* when I asked about her ex.

**2. Clever and careful, especially from experience.** (хитрый, осторожный)

4. The *cagey* veteran pitcher fooled the young hitters.
5. She's a *cagey* negotiator.

---

### calamitous

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈlæmɪtəs/ &middot; *kuh-LAM-i-tuhs*
**Русский:** катастрофический, пагубный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more calamitous*, *most calamitous*
**Form:** -ous from *calamity*

Causing great damage or suffering; disastrous.

1. The flood had *calamitous* effects on local farms.
2. Cutting the budget now would be *calamitous*.
3. The second decision proved even *more calamitous* than the first.

---

### calculated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkælkjəˌleɪtɪd/ &middot; *KAL-kyuh-lay-tid*
**Русский:** рассчитанный, преднамеренный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most
**Takes:** *to*-infinitive (meant to have an effect)
**Form:** past participle of *calculate*
**Contrast:** *Calculated* describes a plan or act; *calculating* describes a person who plans selfishly: *a calculated move* by *a calculating politician*.

Calculated has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Carefully and deliberately planned to have a particular effect.** (рассчитанный, преднамеренный)

1. The remark was a *calculated* insult.
2. The ad is *calculated* to appeal to teenagers.
3. Her decision to leave was cold and *calculated*.

**2. Of a risk: taken after thinking carefully about what could happen.** (обдуманный, взвешенный)

4. Starting the business was a *calculated* risk.
5. The coach took a *calculated* gamble in the fourth quarter.

---

### calculating

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkælkjəˌleɪtɪŋ/ &middot; *KAL-kyuh-lay-ting*
**Русский:** расчетливый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more calculating*, *most calculating*
**Form:** -ing from *calculate*
**Contrast:** *Calculating* is a person who plans selfishly; *calculated* is the plan or act.

Planning carefully to get what you want, often without caring about others.

1. She's a cold, *calculating* businesswoman.
2. Behind his friendly smile, he was *calculating* and ruthless.
3. Her brother was even *more calculating* than she was.

---

### calibrated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkæləˌbreɪtɪd/ &middot; *KAL-uh-bray-tid*
**Русский:** откалиброванный; выверенный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — but *carefully calibrated*, *finely calibrated*
**Form:** past participle of *calibrate*

Calibrated has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of an instrument: adjusted to measure accurately.** (откалиброванный)

1. Make sure the scale is properly *calibrated*.
2. The lab uses only *calibrated* thermometers.

**2. Carefully planned or measured for a particular effect.** (выверенный, взвешенный)

3. The White House gave a carefully *calibrated* response.
4. Her speech was finely *calibrated* to please both sides.

---

### Californian

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkæləˈfɔːrnjən/ &middot; *kal-uh-FORN-yuhn*
**Русский:** калифорнийский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -an from *California*
**Contrast:** For products and places Americans more often use the noun as a modifier: *California wine*, *the California coast*. *Californian* is common for people and style.

Of or from California.

1. She has a relaxed *Californian* attitude.
2. The restaurant serves *Californian* cuisine.
3. Her husband is *Californian*, but she's from Maine.

---

### callous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkæləs/ &middot; *KAL-uhs*
**Русский:** бессердечный, черствый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more callous*, *most callous*
**Contrast:** *Callous* (unkind) sounds the same as the noun *callus* (мозоль). Hands with hard skin are *calloused*, not *callous*.

Not caring about other people's pain or problems.

1. It was a *callous* thing to say to a grieving mother.
2. Officials seemed *callous* toward the victims.
3. The company's response was *more callous* than we feared.

---

### calloused

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkæləst/ &middot; *KAL-uhst*
**Русский:** мозолистый, огрубевший
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more calloused*, *most calloused*
**Form:** -ed from *callus*; also spelled *callused*
**Contrast:** Not *callous*, which means heartless.

Having thick, hard skin from work or use.

1. His hands were *calloused* from years of farm work.
2. Guitar players have *calloused* fingertips.
3. My feet were even *more calloused* after the hike.

---

### calm

**Pronunciation:** /kɑːm/ &middot; *KAHM*
**Русский:** спокойный; безветренный, тихий
**Type:** quality; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *calmer*, *calmest*

Calm has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not upset, worried or excited; peaceful.** (спокойный)

1. Try to stay *calm*.
2. She spoke in a *calm* voice.
3. I felt *calmer* after talking to him.

**2. Of weather or water: still, without strong wind or waves.** (безветренный, тихий, спокойный)

4. The sea was *calm* this morning.
5. It was the *calmest* night of the whole trip.

---

### caloric

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈlɔːrɪk/ &middot; *kuh-LOR-ik*
**Русский:** калорийный; относящийся к калориям
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — but *more caloric* in the sense 'high in calories'
**Form:** -ic from *calorie*
**Contrast:** For food, everyday English says *high-calorie* or *fattening* more often than *caloric*: *high-calorie snacks*.

Caloric has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to calories.** (калорийный (о количестве калорий))

1. Most adults need a daily *caloric* intake of about 2,000 calories.
2. The label lists the *caloric* content per serving.

**2. Informal: high in calories.** (калорийный, высококалорийный)

3. Nuts are healthy but very *caloric*.
4. Fried food is *more caloric* than steamed food.

---

### calorie-free

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkæləriˌfriː/ &middot; *KAL-uh-ree-free*
**Русский:** без калорий, некалорийный
**Type:** compound — noun + *-free*
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *calorie* + *-free* (without)

Containing no calories.

1. Water is *calorie-free*.
2. The soda uses a *calorie-free* sweetener.
3. Unfortunately, nothing that tastes this good is *calorie-free*.

---

### Cambodian

**Pronunciation:** /kæmˈboʊdiən/ &middot; *kam-BOH-dee-uhn*
**Русский:** камбоджийский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -an from *Cambodia*

Of or from Cambodia, its people or its culture.

1. Angkor Wat is the most famous *Cambodian* temple.
2. Long Beach has a large *Cambodian* community.
3. Her parents are *Cambodian*.

---

### Cameroonian

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkæməˈruːniən/ &middot; *kam-uh-ROO-nee-uhn*
**Русский:** камерунский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ian from *Cameroon*

Of or from Cameroon, its people or its culture.

1. He plays for the *Cameroonian* national soccer team.
2. *Cameroonian* cooking often uses plantains and peanuts.
3. My roommate is *Cameroonian* and speaks French and English.

---

### camouflaged

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkæməˌflɑːʒd/ &middot; *KAM-uh-flahzhd*
**Русский:** замаскированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — but *well camouflaged*
**Form:** -ed from *camouflage*
**Contrast:** In *camouflage pants* the word is the noun used as a modifier (камуфляжный); *camouflaged* means hidden by color or shape.

Hidden by colors, patterns or coverings that match the surroundings.

1. The lizard is well *camouflaged* against the rocks.
2. The hunters waited in a *camouflaged* blind.
3. The entrance to the bunker was *camouflaged* with branches.

---

### campy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkæmpi/ &middot; *KAM-pee*
**Русский:** нарочито вычурный, китчевый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *campier*, *campiest*, *more campy*
**Form:** -y from *camp*, a style that enjoys exaggeration and bad taste

Exaggerated and theatrical in a way that is funny or enjoyed on purpose as bad taste.

1. We watched a *campy* 1960s horror movie.
2. The musical is loud, silly and *campy*.
3. The sequel is even *campier* than the original.

---

### can-do

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkænˌduː/ &middot; *KAN-doo*
**Русский:** деятельный, предприимчивый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only — *a can-do attitude*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *can* + *do*: 'I can do it'

Confident, positive and eager to solve problems.

1. We love her *can-do* attitude.
2. The crew has a real *can-do* spirit.
3. He brought a *can-do* approach to the job.

---

### canceled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkænsəld/ &middot; *KAN-suhld*
**Русский:** отмененный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** past participle of *cancel*
**Contrast:** American spelling has one *l*: *canceled*, *canceling*. British English doubles it: *cancelled*. *Cancellation* has two in both.

Called off; no longer going to happen.

1. We were stuck at the airport because of a *canceled* flight.
2. Tonight's game is *canceled* because of rain.
3. She got a refund for the *canceled* order.

---

### cancerous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkænsərəs/ &middot; *KAN-sur-uhs*
**Русский:** раковый, злокачественный; губительный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ous from *cancer*

Cancerous has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having or being cancer.** (раковый, злокачественный)

1. The surgeon removed a *cancerous* tumor.
2. Tests showed the cells were not *cancerous*.
3. Doctors found *cancerous* growths on his lungs.

**2. Harmful and spreading, like a disease.** (губительный, разъедающий)

4. Corruption had a *cancerous* effect on the government.
5. Gossip can be *cancerous* in a small office.

---

### candid

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkændɪd/ &middot; *KAN-did*
**Русский:** откровенный; непостановочный (о фото)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more candid*, *most candid*
**Takes:** *about* + topic; *with* + person

Candid has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Honest and direct, even about difficult things.** (откровенный, искренний)

1. She was very *candid* about her struggle with depression.
2. Let me be *candid* with you.
3. He was *more candid* in private than in interviews.

**2. Of a photo: taken without the person posing or knowing.** (непостановочный, скрытой камерой)

4. The wedding album is full of *candid* shots.
5. I like *candid* photos better than posed ones.

---

### candied

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkændid/ &middot; *KAN-deed*
**Русский:** засахаренный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** past participle of *candy*, to cook in sugar

Cooked in sugar syrup and coated with sugar.

1. The fruitcake is full of *candied* cherries.
2. We make *candied* yams every Thanksgiving.
3. Top the salad with *candied* pecans.

---

### canine

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkeɪnaɪn/ &middot; *KAY-nyn*
**Русский:** собачий; клыковый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a canine unit*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a canine* is a dog in police or veterinary language, or a pointed tooth (клык).

Canine has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to dogs.** (собачий, кинологический)

1. The police *canine* unit found the drugs.
2. Parvo is a serious *canine* disease.
3. She runs a *canine* training center.

**2. Of the pointed teeth between the front teeth and the back teeth.** (клыковый)

4. Humans have four *canine* teeth.
5. The dentist said my upper *canine* tooth is growing in crooked.

---

### canned

**Pronunciation:** /kænd/ &middot; *KAND*
**Русский:** консервированный; записанный заранее, шаблонный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** past participle of *can*, to put in a can
**Contrast:** American *canned* is British *tinned*.

Canned has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of food: kept in a sealed metal can.** (консервированный)

1. *Canned* soup is often high in salt.
2. We donated *canned* goods to the food bank.
3. Are the tomatoes fresh or *canned*?

**2. Recorded or prepared in advance, so not live or natural.** (записанный заранее; шаблонный)

4. The sitcom used *canned* laughter.
5. The spokesman gave a *canned* answer.

---

### cannibalistic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkænəbəˈlɪstɪk/ &middot; *kan-uh-buh-LIS-tik*
**Русский:** каннибальский, людоедский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -istic from *cannibal*

Eating the flesh of animals of the same kind.

1. Some spiders are *cannibalistic*.
2. Scientists study *cannibalistic* behavior in fish.
3. The horror movie is about a *cannibalistic* family in Texas.

---

### canny

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkæni/ &middot; *KAN-ee*
**Русский:** проницательный, смекалистый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *cannier*, *canniest*

Smart and good at judging situations, especially in business or politics.

1. She's a *canny* investor.
2. It was a *canny* move to sell before prices dropped.
3. He was the *canniest* politician in the state.

---

### canonical

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈnɑːnɪkl/ &middot; *kuh-NAH-ni-kuhl*
**Русский:** канонический; классический, общепринятый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ical from *canon*

Canonical has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Accepted as the standard, classic or official version.** (классический, общепринятый)

1. *Moby-Dick* is a *canonical* American novel.
2. This is the *canonical* example of the problem.
3. Fans argue about which movies are *canonical* in the series.

**2. Relating to church law or to the books accepted as part of the Bible.** (канонический)

4. The four *canonical* Gospels are Matthew, Mark, Luke and John.
5. Some early Christian texts were never considered *canonical*.

---

### cantankerous

**Pronunciation:** /kænˈtæŋkərəs/ &middot; *kan-TANG-kur-uhs*
**Русский:** сварливый, вздорный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cantankerous*, *most cantankerous*

Bad-tempered and always complaining or arguing.

1. My *cantankerous* old neighbor yells at kids on his lawn.
2. He grew *cantankerous* in his old age.
3. The cat got *more cantankerous* every year.

---

### capacious

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈpeɪʃəs/ &middot; *kuh-PAY-shuhs*
**Русский:** вместительный, просторный; обширный
**Type:** size
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more capacious*, *most capacious*
**Form:** -ous, related to *capacity*

Capacious has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having a lot of space inside.** (вместительный, просторный)

1. She pulled a notebook out of her *capacious* handbag.
2. The SUV has a *capacious* trunk.
3. The *more capacious* model costs $2,000 extra.

**2. Wide in range; able to hold a lot of information or ideas.** (обширный, емкий)

4. He has a *capacious* memory for baseball statistics.
5. The term is *capacious* enough to include almost anything.

---

### capital

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkæpɪtəl/ &middot; *KAP-i-tuhl*
**Русский:** прописной, заглавный; караемый смертной казнью
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a capital letter*, *a capital crime*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** False friend: Russian *капитальный* (*капитальный ремонт*, *капитальное строительство*) is *major*, *thorough* or *permanent* in English — *a major renovation*, not *a capital renovation*. *The capital* (the main city) and *capital* (money for a business) are nouns.

Capital has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a letter: in the large form used at the start of a sentence or a name.** (прописной, заглавный)

1. Write your name in *capital* letters.
2. Start every sentence with a *capital* letter.
3. Is that a *capital* I or a lowercase L?

**2. Of a crime or punishment: involving the death penalty.** (караемый смертной казнью; смертный (о казни))

4. Texas still uses *capital* punishment.
5. Killing a police officer is a *capital* crime in that state.

---

### capitalist

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkæpɪtəlɪst/ &middot; *KAP-i-tuh-list*
**Русский:** капиталистический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — *a capitalist economy*, *The country is capitalist*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ist from *capital*
**Opposite:** *socialist*; *communist*
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a capitalist* is a person who owns a business or invests money.

Based on private ownership of businesses and free markets.

1. The United States has a *capitalist* economy.
2. China combines *capitalist* markets with one-party rule.
3. Is a system really *capitalist* if the government owns the banks?

---

### capitalistic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkæpɪtəˈlɪstɪk/ &middot; *kap-i-tuh-LIS-tik*
**Русский:** капиталистический (часто неодобр.)
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more capitalistic*, *most capitalistic*
**Form:** -ic from *capitalist*
**Contrast:** *Capitalist* is the neutral, more common word. *Capitalistic* often suggests 'too focused on money and profit'.

Based on or too focused on private profit and free markets.

1. Critics say the health care system is too *capitalistic*.
2. He grew up in a *capitalistic* culture that measured success in dollars.
3. Hong Kong was once called the *most capitalistic* place on earth.

---

### capricious

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈprɪʃəs/ &middot; *kuh-PRISH-uhs*
**Русский:** непредсказуемый, своенравный; переменчивый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more capricious*, *most capricious*
**Contrast:** Russian *капризный* about a child is usually *fussy*, *cranky* or *spoiled* in English. *Capricious* is formal and describes sudden changes with no reason — a boss, a decision, the weather.

Changing suddenly and without a good reason; unpredictable.

1. The judge ruled that the firing was arbitrary and *capricious*.
2. Farmers here depend on *capricious* weather.
3. The new manager was even *more capricious* than the old one.

---

### captivated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkæptɪveɪtɪd/ &middot; *KAP-ti-vay-tid*
**Русский:** очарованный, заворожённый
**Type:** participial; also emotion
**Position:** both — *a captivated audience*, *The audience was captivated*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more captivated*, *most captivated*
**Takes:** *by* + noun
**Form:** -ed — the person who feels the attraction
**Contrast:** *Captivated* is the person watching; *captivating* is the thing that holds their attention: *a captivating show*, *a captivated audience*.

So interested or charmed that you cannot stop watching or listening.

1. The children were *captivated* by the magician.
2. She watched the dancers with a *captivated* expression.
3. I've never seen an audience *more captivated*.

---

### captivating

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkæptɪveɪtɪŋ/ &middot; *KAP-ti-vay-ting*
**Русский:** пленительный, захватывающий
**Type:** participial; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more captivating*, *most captivating*
**Form:** -ing — what causes the feeling

So interesting or attractive that it holds all your attention.

1. She has a *captivating* smile.
2. The documentary was absolutely *captivating*.
3. It was the *most captivating* performance of the night.

---

### captive

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkæptɪv/ &middot; *KAP-tiv*
**Русский:** пленный, в неволе; вынужденный (о слушателях, покупателях)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — *captive animals*; after a verb mostly in *be held captive*, *take someone captive*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *free*; *wild* (of animals)
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *The captives were released*.

Captive has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Kept as a prisoner or in a cage; not free.** (пленный, содержащийся в неволе)

1. The hostages were held *captive* for three weeks.
2. *Captive* elephants often live shorter lives than wild ones.

**2. Of people: unable to leave or to choose something else.** (вынужденный, никуда не девающийся (о слушателях, покупателях))

3. The passengers were a *captive* audience for the pilot's jokes.
4. Airport restaurants charge high prices because they have a *captive* market.

---

### carbonated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːrbəneɪtɪd/ &middot; *KAR-buh-nay-tid*
**Русский:** газированный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from the verb *carbonate*
**Opposite:** *still*; *noncarbonated*
**Contrast:** Russian *газированный* is not *gassy*: *gassy* means having gas in your stomach. Say *carbonated water* or *sparkling water*; soda that has lost its bubbles is *flat*.

Of a drink: containing bubbles of gas.

1. Do you want *carbonated* or still water?
2. *Carbonated* drinks can be hard on your teeth.
3. Is this juice *carbonated*?

---

### carcinogenic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɑːrsɪnəˈdʒɛnɪk/ &middot; *kar-si-nuh-JEN-ik*
**Русский:** канцерогенный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more carcinogenic*, *most carcinogenic*
**Form:** -ic from *carcinogen*, a substance that causes cancer

Able to cause cancer.

1. Asbestos is highly *carcinogenic*.
2. Many chemicals in cigarette smoke are *carcinogenic*.
3. Researchers are testing which of the two compounds is *more carcinogenic*.

---

### cardiac

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːrdiæk/ &middot; *KAR-dee-ak*
**Русский:** сердечный (мед.), кардиологический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *cardiac arrest*, not *The arrest was cardiac*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *Cardiac* is a medical word; everyday speech says *heart*: *a heart attack*, *heart disease*. Russian *сердечный* in the sense 'warm, kind' is *warm* or *heartfelt*, never *cardiac*.

Of or relating to the heart, especially in medicine.

1. He went into *cardiac* arrest on the tennis court.
2. She's a nurse in the *cardiac* unit.
3. The hospital has one of the best *cardiac* surgery programs in the state.

---

### cardinal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːrdənəl/ &middot; *KAR-duh-nuhl*
**Русский:** главный, основной; количественный (о числах)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a cardinal rule*, *cardinal numbers*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** False friend: Russian *кардинальный* (*кардинальные изменения*) is *radical*, *fundamental* or *sweeping* — *sweeping changes*, not *cardinal changes*. The noun *cardinal* is a Catholic church official and a red bird.

Cardinal has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Most important; basic.** (главный, основной)

1. The *cardinal* rule in this kitchen is to wash your hands.
2. Lying to your customers is the *cardinal* sin of business.

**2. Of numbers and directions: basic, as in one, two, three, or north, south, east, west.** (количественный (о числах); основной (о сторонах света))

3. One, two and three are *cardinal* numbers; first, second and third are ordinal.
4. North, south, east and west are the four *cardinal* directions.

---

### cardiopulmonary

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɑːrdioʊˈpʊlmənɛri/ &middot; *kar-dee-oh-PUUL-muh-nair-ee*
**Русский:** сердечно-лёгочный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *cardio-* (heart) + *pulmonary* (lungs)
**Contrast:** Known mainly from *cardiopulmonary resuscitation*, which everyone calls *CPR*.

Of or relating to the heart and lungs together.

1. Every lifeguard must be trained in *cardiopulmonary* resuscitation.
2. After the surgery he spent six weeks in a *cardiopulmonary* rehab program.
3. A *cardiopulmonary* bypass machine does the work of the heart and lungs during surgery.

---

### cardiovascular

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɑːrdioʊˈvæskjələr/ &middot; *kar-dee-oh-VAS-kyuh-lur*
**Русский:** сердечно-сосудистый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *cardio-* (heart) + *vascular* (blood vessels)
**Contrast:** In gyms the short noun is *cardio*: *I do cardio three times a week*.

Of or relating to the heart and blood vessels.

1. *Cardiovascular* disease is the leading cause of death in the US.
2. Swimming is great *cardiovascular* exercise.
3. Smoking damages your *cardiovascular* system.

---

### carefree

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɛrfriː/ &middot; *KAIR-free*
**Русский:** беззаботный
**Type:** quality; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more carefree*, *most carefree*
**Form:** compound — *care* + *free* ('free of care')
**Opposite:** *worried*; *anxious*
**Contrast:** Not *careless*. *Carefree* is a pleasant state (беззаботный); *careless* is a fault (небрежный, неосторожный).

Happy and relaxed, without worries or responsibilities.

1. We spent a *carefree* summer at the lake.
2. She seemed happy and *carefree*.
3. I was a lot *more carefree* before I had kids.

---

### caring

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɛrɪŋ/ &middot; *KAIR-ing*
**Русский:** заботливый, отзывчивый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more caring*, *most caring*
**Form:** -ing from the verb *care*
**Opposite:** *uncaring*
**Contrast:** *Caring* is заботливый (kind to others); *careful* is осторожный, аккуратный.

Kind, and showing that you care about other people.

1. He's a warm, *caring* father.
2. The nurses were so *caring*.
3. She's the *most caring* person I know.

---

### carnal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːrnəl/ &middot; *KAR-nuhl*
**Русский:** плотский
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only — *carnal desires*
**Comparison:** not gradable

Formal: relating to the body and sex, rather than the mind or spirit.

1. The preacher warned against *carnal* desires.
2. The novel is about *carnal* pleasure and the guilt that follows.
3. In old legal language, *carnal* knowledge means sex.

---

### carnivorous

**Pronunciation:** /kɑːrˈnɪvərəs/ &middot; *kar-NIV-ur-uhs*
**Русский:** плотоядный, хищный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ous from *carnivore*, a meat-eating animal
**Opposite:** *herbivorous*

Eating meat.

1. Lions and wolves are *carnivorous*.
2. The Venus flytrap is a *carnivorous* plant.
3. Most sharks are *carnivorous* and hunt fish or seals.

---

### carpeted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːrpɪtɪd/ &middot; *KAR-pi-tid*
**Русский:** покрытый ковролином, устланный ковром
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *carpet*
**Contrast:** American *carpet* usually means wall-to-wall carpet (ковролин); a single piece on the floor is *a rug*.

Covered with carpet.

1. The bedrooms are *carpeted*, and the kitchen has tile.
2. We walked down a long *carpeted* hallway.
3. Is the basement *carpeted*?

---

### carsick

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːrsɪk/ &middot; *KAR-sik*
**Русский:** которого укачало (в машине)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly after *get* or *feel*: *I get carsick*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more carsick*, *most carsick*
**Form:** compound — *car* + *sick*
**Contrast:** One word, like *seasick* and *airsick*. The general term is *motion sickness*. Russian *меня укачивает* is *I get carsick*.

Feeling sick to your stomach from riding in a car.

1. I always get *carsick* on mountain roads.
2. Let the *carsick* kid sit in the front seat.
3. She felt *carsick* after reading in the back seat.

---

### carved

**Pronunciation:** /kɑːrvd/ &middot; *KARVD*
**Русский:** резной, вырезанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from the verb *carve*

Cut into a shape or decorated with patterns cut into the surface.

1. They sat on *carved* wooden chairs.
2. The front door is beautifully *carved*.
3. He brought me a *carved* stone bear from Alaska.

---

### case-by-case

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkeɪs baɪ ˈkeɪs/ &middot; *kays-by-KAYS*
**Русский:** индивидуальный, в каждом случае отдельно
**Type:** compound — noun + *by* + noun
**Position:** attributive only — *on a case-by-case basis*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *case* + *by* + *case*, hyphenated before a noun
**Contrast:** After a verb it is an adverb phrase with no hyphens: *We decide case by case*.

Dealing with each situation separately instead of using one rule for all.

1. Refunds are handled on a *case-by-case* basis.
2. The school takes a *case-by-case* approach to late homework.
3. The judge made *case-by-case* decisions about bail.

---

### cast-iron

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkæstˌaɪərn/ &middot; *KAST-y-urn*
**Русский:** чугунный; железный, несокрушимый
**Type:** material; also quality
**Position:** attributive only — *a cast-iron skillet*; the noun is written open: *made of cast iron*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — past participle *cast* + *iron*

Cast-iron has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Made of cast iron, a hard iron poured into a mold.** (чугунный)

1. My grandmother cooked everything in a *cast-iron* skillet.
2. The park still has its old *cast-iron* benches.

**2. Very strong or certain; impossible to break or doubt.** (железный, несокрушимый)

3. You need a *cast-iron* stomach to eat that chili.
4. The suspect had a *cast-iron* alibi.

---

### cast-off

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkæstˌɔːf/ &middot; *KAST-awf*
**Русский:** ношеный, ненужный (отданный другим)
**Type:** compound — verb + particle
**Position:** attributive only — *cast-off clothes*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — from the phrasal verb *cast off*
**Contrast:** The plural noun *cast-offs* means the things themselves: *I wore my brother's cast-offs*. For clothes Americans also say *hand-me-down*.

No longer wanted by the first owner and given away or thrown out.

1. The kids wore *cast-off* clothes from their cousins.
2. The shelter furnished its rooms with *cast-off* furniture.
3. He drove his father's *cast-off* pickup truck.

---

### casual

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkæʒuəl/ &middot; *KAZH-oo-uhl*
**Русский:** непринуждённый; повседневный; несерьёзный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more casual*, *most casual*
**Opposite:** *formal* (of clothes and events); *serious*
**Contrast:** Russian *случайный* in the sense 'accidental' or 'random' is *accidental*, *random* or *chance*: *a random choice*, *a chance meeting*. *Casual* means relaxed, informal or not serious.

Casual has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Relaxed, and not showing much worry or interest.** (непринуждённый, небрежный, беспечный)

1. He tried to sound *casual*, but he was nervous.
2. She has a very *casual* attitude toward deadlines.

**2. Of clothes, places or events: informal and suitable for everyday.** (повседневный, неформальный)

3. The dress code at our office is *casual*.
4. It's a *casual* restaurant, so jeans are fine.
5. Can I wear something *more casual* tonight?

**3. Not regular, close or serious.** (несерьёзный, поверхностный, нерегулярный)

6. We're just *casual* friends.
7. He's a *casual* fan who only watches the playoffs.
8. Even a *casual* glance shows the numbers are wrong.

---

### cataclysmic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkætəˈklɪzmɪk/ &middot; *kat-uh-KLIZ-mik*
**Русский:** катастрофический, разрушительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — an extreme adjective: *truly cataclysmic*, not *very cataclysmic*
**Form:** -ic from *cataclysm*

Causing sudden and enormous destruction or change.

1. The asteroid had a *cataclysmic* effect on life on earth.
2. Economists warned of *cataclysmic* consequences if the bank failed.
3. The flood was *cataclysmic* for the small town.

---

### Catalan

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkætəlæn/ &middot; *KAT-uh-lan*
**Русский:** каталонский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — *Catalan food*, *Gaudí was Catalan*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun for the language and the people: *She speaks Catalan*. *Catalonian* exists but is less common. Always a capital *C*.

Of or from Catalonia, a region of northeastern Spain, or its people or language.

1. *Catalan* independence is a divisive issue in Spain.
2. We ate a traditional *Catalan* dish of bread rubbed with tomato.
3. The architect Antoni Gaudí was *Catalan*.

---

### catalytic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkætəˈlɪtɪk/ &middot; *kat-uh-LIT-ik*
**Русский:** каталитический; побуждающий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ic, related to *catalyst* and *catalysis*

Catalytic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Speeding up a chemical reaction.** (каталитический)

1. The car needs a new *catalytic* converter.
2. Thieves stole the *catalytic* converters from three cars on our street.

**2. Causing important changes or events to happen.** (побуждающий, служащий катализатором)

3. The protests played a *catalytic* role in the reform.
4. The grant had a *catalytic* effect on the local economy.

---

### catastrophic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkætəˈstrɑːfɪk/ &middot; *kat-uh-STRAH-fik*
**Русский:** катастрофический
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more catastrophic*, *most catastrophic*; with *truly* or *absolutely* rather than *very*
**Form:** -ic from *catastrophe*
**Contrast:** The stress moves: *catastrophe* is kuh-TAS-truh-fee, *catastrophic* is kat-uh-STRAH-fik.

Causing terrible damage or suffering; extremely bad.

1. The hurricane caused *catastrophic* flooding.
2. A mistake like that could be *catastrophic* for the company.
3. It was the *most catastrophic* wildfire in the state's history.

---

### catatonic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkætəˈtɑːnɪk/ &middot; *kat-uh-TAH-nik*
**Русский:** кататонический; оцепеневший
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Catatonic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. In medicine: unable to move or respond because of a mental illness.** (кататонический)

1. The patient remained *catatonic* for several days.
2. Doctors treated her *catatonic* symptoms with medication.

**2. Informal: so shocked, tired or bored that you do not move or react.** (оцепеневший, в ступоре)

3. After finals week I sat on the couch in a *catatonic* state.
4. The kids were *catatonic* after six hours of video games.

---

### catching

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkætʃɪŋ/ &middot; *KATCH-ing*
**Русский:** заразный, заразительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** predicative only — before a noun use *contagious* or *infectious*: *a contagious disease*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *catch*, as in *catch a cold*

Informal: easily passed from one person to another.

1. Don't worry, this kind of rash isn't *catching*.
2. Her laughter was *catching*, and soon we were all laughing.
3. Is the flu *catching* before you have symptoms?

---

### catchy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkætʃi/ &middot; *KATCH-ee*
**Русский:** запоминающийся, прилипчивый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *catchier*, *catchiest*
**Form:** *catch* + -y

Of a tune, phrase or name: pleasant and easy to remember.

1. The song has a really *catchy* chorus.
2. We need a *catchier* name for the app.
3. That's the *catchiest* jingle I've ever heard.

---

### categorical

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkætəˈɡɔːrɪkəl/ &middot; *kat-uh-GOR-i-kuhl*
**Русский:** категорический, безоговорочный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more categorical*, *most categorical*
**Contrast:** Russian *категоричный* about a person is *uncompromising*, *dogmatic* or *black-and-white* in English. *Categorical* goes with statements: *a categorical denial*, *a categorical no*.

Of a statement: clear, direct and without any doubt or exception.

1. The senator issued a *categorical* denial.
2. The answer was a *categorical* no.
3. Her refusal couldn't have been *more categorical*.

---

### cathartic

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈθɑːrtɪk/ &middot; *kuh-THAR-tik*
**Русский:** очищающий, приносящий облегчение
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cathartic*, *most cathartic*
**Form:** -ic from *catharsis*

Helping you release strong emotions and feel better.

1. Crying at the funeral was *cathartic*.
2. Writing the book was a *cathartic* experience for her.
3. For some people, a hard workout is *more cathartic* than therapy.

---

### catty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkæti/ &middot; *KAT-ee*
**Русский:** ехидный, язвительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *cattier*, *cattiest*
**Form:** *cat* + -y, doubling the *t*

Saying unkind things about other people in a quietly nasty way.

1. She made a *catty* remark about my dress.
2. Stop being so *catty*.
3. The comments got *cattier* as the night went on.

---

### Caucasian

**Pronunciation:** /kɔːˈkeɪʒən/ &middot; *kaw-KAY-zhuhn*
**Русский:** белый (о расе); кавказский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** A trap for Russian speakers: in American forms, police reports and medical records *Caucasian* means 'white', with no link to the Caucasus. A man from Dagestan or Georgia is not called *Caucasian* in everyday American speech; say *from the Caucasus*. Many Americans now simply say *white*.

Caucasian has 2 distinct senses.

**1. White; of European ancestry, used as a formal racial label.** (белый, европеоидный (о расе))

1. The suspect was described as a *Caucasian* male in his thirties.
2. The form asks whether you are *Caucasian*, Black, Asian or Hispanic.

**2. Of or from the Caucasus region between the Black Sea and the Caspian Sea.** (кавказский)

3. The museum has a fine collection of *Caucasian* rugs.
4. Chechen belongs to the family of Northeast *Caucasian* languages.

---

### caught up

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɔːt ˈʌp/ &middot; *kawt UP*
**Русский:** увлечённый, втянутый; наверставший
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only — *I got caught up in the story*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *in* + noun (absorbed or involved); *on* + noun (no longer behind with work, reading or episodes)
**Form:** past participle of the phrasal verb *catch up*
**Contrast:** *She caught up with us* is the phrasal verb (догнала). *She was caught up in the scandal* and *I'm caught up on my email* are the adjective.

Caught up has 2 distinct senses.

**1. So involved in something that you forget other things, or cannot get free of it.** (увлечённый, втянутый)

1. I got so *caught up* in the book that I missed my stop.
2. He was *caught up* in a bribery scandal.

**2. Having finished the work, reading or episodes you were behind on.** (наверставший, догнавший)

3. I'm finally *caught up* on my email.
4. Don't tell me what happens — I'm not *caught up* yet.

---

### causal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɔːzəl/ &middot; *KAW-zuhl*
**Русский:** причинный, причинно-следственный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *cause*
**Contrast:** Do not confuse with *casual* — the same letters in a different order. *Causal* is about cause and effect; *casual* is relaxed or informal.

Being the cause of something, or showing that one thing causes another.

1. Scientists found no *causal* link between the vaccine and autism.
2. Is the relationship *causal*, or is it just a correlation?
3. The report describes the *causal* chain of events that led to the crash.

---

### caustic

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɔːstɪk/ &middot; *KAWS-tik*
**Русский:** едкий; язвительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more caustic*, *most caustic*

Caustic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Able to burn or destroy things by chemical action.** (едкий)

1. Keep *caustic* cleaners away from children.
2. Lye is extremely *caustic*.

**2. Of words or humor: very critical and unkind.** (язвительный, колкий)

3. She's known for her *caustic* wit.
4. His reviews got *more caustic* over the years.

---

### cautionary

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɔːʃənɛri/ &middot; *KAW-shuh-nair-ee*
**Русский:** поучительный, предостерегающий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a cautionary tale*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ary from *caution*

Giving a warning.

1. His story is a *cautionary* tale about borrowing money.
2. The doctor added a *cautionary* note about side effects.
3. The company's collapse serves as a *cautionary* example for startups.

---

### cautious

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɔːʃəs/ &middot; *KAW-shuhs*
**Русский:** осторожный, осмотрительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cautious*, *most cautious*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*; *with* + noun
**Form:** -ous from *caution*
**Opposite:** *reckless*; *incautious*
**Contrast:** *Cautious* is about avoiding risk. *Careful* also means doing something with attention to detail: *a careful worker*, *careful work*.

Careful to avoid danger, risk or mistakes.

1. Investors are *cautious* about the new market.
2. She's a very *cautious* driver.
3. You should be *more cautious* with your passwords.

---

### cavalier

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkævəˈlɪr/ &middot; *kav-uh-LEER*
**Русский:** бесцеремонный, беспечно-пренебрежительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cavalier*, *most cavalier*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*; *with* + noun
**Contrast:** False friend: Russian *кавалер* is a noun (a gentleman, a dance partner). The English adjective is negative: not caring enough about something serious.

Not showing enough care or concern about something important.

1. He has a *cavalier* attitude toward safety.
2. The company was *cavalier* about protecting customer data.
3. I've never met anyone *more cavalier* with other people's money.

---

### cavernous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkævərnəs/ &middot; *KAV-ur-nuhs*
**Русский:** огромный (о помещении), похожий на пещеру
**Type:** size
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cavernous*, *most cavernous*
**Form:** -ous from *cavern*

Of a room or space: very large, dark and often empty.

1. The concert was held in a *cavernous* old warehouse.
2. The new arena feels *cavernous* when it's half empty.
3. Their living room was even *more cavernous* than ours.

---

### ceaseless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsiːsləs/ &middot; *SEES-luhs*
**Русский:** непрестанный, беспрерывный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *cease*

Continuing without stopping.

1. The *ceaseless* noise of the traffic kept me awake.
2. Her energy seems *ceaseless*.
3. The nurses worked with *ceaseless* dedication during the pandemic.

---

### celebrated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛləbreɪtɪd/ &middot; *SEL-uh-bray-tid*
**Русский:** знаменитый, прославленный
**Type:** participial; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more celebrated*, *most celebrated*
**Takes:** *for* + noun or *-ing* (the reason); *as* + noun (the role)
**Form:** -ed from *celebrate*

Famous and praised by many people.

1. She's a *celebrated* jazz pianist.
2. The chef is *celebrated* for his seafood.
3. It's one of the *most celebrated* novels of the century.

---

### celebratory

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛləbrətɔːri/ &middot; *SEL-uh-bruh-tor-ee*
**Русский:** праздничный, торжественный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — *a celebratory dinner*, *The mood was celebratory*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more celebratory*, *most celebratory*
**Form:** -ory from *celebrate*

Done or used to celebrate something.

1. We had a *celebratory* dinner after the game.
2. The mood in the office was *celebratory*.
3. This year's parade felt even *more celebratory* than last year's.

---

### celestial

**Pronunciation:** /səˈlɛstʃəl/ &middot; *suh-LES-chuhl*
**Русский:** небесный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *celestial bodies*
**Comparison:** not gradable

Celestial has 2 distinct senses.

**1. In or relating to the sky or outer space.** (небесный (астрономический))

1. Stars and planets are *celestial* bodies.
2. Early sailors relied on *celestial* navigation.

**2. Literary: of heaven; heavenly and very beautiful.** (небесный, божественный)

3. The choir sang with almost *celestial* voices.
4. The ceiling painting shows *celestial* beings with golden wings.

---

### celiac

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsiːliæk/ &middot; *SEE-lee-ak*
**Русский:** целиакийный; страдающий целиакией
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — *celiac disease*; informally *She's celiac*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** American spelling *celiac*; British *coeliac*. Also a noun: *a celiac*, a person with the disease.

Of or having a disease in which the body cannot digest gluten.

1. People with *celiac* disease can't eat wheat.
2. My sister is *celiac*, so we cook without gluten.
3. The bakery has a *celiac*-safe menu.

---

### celibate

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛləbət/ &middot; *SEL-uh-buht*
**Русский:** соблюдающий целибат, безбрачный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Not having sex, often for religious reasons.

1. Catholic priests are expected to remain *celibate*.
2. He lived a *celibate* life as a monk.
3. She decided to stay *celibate* for a year.

---

### cellular

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛljələr/ &middot; *SEL-yuh-lur*
**Русский:** клеточный; сотовый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ar from *cell*
**Contrast:** For phones, everyday American speech shortens it to *cell*: *a cell phone*, *cell service*. British English says *mobile*.

Cellular has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of or made of the cells of living things.** (клеточный)

1. The drug works at the *cellular* level.
2. *Cellular* damage builds up as we age.

**2. Of mobile phone networks.** (сотовый, мобильный)

3. The cabin has no *cellular* service.
4. Our *cellular* data plan costs forty dollars a month.

---

### Celtic

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɛltɪk/ &middot; *KEL-tik*
**Русский:** кельтский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Pronounced KEL-tik for the peoples, languages and music, but the Boston Celtics basketball team is SEL-tiks.

Of the Celts, ancient peoples of Europe, or of the Irish, Scottish, Welsh and Breton cultures and languages.

1. Irish and Welsh are *Celtic* languages.
2. The necklace has a *Celtic* knot design.
3. Most of the music at the festival was *Celtic*.

---

### censored

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛnsərd/ &middot; *SEN-surd*
**Русский:** подвергнутый цензуре
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from the verb *censor*
**Opposite:** *uncensored*
**Contrast:** Not *censured*, which means officially criticized (осуждённый).

With parts removed because they are considered offensive, dangerous or secret.

1. TV stations showed a *censored* version of the movie.
2. Parts of the report were heavily *censored*.
3. The *censored* words were replaced with beeps.

---

### centennial

**Pronunciation:** /sɛnˈtɛniəl/ &middot; *sen-TEN-ee-uhl*
**Русский:** столетний, в честь столетия
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a centennial celebration*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *the town's centennial* (столетие, сотая годовщина). British English prefers *centenary*.

Marking the 100th anniversary of something.

1. The city is planning a big *centennial* celebration.
2. The post office issued a *centennial* stamp.
3. The university's *centennial* year begins in September.

---

### centered

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛntərd/ &middot; *SEN-turd*
**Русский:** расположенный по центру; уравновешенный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
