**Comparison:** more/most — *more centered*, *most centered*
**Form:** -ed from *center*
**Contrast:** American spelling; British *centred*. In compounds it means 'focused on': *child-centered*, *self-centered*. *Centered on* + noun, as in *The talks were centered on trade*, is the verb in the passive.

Centered has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Placed in the middle of something.** (расположенный по центру)

1. Make sure the title is *centered* on the page.
2. The picture on that wall isn't quite *centered*.

**2. Calm, emotionally balanced and sure of what matters to you.** (уравновешенный, собранный)

3. Yoga helps me feel *centered*.
4. She seems *more centered* since she changed jobs.

---

### central

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛntrəl/ &middot; *SEN-truhl*
**Русский:** центральный; главный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both — *central Chicago*, *Family is central to her life*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more central*, *most central*
**Takes:** *to* + noun (in the 'most important' sense)
**Opposite:** *peripheral*; *outlying* (of places)

Central has 3 distinct senses.

**1. In or near the middle of a place.** (центральный)

1. We stayed at a hotel in *central* Chicago.
2. I'd rather rent something smaller in a *more central* neighborhood.

**2. Most important; main.** (главный, основной, ключевой)

3. Family is *central* to her life.
4. Price was the *central* issue in the negotiations.

**3. Controlled from one main place for a whole building or organization.** (централизованный, центральный)

5. The house has *central* air and heat.
6. Please send the forms to the *central* office.

---

### Central American

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛntrəl əˈmɛrɪkən/ &middot; *SEN-truhl uh-MAIR-i-kuhn*
**Русский:** центральноамериканский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *Central America* is the seven countries from Guatemala and Belize to Panama. Mexico is *North American*, and *Latin American* covers all of Spanish- and Portuguese-speaking America. No hyphen, two capitals.

Of or from Central America, the land between Mexico and Colombia.

1. Coffee is a major *Central American* export.
2. Many *Central American* families have settled in Los Angeles.
3. Her parents are *Central American* — they came from Honduras.

---

### centralized

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛntrəlaɪzd/ &middot; *SEN-truh-lyzd*
**Русский:** централизованный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more centralized*, *most centralized*
**Form:** -ed from *centralize*
**Opposite:** *decentralized*

Controlled by one central authority or managed from one place.

1. The Soviet Union had a *centralized* economy.
2. Hiring is *centralized* at the head office.
3. The new system is *more centralized* than the old one.

---

### centrist

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɛntrɪst/ &middot; *SEN-trist*
**Русский:** центристский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more centrist*, *most centrist*
**Form:** -ist from *center*
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a centrist* is a politician or voter in the political center.

Having political views that are neither left nor right.

1. She's running as a *centrist* candidate.
2. His views on taxes are fairly *centrist*.
3. The party has become *more centrist* over the years.

---

### ceramic

**Pronunciation:** /səˈræmɪk/ &middot; *suh-RAM-ik*
**Русский:** керамический
**Type:** material
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** The noun *ceramics* means the craft or the objects: *She teaches ceramics*.

Made of clay that has been baked hard.

1. We put *ceramic* tile in the bathroom.
2. She collects *ceramic* bowls from Mexico.
3. Is this mug *ceramic* or plastic?

---

### cerebral

**Pronunciation:** /səˈriːbrəl/ &middot; *suh-REE-bruhl*
**Русский:** мозговой; рассудочный, интеллектуальный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both — the brain sense only before a noun: *a cerebral hemorrhage*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cerebral*, *most cerebral*; the brain sense is not gradable
**Contrast:** Also pronounced SAIR-uh-bruhl; both are standard in American English.

Cerebral has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of or relating to the brain.** (мозговой, церебральный)

1. He suffered a *cerebral* hemorrhage.
2. *Cerebral* palsy affects movement and muscle control.

**2. Using the mind rather than the feelings; intellectual.** (рассудочный, интеллектуальный)

3. It's a *cerebral* thriller with almost no action.
4. Her new album is *more cerebral* than her earlier hits.

---

### ceremonial

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɛrəˈmoʊniəl/ &middot; *ser-uh-MOH-nee-uhl*
**Русский:** церемониальный, обрядовый; номинальный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — *a ceremonial sword*; after a verb mostly in the 'no real power' sense: *The role is ceremonial*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *ceremony*

Ceremonial has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Used in or connected with a ceremony.** (церемониальный, обрядовый)

1. The chief wore a *ceremonial* headdress.
2. The mayor cut the ribbon with a pair of *ceremonial* scissors.

**2. Having an official title but no real power.** (номинальный, чисто представительский)

3. The king's role is largely *ceremonial*.
4. In Germany the president is mostly a *ceremonial* figure.

---

### certifiable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɜːrtɪfaɪəbəl/ &middot; *SUR-ti-fy-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** чокнутый (разг.); подлежащий сертификации
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -able from *certify*
**Contrast:** The 'crazy' sense comes from the old legal meaning 'able to be officially declared insane'. It is a joke, not a medical term.

Certifiable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Informal: crazy.** (чокнутый, ненормальный)

1. My neighbor is completely *certifiable*.
2. Anyone who swims in that lake in January is *certifiable*.

**2. Able to be officially approved or confirmed.** (подлежащий сертификации, поддающийся подтверждению)

3. The farm has to wait three years before its crops are *certifiable* as organic.
4. Results must be accurate and *certifiable*.

---

### certified

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɜːrtɪfaɪd/ &middot; *SUR-ti-fyd*
**Русский:** дипломированный, сертифицированный; заверенный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *certify*
**Opposite:** *uncertified*
**Contrast:** *Certified mail* in the US is sent with proof that it was delivered (заказное письмо с уведомлением).

Certified has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Officially trained and approved to do a job.** (дипломированный, сертифицированный)

1. She's a *certified* yoga instructor.
2. All our electricians are licensed and *certified*.
3. He works as a *certified* public accountant.

**2. Officially checked and guaranteed as genuine or of good quality.** (заверенный, сертифицированный)

4. Send the contract by *certified* mail.
5. You'll need a *certified* copy of your birth certificate.
6. We bought a *certified* pre-owned car.

---

### cervical

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɜːrvɪkəl/ &middot; *SUR-vi-kuhl*
**Русский:** шейки матки; шейный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** A medical word. For the neck, everyday speech just says *neck*: *neck pain*, not *cervical pain*.

Cervical has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of the cervix, the narrow lower part of the uterus.** (шейки матки)

1. Regular Pap tests help prevent *cervical* cancer.
2. The HPV vaccine protects against most *cervical* cancers.

**2. Of the neck, especially the bones of the spine in the neck.** (шейный)

3. He fractured a *cervical* vertebra in the crash.
4. The doctor ordered an MRI of her *cervical* spine.

---

### cesarean

**Pronunciation:** /sɪˈzɛriən/ &middot; *si-ZAIR-ee-uhn*
**Русский:** кесарево (о сечении)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a cesarean section*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** In speech Americans usually say *a C-section*. Also a noun: *She had a cesarean*. British spelling *caesarean*.

Of a birth: done by cutting through the mother's abdomen.

1. The doctor recommended a *cesarean* section.
2. She had a *cesarean* delivery with her second baby.
3. *Cesarean* births take longer to recover from.

---

### Chadian

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃædiən/ &middot; *CHAD-ee-uhn*
**Русский:** чадский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ian from *Chad*

Of or from Chad, a country in north-central Africa.

1. N'Djamena is the *Chadian* capital.
2. *Chadian* troops joined the peacekeeping mission.
3. Her husband is *Chadian*.

---

### chagrined

**Pronunciation:** /ʃəˈɡrɪnd/ &middot; *shuh-GRIND*
**Русский:** огорчённый, раздосадованный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both — *a chagrined smile*, *He was chagrined*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more chagrined*, *most chagrined*
**Takes:** *to*-infinitive; *at* or *by* + noun
**Form:** -ed from the noun *chagrin*
**Contrast:** Formal and literary. In conversation Americans say *embarrassed* or *annoyed*. The *g* is hard and the stress is on the second syllable: shuh-GRIND, rhyming with *pinned*.

Embarrassed and annoyed, especially because of a mistake or failure.

1. He was *chagrined* to learn that he had missed the deadline.
2. She gave a *chagrined* smile when her phone rang during the speech.
3. I was less *chagrined* than amused by my mistake.

---

### chalky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃɔːki/ &middot; *CHAW-kee*
**Русский:** меловой, как мел
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *chalkier*, *chalkiest*
**Form:** *chalk* + -y; the *l* is silent

Like chalk: dry and powdery, or very pale white.

1. The medicine has a *chalky* taste.
2. His face went *chalky* white.
3. This antacid is even *chalkier* than the other brand.

---

### challenging

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃælɪndʒɪŋ/ &middot; *CHAL-in-jing*
**Русский:** трудный, требующий усилий; вызывающий
**Type:** participial; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more challenging*, *most challenging*
**Form:** -ing from *challenge*
**Contrast:** Americans often use *challenging* as a polite word for *difficult* or even *bad*: *a challenging year*, *a challenging child*.

Challenging has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Difficult in a way that tests your ability, often in an interesting way.** (трудный, сложный, требующий усилий)

1. Teaching middle school is *challenging* but rewarding.
2. The hike was *more challenging* than we expected.
3. She wanted a *challenging* job, not an easy one.

**2. Of a look or question: questioning someone's authority or honesty.** (вызывающий)

4. He gave the teacher a *challenging* look.
5. Reporters asked the mayor some *challenging* questions.

---

### chance

**Pronunciation:** /tʃæns/ &middot; *CHANS*
**Русский:** случайный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only — after a verb say *by chance*: *We met by chance*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *Chance* is mostly a noun (*a chance to win*, *by chance*). Only before a noun is it an adjective meaning 'accidental': *a chance meeting*.

Happening by accident, without being planned.

1. A *chance* meeting at a coffee shop changed her career.
2. The discovery of penicillin was the result of a *chance* observation.
3. His *chance* remark started a long argument.

---

### chancy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃænsi/ &middot; *CHAN-see*
**Русский:** рискованный, ненадёжный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *chancier*, *chanciest*
**Form:** *chance* + -y, dropping the *e*

Informal: risky and uncertain.

1. Putting all your savings into one stock is a *chancy* move.
2. The weather in April can be *chancy*.
3. Driving over the pass got *chancier* after dark.

---

### changeable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃeɪndʒəbəl/ &middot; *CHAYN-juh-buhl*
**Русский:** переменчивый, изменчивый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more changeable*, *most changeable*
**Form:** -able from *change*, keeping the *e* so the *g* stays soft
**Opposite:** *stable*; *constant*

Likely to change often.

1. The weather in Denver is very *changeable*.
2. Teenagers' moods can be *changeable*.
3. Spring is the *most changeable* season here.

---

### changed

**Pronunciation:** /tʃeɪndʒd/ &middot; *CHAYNJD*
**Русский:** изменившийся
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *a changed man*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *change*
**Opposite:** *unchanged*

Different from before, often in an important way.

1. He came back from the war a *changed* man.
2. Companies are adjusting to a *changed* world after the pandemic.
3. The neighborhood looked completely *changed* when I visited last year.

---

### changing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃeɪndʒɪŋ/ &middot; *CHAYN-jing*
**Русский:** меняющийся
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — after a verb it is the verb: *Things are changing*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *change*
**Contrast:** In *changing room* and *changing table* the *-ing* word is a noun: a room for changing clothes, a table for changing diapers.

In the process of becoming different.

1. Companies must keep up with *changing* technology.
2. We live in a fast-*changing* world.
3. *Changing* weather patterns are hurting farmers.

---

### chaotic

**Pronunciation:** /keɪˈɑːtɪk/ &middot; *kay-AH-tik*
**Русский:** хаотичный, беспорядочный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more chaotic*, *most chaotic*
**Form:** -ic from *chaos*
**Contrast:** The *ch* is /k/: kay-AH-tik, as in *chaos* (KAY-ahs).

Completely confused and without order.

1. The first day of school is always *chaotic*.
2. Traffic was *chaotic* after the power outage.
3. Our house got even *more chaotic* when the twins were born.

---

### chapped

**Pronunciation:** /tʃæpt/ &middot; *CHAPT*
**Русский:** обветренный, потрескавшийся
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more chapped*, *most chapped*
**Form:** -ed from the verb *chap*

Of skin or lips: dry, rough and cracked, especially from cold or wind.

1. My lips get *chapped* every winter.
2. Put some lotion on your *chapped* hands.
3. His cheeks were red and *chapped* from the wind.

---

### characteristic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɛrəktəˈrɪstɪk/ &middot; *kair-uhk-tuh-RIS-tik*
**Русский:** характерный, типичный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more characteristic*, *most characteristic*
**Takes:** *of* + noun
**Preposition patterns:** [characteristic of](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#characteristic-of)
**Opposite:** *uncharacteristic*
**Contrast:** Russian *характерный* also means 'with a strong personality' (*характерный актёр* is *a character actor*). English *characteristic* only means 'typical'. *A characteristic* (noun) is a feature.

Typical of a particular person, place or thing.

1. She answered with her *characteristic* honesty.
2. This kind of dark humor is *characteristic* of his early films.
3. Sharp spines are the *most characteristic* feature of this cactus.

---

### charcoal-gray

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtʃɑːrkoʊlˈɡreɪ/ &middot; *char-kohl-GRAY*
**Русский:** тёмно-серый, графитовый
**Type:** color
**Position:** both — hyphenated before a noun (*a charcoal-gray suit*), usually open after a verb (*The suit is charcoal gray*)
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *charcoal* + *gray*
**Contrast:** American spelling *gray*; British *grey*. Often shortened to *charcoal*: *a charcoal sweater*.

Very dark gray, like the color of charcoal.

1. He wore a *charcoal-gray* suit to the interview.
2. We painted the front door *charcoal gray*.
3. A *charcoal-gray* sky warned of snow.

---

### charged

**Pronunciation:** /tʃɑːrdʒd/ &middot; *CHARJD*
**Русский:** напряжённый, накалённый; заряженный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more charged*, *most charged*
**Form:** -ed from *charge*
**Contrast:** *He was charged with robbery* is the verb *charge* in the passive (обвинён), not this adjective.

Charged has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Full of strong feelings that could lead to anger or argument.** (напряжённый, эмоционально накалённый)

1. Immigration is a politically *charged* subject.
2. The atmosphere in the courtroom was *charged*.
3. It was the *most charged* debate of the campaign.

**2. Holding electrical energy, or carrying an electric charge.** (заряженный)

4. Make sure your phone is fully *charged* before the trip.
5. Electrons are negatively *charged* particles.

---

### charismatic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɛrɪzˈmætɪk/ &middot; *kair-iz-MAT-ik*
**Русский:** харизматичный; харизматический (о церкви)
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both — the religious sense mostly before a noun: *a charismatic church*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more charismatic*, *most charismatic*
**Form:** -ic from *charisma*

Charismatic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having a strong personal charm that attracts and inspires people.** (харизматичный)

1. He's a *charismatic* speaker.
2. Voters found her warm and *charismatic*.
3. He's far *more charismatic* in person than on TV.

**2. Of Christian groups: stressing gifts of the Holy Spirit, such as healing and speaking in tongues.** (харизматический (о христианских церквях))

4. They belong to a *charismatic* church in Houston.
5. *Charismatic* services often include loud music and raised hands.

---

### charitable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃɛrɪtəbəl/ &middot; *CHER-i-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** благотворительный; снисходительный, великодушный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more charitable*, *most charitable*; the 'charity' sense is not gradable
**Form:** -able from *charity*
**Opposite:** *uncharitable*

Charitable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to giving money or help to people in need.** (благотворительный)

1. She works for a *charitable* foundation.
2. Your donation is a *charitable* gift and is tax-deductible.

**2. Kind and generous in judging other people.** (снисходительный, великодушный)

3. The *most charitable* explanation is that he simply forgot.
4. Try to be *more charitable* toward your coworkers.

---

### charmed

**Pronunciation:** /tʃɑːrmd/ &middot; *CHARMD*
**Русский:** очарованный; счастливый, заговорённый (о жизни)
**Type:** participial; also emotion
**Position:** both — the 'lucky' sense only before a noun: *a charmed life*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more charmed*, *most charmed*
**Takes:** *by* or *with* + noun (in the 'delighted' sense)
**Form:** -ed — the person who feels the pleasure
**Contrast:** *Charming* is the person or thing that pleases; *charmed* is the person who is pleased: *a charming host*, *charmed guests*.

Charmed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Pleased and delighted by someone or something.** (очарованный)

1. We were *charmed* by the little town.
2. The guests were *charmed* by her speech.
3. I was even *more charmed* by her sister.

**2. Very lucky, as if protected by magic.** (счастливый, заговорённый)

4. She has led a *charmed* life.
5. It was a *charmed* season for the team.

---

### charming

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃɑːrmɪŋ/ &middot; *CHAR-ming*
**Русский:** очаровательный, обаятельный
**Type:** evaluative; also participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more charming*, *most charming*
**Form:** -ing — what causes the feeling
**Contrast:** Said flatly, *Oh, charming* is sarcastic and means 'how rude' or 'how unpleasant'.

Very pleasant and attractive.

1. What a *charming* little house!
2. Her husband is smart and *charming*.
3. He was even *more charming* than I'd heard.

---

### chartered

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃɑːrtərd/ &middot; *CHAR-turd*
**Русский:** зафрахтованный, заказной
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a chartered plane*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from the verb *charter* (to hire)
**Contrast:** *A charter flight* (noun + noun) is at least as common as *a chartered flight*. *Chartered accountant* is British; Americans say *certified public accountant* or *CPA*.

Of a plane, bus or boat: hired by a group for a particular trip.

1. The team flew to Denver on a *chartered* plane.
2. We took a *chartered* bus to the game.
3. They went deep-sea fishing on a *chartered* boat.

---

### chaste

**Pronunciation:** /tʃeɪst/ &middot; *CHAYST*
**Русский:** целомудренный, невинный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more chaste*, *most chaste*

Not having sex, or not involving sexual feeling.

1. Their romance was *chaste* and old-fashioned.
2. He gave her a *chaste* kiss on the cheek.
3. The nuns took a vow to remain *chaste*.

---

### chatty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃæti/ &middot; *CHAT-ee*
**Русский:** разговорчивый, болтливый; непринуждённый (о стиле)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *chattier*, *chattiest*
**Form:** *chat* + -y, doubling the *t*

Chatty has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a person: liking to talk in a friendly way.** (разговорчивый, болтливый)

1. Our Uber driver was very *chatty*.
2. She gets *chattier* after a glass of wine.

**2. Of writing or speech: informal and friendly.** (непринуждённый, в разговорном тоне)

3. She sent me a long, *chatty* email.
4. The book is written in a *chatty* style.

---

### chauvinistic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌʃoʊvəˈnɪstɪk/ &middot; *shoh-vuh-NIS-tik*
**Русский:** сексистский; шовинистический
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more chauvinistic*, *most chauvinistic*
**Form:** -ic from *chauvinist*
**Contrast:** In American English *chauvinist* most often means *male chauvinist*, a man who treats women as inferior. Russian *шовинизм* is usually about nationality; English uses *chauvinistic* that way too, but less often.

Chauvinistic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Believing that men are better than women, and treating women unfairly.** (сексистский, мужской шовинистический)

1. His *chauvinistic* comments about female engineers got him fired.
2. Some ads from the 1960s look shockingly *chauvinistic* today.
3. The *most chauvinistic* jokes came from the manager himself.

**2. Believing that your own country or group is better than all others.** (шовинистический, ура-патриотический)

4. The speech was full of *chauvinistic* national pride.
5. *Chauvinistic* nationalism grew in the years before the war.

---

### cheap

**Pronunciation:** /tʃiːp/ &middot; *CHEEP*
**Русский:** дешёвый; некачественный; скупой; низкий
**Type:** quality; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *cheaper*, *cheapest*
**Opposite:** *expensive* (price); *generous* (of a person)
**Contrast:** *Cheap* often suggests poor quality, so to praise a low price say *inexpensive* or *affordable*. About a person, American *cheap* means 'stingy' (жадный), not дешёвый. *Cheaply* is the adverb, though *buy it cheap* is common in speech.

Cheap has 4 distinct senses.

**1. Low in price.** (дешёвый, недорогой)

1. Gas is *cheaper* in New Jersey than in New York.
2. We found a *cheap* flight to Miami.
3. This is the *cheapest* phone they sell.

**2. Of poor quality.** (дешёвый, некачественный)

4. The chair broke because it was made of *cheap* plastic.
5. Her shoes looked *cheap*.

**3. Of a person: not willing to spend money.** (скупой, жадный)

6. Don't be so *cheap* — leave a real tip.
7. My uncle is too *cheap* to turn on the heat.

**4. Unfair or unkind in a way that shows no respect.** (низкий, подлый)

8. Bringing up his divorce was a *cheap* shot.
9. Jokes about her weight were a *cheap* way to get laughs.

---

### cheating

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃiːtɪŋ/ &middot; *CHEE-ting*
**Русский:** изменяющий, неверный; жульничающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — after a verb it is the verb: *He is cheating*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *cheat*
**Contrast:** In *a cheating scandal* the *-ing* word is a noun (the act of cheating), not this adjective.

Cheating has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having a secret sexual relationship with someone other than your partner.** (изменяющий, неверный)

1. She finally left her *cheating* husband.
2. The song is about a lying, *cheating* boyfriend.

**2. Breaking the rules to win or get an advantage.** (жульничающий, нечестный)

3. Fans booed the *cheating* team.
4. The *cheating* students were suspended for a week.

---

### Chechen

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃɛtʃɛn/ &middot; *CHECH-en*
**Русский:** чеченский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun for the people and the language: *He speaks Chechen*.

Of or from Chechnya, a republic in the North Caucasus, or its people or language.

1. Grozny is the *Chechen* capital.
2. Many *Chechen* refugees settled in Austria and Germany.
3. Her grandparents were *Chechen*.

---

### checked

**Pronunciation:** /tʃɛkt/ &middot; *CHEKT*
**Русский:** клетчатый; сдаваемый в багаж
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — the luggage sense only before a noun: *a checked bag*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *check* (a square pattern; to hand over luggage)
**Contrast:** For patterns Americans more often say *checkered* or *plaid*. The opposite of *checked luggage* is *carry-on luggage*.

Checked has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of cloth: having a pattern of squares in different colors.** (клетчатый)

1. She wore a red-and-white *checked* shirt.
2. The tablecloth in the kitchen is *checked*.

**2. Of luggage: handed to an airline to carry in the hold of the plane.** (сдаваемый в багаж)

3. Each *checked* bag costs thirty-five dollars.
4. Put the shampoo in your *checked* luggage.

---

### checkered

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃɛkərd/ &middot; *CHEK-urd*
**Русский:** клетчатый, в шашечку; неоднозначный (о прошлом)
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both — the 'mixed history' sense only before a noun: *a checkered past*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *checker*, a square on a checkerboard
**Contrast:** American spelling; British *chequered*. *A checkered past* is not a colorful past but one with scandals or failures in it.

Checkered has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having a pattern of squares in two colors, like a checkerboard.** (клетчатый, в шашечку)

1. The race ends when the *checkered* flag waves.
2. The diner has a black-and-white *checkered* floor.

**2. Having good and bad parts, especially trouble or scandal.** (неоднозначный, с тёмными пятнами)

3. The mayor has a *checkered* past.
4. The company has a *checkered* history with safety regulators.

---

### cheeky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃiːki/ &middot; *CHEE-kee*
**Русский:** дерзкий, нахальный (шутливо)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *cheekier*, *cheekiest*
**Form:** *cheek* ('rude confidence') + -y
**Contrast:** More common in British English. Americans use it for playful, slightly rude behavior; *sassy* is the more American word.

Slightly rude or disrespectful, often in a funny or charming way.

1. He gave me a *cheeky* grin.
2. Don't be *cheeky* with your grandmother.
3. The *cheekiest* kid in class asked the principal how old she was.

---

### cheerful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃɪrfəl/ &middot; *CHEER-fuhl*
**Русский:** весёлый, жизнерадостный; яркий, радостный
**Type:** quality; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cheerful*, *most cheerful*
**Form:** -ful from *cheer*
**Opposite:** *gloomy*; *cheerless*

Cheerful has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Happy and positive in the way you look or behave.** (весёлый, жизнерадостный)

1. She's always *cheerful* in the morning.
2. The nurse greeted us with a *cheerful* smile.
3. You seem *more cheerful* today.

**2. Of a place or color: bright and pleasant.** (яркий, радостный)

4. They painted the kitchen a *cheerful* yellow.
5. The waiting room was small but *cheerful*.

---

### cheery

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃɪri/ &middot; *CHEER-ee*
**Русский:** весёлый, бодрый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *cheerier*, *cheeriest*
**Form:** *cheer* + -y

Happy and friendly, often in an obvious way.

1. The waiter gave us a *cheery* hello.
2. The room felt *cheerier* with the new curtains.
3. She's annoyingly *cheery* at six in the morning.

---

### cheesy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃiːzi/ &middot; *CHEE-zee*
**Русский:** сырный; пошловатый, безвкусный, банальный
**Type:** quality; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *cheesier*, *cheesiest*
**Form:** *cheese* + -y, dropping the *e*
**Contrast:** *A cheesy grin* is a wide, forced smile, like the one people make when they say 'cheese' for a photo.

Cheesy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Tasting of cheese or containing a lot of cheese.** (сырный, с большим количеством сыра)

1. The kids love *cheesy* pasta.
2. This pizza is *cheesier* than the one we had last week.

**2. Informal: cheap, silly or too sentimental in an embarrassing way.** (пошловатый, безвкусный, банальный)

3. He told a *cheesy* joke about accountants.
4. The movie is really *cheesy*, but I love it.
5. That was the *cheesiest* pickup line I've ever heard.

---

### chemical

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɛmɪkəl/ &middot; *KEM-i-kuhl*
**Русский:** химический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — *a chemical reaction*, *The change is chemical, not physical*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *chemic*, related to *chemistry*
**Contrast:** Russian *химический* before a subject or a person is often the noun *chemistry* in English: *a chemistry teacher*, *a chemistry lab*, not *a chemical teacher*. *Chemicals* (noun) are substances.

Of or produced by chemistry or chemicals.

1. Rust forms through a *chemical* reaction between iron and oxygen.
2. The factory was fined for a *chemical* spill.
3. The difference between the two samples is *chemical*, not physical.

---

### cherished

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃɛrɪʃt/ &middot; *CHER-isht*
**Русский:** заветный, дорогой сердцу
**Type:** participial; also evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *a cherished memory*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cherished*, *most cherished*
**Form:** -ed from *cherish*

Loved, valued and kept with great care.

1. The ring is one of her *most cherished* possessions.
2. I have *cherished* memories of summers at the lake.
3. Free speech is a *cherished* American value.

---

### cherry-red

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtʃɛriˈrɛd/ &middot; *cher-ee-RED*
**Русский:** ярко-красный, вишнёво-красный
**Type:** color
**Position:** both — hyphenated before a noun (*a cherry-red car*), usually open after a verb (*The car is cherry red*)
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *cherry* + *red*
**Contrast:** Russian *вишнёвый* is a dark red, closer to *burgundy* or *dark red*; *cherry-red* is a bright, shiny red.

Bright red, like a ripe cherry.

1. He drove a *cherry-red* convertible.
2. Her lipstick was *cherry red*.
3. The *cherry-red* barn stood out against the snow.

---

### cherubic

**Pronunciation:** /tʃəˈruːbɪk/ &middot; *chuh-ROO-bik*
**Русский:** ангельский, пухленький (как у херувима)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cherubic*, *most cherubic*
**Form:** -ic from *cherub*

Looking sweet and innocent, often with a round face, like a small child or angel.

1. The baby had a *cherubic* face and big blue eyes.
2. With his round red cheeks, he looked *cherubic* in his choir robe.
3. He looks *cherubic*, but he's the class troublemaker.

---

### chestnut

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃɛsnʌt/ &middot; *CHES-nuht*
**Русский:** каштановый
**Type:** color
**Position:** both — *chestnut hair*, *Her hair is chestnut*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *Chestnut* is mainly a noun (the nut or the tree). As a color it is used mostly for hair and horses; for Russian *каштановые волосы* Americans also say *brown* or *auburn* hair.

Reddish brown, like the color of a chestnut.

1. She has long *chestnut* hair.
2. He rode a *chestnut* mare in the parade.
3. Her hair was *chestnut* when she was young.

---

### chewable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃuːəbəl/ &middot; *CHOO-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** жевательный (о таблетке)
**Type:** classifying — can be chewed
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -able from *chew*

Made to be chewed before swallowing, not swallowed whole.

1. The kids take *chewable* vitamins every morning.
2. Are these allergy tablets *chewable*, or do I swallow them?
3. The pharmacist gave me a *chewable* form of the medicine.

---

### chewy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃuːi/ &middot; *CHOO-ee*
**Русский:** тягучий; жёсткий (о мясе)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *chewier*, *chewiest*
**Form:** -y from *chew*

Needing a lot of chewing; soft but firm or sticky.

1. I like my chocolate chip cookies soft and *chewy*.
2. The steak was dry and *chewy*.
3. This bagel is *chewier* than the ones from the other bakery.

---

### chic

**Pronunciation:** /ʃiːk/ &middot; *SHEEK*
**Русский:** элегантный, стильный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more chic*, *most chic*
**Contrast:** Pronounced SHEEK, not like *chick*. *Chic* means elegant in a fashionable, understated way; Russian *шикарный* can also mean luxurious and showy, which is closer to *lavish* or *flashy*.

Fashionable and elegant in style.

1. She always looks *chic*, even in jeans.
2. They opened a *chic* little wine bar downtown.
3. The new lobby is much more *chic* than the old one.

---

### chicken

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃɪkɪn/ &middot; *CHIK-in*
**Русский:** трусливый (разг.)
**Type:** quality — informal
**Position:** predicative only — never before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more chicken*; usually *too chicken* or *so chicken*
**Contrast:** In *chicken soup* or *chicken wings*, *chicken* is a noun used as a modifier, not this adjective. The verb phrase *chicken out* means to lose your nerve (*He chickened out at the last minute*), and the noun works too: *You're such a chicken!*

Afraid to do something; cowardly.

1. Come on, jump in — don't be *chicken*!
2. I was too *chicken* to ask her out.
3. Everyone knew he was *chicken* when it came to roller coasters.

---

### child-centered

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtʃaɪldˈsɛntərd/ &middot; *chyld-SEN-turd*
**Русский:** ориентированный на ребёнка
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more child-centered*, *most child-centered*
**Form:** compound — noun + participle

Built around children's needs and interests rather than adults' plans.

1. The school takes a *child-centered* approach to learning.
2. Our pediatrician's office is very *child-centered*.
3. Parenting has become far more *child-centered* than it was fifty years ago.

---

### childless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃaɪldləs/ &middot; *CHYLD-luhs*
**Русский:** бездетный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *child*

Having no children.

1. They were a *childless* couple in their forties.
2. She stayed *childless* by choice.
3. The survey compared parents with *childless* adults.

---

### Chilean

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃɪliən/ &middot; *CHIL-ee-uhn*
**Русский:** чилийский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -an from *Chile*
**Contrast:** Always a capital *C*, unlike Russian *чилийский*. It is also a noun: *a Chilean*. Some Americans say chi-LAY-uhn, closer to Spanish.

Of or from Chile, its people or its culture.

1. We had a bottle of *Chilean* wine with dinner.
2. Her husband is *Chilean*.
3. The *Chilean* coastline stretches for thousands of miles.

---

### chilling

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃɪlɪŋ/ &middot; *CHIL-ing*
**Русский:** леденящий душу; сдерживающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more chilling*, *most chilling*
**Form:** -ing — what causes the feeling; from *chill*
**Contrast:** *Chilling* frightens you; *chilled* means made cold (*chilled wine*). In slang, *We're just chilling* is the verb *chill*, meaning relaxing, not this adjective.

Chilling has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Frightening, especially in a cold, quiet way.** (леденящий душу, жуткий)

1. The movie tells a *chilling* story about a small-town murder.
2. His calm description of the crime was *chilling*.
3. The most *chilling* part was the silence afterward.

**2. Making people afraid to act or speak freely; used mainly in *a chilling effect*.** (сдерживающий, отпугивающий)

4. Critics say the new law will have a *chilling* effect on free speech.
5. The lawsuits had a *chilling* effect on reporters.

---

### chintzy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃɪntsi/ &middot; *CHINT-see*
**Русский:** дешёвый, безвкусный; скупой
**Type:** evaluative — informal
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *chintzier*, *chintziest*
**Form:** -y from *chintz*, a cheap printed cotton

Chintzy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Cheap-looking and poorly made.** (дешёвый, безвкусный)

1. The hotel room had *chintzy* plastic furniture.
2. That trophy looks kind of *chintzy*.
3. They put up the *chintziest* decorations I've ever seen.

**2. Unwilling to spend money; stingy.** (скупой, прижимистый)

4. Don't be *chintzy* — leave a decent tip.
5. The company has always been *chintzy* about raises.

---

### chipper

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃɪpər/ &middot; *CHIP-ur*
**Русский:** бодрый, жизнерадостный
**Type:** emotion — informal
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more chipper*, *most chipper*

Cheerful and full of energy.

1. You're awfully *chipper* for seven in the morning.
2. She sounded *chipper* on the phone.
3. He seemed a lot more *chipper* after his nap.

---

### chirpy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃɜːrpi/ &middot; *CHUR-pee*
**Русский:** весёлый, бодрый
**Type:** emotion — informal
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *chirpier*, *chirpiest*
**Form:** -y from *chirp*, a bird's short high sound

Cheerful and lively, sometimes a little too bright.

1. The receptionist was annoyingly *chirpy*.
2. She left a *chirpy* message on my voicemail.
3. He's always *chirpier* after his first cup of coffee.

---

### chiseled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃɪzəld/ &middot; *CHIZ-uhld*
**Русский:** точёный, чётко очерченный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more chiseled*, *most chiseled*
**Form:** -ed from *chisel*, a tool for cutting stone or wood
**Contrast:** American spelling has one *l*: *chiseled*. *Chiselled* is British.

Having strong, sharp, clearly defined lines, especially of a face or muscles.

1. The actor is known for his *chiseled* jaw.
2. His arms looked *chiseled* after months at the gym.
3. She has high, *chiseled* cheekbones.

---

### chivalrous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈʃɪvəlrəs/ &middot; *SHIV-uhl-ruhs*
**Русский:** рыцарский, галантный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more chivalrous*, *most chivalrous*
**Form:** -ous from *chivalry*
**Contrast:** It starts with a *sh* sound, not *ch*.

Polite, kind and protective, especially of a man toward women.

1. It was *chivalrous* of him to give up his seat.
2. He made a *chivalrous* offer to walk her home.
3. Some women find the gesture *chivalrous*; others find it old-fashioned.

---

### chock-full

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtʃɑːkˈfʊl/ &middot; *chahk-FUUL*
**Русский:** битком набитый, переполненный
**Type:** quantity — informal
**Position:** predicative only — or right after a noun: *a book chock-full of ideas*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *of* + noun
**Form:** compound — *chock* (tightly) + *full*

Completely full; packed.

1. The fridge is *chock-full* of leftovers.
2. Her essay was *chock-full* of typos.
3. It's a guidebook *chock-full* of useful tips.

---

### choice

**Pronunciation:** /tʃɔɪs/ &middot; *CHOYS*
**Русский:** отборный; крепкий (о словах)
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *choicer*, *choicest*
**Contrast:** Usually a noun (*make a choice*). The adjective goes only before a noun and sounds a little formal, except in *a few choice words*, which means angry or rude words, not carefully chosen polite ones.

Choice has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of very high quality; carefully picked as the best.** (отборный, первосортный)

1. The butcher saves the *choicest* cuts for his regular customers.
2. We got *choice* seats near the stage.
3. The lake has some *choice* spots for fishing.

**2. Angry or rude; used mainly in *a few choice words*.** (крепкий, резкий (о словах))

4. He had a few *choice* words for the referee.
5. When she saw the bill, she used some *choice* language.

---

### choosy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃuːzi/ &middot; *CHOO-zee*
**Русский:** разборчивый, привередливый
**Type:** quality — informal
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *choosier*, *choosiest*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*
**Form:** -y from *choose*

Very careful about what you choose; hard to please.

1. She's *choosy* about the guys she dates.
2. He's a *choosy* eater, so we never go anywhere new.
3. Cats are often *choosier* about food than dogs.

---

### chopped

**Pronunciation:** /tʃɑːpt/ &middot; *CHAHPT*
**Русский:** нарезанный, рубленый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *chop*

Cut into small pieces with a knife.

1. Add a cup of *chopped* onions.
2. Top the salad with *chopped* walnuts.
3. Make sure the garlic is finely *chopped*.

---

### choppy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃɑːpi/ &middot; *CHAH-pee*
**Русский:** неспокойный (о воде); отрывистый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *choppier*, *choppiest*
**Form:** -y from *chop*

Choppy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of water: rough, with many small waves.** (неспокойный, с мелкой волной)

1. The lake was too *choppy* for kayaking.
2. We hit *choppy* water on the way back.
3. The bay gets *choppier* in the afternoon.

**2. Not smooth; broken into short, jerky parts.** (отрывистый, прерывистый)

4. The video call was *choppy*, so I couldn't hear her.
5. His writing is *choppy*, with lots of short sentences.

---

### choral

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɔːrəl/ &middot; *KOR-uhl*
**Русский:** хоровой
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *chorus*
**Contrast:** The *ch* is a *k* sound, and in American English *choral* sounds the same as *coral*.

For or sung by a choir.

1. She directs the *choral* program at the high school.
2. They performed a *choral* version of the hymn.
3. The concert features *choral* music from the 1700s.

---

### chronic

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkrɑːnɪk/ &middot; *KRAH-nik*
**Русский:** хронический; постоянный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *acute* (of an illness)
**Contrast:** British informal *chronic* meaning 'very bad' (*The food was chronic*) is not used in American English.

Chronic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of an illness or pain: lasting a long time or keeping coming back.** (хронический)

1. She has suffered from *chronic* back pain for years.
2. Diabetes is a *chronic* disease.
3. His cough eventually became *chronic*.

**2. Of a problem or habit: always happening; habitual.** (постоянный, закоренелый)

4. The city has a *chronic* shortage of affordable housing.
5. He's a *chronic* liar.
6. *Chronic* lateness got her fired.

---

### chronological

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkrɑːnəˈlɑːdʒɪkəl/ &middot; *krah-nuh-LAH-ji-kuhl*
**Русский:** хронологический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ical from *chronology*

Arranged in the order in which events happened.

1. List your jobs in reverse *chronological* order.
2. The museum tells the city's story in *chronological* order.
3. The novel's timeline isn't strictly *chronological*.

---

### chubby

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃʌbi/ &middot; *CHUH-bee*
**Русский:** пухлый, полненький
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *chubbier*, *chubbiest*
**Contrast:** Friendly when said about babies and cheeks; about an adult it can sound rude. The neutral words are *heavy* and *overweight*.

Slightly fat, in a round and often cute way.

1. Look at the baby's *chubby* little cheeks!
2. I was a *chubby* kid, but I grew out of it.
3. Our cat has gotten *chubbier* since we moved.

---

### chummy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃʌmi/ &middot; *CHUH-mee*
**Русский:** дружеский, закадычный
**Type:** quality — informal
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *chummier*, *chummiest*
**Takes:** *with* + person
**Form:** -y from *chum*, an old word for a friend

Very friendly, sometimes too friendly.

1. He's gotten pretty *chummy* with the boss.
2. The two senators seemed awfully *chummy* at dinner.
3. They're *chummier* now than they were in college.

---

### chunky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃʌŋki/ &middot; *CHUHNG-kee*
**Русский:** массивный; с кусочками; коренастый
**Type:** quality; also size
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *chunkier*, *chunkiest*
**Form:** -y from *chunk*

Chunky has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Thick, heavy and solid-looking.** (массивный, толстый, крупный)

1. She wore a *chunky* knit sweater.
2. *Chunky* gold necklaces are back in style.
3. These boots have a *chunkier* heel.

**2. Of food: containing pieces rather than smooth.** (с кусочками)

4. Do you like smooth or *chunky* peanut butter?
5. The salsa is nice and *chunky*.

**3. Of a person or animal: short, broad and a little heavy.** (коренастый, крепко сбитый)

6. He was a *chunky* linebacker from Ohio.
7. Our dog is a *chunky* little beagle.

---

### churchgoing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃɜːrtʃˌɡoʊɪŋ/ &middot; *CHURCH-goh-ing*
**Русский:** регулярно посещающий церковь
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *church* + *going*

Going to church regularly.

1. She grew up in a *churchgoing* family in Georgia.
2. The town is small and mostly *churchgoing*.
3. *Churchgoing* Americans are less common than they were in the 1970s.

---

### churlish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈtʃɜːrlɪʃ/ &middot; *CHUR-lish*
**Русский:** грубый, неучтивый
**Type:** quality — formal
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more churlish*, *most churlish*
**Takes:** *to*-infinitive, mainly in *It would be churlish to...*
**Form:** -ish from *churl*, an old word for a rude man

Rude and unfriendly, especially when you ought to be grateful or polite.

1. It would be *churlish* to complain about a free meal.
2. He gave a *churlish* reply and walked away.
3. Refusing their invitation seemed *churlish*.

---

### cinematic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɪnəˈmætɪk/ &middot; *sin-uh-MAT-ik*
**Русский:** кинематографический; зрелищный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cinematic* in the 'like a movie' sense; the first sense is not gradable
**Form:** -ic from *cinema*

Cinematic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to movies and moviemaking.** (кинематографический)

1. Hitchcock's *cinematic* techniques are still studied today.
2. It was her first *cinematic* role.

**2. Looking or feeling like a movie; dramatic and visual.** (зрелищный, кинематографичный)

3. The video game has *cinematic* graphics.
4. The new season feels even more *cinematic* than the last.
5. Her novels are so *cinematic* that they read like screenplays.

---

### circadian

**Pronunciation:** /sərˈkeɪdiən/ &middot; *sur-KAY-dee-uhn*
**Русский:** циркадный, суточный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Relating to the body's natural 24-hour cycle.

1. Jet lag throws off your *circadian* rhythm.
2. Blue light at night can disrupt *circadian* cycles.
3. Scientists study the *circadian* clock in fruit flies.

---

### circuitous

**Pronunciation:** /sərˈkjuːɪtəs/ &middot; *sur-KYOO-i-tuhs*
**Русский:** окольный, обходной
**Type:** quality — formal
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more circuitous*, *most circuitous*
**Form:** -ous from *circuit*

Long and indirect; not going straight to a place or to the point.

1. We took a *circuitous* route to avoid traffic.
2. His explanation was long and *circuitous*.
3. The trail to the summit is more *circuitous* than it looks on the map.

---

### circulatory

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɜːrkjələˌtɔːri/ &middot; *SUR-kyuh-luh-tor-ee*
**Русский:** кровеносный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ory from *circulate*

Relating to the movement of blood through the body.

1. The heart is the center of the *circulatory* system.
2. Smoking can cause *circulatory* problems.
3. He has a rare *circulatory* disorder.

---

### circumspect

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɜːrkəmˌspɛkt/ &middot; *SUR-kuhm-spekt*
**Русский:** осмотрительный, осторожный
**Type:** quality — formal
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more circumspect*, *most circumspect*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*

Careful not to take risks or say too much.

1. The senator was *circumspect* about her plans.
2. Investors have become more *circumspect* since the crash.
3. He gave a *circumspect* answer.

---

### circumstantial

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɜːrkəmˈstænʃəl/ &middot; *sur-kuhm-STAN-shuhl*
**Русский:** косвенный (о доказательствах)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *circumstance*

Based on facts that suggest something is true but do not prove it directly.

1. The case against him was purely *circumstantial*.
2. Prosecutors relied on *circumstantial* evidence.
3. The jury convicted her on *circumstantial* evidence alone.

---

### citywide

**Pronunciation:** /ˌsɪtiˈwaɪd/ &middot; *sit-ee-WYD*
**Русский:** общегородской
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *city* + *-wide*
**Contrast:** Also an adverb: *The ban applies citywide*.

Covering or happening in the whole city.

1. The mayor announced a *citywide* curfew.
2. There was a *citywide* power outage last night.
3. The recycling program is now *citywide*.

---

### civic

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪvɪk/ &middot; *SIV-ik*
**Русский:** гражданский; городской
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *Civic* is about a citizen's part in the life of a town or country (*civic duty*, *civic pride*). *Civil* is about citizens as opposed to the military or the church, and also means polite (*civil rights*, *a civil answer*). *Civilian* means not in the armed forces (*civilian clothes*).

Civic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to the duties and activities of citizens.** (гражданский)

1. Voting is a *civic* duty.
2. She's active in several *civic* organizations.
3. The school teaches *civic* responsibility.

**2. Relating to a town or city and its public life.** (городской, муниципальный)

4. The concert is at the *civic* center.
5. The new park is a source of *civic* pride.

---

### civil

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪvəl/ &middot; *SIV-uhl*
**Русский:** гражданский; вежливый
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both — but *civil* meaning 'of citizens' or 'in law' goes only before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more civil*, *most civil* in the 'polite' sense; the other senses are not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + person (polite sense)
**Opposite:** *uncivil*, *rude* (polite sense); *military* (citizens sense); *criminal* (law sense)
**Contrast:** *Civil* and *civic* both translate as *гражданский*: *civil rights*, *civil war* and *a civil ceremony* are about citizens as opposed to the army or the church, while *civic duty* is about taking part in public life. *Civilian clothes* (not *civil clothes*) are clothes that are not a uniform.

Civil has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to ordinary citizens and their rights, not to the military or religion.** (гражданский)

1. Martin Luther King Jr. was a leader of the *civil* rights movement.
2. The country was torn apart by *civil* war.
3. They had a *civil* ceremony at city hall instead of a church wedding.

**2. In law: about private disputes between people or companies, not about crimes.** (гражданский (о праве, иске))

4. She filed a *civil* lawsuit against the company.
5. It's a *civil* case, so nobody is going to prison.

**3. Polite, but not especially friendly.** (вежливый, корректный)

6. Try to stay *civil* even if he gets angry.
7. They're divorced, but they're *civil* to each other.
8. The debate was more *civil* than anyone expected.

---

### civilian

**Pronunciation:** /sɪˈvɪljən/ &middot; *si-VIL-yuhn*
**Русский:** гражданский, штатский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Usually a noun (*Two civilians were hurt*). The adjective goes before a noun: *civilian clothes*, *civilian life*; not *civil clothes*.

Not belonging to the armed forces or the police.

1. He found it hard to adjust to *civilian* life after the Army.
2. The bombing caused heavy *civilian* casualties.
3. The officers were in *civilian* clothes.

---

### civilized

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪvəˌlaɪzd/ &middot; *SIV-uh-lyzd*
**Русский:** цивилизованный; культурный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more civilized*, *most civilized*
**Form:** -ed from *civilize*
**Opposite:** *uncivilized*
**Contrast:** American spelling uses *z*: *civilized*, not *civilised*.

Civilized has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a society: having an advanced system of laws, government and culture.** (цивилизованный)

1. Torture has no place in a *civilized* society.
2. Every *civilized* nation has laws against slavery.

**2. Polite, calm and reasonable; also pleasant and comfortable.** (культурный, воспитанный; приличный)

3. Let's discuss this like *civilized* adults.
4. Dinner at eight is a much more *civilized* hour than dinner at five.
5. The divorce was surprisingly *civilized*.

---

### clad

**Pronunciation:** /klæd/ &middot; *KLAD*
**Русский:** одетый; покрытый
**Type:** participial — formal
**Position:** predicative only — or right after a noun: *a woman clad in black*; before a noun only in compounds like *ivy-clad*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *in* + clothing or material
**Form:** old past participle of *clothe*

Dressed in something, or covered with something.

1. She was *clad* in a long red coat.
2. Two men *clad* in black stood by the door.
3. The building is *clad* in glass and steel.

---

### clairvoyant

**Pronunciation:** /klɛrˈvɔɪənt/ &middot; *klair-VOY-uhnt*
**Русский:** ясновидящий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a clairvoyant*. Often used as a joke about someone who guesses well.

Able to see the future or know things beyond the normal senses.

1. She claims to have *clairvoyant* powers.
2. I'm not *clairvoyant* — you have to tell me what's wrong.
3. He said a *clairvoyant* dream warned him about the crash.

---

### clammy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈklæmi/ &middot; *KLA-mee*
**Русский:** липкий и влажный, холодный и потный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *clammier*, *clammiest*

Unpleasantly damp and sticky, and usually cold.

1. My hands get *clammy* when I'm nervous.
2. The basement air was cold and *clammy*.
3. His forehead felt *clammier* by the minute.

---

### clandestine

**Pronunciation:** /klænˈdɛstɪn/ &middot; *klan-DES-tin*
**Русский:** тайный, подпольный
**Type:** quality — formal
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more clandestine*, *most clandestine*

Done secretly, often because it is illegal or not allowed.

1. They had a *clandestine* meeting in a parking garage.
2. The agency ran *clandestine* operations overseas.
3. Their affair stayed *clandestine* for years.

---

### clarifying

**Pronunciation:** /ˈklɛrəˌfaɪɪŋ/ &middot; *KLAIR-uh-fy-ing*
**Русский:** уточняющий; очищающий (о косметике)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — but the cosmetics sense only before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more clarifying* in the first sense
**Form:** -ing from *clarify*

Clarifying has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Making something easier to understand.** (уточняющий, проясняющий)

1. Can I ask a *clarifying* question?
2. The lawyer issued a *clarifying* statement.
3. Talking it through with my sister was really *clarifying*.

**2. Of a beauty product: removing oil and buildup.** (очищающий)

4. Use a *clarifying* shampoo once a week.
5. This *clarifying* toner works well on oily skin.

---

### class-conscious

**Pronunciation:** /ˌklæsˈkɑːnʃəs/ &middot; *klas-KAHN-shuhs*
**Русский:** остро чувствующий классовые различия
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more class-conscious*, *most class-conscious*
**Form:** compound — noun + adjective

Very aware of social class and of differences between classes.

1. Victorian England was a deeply *class-conscious* society.
2. My grandmother was very *class-conscious*.
3. Americans are more *class-conscious* than they like to admit.

---

### classified

**Pronunciation:** /ˈklæsəˌfaɪd/ &middot; *KLA-suh-fyd*
**Русский:** секретный; (об объявлениях) частный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — but *classified* ads only before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *classify*

Classified has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Officially secret and available only to certain people.** (секретный, засекреченный)

1. He was charged with leaking *classified* documents.
2. I'm sorry, that information is *classified*.
3. The full report remains *classified*.

**2. Of ads: short, private and arranged by subject in a newspaper or on a website.** (частный, тематический (об объявлениях))

4. She found her first apartment in the *classified* ads.
5. He sold his old truck through the *classified* section.

---

### classless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈklæsləs/ &middot; *KLAS-luhs*
**Русский:** бесклассовый; (разг.) некрасивый, хамский
**Type:** classifying; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — but informally *totally classless* in the 'rude' sense
**Form:** -less from *class*
**Opposite:** *classy* (in the 'rude' sense)

Classless has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Without divisions between social classes.** (бесклассовый)

1. Marx imagined a *classless* society.
2. America likes to see itself as *classless*, but it isn't.

**2. Informal: rude and lacking good manners, especially in sports.** (некрасивый, недостойный, хамский)

3. Mocking the injured player was a *classless* move.
4. The fans' behavior was totally *classless*.

---

### classy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈklæsi/ &middot; *KLA-see*
**Русский:** стильный, шикарный; достойный
**Type:** evaluative — informal
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *classier*, *classiest*
**Form:** -y from *class*
**Opposite:** *tacky*

Classy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Stylish and expensive-looking; elegant.** (стильный, шикарный)

1. They went to a *classy* restaurant for their anniversary.
2. That dress is really *classy*.
3. It's the *classiest* hotel in town.

**2. Behaving with dignity and good manners.** (достойный, благородный)

4. It was *classy* of him to congratulate the winner.
5. She handled the criticism in a very *classy* way.

---

### claustrophobic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌklɔːstrəˈfoʊbɪk/ &middot; *klaws-truh-FOH-bik*
**Русский:** страдающий клаустрофобией; тесный, давящий
**Type:** quality; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more claustrophobic*, *most claustrophobic*
**Form:** -ic from *claustrophobia*

Claustrophobic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Afraid of being in small or crowded spaces.** (страдающий клаустрофобией)

1. I get *claustrophobic* in elevators.
2. She's too *claustrophobic* to have an MRI.

**2. Of a place or situation: making you feel shut in.** (тесный, давящий)

3. The apartment was dark and *claustrophobic*.
4. Living with my parents again felt *claustrophobic*.
5. The basement office is even more *claustrophobic* than the old one.

---

### clean

**Pronunciation:** /kliːn/ &middot; *KLEEN*
**Русский:** чистый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *cleaner*, *cleanest*
**Opposite:** *dirty*
**Contrast:** In *I clean forgot* and *The bullet went clean through*, *clean* is an informal adverb meaning 'completely'. Russian *чистый* in *чистый вес* or *чистая прибыль* is *net* in English: *net weight*, *net profit*, never *clean*.

Clean has 6 distinct senses.

**1. Not dirty.** (чистый, опрятный)

1. Are these towels *clean*?
2. Put on a *clean* shirt before dinner.
3. The kitchen is *cleaner* than it's been in weeks.

**2. Not causing or containing pollution.** (экологически чистый, незагрязнённый)

4. The state is investing in *clean* energy.
5. Everyone has a right to *clean* drinking water.
6. Electric buses are *cleaner* than diesel ones.

**3. Free of crimes, drugs or anything wrong.** (незапятнанный; (разг.) завязавший с наркотиками)

7. He has a *clean* driving record.
8. She's been *clean* for three years.
9. The athlete's drug test came back *clean*.

**4. Not offensive or sexual.** (приличный, пристойный)

10. Keep your jokes *clean* — there are kids here.
11. It's a *clean* comedy the whole family can watch.

**5. Smooth, simple and neat, with no rough or extra parts.** (чёткий, ровный, аккуратный)

12. I like the *clean* lines of modern furniture.
13. It was a *clean* break, so the bone should heal quickly.
14. The surgeon made a small, *clean* cut.

**6. Fair and played by the rules.** (честный, по правилам)

15. It was a hard but *clean* game.
16. Both candidates promised to run a *clean* campaign.

---

### clean-cut

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkliːnˈkʌt/ &middot; *kleen-KUHT*
**Русский:** опрятный, приличный на вид
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more clean-cut*, *most clean-cut*
**Form:** compound — adjective + participle
**Contrast:** Do not confuse it with *clear-cut*, which means definite or obvious (*a clear-cut case*).

Neat, tidy and respectable-looking, often with short hair.

1. He looked like a *clean-cut* college kid.
2. The candidate's *clean-cut* image helped him with older voters.
3. The band looked more *clean-cut* back in the '60s.

---

### clean-shaven

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkliːnˈʃeɪvən/ &middot; *kleen-SHAY-vuhn*
**Русский:** чисто выбритый
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *clean* + *shaven*, an old participle of *shave*
**Opposite:** *bearded*

With no beard or mustache.

1. He showed up *clean-shaven* for the interview.
2. The suspect is a tall, *clean-shaven* man in his thirties.
3. Most of the players are *clean-shaven*.

---

### cleansing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈklɛnzɪŋ/ &middot; *KLEN-zing*
**Русский:** очищающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — but the skin-care sense only before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cleansing* in the emotional sense
**Form:** -ing from *cleanse*
**Contrast:** The vowel is short, as in *cleanse*: KLEN-zing, not KLEEN as in *clean*.

Cleansing has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Used to clean the skin or body.** (очищающий (о косметике))

1. Wash your face with a gentle *cleansing* lotion.
2. She uses *cleansing* wipes to take off her makeup.

**2. Making you feel emotionally fresh and free of bad feelings.** (очищающий, освобождающий)

3. Take a deep, *cleansing* breath.
4. Crying can be very *cleansing*.
5. Finally telling the truth felt *cleansing*.

---

### clear-cut

**Pronunciation:** /ˌklɪrˈkʌt/ &middot; *klir-KUHT*
**Русский:** чёткий, однозначный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more clear-cut*, *most clear-cut*
**Form:** compound — adjective + participle
**Opposite:** *ambiguous*
**Contrast:** Compare *clean-cut*, which means neat and respectable-looking. The verb *clear-cut* means to cut down every tree in an area.

Definite and easy to see or decide; not in doubt.

1. It's not a *clear-cut* case of fraud.
2. The rules are *clear-cut*.
3. The answer was less *clear-cut* than we hoped.

---

### clear-eyed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌklɪrˈaɪd/ &middot; *klir-YD*
**Русский:** трезвый, реалистичный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more clear-eyed*, *most clear-eyed*
**Takes:** *about* + noun
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun + -ed

Seeing a situation clearly and realistically, without illusions.

1. We need a *clear-eyed* look at the budget.
2. She's *clear-eyed* about the risks of starting a business.
3. Her book offers a *clear-eyed* view of the war.

---

### clearheaded

**Pronunciation:** /ˌklɪrˈhɛdɪd/ &middot; *klir-HE-did*
**Русский:** ясно мыслящий, с ясной головой
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more clearheaded*, *most clearheaded*
**Form:** compound — *clear* + *head* + *-ed*
**Contrast:** Also spelled *clear-headed*.

Able to think clearly and sensibly, especially when not tired, drunk or upset.

1. I need to be *clearheaded* for the exam tomorrow.
2. She stayed calm and *clearheaded* during the emergency.
3. I feel more *clearheaded* in the morning.

---

### clenched

**Pronunciation:** /klɛntʃt/ &middot; *KLENCHT*
**Русский:** стиснутый, сжатый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *clench*

Of fists, teeth or a jaw: pressed together tightly, usually from anger or effort.

1. He answered through *clenched* teeth.
2. She stood there with *clenched* fists.
3. His jaw was *clenched* the whole meeting.

---

### clerical

**Pronunciation:** /ˈklɛrɪkəl/ &middot; *KLE-ri-kuhl*
**Русский:** канцелярский, конторский; церковный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al; related to both *clerk* (office sense) and *cleric* (church sense)

Clerical has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to office work such as typing, filing and keeping records.** (канцелярский, конторский)

1. She took a *clerical* job at the hospital.
2. The mistake was just a *clerical* error.
3. We need more *clerical* staff.

**2. Relating to priests and ministers.** (церковный, духовный)

4. The priest wore a *clerical* collar.
5. The law was opposed by *clerical* leaders.

---

### cliched

**Pronunciation:** /kliˈʃeɪd/ &middot; *klee-SHAYD*
**Русский:** избитый, шаблонный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cliched*, *most cliched*
**Form:** -ed from *cliche*
**Contrast:** Also written *clichéd*. The stress is on the last syllable: klee-SHAYD.

Used so often that it has become boring and unoriginal.

1. The movie's ending was predictable and *cliched*.
2. Avoid *cliched* phrases like 'think outside the box.'
3. It sounds *cliched*, but money can't buy happiness.

---

### climactic

**Pronunciation:** /klaɪˈmæktɪk/ &middot; *kly-MAK-tik*
**Русский:** кульминационный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more climactic*, *most climactic*
**Form:** -ic from *climax*
**Contrast:** Do not confuse it with *climatic*, which is about climate. *Climactic* has a *c* before the *t*, like *climax*.

Forming the most exciting or important point of a story or event.

1. In the *climactic* scene, the hero finally faces his father.
2. The *climactic* battle takes up the last hour of the movie.
3. The final game of the series was truly *climactic*.

---

### clinical

**Pronunciation:** /ˈklɪnɪkəl/ &middot; *KLI-ni-kuhl*
**Русский:** клинический; бесстрастный; стерильный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both — but the medical sense mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more clinical* in the 'cold' and 'bare' senses; the medical sense is not gradable
**Contrast:** About a person, a style or a room, *clinical* is usually criticism: cold and without feeling. It is not an intensifier like Russian *клинический* in *клинический идиот*.

Clinical has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to the examination and treatment of patients, not to theory or lab work.** (клинический)

1. The drug is still in *clinical* trials.
2. She has twenty years of *clinical* experience as a nurse.
3. He was diagnosed with *clinical* depression.

**2. Cold and without emotion; efficient but unfeeling.** (бесстрастный, холодный)

4. She described the accident in a calm, *clinical* way.
5. The team finished the game with *clinical* efficiency.
6. His writing is a little too *clinical* for my taste.

**3. Of a room or building: plain, very clean and unwelcoming, like a hospital.** (стерильный, казённый)

7. The new office feels cold and *clinical*.
8. They painted over the *clinical* white walls with a warm yellow.

---

### clip-on

**Pronunciation:** /ˈklɪpˌɑːn/ &middot; *KLIP-ahn*
**Русский:** на клипсе, на зажиме
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — verb + particle
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a pair of clip-ons*.

Attached with a clip rather than pinned, tied or put through a hole.

1. She wears *clip-on* earrings because her ears aren't pierced.
2. He showed up to the wedding in a *clip-on* tie.
3. I bought a *clip-on* reading light for my book.

---

### clipped

**Pronunciation:** /klɪpt/ &middot; *KLIPT*
**Русский:** отрывистый; подстриженный
**Type:** quality; also participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more clipped*, *most clipped*
**Form:** -ed from *clip*

Clipped has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of speech: short, quick and unfriendly.** (отрывистый, резкий)

1. She answered in a *clipped* voice.
2. His replies grew more *clipped* as the interview went on.
3. The officer spoke in short, *clipped* sentences.

**2. Cut short and neat.** (подстриженный, обрезанный)

4. The house has a neatly *clipped* hedge.
5. He has a *clipped* gray mustache.

---

### cliquish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkliːkɪʃ/ &middot; *KLEE-kish*
**Русский:** замкнутый (о группе), кастовый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cliquish*, *most cliquish*
**Form:** -ish from *clique*

Forming small, closed groups that do not welcome outsiders.

1. The kids at her new school were really *cliquish*.
2. It's a *cliquish* industry where everyone knows everyone.
3. The office got more *cliquish* after the merger.

---

### cloak-and-dagger

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkloʊkənˈdæɡər/ &middot; *klohk-uhn-DAG-ur*
**Русский:** шпионский, таинственный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable — though informally *very cloak-and-dagger*
**Form:** compound — *cloak* + *and* + *dagger*

Involving secret, mysterious actions, like a spy story.

1. The meeting had a *cloak-and-dagger* feel to it.
2. Why all the *cloak-and-dagger* stuff? Just tell me.
3. The whole deal was very *cloak-and-dagger*.

---

### clockwise

**Pronunciation:** /ˈklɑːkˌwaɪz/ &middot; *KLAHK-wyz*
**Русский:** (движущийся) по часовой стрелке
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — after a verb it is the adverb: *Turn it clockwise*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *clock* + *-wise* (in the direction of)
**Opposite:** *counterclockwise*
**Contrast:** The adverb is more common than the adjective: *Turn the lid clockwise*. American English says *counterclockwise*; *anticlockwise* is British.

Moving in the same direction as the hands of a clock.

1. Stir the sauce in a *clockwise* direction.
2. The dancers moved in a slow *clockwise* circle.
3. Most screws tighten with a *clockwise* turn.

---

### clogged

**Pronunciation:** /klɑːɡd/ &middot; *KLAHGD*
**Русский:** засорённый, забитый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more clogged*, *most clogged*
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *clog*

Blocked so that liquid, air or traffic cannot move through easily.

1. The kitchen sink is *clogged* again.
2. The highway was *clogged* with holiday traffic.
3. Plaque can lead to *clogged* arteries.

---

### close

**Pronunciation:** /kloʊs/ &middot; *KLOHS*
**Русский:** близкий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — but *close* meaning careful or narrow usually goes before a noun: *a close look*, *a close call*
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *closer*, *closest*
**Takes:** *to* + noun or *-ing*
**Preposition patterns:** [close to](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#close-to)
**Opposite:** *far*, *distant*
**Contrast:** The adjective ends in an /s/ sound (KLOHS); the verb *close*, meaning shut, ends in /z/ (KLOHZ). *Close* is also an adverb (*Come close*; *They live close by*). For people, use *close*, not *near*: *a close friend*, never *a near friend*.

Close has 7 distinct senses.

**1. Near in space or time.** (близкий, недалёкий)

1. The hotel is *close* to the beach.
2. Christmas is getting *close*.
3. Where's the *closest* gas station?

**2. Knowing and liking each other very well.** (близкий (о дружбе, отношениях))

4. She's my *closest* friend.
5. I'm very *close* to my brother.
6. They have a *close* relationship.

**3. Directly related in a family.** (близкий (о родстве))

7. Only *close* relatives were invited to the funeral.
8. Her *closest* living relative is a cousin in Texas.

**4. Won or lost by a very small difference.** (равный, упорный (о борьбе))

9. It was a *close* game until the last minute.
10. The election is too *close* to call.
11. This year's race was *closer* than last year's.

**5. Careful and thorough.** (пристальный, тщательный)

12. Take a *close* look at the contract before you sign.
13. Pay *close* attention to the instructions.

**6. Very similar.** (похожий, близкий)

14. There's a *close* resemblance between the two sisters.
15. The translation is very *close* to the original.

**7. Almost happening, especially about something bad.** (едва не случившийся (о беде))

16. Wow, that was *close* — that truck almost hit us!
17. We made our flight, but it was a *close* call.

---

### close-knit

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkloʊsˈnɪt/ &middot; *klohs-NIT*
**Русский:** сплочённый, дружный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more close-knit*, *most close-knit*
**Form:** compound — adverb + participle

Of a group: whose members know, help and care about each other.

1. We grew up in a *close-knit* neighborhood.
2. Her family is very *close-knit*.
3. Small towns tend to be more *close-knit* than big cities.

---

### close-minded

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkloʊs ˈmaɪndɪd/ &middot; *klohs-MYN-did*
**Русский:** закрытый для новых идей, косный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more close-minded*, *most close-minded*
**Form:** compound — *close* + *mind* + *-ed*
**Opposite:** *open-minded*
**Contrast:** The first part is the adjective *close* with an /s/ sound, not the verb /kloʊz/. *Closed-minded* is also written and means the same thing.

Unwilling to listen to new ideas or other opinions.

1. Don't be so *close-minded* — at least try the food.
2. He's the most *close-minded* person on the committee.
3. A *close-minded* teacher can ruin a subject for a kid.

---

### closed

**Pronunciation:** /kloʊzd/ &middot; *KLOHZD*
**Русский:** закрытый
**Type:** participial; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — but *more closed* or *very closed* when it means not open to people or ideas
**Opposite:** *open*
**Contrast:** A store that is not open is *closed*, not *close*: *The bank is closed on Sundays*. *Close* (KLOHS) is a different adjective meaning near.

Closed has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Not open; shut, or not doing business.** (закрытый)

1. Keep the windows *closed* — it's freezing.
2. Sorry, we're *closed*.
3. The road is *closed* for repairs.

**2. Not open to outsiders or to new ideas.** (закрытый, замкнутый)

4. The hearing was *closed* to the public.
5. It's a very *closed* community.
6. He has a *closed* mind when it comes to politics.

**3. Finished and no longer being discussed or investigated.** (закрытый, завершённый)

7. As far as I'm concerned, the matter is *closed*.
8. Police say the case is now *closed*.

---

### closed-captioned

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkloʊzdˈkæpʃənd/ &middot; *klohzd-KAP-shuhnd*
**Русский:** со скрытыми субтитрами (для глухих)
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — adjective + participle

Of a TV show or video: with words on the screen that can be turned on for people who are deaf or hard of hearing.

1. This program is *closed-captioned* for viewers who are deaf or hard of hearing.
2. All of our training videos are *closed-captioned*.
3. Look for the CC symbol on *closed-captioned* shows.

---

### closed-circuit

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkloʊzdˈsɜːrkɪt/ &middot; *klohzd-SUR-kit*
**Русский:** замкнутый (о телевидении); камер наблюдения
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun
**Contrast:** British *CCTV* is usually *security cameras* or *surveillance cameras* in everyday American speech.

Of TV or video: sent only to certain screens, not broadcast to the public.

1. The store is monitored by *closed-circuit* cameras.
2. The fight was shown on *closed-circuit* TV in theaters.
3. Police reviewed the *closed-circuit* footage.

---

### closed-door

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkloʊzdˈdɔːr/ &middot; *klohzd-DOR*
**Русский:** закрытый, за закрытыми дверями
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun
**Contrast:** After a verb, say *behind closed doors*: *The talks were held behind closed doors*.

Held in private, without the public or reporters.

1. Senators met in a *closed-door* session.
2. The deal was made during *closed-door* negotiations.
3. The committee held a *closed-door* hearing on the leak.

---

### closing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkloʊzɪŋ/ &middot; *KLOH-zing*
**Русский:** заключительный, последний
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *close* (to end)
**Opposite:** *opening*

Coming at the end of something.

1. The lawyer gave a powerful *closing* argument.
2. They scored twice in the *closing* minutes.
3. The stock's *closing* price was $42.

---

### clothed

**Pronunciation:** /kloʊðd/ &middot; *KLOHTHD*
**Русский:** одетый
**Type:** participial — formal
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *in* + clothing
**Form:** -ed from *clothe*
**Opposite:** *naked*, *unclothed*
**Contrast:** Everyday English says *dressed* (*Get dressed*). *Clothed* is more formal and common in *fully clothed*.

Wearing clothes.

1. He fell asleep fully *clothed*.
2. The children were poorly fed and poorly *clothed*.
3. She was *clothed* in a white robe.

---

### clouded

**Pronunciation:** /ˈklaʊdɪd/ &middot; *KLOW-did*
**Русский:** затуманенный; мутный; омрачённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *by* or *with* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *cloud*

Clouded has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of thinking or judgment: confused or affected by a strong feeling.** (затуманенный, помутнённый)

1. His judgment was *clouded* by anger.
2. For weeks, her mind was *clouded* with grief.

**2. Not clear or bright; also made less certain or less happy.** (мутный; омрачённый)

3. The bathroom has a *clouded* glass window.
4. Her eyes were *clouded* with tears.
5. The future of the project is *clouded* by money problems.

---

### cloudless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈklaʊdləs/ &middot; *KLOWD-luhs*
**Русский:** безоблачный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
