**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *cloud*
**Opposite:** *cloudy*

With no clouds.

1. It was a warm day with a *cloudless* blue sky.
2. The sky over the desert was *cloudless*.
3. We watched the stars on a *cloudless* night.

---

### cloudy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈklaʊdi/ &middot; *KLOW-dee*
**Русский:** облачный; мутный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *cloudier*, *cloudiest*
**Form:** -y from *cloud*
**Opposite:** *sunny*, *clear*

Cloudy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of the sky or weather: full of clouds.** (облачный, пасмурный)

1. It's *cloudy* but not raining.
2. Expect a *cloudy* morning with sun later.
3. Seattle is much *cloudier* than Los Angeles.

**2. Of a liquid or glass: not clear.** (мутный)

4. The water in the pool looks *cloudy*.
5. Throw out the medicine if it turns *cloudy*.

---

### clownish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈklaʊnɪʃ/ &middot; *KLOW-nish*
**Русский:** клоунский, шутовской
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more clownish*, *most clownish*
**Form:** -ish from *clown*

Silly and foolish, like a clown.

1. His *clownish* behavior annoyed the teacher.
2. The senator's *clownish* antics made headlines.
3. Critics called the performance *clownish*.

---

### cloying

**Pronunciation:** /ˈklɔɪɪŋ/ &middot; *KLOY-ing*
**Русский:** приторный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cloying*, *most cloying*
**Form:** -ing from *cloy*, an old verb

Too sweet or too sentimental, in an unpleasant way.

1. The frosting was so sweet it was *cloying*.
2. The movie's *cloying* ending made me roll my eyes.
3. His letters got more *cloying* over time.

---

### clueless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkluːləs/ &middot; *KLOO-luhs*
**Русский:** ничего не понимающий, бестолковый
**Type:** quality — informal
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more clueless*, *most clueless*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*
**Form:** -less from *clue*

Knowing nothing about something; not understanding what is going on.

1. I'm totally *clueless* about cars.
2. The new manager seems *clueless*.
3. He gave me a *clueless* look.

---

### clumsy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈklʌmzi/ &middot; *KLUHM-zee*
**Русский:** неуклюжий; неумелый; громоздкий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *clumsier*, *clumsiest*

Clumsy has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Moving awkwardly and often dropping or breaking things.** (неуклюжий, неловкий)

1. I'm so *clumsy* — I just spilled coffee on my laptop.
2. The puppy is still *clumsy* on its feet.
3. He's the *clumsiest* person I know.

**2. Done without skill or tact.** (неумелый, топорный, бестактный)

4. He made a *clumsy* attempt at an apology.
5. The translation is *clumsy* in places.

**3. Awkward to use or carry because of its size or shape.** (громоздкий, неудобный)

6. The first cell phones were big and *clumsy*.
7. Firefighters used to wear heavy, *clumsy* gear.

---

### clunky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈklʌŋki/ &middot; *KLUHNG-kee*
**Русский:** громоздкий, неуклюжий, топорный
**Type:** quality — informal
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *clunkier*, *clunkiest*
**Form:** -y from *clunk*, a dull heavy sound

Heavy, awkward and badly designed; not smooth or elegant.

1. She wore *clunky* black boots.
2. The old software is slow and *clunky*.
3. The dialogue in the second book is even *clunkier*.

---

### clustered

**Pronunciation:** /ˈklʌstərd/ &middot; *KLUHS-turd*
**Русский:** сгруппированный, расположенный кучно
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *cluster*

Grouped closely together.

1. A few houses sit *clustered* around the church.
2. Most of the restaurants are *clustered* near the waterfront.
3. From the hill we could see the *clustered* tents of the camp.

---

### cluttered

**Pronunciation:** /ˈklʌtərd/ &middot; *KLUH-turd*
**Русский:** захламлённый, загромождённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cluttered*, *most cluttered*
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *clutter*
**Opposite:** *tidy*, *neat*

Full of too many things, in an untidy way.

1. His desk is always *cluttered* with papers.
2. The *cluttered* garage hadn't been cleaned in years.
3. The website's home page is too *cluttered*.

---

### co-ed

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkoʊˌɛd/ &middot; *KOH-ed*
**Русский:** совместный (для юношей и девушек)
**Type:** classifying — informal
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** short for *coeducational*
**Contrast:** Often written *coed*. The noun *a coed*, meaning a female college student, is old-fashioned and now sounds sexist.

For both male and female students or members.

1. She went to a *co-ed* boarding school.
2. The dorms on campus are *co-ed*.
3. We play in a *co-ed* softball league.

---

### coal-fired

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkoʊlˈfaɪərd/ &middot; *kohl-FY-urd*
**Русский:** угольный, работающий на угле
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + participle

Producing heat or power by burning coal.

1. The state plans to close its last *coal-fired* power plant.
2. *Coal-fired* plants are a major source of carbon emissions.
3. The old ship had a *coal-fired* engine.

---

### coarse

**Pronunciation:** /kɔːrs/ &middot; *KORS*
**Русский:** грубый; крупный (о помоле)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *coarser*, *coarsest*
**Opposite:** *fine*, *smooth*; *refined*
**Contrast:** It sounds exactly like *course* (*of course*, *a college course*).

Coarse has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Rough to touch, or made of large grains or thick threads.** (грубый, жёсткий; крупный)

1. The dog has *coarse* black fur.
2. Sprinkle the steak with *coarse* salt.
3. Start with *coarser* sandpaper, then switch to a fine one.

**2. Rude and offensive, especially about sex or the body.** (грубый, вульгарный)

4. He has a *coarse* sense of humor.
5. Her language got pretty *coarse* after a few drinks.

---

### coastal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkoʊstəl/ &middot; *KOHS-tuhl*
**Русский:** прибрежный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *coast*

On or near a coast.

1. They live in a small *coastal* town in Maine.
2. *Coastal* areas face a growing risk of flooding.
3. Most of the state's population is *coastal*.

---

### coated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkoʊtɪd/ &middot; *KOH-tid*
**Русский:** покрытый (слоем)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* or *in* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *coat* (a layer)

Covered with a thin layer of something.

1. The car was *coated* in dust.
2. These pills are *coated* so they're easier to swallow.
3. She bought a bag of *chocolate-coated* almonds.

---

### cockamamie

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɑːkəˈmeɪmi/ &middot; *kah-kuh-MAY-mee*
**Русский:** дурацкий, нелепый
**Type:** evaluative — informal
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cockamamie*, *most cockamamie*
**Contrast:** American slang; also spelled *cockamamy*.

Silly, crazy and hard to believe.

1. Where did you get that *cockamamie* idea?
2. He told some *cockamamie* story about losing his keys in a lake.
3. It was the most *cockamamie* plan I'd ever heard.

---

### cockeyed

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːkˌaɪd/ &middot; *KAHK-yd*
**Русский:** кривой, перекошенный; нелепый
**Type:** quality — informal
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cockeyed*, *most cockeyed*
**Form:** compound — *cock* (to tilt) + *eye* + *-ed*

Cockeyed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Crooked; not straight or level.** (кривой, перекошенный)

1. The picture on the wall is *cockeyed*.
2. He wore his cap at a *cockeyed* angle.

**2. Silly and not sensible.** (нелепый, дурацкий)

3. That's the most *cockeyed* plan I've ever heard.
4. He has some *cockeyed* theory about the moon landing.

---

### cocksure

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɑːkˈʃʊr/ &middot; *kahk-SHUUR*
**Русский:** самоуверенный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cocksure*, *most cocksure*
**Form:** compound — *cock* + *sure*

Too confident, in a way that annoys people.

1. He was young and *cocksure* and thought he knew everything.
2. Her *cocksure* attitude cost her the job.
3. Don't get *cocksure* just because you won one game.

---

### cocky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːki/ &middot; *KAH-kee*
**Русский:** дерзкий, самонадеянный
**Type:** quality — informal
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *cockier*, *cockiest*
**Opposite:** *modest*, *humble*

Too confident and proud of yourself, in a way that annoys people.

1. He got a little *cocky* after his first big win.
2. Don't be so *cocky* — the season just started.
3. He's the *cockiest* guy on the team.

---

### codified

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːdəˌfaɪd/ &middot; *KAH-duh-fyd*
**Русский:** кодифицированный, закреплённый в законе
**Type:** participial — formal
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *codify*

Written down as a formal set of rules or laws.

1. The right is *codified* in federal law.
2. The company has no *codified* dress code.
3. Many of these customs were never *codified*.

---

### coeducational

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkoʊˌɛdʒəˈkeɪʃənəl/ &middot; *koh-ej-uh-KAY-shuh-nuhl*
**Русский:** с совместным обучением
**Type:** classifying — formal
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** co- + *educational*
**Contrast:** In everyday speech Americans say *co-ed* or *coed*.

Of a school or college: for both male and female students.

1. Yale College became *coeducational* in 1969.
2. It is one of the oldest *coeducational* colleges in the country.
3. The academy runs a *coeducational* summer program.

---

### coercive

**Pronunciation:** /koʊˈɜːrsɪv/ &middot; *koh-UR-siv*
**Русский:** принудительный
**Type:** quality — formal
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more coercive*, *most coercive*
**Form:** -ive from *coerce*

Using force or threats to make people do things.

1. The police were accused of using *coercive* interrogation methods.
2. Critics say the policy is *coercive*.
3. *Coercive* control is a form of domestic abuse.

---

### cogent

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkoʊdʒənt/ &middot; *KOH-juhnt*
**Русский:** убедительный, веский
**Type:** evaluative — formal
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cogent*, *most cogent*

Clear, logical and convincing.

1. She made a *cogent* argument for raising taxes.
2. His reasons were *cogent* and well researched.
3. I've never heard a more *cogent* explanation of the problem.

---

### cognitive

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːɡnətɪv/ &middot; *KAHG-nuh-tiv*
**Русский:** когнитивный, познавательный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Relating to thinking, learning, memory and understanding.

1. The disease slowly affects *cognitive* function.
2. Reading aloud helps children's *cognitive* development.
3. She's a researcher in *cognitive* psychology.

---

### cognizant

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːɡnəzənt/ &middot; *KAHG-nuh-zuhnt*
**Русский:** осведомлённый
**Type:** quality — formal
**Position:** predicative only
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cognizant*, *most cognizant*
**Takes:** *of* + noun; *of the fact that*
**Preposition patterns:** [cognizant of](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#cognizant-of)
**Contrast:** Formal. In everyday English say *aware of*.

Knowing about something; aware.

1. We are *cognizant* of the risks.
2. Employees must be *cognizant* of the new safety rules.
3. Parents need to be more *cognizant* of what their kids see online.

---

### coherent

**Pronunciation:** /koʊˈhɪrənt/ &middot; *koh-HIR-uhnt*
**Русский:** связный, последовательный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more coherent*, *most coherent*
**Opposite:** *incoherent*

Coherent has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Logical, clear and well organized.** (связный, последовательный, стройный)

1. We need a *coherent* plan for the next five years.
2. Her essay was clear and *coherent*.
3. The second draft is much more *coherent*.

**2. Of a person: able to speak clearly and sensibly.** (говорящий внятно)

4. He was so tired he was barely *coherent*.
5. By the time the ambulance came, she was *coherent* again.

---

### cohesive

**Pronunciation:** /koʊˈhiːsɪv/ &middot; *koh-HEE-siv*
**Русский:** сплочённый; связный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cohesive*, *most cohesive*
**Form:** -ive from *cohesion*

Forming a united whole; working or fitting together well.

1. The new coach turned them into a *cohesive* team.
2. Your essay needs a more *cohesive* structure.
3. The neighborhood has always been close and *cohesive*.

---

### coiled

**Pronunciation:** /kɔɪld/ &middot; *KOYLD*
**Русский:** свёрнутый кольцами, спиральный
**Type:** shape; also participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *coil*

Wound into rings or a spiral.

1. A *coiled* rattlesnake lay under the rock.
2. The garden hose was neatly *coiled* by the door.
3. She wore her hair in a tight *coiled* bun.

---

### coin-operated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɔɪn ˌɑːpəreɪtɪd/ &middot; *KOYN-ah-puh-ray-tid*
**Русский:** работающий от монет, монетный
**Type:** compound; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *coin* + *operated*

Working only when you put coins into it.

1. The building has *coin-operated* washers in the basement.
2. All the parking meters here are *coin-operated*.
3. We found an old *coin-operated* arcade game at the diner.

---

### coincidental

**Pronunciation:** /koʊˌɪnsɪˈdɛntəl/ &middot; *koh-in-suh-DEN-tuhl*
**Русский:** случайный, совпадающий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *coincidence*
**Opposite:** *deliberate*; *intentional*

Happening by chance, not because it was planned.

1. Any resemblance to real people is purely *coincidental*.
2. It was a *coincidental* meeting at the airport.
3. I don't think the timing was *coincidental*.

---

### cold

**Pronunciation:** /koʊld/ &middot; *KOHLD*
**Русский:** холодный; замёрзший; неприветливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *colder*, *coldest*
**Opposite:** *hot*; *warm*
**Contrast:** To say you feel cold, English uses the person as subject: *I'm cold* = мне холодно, not «я холодный». *It's cold* is about the weather or a room. *A cold* is a noun (простуда): *I have a cold*.

Cold has 4 distinct senses.

**1. Having a low temperature.** (холодный)

1. It's *cold* outside, so take a jacket.
2. January is usually the *coldest* month in Chicago.
3. Can I get a *cold* glass of water?

**2. Feeling that your body is not warm enough.** (замёрзший (мне холодно))

4. I'm *cold* — can you close the window?
5. The kids were wet and *cold* after the game.

**3. Unfriendly and showing no warm feelings.** (холодный, неприветливый)

6. She gave me a *cold* look and walked away.
7. His voice got *colder* when I mentioned the money.

**4. Unconscious, especially after being hit; used in *out cold*.** (без сознания)

8. The boxer was out *cold* for a full minute.
9. He hit his head on the ice and was knocked out *cold*.

---

### cold-blooded

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkoʊld ˈblʌdɪd/ &middot; *kohld-BLUH-did*
**Русский:** хладнокровный; холоднокровный
**Type:** compound; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cold-blooded*, *most cold-blooded*
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun + -ed
**Opposite:** *warm-blooded* (of animals)
**Contrast:** Russian *хладнокровный* is usually praise (calm under pressure); English *cold-blooded* is always negative about people — it means cruel. For calm, say *cool-headed* or *calm*.

Cold-blooded has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Cruel and showing no emotion.** (жестокий, безжалостный)

1. It was a *cold-blooded* murder.
2. The jury saw him as a *cold-blooded* killer.
3. That was the most *cold-blooded* thing I've ever seen.

**2. Having a body temperature that changes with the surroundings, like reptiles and fish.** (холоднокровный)

4. Snakes are *cold-blooded*, so they lie in the sun.
5. *Cold-blooded* animals slow down in cold weather.

---

### colicky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːlɪki/ &middot; *KAH-li-kee*
**Русский:** страдающий коликами
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more colicky*, *most colicky*
**Form:** -y from *colic*

Crying a lot because of stomach pain; used mostly about babies.

1. Our *colicky* baby cried every night for three months.
2. She gets *colicky* after feedings.
3. Some babies are just more *colicky* than others.

---

### collaborative

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈlæbərətɪv/ &middot; *kuh-LAB-uh-ruh-tiv*
**Русский:** совместный
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more collaborative*, *most collaborative*
**Form:** -ive from *collaborate*

Done by people working together.

1. The album was a *collaborative* project between two bands.
2. We want a more *collaborative* office culture.
3. The whole process was very *collaborative*.

---

### collapsible

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈlæpsəbəl/ &middot; *kuh-LAP-suh-buhl*
**Русский:** складной, раскладной
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ible from *collapse*

Able to be folded into a smaller shape.

1. We keep two *collapsible* chairs in the trunk.
2. The stroller is *collapsible*, so it fits in the closet.
3. Take a *collapsible* water bottle on the hike.

---

### collateral

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈlætərəl/ &middot; *kuh-LAT-ur-uhl*
**Русский:** побочный, сопутствующий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** The noun *collateral* is залог — property you promise to a lender: *He used his house as collateral*. The adjective appears mainly in *collateral damage*.

Happening as an extra, unintended result of something.

1. The airstrike caused heavy *collateral* damage.
2. Innocent people became *collateral* victims of the feud.
3. Budget cuts had *collateral* effects on local schools.

---

### collected

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈlɛktɪd/ &middot; *kuh-LEK-tid*
**Русский:** собранный, спокойный; собранный (в одном издании)
**Type:** quality; also participial
**Position:** both — sense 2 before a noun only
**Comparison:** more/most — *more collected*, *most collected*
**Form:** -ed from *collect*

Collected has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Calm and in control of your feelings.** (собранный, спокойный, невозмутимый)

1. She stayed calm and *collected* during the interview.
2. He seemed more *collected* after a glass of water.

**2. Brought together in one book or set.** (собранный, полный (о собрании сочинений))

3. I bought the *collected* works of Mark Twain.
4. Her *collected* essays came out last fall.

---

### collectible

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈlɛktəbəl/ &middot; *kuh-LEK-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** коллекционный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more collectible*, *most collectible*
**Form:** -ible from *collect*
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *He sells collectibles online*. The spelling *collectable* exists but *collectible* is the usual American form.

Worth collecting because people value it.

1. Old baseball cards are highly *collectible*.
2. The store sells *collectible* coins and stamps.
3. First editions are the most *collectible* copies.

---

### collective

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈlɛktɪv/ &middot; *kuh-LEK-tiv*
**Русский:** коллективный, общий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ive from *collect*
**Contrast:** Russian *коллектив* (the staff, the team) is not English *collective*: say *the staff*, *the team*, *my coworkers*. The noun *a collective* is a group that owns or runs something together, like a farm or an art space.

Shared or done by all the members of a group.

1. It was a *collective* decision.
2. We breathed a *collective* sigh of relief.
3. The union won the right to *collective* bargaining.

---

### collegial

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈliːdʒiəl/ &middot; *kuh-LEE-jee-uhl*
**Русский:** дружеский, товарищеский (между коллегами)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more collegial*, *most collegial*
**Form:** -al from *colleague*

Friendly and respectful between people who work together.

1. The department has a very *collegial* atmosphere.
2. Despite their disagreements, the debate stayed *collegial*.
3. The new manager is more *collegial* than the last one.

---

### collegiate

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈliːdʒiət/ &middot; *kuh-LEE-jee-uht*
**Русский:** студенческий, университетский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from *college*
**Contrast:** A false friend: *collegiate* is about colleges and college students, not *коллегиальный* (decided together) — that is *collective* or *joint*.

Relating to a college or to college students.

1. She won three *collegiate* swimming titles.
2. *Collegiate* athletes can now earn money from sponsorships.
3. He kept his *collegiate* dictionary on the desk.

---

### colloquial

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈloʊkwiəl/ &middot; *kuh-LOH-kwee-uhl*
**Русский:** разговорный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more colloquial*, *most colloquial*
**Opposite:** *formal*; *literary*

Used in informal conversation rather than formal writing.

1. "Gonna" is a *colloquial* form of "going to."
2. Her English sounds natural and *colloquial*.
3. The translation is more *colloquial* than the original.

---

### Colombian

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈlʌmbiən/ &middot; *kuh-LUHM-bee-uhn*
**Русский:** колумбийский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** The country is spelled *Colombia*, with two *o*s; *Columbia* is a US river, a university and the District of Columbia. It is also a noun: *a Colombian* (колумбиец).

From or relating to Colombia.

1. We only buy *Colombian* coffee.
2. Her mother is *Colombian* and her father is Mexican.
3. The restaurant serves traditional *Colombian* food.

---

### colonial

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈloʊniəl/ &middot; *kuh-LOH-nee-uhl*
**Русский:** колониальный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *colony*

Colonial has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to a colony or to a country's rule over colonies.** (колониальный)

1. The country won independence from *colonial* rule in 1960.
2. Many borders in Africa are *colonial* inventions.

**2. In the US, from the time before independence, or built in that style.** (колониальный (о периоде до 1776 г. и стиле))

3. They live in a white *colonial* house with black shutters.
4. Williamsburg recreates *colonial* life in Virginia.

---

### color-blind

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkʌlər ˌblaɪnd/ &middot; *KUH-lur-blynd*
**Русский:** страдающий дальтонизмом; не учитывающий расу
**Type:** compound; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *color* + *blind*

Color-blind has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Unable to see the difference between certain colors.** (страдающий дальтонизмом)

1. My dad is *color-blind* and can't tell red from green.
2. *Color-blind* drivers learn the order of the traffic lights.

**2. Treating people the same regardless of race.** (не делающий различий по расе)

3. The company claims its hiring is *color-blind*.
4. Critics say a *color-blind* approach ignores real inequality.

---

### colorectal

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkoʊləˈrɛktəl/ &middot; *koh-luh-REK-tuhl*
**Русский:** колоректальный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** *colon* + *rectal*

Relating to the colon and rectum.

1. Doctors recommend *colorectal* cancer screening after 45.
2. She was referred to a *colorectal* surgeon.
3. Diet can affect *colorectal* health.

---

### colored

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkʌlərd/ &middot; *KUH-lurd*
**Русский:** цветной, окрашенный
**Type:** color; also participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *color*
**Contrast:** About people, *colored* is dated and offensive in the US; say *people of color* or name the group. For things it is neutral, often with an adverb or color word: *brightly colored*, *cream-colored*.

Having a color, or a particular color, rather than being white, black or clear.

1. The kids drew with *colored* pencils.
2. The birds were brightly *colored*.
3. She wore a cream-*colored* dress to the wedding.

---

### colorful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkʌlərfəl/ &middot; *KUH-lur-fuhl*
**Русский:** красочный, яркий; колоритный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more colorful*, *most colorful*
**Form:** -ful from *color*
**Opposite:** *colorless*; *dull*

Colorful has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Having many bright colors.** (красочный, яркий)

1. The market was full of *colorful* fruit.
2. Her kitchen is much more *colorful* than mine.

**2. Interesting, lively and a little unusual.** (колоритный, яркий)

3. My grandfather was a *colorful* character.
4. The town has a long and *colorful* history.

**3. Of language, rude or full of swearing; a polite way to say it.** ((о языке) крепкий, грубый)

5. The coach used some *colorful* language after the loss.
6. His description of his boss was *colorful*, to say the least.

---

### colorless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkʌlərləs/ &middot; *KUH-lur-luhs*
**Русский:** бесцветный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more colorless*, *most colorless*
**Form:** -less from *color*
**Opposite:** *colorful*

Colorless has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having no color.** (бесцветный)

1. Water is a *colorless* liquid.
2. Carbon monoxide is *colorless* and has no smell.

**2. Dull and not interesting.** (бесцветный, скучный)

3. He gave a *colorless* speech that nobody remembers.
4. The sequel was even more *colorless* than the first movie.

---

### colossal

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈlɑːsəl/ &middot; *kuh-LAH-suhl*
**Русский:** колоссальный, огромный
**Type:** size
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — an extreme adjective
**Contrast:** An extreme adjective: use *absolutely* or *truly*, not *very* — ✗ *very colossal* → *a truly colossal mistake*. In American speech it is often about mistakes and failures: *a colossal waste of time*.

Extremely large.

1. The project was a *colossal* waste of money.
2. A *colossal* statue stands at the entrance.
3. The cost of the repairs was *colossal*.

---

### comatose

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkoʊmətoʊs/ &middot; *KOH-muh-tohs*
**Русский:** коматозный; полусонный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from *coma*

Comatose has 2 distinct senses.

**1. In a coma.** (коматозный, находящийся в коме)

1. The patient remained *comatose* for two weeks.
2. Doctors spoke to his family at the *comatose* man's bedside.

**2. Very tired, sleepy or inactive; used humorously.** (полусонный, вялый)

3. After Thanksgiving dinner, everyone was *comatose* on the couch.
4. The class looked *comatose* by the end of the lecture.

---

### combat-ready

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːmbæt ˌrɛdi/ &middot; *KAHM-bat-red-ee*
**Русский:** боеготовый
**Type:** compound; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *combat* + *ready*

Trained and equipped to fight.

1. The general said the troops were *combat-ready*.
2. The Navy needs more *combat-ready* ships.
3. It takes months to make a new unit *combat-ready*.

---

### combative

**Pronunciation:** /kəmˈbætɪv/ &middot; *kuhm-BAT-iv*
**Русский:** воинственный, драчливый, агрессивный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more combative*, *most combative*
**Form:** -ive from *combat*

Ready and eager to argue or fight.

1. The senator was *combative* with reporters.
2. He gave a *combative* interview on cable news.
3. The second debate was even more *combative* than the first.

---

### combined

**Pronunciation:** /kəmˈbaɪnd/ &middot; *kuhm-BYND*
**Русский:** совместный, общий, вместе взятый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — also right after a noun in *…combined* (вместе взятые)
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *combine*

Added or joined together.

1. Their *combined* income is about $150,000.
2. It took a *combined* effort to move the piano.
3. He earns more than the rest of us *combined*.

---

### combustible

**Pronunciation:** /kəmˈbʌstəbəl/ &middot; *kuhm-BUHS-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** горючий, воспламеняемый; взрывоопасный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more combustible*, *most combustible*
**Form:** -ible from *combustion*
**Opposite:** *noncombustible*

Combustible has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Able to catch fire and burn easily.** (горючий, воспламеняемый)

1. Keep *combustible* materials away from the stove.
2. Dry leaves are highly *combustible*.

**2. Likely to become angry or violent quickly.** (взрывоопасный, вспыльчивый)

3. The protest created a *combustible* situation.
4. The mix of anger and alcohol made the crowd more *combustible*.

---

### comfortable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkʌmftərbəl/ &middot; *KUHMF-tur-buhl*
**Русский:** удобный; комфортно себя чувствующий; обеспеченный
**Type:** quality; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more comfortable*, *most comfortable*
**Takes:** *with* + noun or *-ing* (at ease with); *doing* or *about* in speech
**Preposition patterns:** [comfortable with](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#comfortable-with)
**Form:** -able from *comfort*
**Opposite:** *uncomfortable*
**Contrast:** Say it in three syllables: KUHMF-tur-buhl, not com-FOR-ta-ble. The person is the subject for how you feel: *I'm comfortable* = мне удобно; *The chair is comfortable* = стул удобный.

Comfortable has 4 distinct senses.

**1. Pleasant to wear, sit on or be in; giving physical comfort.** (удобный)

1. These are the most *comfortable* shoes I own.
2. The hotel beds were really *comfortable*.

**2. Relaxed and not worried or embarrassed.** (комфортно себя чувствующий, спокойный)

3. Make yourself *comfortable* while I get the coffee.
4. I'm not *comfortable* with the idea of lying to her.
5. She felt more *comfortable* speaking English after a year.

**3. Having enough money to live well.** (обеспеченный, зажиточный)

6. They live a *comfortable* life in the suburbs.
7. His parents are pretty *comfortable* financially.

**4. Of a lead or win, large enough to be safe.** (уверенный (о победе, отрыве))

8. The Yankees had a *comfortable* lead in the ninth.
9. She won by a *comfortable* margin.

---

### comforting

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkʌmfərtɪŋ/ &middot; *KUHM-fur-ting*
**Русский:** утешительный, успокаивающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more comforting*, *most comforting*
**Form:** -ing — what gives comfort
**Contrast:** *Comforting* words make you feel less sad or worried; a *comfortable* chair is pleasant to sit in. ✗ *a comforting sofa* → *a comfortable sofa*.

Making you feel less worried, sad or afraid.

1. Thanks for the *comforting* words.
2. It's *comforting* to know you're nearby.
3. Nothing is more *comforting* than a bowl of chicken soup.

---

### comfy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkʌmfi/ &middot; *KUHM-fee*
**Русский:** уютный, удобный (разг.)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *comfier*, *comfiest*
**Form:** short for *comfortable*

Comfortable; an informal word.

1. I changed into something *comfy* after work.
2. This is the *comfiest* couch in the house.
3. Are you *comfy* back there?

---

### comic

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːmɪk/ &middot; *KAH-mik*
**Русский:** комический, комедийный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** attributive only — after a verb use *comical* or *funny*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *Comic* mostly means "of comedy" and goes before a noun: *a comic actor*. *Comical* means funny, often unintentionally, and goes in both positions: *The situation was comical*. The noun *a comic* is a comedian or a comic book.

Comic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to comedy.** (комедийный, комический)

1. He's best known for his *comic* roles.
2. The novel is a *comic* look at small-town life.

**2. Meant to make people laugh.** (смешной, комичный)

3. The sidekick provides some *comic* relief.
4. She has perfect *comic* timing.

---

### comical

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːmɪkəl/ &middot; *KAH-mi-kuhl*
**Русский:** комичный, смешной, нелепый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more comical*, *most comical*
**Form:** -al from *comic*

Funny, often in a strange or silly way.

1. The dog looked *comical* in its tiny sweater.
2. His attempts to dance were almost *comical*.
3. It was the most *comical* wedding speech I've ever heard.

---

### coming

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkʌmɪŋ/ &middot; *KUH-ming*
**Русский:** предстоящий, наступающий, грядущий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *come*
**Contrast:** Only before a noun and usually after *the*: *in the coming days*. *Upcoming* means the same and is more common for events: *the upcoming election*.

Happening soon; in the near future.

1. We'll know more in the *coming* weeks.
2. Prices are expected to rise in the *coming* year.
3. The team is preparing for the *coming* season.

---

### commanding

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈmændɪŋ/ &middot; *kuh-MAN-ding*
**Русский:** властный; господствующий; командующий
**Type:** quality; also participial
**Position:** both — senses 2 and 3 usually before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more commanding*, *most commanding*
**Form:** -ing from *command*

Commanding has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Having authority and making people respect and obey you.** (властный, внушительный)

1. She has a *commanding* presence on stage.
2. His voice was calm but *commanding*.

**2. Of a lead or position, strong and likely to win; of a view, seen from a high place.** (уверенный, господствующий)

3. The Democrats hold a *commanding* lead in the polls.
4. The house has a *commanding* view of the bay.

**3. In charge of a military unit.** (командующий)

5. Report to your *commanding* officer.
6. The *commanding* general visited the base.

---

### commemorative

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈmɛmərətɪv/ &middot; *kuh-MEM-uh-ruh-tiv*
**Русский:** памятный, юбилейный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ive from *commemorate*

Made or done to remember a person or event.

1. The Mint released a *commemorative* coin.
2. Visitors received a *commemorative* program.
3. A *commemorative* plaque marks the spot.

---

### commendable

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈmɛndəbəl/ &middot; *kuh-MEN-duh-buhl*
**Русский:** похвальный, достойный похвалы
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more commendable*, *most commendable*
**Form:** -able from *commend*

Deserving praise.

1. Her honesty is *commendable*.
2. The volunteers did a *commendable* job.
3. What he did was even more *commendable* given his age.

---

### commensurate

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈmɛnsərət/ &middot; *kuh-MEN-sur-uht*
**Русский:** соразмерный, соответствующий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — often right after a noun with *with*: *salary commensurate with experience*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun

Matching something in size, amount or level; formal.

1. Salary is *commensurate* with experience.
2. The punishment should be *commensurate* with the crime.
3. She wants a *commensurate* increase in responsibility.

---

### commercial

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈmɜːrʃəl/ &middot; *kuh-MUR-shuhl*
**Русский:** коммерческий, торговый
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more commercial*; not gradable in sense 1
**Contrast:** The noun *a commercial* is a TV or radio ad (рекламный ролик). A *commercial* flight is a passenger flight on an airline, not a business trip.

Commercial has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to business and buying and selling.** (коммерческий, торговый)

1. The building has *commercial* space on the ground floor.
2. He's a *commercial* airline pilot.

**2. Made to earn money or be popular rather than for art or quality.** (коммерческий, рассчитанный на прибыль)

3. Her new album is more *commercial* than her early work.
4. The movie was a huge *commercial* success.

---

### commercial-grade

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈmɜːrʃəl ˌɡreɪd/ &middot; *kuh-MUR-shuhl-grayd*
**Русский:** профессиональный, промышленного класса
**Type:** compound; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *commercial* + *grade*

Strong or good enough to be used in a business, not just at home.

1. We put a *commercial-grade* stove in the new kitchen.
2. The carpet in the lobby is *commercial-grade*.
3. They sell *commercial-grade* equipment to gyms.

---

### commercialized

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈmɜːrʃəlaɪzd/ &middot; *kuh-MUR-shuh-lyzd*
**Русский:** коммерциализированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more commercialized*, *most commercialized*
**Form:** -ed from *commercialize*

Changed so that making money matters more than anything else.

1. Christmas has become way too *commercialized*.
2. College sports are more *commercialized* than ever.
3. Some people avoid *commercialized* tourist spots.

---

### commissioned

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈmɪʃənd/ &middot; *kuh-MISH-uhnd*
**Русский:** офицерский (о звании); заказанный
**Type:** classifying; also participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *commission*

Commissioned has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a military officer, holding an official rank such as lieutenant or higher.** (имеющий офицерское звание)

1. She became a *commissioned* officer after West Point.
2. Only *commissioned* officers may attend the briefing.

**2. Made or done because someone officially asked and paid for it.** (заказанный, выполненный по заказу)

3. The museum displayed a *commissioned* portrait of the mayor.
4. The orchestra premiered a newly *commissioned* piece.

---

### committed

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈmɪtɪd/ &middot; *kuh-MIT-id*
**Русский:** преданный, приверженный; серьёзный (об отношениях)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more committed*, *most committed*
**Takes:** *to* + noun or *-ing*
**Preposition patterns:** [committed to](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#committed-to)
**Form:** -ed from *commit*
**Contrast:** *To* here is a preposition, so it takes *-ing*: ✗ *committed to improve* → *committed to improving*.

Committed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Willing to give time and energy to a job, cause or activity.** (преданный, приверженный)

1. We're *committed* to reducing waste.
2. She's one of the most *committed* teachers in the school.
3. The company needs *committed* volunteers.

**2. In a serious, long-term romantic relationship.** (серьёзный, постоянный (об отношениях))

4. They've been in a *committed* relationship for years.
5. He isn't ready to be *committed* to anyone.

---

### common

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːmən/ &middot; *KAH-muhn*
**Русский:** распространённый, частый; общий; обычный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *more common*, *most common*; *commoner*, *commonest* are less usual
**Takes:** *to* + noun (shared by); *for* + noun + *to*-infinitive (*It's common for kids to…*)
**Preposition patterns:** [common to](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#common-to)
**Opposite:** *rare*; *uncommon*
**Contrast:** Russian *общий* is wider: *the total cost* (not *common cost*), *general knowledge* (not *common knowledge*, which means "something everybody knows"). *Common sense* is a noun — здравый смысл.

Common has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Happening often or existing in large numbers.** (распространённый, частый)

1. Smith is the *most common* last name in the US.
2. It's *common* for kids to be afraid of the dark.
3. Back pain is becoming more *common* among office workers.

**2. Shared by two or more people or groups.** (общий)

4. We have a *common* interest in jazz.
5. This problem is *common* to all big cities.

**3. Ordinary; not special or of high rank.** (обычный, простой)

6. There's no cure for the *common* cold.
7. He talks like a *common* working man.

---

### common-law

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːmən ˌlɔː/ &middot; *KAH-muhn-law*
**Русский:** гражданский (о браке без регистрации); основанный на общем праве
**Type:** compound; also classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *common* + *law*
**Contrast:** A false friend: Russian *гражданский брак* in everyday speech means living together without registering — that is *common-law marriage*. English *civil marriage* is a registered marriage without a religious ceremony.

Common-law has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a husband, wife or marriage, treated as married because the couple has lived together for a long time.** (гражданский, незарегистрированный (о браке))

1. She's been his *common-law* wife for ten years.
2. Only a few states still recognize *common-law* marriage.

**2. Based on law made by past court decisions rather than written statutes.** (основанный на общем (прецедентном) праве)

3. The lawyer made a *common-law* argument.
4. Fraud is also a *common-law* offense.

---

### commonplace

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːmənpleɪs/ &middot; *KAH-muhn-plays*
**Русский:** обычный, повседневный, заурядный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more commonplace*, *most commonplace*
**Form:** compound — *common* + *place*
**Opposite:** *rare*; *unusual*

Happening or seen so often that it is no longer unusual.

1. Working from home is now *commonplace*.
2. Electric cars are becoming more *commonplace*.
3. It's a *commonplace* problem in small businesses.

---

### commonsense

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɑːmənˈsɛns/ &middot; *kah-muhn-SENS*
**Русский:** здравый, разумный, основанный на здравом смысле
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** more/most — *more commonsense*, *most commonsense*
**Form:** compound — from the noun *common sense*
**Contrast:** Before a noun it is written as one word: *a commonsense solution*. After a verb, use the two-word noun: *It's just common sense*.

Based on practical good judgment.

1. We need a *commonsense* approach to gun safety.
2. She offered some *commonsense* advice.
3. The bill includes a few *commonsense* reforms.

---

### commonsensical

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɑːmənˈsɛnsɪkəl/ &middot; *kah-muhn-SEN-si-kuhl*
**Русский:** здравомыслящий, разумный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more commonsensical*, *most commonsensical*
**Form:** -ical from *common sense*

Showing practical good judgment.

1. Her answer was simple and *commonsensical*.
2. He's a *commonsensical* guy who doesn't panic.
3. The second plan seems more *commonsensical*.

---

### communal

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈmjuːnəl/ &middot; *kuh-MYOO-nuhl*
**Русский:** общий, совместный; общинный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *commune*
**Contrast:** Not Russian *коммунальный*: *коммунальные услуги* are *utilities*, and *коммунальная квартира* is a *shared apartment*. English *communal* means shared by a group: *a communal kitchen*.

Shared by a group of people.

1. The dorm has a *communal* kitchen on each floor.
2. Dinner is served at long *communal* tables.
3. In the old days, the well was *communal*.

---

### communicable

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈmjuːnɪkəbəl/ &middot; *kuh-MYOO-ni-kuh-buhl*
**Русский:** заразный, инфекционный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -able from *communicate*

Of a disease, able to pass from one person to another.

1. Measles is a highly *communicable* disease.
2. The CDC tracks *communicable* diseases nationwide.
3. Is the infection *communicable*?

---

### communicative

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈmjuːnɪkətɪv/ &middot; *kuh-MYOO-ni-kuh-tiv*
**Русский:** разговорчивый, открытый; коммуникативный
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more communicative*, *most communicative*
**Form:** -ive from *communicate*
**Contrast:** For a person who loves meeting people, English says *outgoing* or *sociable*; *communicative* means willing to talk and share information: *He isn't very communicative about his plans*.

Communicative has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Willing to talk and give information.** (разговорчивый, открытый)

1. Teenagers aren't always very *communicative*.
2. The new manager is much more *communicative* than the last one.

**2. Relating to communication.** (коммуникативный)

3. The course focuses on *communicative* skills.
4. Many teachers use a *communicative* approach to language learning.

---

### communist

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːmjənɪst/ &middot; *KAHM-yuh-nist*
**Русский:** коммунистический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a communist* (коммунист). Written with a capital C for a specific party: *the Communist Party*.

Relating to communism or a communist party.

1. Cuba still has a *communist* government.
2. Her grandparents left a *communist* country in the 1970s.
3. Was East Germany *communist*?

---

### compact

**Pronunciation:** /kəmˈpækt/ &middot; *kuhm-PAKT*
**Русский:** компактный; плотный
**Type:** size
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more compact*, *most compact*
**Contrast:** The adjective is often stressed on the second syllable (kuhm-PAKT), though KAHM-pakt is also heard; the noun *a compact* (a small car, a powder case, an agreement) is always KAHM-pakt.

Compact has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Small and using space efficiently.** (компактный)

1. We rented a *compact* car to save on gas.
2. The new model is lighter and more *compact*.
3. Their apartment is *compact* but comfortable.

**2. Pressed closely together; dense.** (плотный, сжатый)

4. The soil was too *compact* for the roots to grow.
5. He has a short, *compact* build, like a wrestler.

---

### companionable

**Pronunciation:** /kəmˈpænjənəbəl/ &middot; *kuhm-PAN-yuh-nuh-buhl*
**Русский:** дружеский, приятный в общении
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more companionable*, *most companionable*
**Form:** -able from *companion*

Friendly and pleasant to be with.

1. We sat in *companionable* silence by the fire.
2. Her dog is gentle and *companionable*.
3. The trip made them more *companionable* than before.

---

### comparable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːmpərəbəl/ &middot; *KAHM-pur-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** сравнимый, сопоставимый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more comparable*, *most comparable*
**Takes:** *to* or *with* + noun
**Form:** -able from *compare*
**Opposite:** *incomparable*
**Contrast:** The stress moves to the first syllable: KAHM-pur-uh-buhl, not kuhm-PAIR-uh-buhl as in *compare*, though the second is also heard.

Similar in size, amount or quality, so that it makes sense to compare them.

1. Rents here are *comparable* to Boston's.
2. She found a job at a *comparable* salary.
3. The two studies aren't directly *comparable*.

---

### comparative

**Pronunciation:** /kəmˈpɛrətɪv/ &middot; *kuhm-PAIR-uh-tiv*
**Русский:** сравнительный; относительный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ative from *compare*

Comparative has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Involving the comparison of two or more things.** (сравнительный)

1. She teaches *comparative* literature.
2. The report is a *comparative* study of US and Canadian schools.
3. "Bigger" is the *comparative* form of "big."

**2. Measured by comparing with something else.** (относительный)

4. After the city, we enjoyed the *comparative* quiet of the farm.
5. He passed the test with *comparative* ease.

---

### compartmentalized

**Pronunciation:** /kəmˌpɑːrtˈmɛntəlaɪzd/ &middot; *kuhm-pahrt-MEN-tuh-lyzd*
**Русский:** разделённый на отсеки, изолированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more compartmentalized*, *most compartmentalized*
**Form:** -ed from *compartmentalize*

Divided into separate sections or kept strictly apart.

1. The bag has a *compartmentalized* interior.
2. Information in the agency is highly *compartmentalized*.
3. He keeps his work and family life very *compartmentalized*.

---

### compassionate

**Pronunciation:** /kəmˈpæʃənət/ &middot; *kuhm-PASH-uh-nuht*
**Русский:** сострадательный, отзывчивый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more compassionate*, *most compassionate*
**Takes:** *toward* or *to* + noun (optional)
**Form:** -ate from *compassion*
**Opposite:** *callous*; *heartless*

Feeling or showing sympathy and a wish to help people who suffer.

1. She's a kind and *compassionate* nurse.
2. The judge was *compassionate* toward the young defendant.
3. We need a more *compassionate* immigration system.

---

### compatible

**Pronunciation:** /kəmˈpætəbəl/ &middot; *kuhm-PAT-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** совместимый; подходящий друг другу
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more compatible*, *most compatible*
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Preposition patterns:** [compatible with](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#compatible-with)
**Opposite:** *incompatible*

Compatible has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Able to work with another device, program or system.** (совместимый)

1. Is this charger *compatible* with my phone?
2. The software isn't *compatible* with older Macs.

**2. Able to live or work together happily.** (подходящий друг другу, совместимый)

3. We're just not *compatible* as roommates.
4. The app matches you with the most *compatible* people nearby.

**3. Able to exist together without conflict.** (совместимый, согласующийся)

5. Is economic growth *compatible* with protecting the environment?
6. His actions aren't *compatible* with his promises.

---

### compelling

**Pronunciation:** /kəmˈpɛlɪŋ/ &middot; *kuhm-PEL-ing*
**Русский:** убедительный; захватывающий
**Type:** evaluative; also participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more compelling*, *most compelling*
**Form:** -ing from *compel*

Compelling has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Strong enough to make you believe or act.** (убедительный, веский)

1. The prosecution presented *compelling* evidence.
2. Give me one *compelling* reason to stay.

**2. So interesting that you want to keep watching, reading or listening.** (захватывающий)

3. It's the most *compelling* documentary I've seen this year.
4. The characters are *compelling* from the first page.

---

### competent

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːmpɪtənt/ &middot; *KAHM-puh-tuhnt*
**Русский:** компетентный, умелый; вполне приличный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more competent*, *most competent*
**Takes:** *at* or *in* + noun or *-ing*; *to*-infinitive (legal: *competent to stand trial*)
**Opposite:** *incompetent*
**Contrast:** English *competent* often means only "good enough": *a competent performance* is solid, not brilliant. Russian *компетентный* sounds like higher praise — for that, say *highly skilled* or *expert*.

Competent has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Having enough skill or knowledge to do something well.** (компетентный, умелый)

1. We need a *competent* lawyer.
2. She's very *competent* at managing people.
3. He's more *competent* than his résumé suggests.

**2. Satisfactory but not excellent.** (вполне приличный, добротный)

4. The band gave a *competent* but boring show.
5. His Spanish is *competent*, not fluent.

**3. Legally able to make decisions or take part in a trial.** (дееспособный, правомочный)

6. The judge ruled that he was *competent* to stand trial.
7. A doctor must confirm the patient is mentally *competent*.

---

### competitive

**Pronunciation:** /kəmˈpɛtətɪv/ &middot; *kuhm-PET-uh-tiv*
**Русский:** соревновательный; честолюбивый; конкурентоспособный
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more competitive*, *most competitive*
**Form:** -ive from *compete*

Competitive has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Involving competition, where people try to win or be better than others.** (соревновательный, конкурентный)

1. She played *competitive* tennis in college.
2. Getting into medical school is extremely *competitive*.

**2. Of a person, wanting very much to win or be the best.** (стремящийся к победе, азартный)

3. My brother is way too *competitive* at board games.
4. She's the most *competitive* person on the team.

**3. Of prices, pay or products, as good as or better than others.** (конкурентоспособный)

5. We offer *competitive* salaries and good benefits.
6. Their prices are more *competitive* than the big chains'.

---

### complacent

**Pronunciation:** /kəmˈpleɪsənt/ &middot; *kuhm-PLAY-suhnt*
**Русский:** самоуспокоенный, благодушный, беспечный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more complacent*, *most complacent*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*

So pleased with how things are that you stop trying or stop seeing danger.

1. We can't get *complacent* just because we're winning.
2. People have grown *complacent* about wearing seat belts.
3. Success made the company more *complacent*.

---

### complementary

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɑːmpləˈmɛntəri/ &middot; *kahm-pluh-MEN-tuh-ree*
**Русский:** дополняющий друг друга; взаимодополняющий
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + noun
**Form:** -ary from *complement*
**Contrast:** *Complementary* (with *e*) means completing each other; *complimentary* (with *i*) means praising or free. They sound exactly the same: *complementary colors*, *complimentary drinks*.

Complementary has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Different but going well together, each adding what the other lacks.** (дополняющий друг друга)

1. The two partners have *complementary* skills.
2. Blue and orange are *complementary* colors.
3. The wine is *complementary* to the cheese.

**2. Of medicine, used together with standard medical treatment.** (дополнительный, альтернативный (о медицине))

4. The hospital offers *complementary* therapies like acupuncture.
5. Yoga is often used as a *complementary* treatment.

---

### compliant

**Pronunciation:** /kəmˈplaɪənt/ &middot; *kuhm-PLY-uhnt*
**Русский:** соответствующий требованиям; послушный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more compliant*, *most compliant*
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Form:** -ant from *comply*
**Opposite:** *noncompliant*

Compliant has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Following rules, laws or standards.** (соответствующий требованиям, отвечающий нормам)

1. The building is fully *compliant* with the fire code.
2. All our products are FDA-*compliant*.

**2. Too willing to do what other people want.** (послушный, уступчивый)

3. The dictator surrounded himself with *compliant* advisers.
4. The medication made him quiet and more *compliant*.

---

### complicated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːmplɪkeɪtɪd/ &middot; *KAHM-pli-kay-tid*
**Русский:** сложный, запутанный
**Type:** quality; also participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more complicated*, *most complicated*
**Form:** -ed from *complicate*
**Opposite:** *simple*; *straightforward*

Complicated has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having many parts or steps and hard to understand or do.** (сложный, запутанный)

1. The instructions are too *complicated*.
2. Taxes are more *complicated* than they need to be.
3. It's a *complicated* piece of machinery.

**2. Of a situation or relationship, difficult because of problems and mixed feelings.** (непростой, сложный)

4. "Are you two together?" "It's *complicated*."
5. She has a *complicated* relationship with her father.

---

### complimentary

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɑːmpləˈmɛntəri/ &middot; *kahm-pluh-MEN-tuh-ree*
**Русский:** хвалебный, лестный; бесплатный
**Type:** evaluative; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more complimentary*; not gradable in sense 2
**Takes:** *about* + noun (sense 1)
**Form:** -ary from *compliment*
**Contrast:** *Complimentary* (with *i*) means praising or free; *complementary* (with *e*) means completing each other. Same pronunciation.

Complimentary has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Saying something nice about someone or something.** (хвалебный, лестный)

1. The reviews were very *complimentary*.
2. She was more *complimentary* about my cooking than I expected.

**2. Given free, especially by a hotel, restaurant or business.** (бесплатный)

3. The hotel offers a *complimentary* breakfast.
4. We got two *complimentary* tickets to the game.

---

### composed

**Pronunciation:** /kəmˈpoʊzd/ &middot; *kuhm-POHZD*
**Русский:** спокойный, сдержанный
**Type:** quality; also participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more composed*, *most composed*
**Form:** -ed from *compose*
**Contrast:** *Be composed of* (состоять из) is a passive verb, not this adjective: *The team is composed of ten players*.

Calm and in control of your feelings.

1. She looked *composed* during the press conference.
2. He took a deep breath and tried to stay *composed*.
3. The younger player was more *composed* under pressure.

---

### composite

**Pronunciation:** /kəmˈpɑːzɪt/ &middot; *kuhm-PAH-zit*
**Русский:** составной, сборный; композитный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Made of different parts or materials put together.

1. Police released a *composite* sketch of the suspect.
2. The deck is built from *composite* boards, not wood.
3. The final picture is *composite*, made from five photos.

---

### compound

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːmpaʊnd/ &middot; *KAHM-pownd*
**Русский:** сложный, составной
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** The verb *compound* (усугублять) is stressed kuhm-POWND: *The delay compounded the problem*. The noun *a compound* is a chemical compound or a fenced group of buildings.

Made of two or more parts or elements.

1. "Toothbrush" is a *compound* word.
2. Flies have *compound* eyes.
3. He suffered a *compound* fracture of the leg.

---

### compressed

**Pronunciation:** /kəmˈprɛst/ &middot; *kuhm-PREST*
**Русский:** сжатый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *compress*

Compressed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Pressed into a smaller space or size.** (сжатый)

1. The tires are filled with *compressed* air.
2. Send the photos as a *compressed* file.

**2. Shortened so that it takes less time.** (сжатый, сокращённый)

3. We're working on a *compressed* schedule this year.
4. The whole story is *compressed* into ninety minutes.

---

### compromising

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːmprəmaɪzɪŋ/ &middot; *KAHM-pruh-my-zing*
**Русский:** компрометирующий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more compromising*, *most compromising*
**Form:** -ing from *compromise*
**Contrast:** It does not mean "ready to compromise" — that is *willing to compromise* or *flexible*. The opposite-looking *uncompromising* does mean refusing to compromise.

Showing that someone did something embarrassing, secret or wrong.

1. The tabloid published *compromising* photos of the senator.
2. He was caught in a *compromising* position.
3. The emails were even more *compromising* than we thought.

---

### computational

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɑːmpjəˈteɪʃənəl/ &middot; *kahm-pyuh-TAY-shuh-nuhl*
**Русский:** вычислительный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *computation*

Relating to computing or calculation, especially by computers.

1. She has a PhD in *computational* biology.
2. Training the model takes huge *computational* power.
3. The method reduces *computational* costs.

---

### concave

**Pronunciation:** /kɑːnˈkeɪv/ &middot; *kahn-KAYV*
**Русский:** вогнутый
**Type:** shape
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more concave*, *most concave*
**Opposite:** *convex*

Curved inward, like the inside of a bowl.

1. A *concave* mirror makes things look bigger.
2. The lens is slightly *concave*.
3. The newer design has a more *concave* shape.

---

### concealed

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈsiːld/ &middot; *kuhn-SEELD*
**Русский:** скрытый, спрятанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *conceal*
**Opposite:** *visible*; *open*

Hidden from view.

1. He has a permit to carry a *concealed* weapon.
2. The lights are *concealed* under the cabinets.
3. A *concealed* camera recorded the break-in.

---

### conceited

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈsiːtɪd/ &middot; *kuhn-SEE-tid*
**Русский:** самовлюблённый, тщеславный, зазнавшийся
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more conceited*, *most conceited*
**Form:** -ed from *conceit*
**Opposite:** *modest*; *humble*

Thinking too highly of yourself.

1. He's talented but so *conceited*.
2. I don't want to sound *conceited*, but I was right.
3. She's the most *conceited* person I've ever met.

---

### conceivable

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈsiːvəbəl/ &middot; *kuhn-SEE-vuh-buhl*
**Русский:** мыслимый, возможный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *that*-clause, with *it*: *It is conceivable that…*
**Form:** -able from *conceive*
**Opposite:** *inconceivable*

Possible to imagine or believe.

1. We tried every *conceivable* solution.
2. It's *conceivable* that the test was wrong.
3. I can't think of any *conceivable* reason for the delay.

---

### concentrated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːnsəntreɪtɪd/ &middot; *KAHN-suhn-tray-tid*
**Русский:** концентрированный; сосредоточенный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more concentrated*, *most concentrated*
**Takes:** *in* + noun (gathered in a place)
**Form:** -ed from *concentrate*

Concentrated has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Made stronger by removing water.** (концентрированный)

1. Mix the *concentrated* juice with three cans of water.
2. This detergent is more *concentrated*, so use less.

**2. Directed with a lot of effort at one aim.** (сосредоточенный, целенаправленный)

3. It took a *concentrated* effort to finish on time.
4. The city faced a *concentrated* attack from the air.

**3. Gathered together in one place.** (сосредоточенный (в одном месте))

5. Most of the population is *concentrated* in the cities.
6. Wealth is *concentrated* in a few hands.

---

### concentric

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈsɛntrɪk/ &middot; *kuhn-SEN-trik*
**Русский:** концентрический
**Type:** shape
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Of circles, having the same center.

1. The stone made *concentric* ripples in the pond.
2. A target is a set of *concentric* circles.
3. The rings in a tree trunk are roughly *concentric*.

---

### conceptual

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈsɛptʃuəl/ &middot; *kuhn-SEP-choo-uhl*
**Русский:** концептуальный, понятийный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *concept*

Relating to ideas and concepts rather than real objects or details.

1. The design is still at the *conceptual* stage.
2. The museum has a room of *conceptual* art.
3. Our disagreement is *conceptual*, not practical.

---

### concerted

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈsɜːrtɪd/ &middot; *kuhn-SUR-tid*
**Русский:** согласованный, совместный; целенаправленный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Done with real effort and determination, often by several people together.

1. We made a *concerted* effort to cut costs.
2. The two countries launched a *concerted* campaign against smuggling.
3. It will take *concerted* action from every state.

---

### conciliatory

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈsɪliətɔːri/ &middot; *kuhn-SIL-ee-uh-tor-ee*
**Русский:** примирительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more conciliatory*, *most conciliatory*
**Form:** -ory from *conciliate*
**Opposite:** *hostile*; *confrontational*

Meant to end an argument or make someone less angry.

1. The president struck a *conciliatory* tone in his speech.
2. After the fight, she sent a *conciliatory* text.
3. The union has become more *conciliatory* in recent talks.

---

### concise

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈsaɪs/ &middot; *kuhn-SYS*
**Русский:** краткий, сжатый, лаконичный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more concise*, *most concise*
**Opposite:** *wordy*; *long-winded*

Giving the needed information in few words.

1. Keep your answers clear and *concise*.
2. She wrote a *concise* summary of the report.
3. Can you make the email a little more *concise*?

---

### concluding

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈkluːdɪŋ/ &middot; *kuhn-KLOO-ding*
**Русский:** заключительный
**Type:** classifying; also participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *conclude*
**Opposite:** *opening*; *introductory*

Coming at the end of something.

1. Let me make a few *concluding* remarks.
2. The *concluding* chapter ties the story together.
3. Your essay needs a stronger *concluding* paragraph.

---

### conclusive

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈkluːsɪv/ &middot; *kuhn-KLOO-siv*
**Русский:** окончательный, неопровержимый, убедительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more conclusive*, *most conclusive*
**Form:** -ive from *conclude*
**Opposite:** *inconclusive*

Proving something so that there is no doubt.

1. There's no *conclusive* evidence that the drug works.
2. The DNA results were *conclusive*.
3. The second study was more *conclusive* than the first.

---

### concomitant

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈkɑːmɪtənt/ &middot; *kuhn-KAH-mi-tuhnt*
**Русский:** сопутствующий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Preposition patterns:** [concomitant with](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#concomitant-with)

Happening at the same time as something else and connected with it; formal.

1. Rapid growth brought *concomitant* problems like traffic.
2. Higher pay comes with *concomitant* responsibilities.
3. The risks are *concomitant* with the rewards.

---

### concurrent

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈkɜːrənt/ &middot; *kuhn-KUR-uhnt*
**Русский:** одновременный, параллельный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Preposition patterns:** [concurrent with](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#concurrent-with)
**Form:** -ent from *concur*
**Contrast:** Not Russian *конкурентный* (competitive) — that is *competitive*. *Concurrent* means at the same time.

Happening or existing at the same time.

1. He's serving two *concurrent* ten-year sentences.
2. The festival has three *concurrent* stages.
3. The layoffs were *concurrent* with record profits.

---

### concurring

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈkɜːrɪŋ/ &middot; *kuhn-KUR-ing*
**Русский:** совпадающий, согласный
**Type:** classifying; also participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *concur*

Agreeing; in law, agreeing with a court's decision but for different reasons.

1. Justice Kagan wrote a *concurring* opinion.
2. Two *concurring* judges added their own reasons.
3. The report cites several *concurring* expert views.

---

### condescending

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɑːndɪˈsɛndɪŋ/ &middot; *kahn-duh-SEN-ding*
**Русский:** снисходительный, высокомерный
**Type:** participial; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more condescending*, *most condescending*
**Takes:** *to* or *toward* + noun
**Form:** -ing from *condescend*
**Contrast:** Russian *снисходительный* can be kind (lenient); English *condescending* is always negative — treating someone as less intelligent. For lenient, say *lenient* or *forgiving*.

Treating people as if they are less smart or less important than you.

1. Don't be so *condescending* to the new guys.
2. He explained it in a *condescending* tone.
3. Her second email was even more *condescending*.

---

### conditional

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈdɪʃənəl/ &middot; *kuhn-DISH-uh-nuhl*
**Русский:** условный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *on* or *upon* + noun or *-ing*
**Preposition patterns:** [conditional on](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#conditional-on-conditional-upon) &middot; [conditional upon](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#conditional-on-conditional-upon)
**Form:** -al from *condition*
**Opposite:** *unconditional*

Conditional has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Depending on something else being done or happening first.** (условный, зависящий от условия)

1. The job offer is *conditional* on a background check.
2. They gave us *conditional* approval for the loan.

**2. In grammar, expressing a condition, usually with *if*.** (условный (в грамматике))

3. "If it rains, we'll stay home" is a *conditional* sentence.
4. We studied the *conditional* mood in Spanish class.

---

### conditioned

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈdɪʃənd/ &middot; *kuhn-DISH-uhnd*
**Русский:** условный (о рефлексе); приученный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to*-infinitive (trained to do something)
**Form:** -ed from *condition*

Conditioned has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a response, learned through training or repeated experience.** (условный (о рефлексе, реакции))

1. Salivating at a bell is a *conditioned* reflex.
2. Fear of dentists can be a *conditioned* response.

**2. Trained or used to behaving in a certain way.** (приученный)

3. We've been *conditioned* to check our phones constantly.
4. The dogs are *conditioned* to wait for the whistle.

---

### conducive

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈduːsɪv/ &middot; *kuhn-DOO-siv*
**Русский:** способствующий, благоприятный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb with *to*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more conducive*, *most conducive*
**Takes:** *to* + noun or *-ing*
**Preposition patterns:** [conducive to](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#conducive-to)
**Contrast:** Do not confuse with *conductive* (проводящий), which is about electricity and heat.

Making it easier for something to happen.

1. A noisy office isn't *conducive* to concentration.
2. The weather was *conducive* to a day at the beach.
3. Soft lighting is more *conducive* to sleep.

---

### conductive

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈdʌktɪv/ &middot; *kuhn-DUHK-tiv*
**Русский:** проводящий (ток, тепло)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more conductive*, *most conductive*
**Form:** -ive from *conduct*
**Contrast:** Do not confuse with *conducive* (способствующий): *conducive to learning*, but *conductive metal*.

Able to carry electricity or heat.

1. Copper is highly *conductive*.
2. Silver is even more *conductive* than copper.
3. The screen has a *conductive* coating.

---

### Confederate

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈfɛdərət/ &middot; *kuhn-FED-ur-uht*
**Русский:** конфедеративный (относящийся к Конфедерации южных штатов)
**Type:** classifying; also origin
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** With a capital C it refers to the Southern states that left the US in 1861. Lowercase *a confederate* is a noun — an accomplice (сообщник); the verb *confederate* ends in -rayt.

Relating to the Confederacy, the Southern states that fought the North in the US Civil War.

1. Several cities removed *Confederate* statues.
2. His ancestor was a *Confederate* soldier.
3. Richmond was the *Confederate* capital.

---

### confident

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːnfɪdənt/ &middot; *KAHN-fi-duhnt*
**Русский:** уверенный
**Type:** quality; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more confident*, *most confident*
**Takes:** *that*-clause; *of* or *about* + noun or *-ing*; *in* + noun (trust)
**Preposition patterns:** [confident of](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#confident-of)
**Form:** -ent from *confide*
**Opposite:** *insecure*; *unsure*
**Contrast:** A false friend: *confident* is уверенный, not *конфиденциальный* — that is *confidential*. *A confidant* (with *a*) is a noun: a person you tell secrets to.

Confident has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Believing in your own abilities.** (уверенный в себе)

1. She's a *confident* speaker.
2. He feels more *confident* behind the wheel now.

**2. Sure that something will happen or is true.** (уверенный (в чём-то))

3. I'm *confident* that we'll win.
4. The team is *confident* of a good result.
5. Are you *confident* about your answer?

---

### confidential

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɑːnfɪˈdɛnʃəl/ &middot; *kahn-fi-DEN-shuhl*
**Русский:** конфиденциальный, секретный; доверительный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ial from *confidence*
**Contrast:** Russian *конфиденциальный* is *confidential*, not *confident* (уверенный).

Confidential has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Meant to be kept secret.** (конфиденциальный, секретный)

1. Your answers will be kept strictly *confidential*.
2. He leaked *confidential* documents to the press.

**2. Of a voice or manner, showing that you are telling a secret.** (доверительный)

3. She lowered her voice to a *confidential* whisper.
4. He leaned in with a *confidential* smile.

---

### configured

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈfɪɡjərd/ &middot; *kuhn-FIG-yurd*
**Русский:** настроенный, сконфигурированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — usually with an adverb: *a properly configured server*
**Comparison:** not gradable — *properly configured*, *badly configured*
**Takes:** *for* + noun; *to*-infinitive
**Form:** -ed from *configure*

Set up with particular settings so that it works a particular way.

1. The laptop comes *configured* for the school network.
2. A badly *configured* firewall leaves the whole office exposed.
3. The alarm is *configured* to text me when the door opens.

---

### confined

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈfaɪnd/ &middot; *kuhn-FYND*
**Русский:** ограниченный, тесный; прикованный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more confined*, *most confined*
**Takes:** *to* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *confine*

Confined has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Of a space, small and enclosed.** (тесный, замкнутый)

1. Don't run the generator in a *confined* space.
2. The cabin felt even more *confined* with four people.

**2. Existing only in a certain place, group or time.** (ограниченный (чем-то))

3. The damage was *confined* to the kitchen.
4. The problem isn't *confined* to big cities.

**3. Forced to stay in a place.** (прикованный, вынужденный оставаться)

5. She was *confined* to bed for a week with the flu.
6. He has been *confined* to a wheelchair since the accident.

---

### confining

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈfaɪnɪŋ/ &middot; *kuhn-FY-ning*
**Русский:** стесняющий, ограничивающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more confining*, *most confining*
**Form:** -ing — what limits you

Limiting your freedom to move or act.

1. He found office life too *confining*.
2. The dress was pretty but *confining*.
3. Small-town life felt more *confining* as she got older.

---

### confirmed

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈfɜːrmd/ &middot; *kuhn-FURMD*
**Русский:** подтверждённый; закоренелый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — the 'habitual' sense only before a noun: *a confirmed bachelor*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *confirm*

Confirmed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Shown or officially stated to be true or definite.** (подтверждённый)

1. There are now twelve *confirmed* cases in the county.
2. Your reservation is *confirmed* for Friday at seven.
3. We have no *confirmed* reports of injuries.

**2. Firmly fixed in a habit or way of life and unlikely to change.** (закоренелый, убеждённый)

4. He's a *confirmed* bachelor who likes living alone.
5. I'm a *confirmed* night owl, so don't call me before ten.

---

### conflicting

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈflɪktɪŋ/ &middot; *kuhn-FLIK-ting*
**Русский:** противоречивый, противоречащий друг другу
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *conflict*

Different from each other and impossible to agree or be true at the same time.

1. We got *conflicting* reports about what happened.
2. The advice from the two doctors was *conflicting*.
3. She had *conflicting* feelings about moving away.

---

### conformist

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈfɔːrmɪst/ &middot; *kuhn-FOR-mist*
**Русский:** конформистский
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more conformist*, *most conformist*
**Form:** -ist from *conform*
**Opposite:** *nonconformist*

Following the usual rules and behaving the way most people expect.

1. The school had a very *conformist* culture.
2. His parents were more *conformist* than he was.
3. She rejected the *conformist* life of the suburbs.

---

### confounding

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈfaʊndɪŋ/ &middot; *kuhn-FOWN-ding*
**Русский:** озадачивающий; искажающий (о факторе)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — the research sense only before a noun: *a confounding variable*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more confounding*, *most confounding*
**Form:** -ing from *confound*

Confounding has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Confusing and hard to understand or explain.** (озадачивающий, непонятный)

1. The disappearance remains one of the most *confounding* mysteries of the decade.
2. The new tax rules are *confounding* even to accountants.

**2. In research, affecting the result in a way that hides the real cause.** (искажающий, вмешивающийся (о факторе))

3. Age was a *confounding* factor in the study.
4. The researchers tried to control for *confounding* variables.

---

### confrontational

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɑːnfrənˈteɪʃənəl/ &middot; *kahn-fruhn-TAY-shuh-nuhl*
**Русский:** конфронтационный, вызывающий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more confrontational*, *most confrontational*
**Form:** -al from *confrontation*

Tending to argue with or challenge people in an aggressive way.

1. He has a very *confrontational* style of management.
2. Try not to be so *confrontational* when you talk to her.
3. The second debate was more *confrontational* than the first.

---

### Confucian

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈfjuːʃən/ &middot; *kuhn-FYOO-shuhn*
**Русский:** конфуцианский
**Type:** origin — religion and philosophy
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -an from *Confucius*

Relating to Confucius or the ideas he taught about family, duty and social order.

1. Respect for elders is a central *Confucian* value.
2. The book traces *Confucian* influence on Korean society.
3. Her upbringing was strict and deeply *Confucian*.

---

### congealed

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈdʒiːld/ &middot; *kuhn-JEELD*
**Русский:** застывший, загустевший
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
