**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *congeal*

Changed from a liquid into a thick, almost solid state, usually by cooling.

1. There was *congealed* gravy on the plates.
2. By morning, the grease in the pan was *congealed*.
3. He wiped the *congealed* blood from the cut.

---

### congenial

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈdʒiːniəl/ &middot; *kuhn-JEE-nee-uhl*
**Русский:** приятный, располагающий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more congenial*, *most congenial*
**Takes:** *to* + noun — suited to someone's taste or needs
**Opposite:** *uncongenial*
**Contrast:** A false friend: Russian *конгениальный* means 'equal in talent or spirit' (*конгениальный перевод*). English *congenial* just means pleasant and friendly.

Pleasant and friendly, or suited to your tastes.

1. Our host was *congenial* and made everyone feel at home.
2. The office has a *congenial* atmosphere.
3. She found the small town more *congenial* than the city.

---

### congenital

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈdʒɛnɪtəl/ &middot; *kuhn-JEN-ih-tuhl*
**Русский:** врождённый; прирождённый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — the 'by nature' sense only before a noun: *a congenital liar*
**Comparison:** not gradable

Congenital has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Present from birth.** (врождённый)

1. The baby was born with a *congenital* heart defect.
2. Doctors said the condition was *congenital*.

**2. Having a bad habit or quality so strongly that it seems part of your nature.** (прирождённый, неисправимый)

3. He's a *congenital* liar.
4. She has a *congenital* inability to be on time.

---

### congested

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈdʒɛstɪd/ &middot; *kuhn-JES-tid*
**Русский:** перегруженный; заложенный (о носе)
**Type:** participial; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more congested*, *most congested*
**Form:** -ed from *congest*

Congested has 2 distinct senses.

**1. So full of traffic or people that movement is difficult.** (перегруженный, забитый (транспортом))

1. The freeway is always *congested* at rush hour.
2. Los Angeles has some of the most *congested* roads in the country.
3. We avoided the *congested* downtown streets.

**2. Blocked with mucus, so that breathing is hard.** (заложенный (о носе, груди))

4. My nose is so *congested* I can't smell anything.
5. The medicine helps clear a *congested* chest.

---

### congestive

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈdʒɛstɪv/ &middot; *kuhn-JES-tiv*
**Русский:** застойный
**Type:** classifying — medical
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

In medicine, caused by too much blood or fluid collecting in part of the body.

1. His grandfather died of *congestive* heart failure.
2. Swollen ankles can be a sign of *congestive* heart failure.
3. She is being treated for a *congestive* condition.

---

### Congolese

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɑːŋɡəˈliːz/ &middot; *kahng-guh-LEEZ*
**Русский:** конголезский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ese from *Congo*
**Contrast:** It covers both countries, the Democratic Republic of the Congo and the Republic of the Congo. It is also the noun for the people, and the plural does not change: *two Congolese*, *the Congolese*.

From or relating to the Congo, its people or its culture.

1. *Congolese* music is popular all over Africa.
2. The restaurant is run by a *Congolese* family.
3. Her husband is *Congolese*.

---

### congratulatory

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈɡrætʃələˌtɔːri/ &middot; *kuhn-GRACH-uh-luh-tor-ee*
**Русский:** поздравительный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ory from *congratulate*

Expressing congratulations or praise.

1. The coach got a *congratulatory* call from the governor.
2. She received dozens of *congratulatory* messages.
3. The tone of the email was warm and *congratulatory*.

---

### congressional

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈɡrɛʃənəl/ &middot; *kuhn-GRESH-uh-nuhl*
**Русский:** относящийся к Конгрессу
**Type:** classifying — politics
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *congress*

Relating to the US Congress.

1. She's running for a *congressional* seat in Ohio.
2. The CEO testified at a *congressional* hearing.
3. Our *congressional* district was redrawn last year.

---

### congruent

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈɡruːənt/ &middot; *kuhn-GROO-uhnt*
**Русский:** согласующийся; конгруэнтный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — but *more congruent* in the 'in agreement' sense
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Preposition patterns:** [congruent to](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#congruent-to-congruent-with) &middot; [congruent with](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#congruent-to-congruent-with)
**Opposite:** *incongruent*

Congruent has 2 distinct senses.

**1. In agreement or fitting well with something.** (согласующийся, соответствующий)

1. His actions are not *congruent* with his words.
2. We want goals that are *congruent* with our values.

**2. In geometry, having exactly the same size and shape.** (конгруэнтный, равный)

3. The two triangles are *congruent*.
4. Prove that the angles are *congruent*.

---

### conical

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːnɪkəl/ &middot; *KAH-nih-kuhl*
**Русский:** конический, конусообразный
**Type:** shape
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ical from *cone*

Shaped like a cone.

1. The workers wore *conical* straw hats.
2. The volcano is almost perfectly *conical*.
3. Pour the batter into a *conical* bag.

---

### coniferous

**Pronunciation:** /koʊˈnɪfərəs/ &middot; *koh-NIF-ur-uhs*
**Русский:** хвойный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *deciduous*

Of trees, having cones and needles that usually stay green all year.

1. Pines and spruces are *coniferous* trees.
2. The mountains are covered in *coniferous* forest.
3. Most of the trees in the park are *coniferous*.

---

### conjoined

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈdʒɔɪnd/ &middot; *kuhn-JOYND*
**Русский:** сросшийся, соединённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *conjoin*
**Contrast:** The usual modern term is *conjoined twins*; the older *Siamese twins* is now avoided.

Joined together, especially of twins born physically connected.

1. Surgeons separated the *conjoined* twins in a twelve-hour operation.
2. The girls were *conjoined* at the hip.
3. The two buildings are *conjoined* by a glass walkway.

---

### conjugal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːndʒəɡəl/ &middot; *KAHN-juh-guhl*
**Русский:** супружеский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Relating to marriage or to the relationship between a husband and wife.

1. Some prisons allow *conjugal* visits.
2. The couple lived in *conjugal* harmony for fifty years.
3. The law once gave husbands *conjugal* rights over their wives.

---

### connected

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈnɛktɪd/ &middot; *kuh-NEK-tid*
**Русский:** связанный; подключённый; со связями
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more connected*, *most connected*
**Takes:** *to* or *with* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *connect*
**Opposite:** *unconnected*; *disconnected*

Connected has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Related to something else in some way.** (связанный)

1. Police believe the two robberies are *connected*.
2. She's had several jobs *connected* with the film industry.
3. Stress and sleep are closely *connected*.

**2. Joined physically, or linked to a network such as the internet.** (соединённый, подключённый)

4. Make sure the printer is *connected* to your laptop.
5. The kitchen is *connected* to the garage by a short hallway.
6. Video calls help grandparents stay *connected* with their grandkids.

**3. Knowing important or powerful people.** (со связями)

7. Her father is very well *connected* in Washington.
8. He's one of the most *connected* lobbyists in the state.

---

### conniving

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈnaɪvɪŋ/ &middot; *kuh-NY-ving*
**Русский:** коварный, интриганский
**Type:** evaluative; also participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more conniving*, *most conniving*
**Form:** -ing from *connive*

Secretly planning to do something harmful or dishonest.

1. The show's villain is a *conniving* stepmother.
2. I didn't realize how *conniving* he could be.
3. She's the most *conniving* person I've ever worked with.

---

### conscientious

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɑːnʃiˈɛnʃəs/ &middot; *kahn-shee-EN-shuhs*
**Русский:** добросовестный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more conscientious*, *most conscientious*
**Contrast:** Do not confuse with *conscious* (aware, awake). A *conscientious objector* refuses military service on moral grounds.

Careful to do your work or duty well and thoroughly.

1. She's the most *conscientious* employee on the team.
2. He's very *conscientious* about checking his work.
3. *Conscientious* drivers slow down near schools.

---

### conscious

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːnʃəs/ &middot; *KAHN-shuhs*
**Русский:** в сознании; осознающий; сознательный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — the 'deliberate' sense mostly before a noun: *a conscious decision*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more conscious*, *most conscious*, in the 'aware' sense
**Takes:** *of* + noun or *-ing*; *that*-clause
**Preposition patterns:** [conscious of](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#conscious-of)
**Opposite:** *unconscious*
**Contrast:** Russian *сознательный* about a responsible person is not *conscious*: say *responsible* or *socially aware*. *Self-conscious* means shy and embarrassed, not *сознательный*. Do not confuse with *conscientious*.

Conscious has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Awake and able to see, hear and think.** (в сознании)

1. The driver was *conscious* when the paramedics arrived.
2. He was barely *conscious* after the fall.

**2. Knowing about something or noticing it.** (осознающий, сознающий)

3. I was *conscious* of people staring at me.
4. People are more *conscious* of their health than they used to be.
5. She's very *conscious* that time is running out.

**3. Done on purpose, with full awareness.** (сознательный, намеренный)

6. We made a *conscious* decision not to have a TV.
7. It takes a *conscious* effort to slow down.

---

### consecrated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːnsəˌkreɪtɪd/ &middot; *KAHN-suh-kray-tid*
**Русский:** освящённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *consecrate*

Officially made holy in a religious ceremony.

1. The soldiers are buried in *consecrated* ground.
2. The bread and wine are *consecrated* during the service.
3. The priest placed the *consecrated* host on the altar.

---

### consecutive

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈsɛkjətɪv/ &middot; *kuhn-SEK-yuh-tiv*
**Русский:** последовательный, идущий подряд
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Russian *последовательный* also means 'logical, not changing your views'; that is *consistent*, not *consecutive*. *Consecutive* only means one after another: *three consecutive wins* = *три победы подряд*.

Following one after another without a break.

1. It rained for five *consecutive* days.
2. The team won its third *consecutive* championship.
3. Make sure the page numbers are *consecutive*.

---

### consensual

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈsɛnʃuəl/ &middot; *kuhn-SEN-shoo-uhl*
**Русский:** добровольный, по обоюдному согласию
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *consensus*

Done with the agreement of everyone involved.

1. Both sides said the relationship was *consensual*.
2. The officer said the search of the car was *consensual*.
3. The group prefers a *consensual* approach to decisions.

---

### consenting

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈsɛntɪŋ/ &middot; *kuhn-SEN-ting*
**Русский:** дающий согласие
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *consent*

Agreeing freely to take part in something.

1. What *consenting* adults do in private is their own business.
2. Only *consenting* patients were included in the study.
3. The law applies to relationships between *consenting* adults.

---

### consequential

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɑːnsəˈkwɛnʃəl/ &middot; *kahn-suh-KWEN-shuhl*
**Русский:** важный, значимый; косвенный
**Type:** evaluative; also classifying
**Position:** both — the 'resulting' sense only before a noun: *consequential damages*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more consequential*, *most consequential*
**Opposite:** *inconsequential*

Consequential has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Important, with serious effects.** (важный, значимый, судьбоносный)

1. This could be the most *consequential* election of our lifetime.
2. The decision turned out to be very *consequential*.

**2. Happening as a result of something else.** (косвенный, вытекающий из чего-либо)

3. The contract does not cover *consequential* damages.
4. The flood caused a *consequential* loss of business.

---

### conservative

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈsɜːrvətɪv/ &middot; *kuhn-SUR-vuh-tiv*
**Русский:** консервативный; сдержанный; осторожный
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more conservative*, *most conservative*
**Opposite:** *liberal*; *progressive*
**Contrast:** In US politics *conservative* means on the right, usually closer to the Republican Party. A *conservative estimate* is deliberately low, not old-fashioned.

Conservative has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Holding traditional political or social views and wanting little change.** (консервативный)

1. She grew up in a *conservative* town in Texas.
2. Rural voters tend to be more *conservative* than city voters.
3. He writes for a *conservative* magazine.

**2. Plain and traditional in style, not showy.** (консервативный, строгий, сдержанный)

4. Wear a *conservative* suit to the interview.
5. Her taste in furniture is pretty *conservative*.

**3. Of a guess or estimate, deliberately low or cautious.** (осторожный, заниженный)

6. At a *conservative* estimate, the repairs will cost $5,000.
7. The company's forecast was *conservative*.

---

### considerable

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈsɪdərəbəl/ &middot; *kuhn-SID-ur-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** значительный
**Type:** quantity
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -able from *consider*
**Contrast:** Do not confuse with *considerate* (thoughtful toward others). *A considerable person* is not a thing; say *a considerate person*.

Fairly large in size, amount or degree.

1. The project took a *considerable* amount of time.
2. The storm caused *considerable* damage to the roof.
3. The cost of moving was *considerable*.

---

### considerate

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈsɪdərət/ &middot; *kuhn-SID-ur-uht*
**Русский:** внимательный, заботливый, тактичный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more considerate*, *most considerate*
**Takes:** *of* + person (*it was considerate of you*); *to* or *toward* + person
**Opposite:** *inconsiderate*
**Contrast:** Do not confuse with *considerable* (large in amount).

Thinking about other people's feelings and needs.

1. It was very *considerate* of you to call ahead.
2. Our neighbors are quiet and *considerate*.
3. You could be a little more *considerate* toward your sister.

---

### consistent

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈsɪstənt/ &middot; *kuhn-SIS-tuhnt*
**Русский:** стабильный, последовательный; согласующийся
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more consistent*, *most consistent*
**Takes:** *with* + noun — in agreement with; *in* + noun or *-ing* — steady in
**Opposite:** *inconsistent*
**Contrast:** *Consistent* means 'always the same' or 'in agreement'; it has nothing to do with *consist of*. Russian *консистенция* is *texture* or *consistency* (a noun).

Consistent has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Always behaving or happening in the same way, or keeping the same quality.** (стабильный, последовательный, постоянный)

1. He's the most *consistent* player on the team.
2. You need to be *consistent* with the rules you set for kids.
3. The restaurant's food is good and *consistent*.

**2. In agreement with other facts or ideas.** (согласующийся, совместимый)

4. The injuries are *consistent* with a fall.
5. His story wasn't *consistent* with the evidence.

---

### consolidated

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈsɑːləˌdeɪtɪd/ &middot; *kuhn-SAH-luh-day-tid*
**Русский:** объединённый, консолидированный
**Type:** participial; also classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *consolidate*

Formed by joining several things into one.

1. The company publishes *consolidated* financial statements.
2. The kids attend a *consolidated* school district.
3. We paid off our credit cards with a *consolidated* loan.

---

### conspicuous

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈspɪkjuəs/ &middot; *kuhn-SPIK-yoo-uhs*
**Русский:** заметный, бросающийся в глаза
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more conspicuous*, *most conspicuous*
**Opposite:** *inconspicuous*

Very easy to see or notice.

1. Put the sign in a *conspicuous* place.
2. In his orange jacket, he was more *conspicuous* than he wanted to be.
3. The mayor was *conspicuous* by his absence.

---

### conspiratorial

**Pronunciation:** /kənˌspɪrəˈtɔːriəl/ &middot; *kuhn-spir-uh-TOR-ee-uhl*
**Русский:** заговорщицкий; конспирологический
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more conspiratorial*, *most conspiratorial*
**Form:** -al from *conspirator*

Conspiratorial has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Suggesting that people share a secret.** (заговорщицкий)

1. She gave me a *conspiratorial* wink.
2. He lowered his voice to a *conspiratorial* whisper.

**2. Relating to a secret plot, or believing that one exists.** (конспирологический, связанный с заговором)

3. The forum is full of *conspiratorial* thinking.
4. His explanations grew more *conspiratorial* every year.

---

### constant

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːnstənt/ &middot; *KAHN-stuhnt*
**Русский:** постоянный; неизменный; верный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — the 'loyal' sense only before a noun: *a constant companion*
**Comparison:** not gradable

Constant has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Happening all the time or again and again.** (постоянный, непрекращающийся)

1. I'm tired of the *constant* noise from the construction site.
2. The baby needs *constant* attention.
3. Their arguments were *constant*.

**2. Staying at the same level or rate.** (неизменный, постоянный)

4. Keep the oven at a *constant* temperature.
5. The car was moving at a *constant* speed.

**3. Always there and loyal.** (верный, неизменный)

6. The dog was his *constant* companion.
7. Her mother was a *constant* source of support.

---

### constipated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːnstəˌpeɪtɪd/ &middot; *KAHN-stuh-pay-tid*
**Русский:** страдающий запором
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more constipated*, *most constipated*

Unable to empty your bowels easily.

1. Drink more water if you're *constipated*.
2. This medication can make you *constipated*.
3. The vet said the *constipated* cat needed more fiber.

---

### constitutional

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɑːnstəˈtuːʃənəl/ &middot; *kahn-stuh-TOO-shuh-nuhl*
**Русский:** конституционный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *constitution*
**Opposite:** *unconstitutional*

Relating to a constitution, or allowed by it.

1. Free speech is a *constitutional* right.
2. The Supreme Court will decide whether the law is *constitutional*.
3. She teaches *constitutional* law at Georgetown.

---

### constrained

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈstreɪnd/ &middot; *kuhn-STRAYND*
**Русский:** ограниченный; скованный, натянутый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more constrained*, *most constrained*
**Takes:** *by* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *constrain*

Constrained has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Limited by rules, money or circumstances.** (ограниченный, стеснённый)

1. Schools are *constrained* by tight budgets.
2. We're working in a very *constrained* time frame.

**2. Not natural or relaxed.** (скованный, натянутый)

3. He gave a *constrained* smile.
4. The conversation felt more *constrained* after the argument.

---

### constricting

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈstrɪktɪŋ/ &middot; *kuhn-STRIK-ting*
**Русский:** стесняющий, сковывающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more constricting*, *most constricting*
**Form:** -ing from *constrict*

Too tight, or limiting what you can do.

1. I can't run in these *constricting* jeans.
2. She found office life *constricting*.
3. The old rules were even more *constricting*.

---

### constructive

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈstrʌktɪv/ &middot; *kuhn-STRUHK-tiv*
**Русский:** конструктивный, полезный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more constructive*, *most constructive*
**Opposite:** *destructive*

Helpful and meant to improve things.

1. Thanks for the *constructive* criticism.
2. The meeting was short but *constructive*.
3. Let's find a more *constructive* way to deal with this.

---

### consular

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːnsələr/ &middot; *KAHN-suh-lur*
**Русский:** консульский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ar from *consul*

Relating to a consul or a consulate.

1. The embassy offers *consular* services to citizens abroad.
2. He asked to speak to a *consular* officer.
3. You'll need to schedule a *consular* interview for your visa.

---

### consultative

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈsʌltətɪv/ &middot; *kuhn-SUHL-tuh-tiv*
**Русский:** консультативный, совещательный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ive from *consult*

Giving advice rather than making decisions.

1. The committee has a purely *consultative* role.
2. She takes a *consultative* approach with her clients.
3. The council's function is *consultative*, not legislative.

---

### consumable

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈsuːməbəl/ &middot; *kuhn-SOO-muh-buhl*
**Русский:** расходный; потребляемый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -able from *consume*
**Contrast:** The plural noun *consumables* means supplies that get used up, such as ink or paper.

Meant to be used up and then replaced, or able to be eaten or used.

1. The budget covers *consumable* supplies like paper and ink.
2. Filters are *consumable* parts that need replacing.
3. Only *consumable* items were allowed in the care package.

---

### consummate

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːnsəmət/ &middot; *KAHN-suh-muht*
**Русский:** непревзойдённый, совершенный
**Type:** evaluative; also emphasizing
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** The verb *consummate* ends in /eɪt/: KAHN-suh-mayt. The adjective ends in a weak /ət/.

Extremely skilled; perfect in a particular role.

1. She's a *consummate* professional.
2. He played the piece with *consummate* skill.
3. The senator was a *consummate* politician.

---

### contagious

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈteɪdʒəs/ &middot; *kuhn-TAY-juhs*
**Русский:** заразный; заразительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more contagious*, *most contagious*

Contagious has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a disease or person, able to pass illness to others by contact.** (заразный, инфекционный)

1. Measles is one of the most *contagious* diseases.
2. Stay home while you're still *contagious*.

**2. Of a feeling or behavior, quickly spreading to other people.** (заразительный)

3. She has a *contagious* laugh.
4. His enthusiasm was *contagious*.

---

### contaminated

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈtæməˌneɪtɪd/ &middot; *kuhn-TAM-uh-nay-tid*
**Русский:** загрязнённый, заражённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more contaminated*, *most contaminated*
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *contaminate*

Made dirty or harmful by something such as chemicals, bacteria or radiation.

1. Residents were told not to drink the *contaminated* water.
2. The lettuce was *contaminated* with E. coli.
3. It's one of the most *contaminated* sites in the country.

---

### contemplative

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈtɛmplətɪv/ &middot; *kuhn-TEM-pluh-tiv*
**Русский:** созерцательный, задумчивый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more contemplative*, *most contemplative*
**Form:** -ive from *contemplate*

Quiet and thoughtful, or spent in deep thought or prayer.

1. He was in a *contemplative* mood after the funeral.
2. The monks lead a *contemplative* life.
3. Her later paintings are quieter and more *contemplative*.

---

### contemporaneous

**Pronunciation:** /kənˌtɛmpəˈreɪniəs/ &middot; *kuhn-tem-puh-RAY-nee-uhs*
**Русский:** одновременный, современный той эпохе
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Preposition patterns:** [contemporaneous with](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#contemporaneous-with)

Existing or happening at the same time as something else.

1. The detective relied on *contemporaneous* notes.
2. The two civilizations were roughly *contemporaneous*.
3. His career was *contemporaneous* with the rise of television.

---

### contemporary

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈtɛmpəˌrɛri/ &middot; *kuhn-TEM-puh-rer-ee*
**Русский:** современный
**Type:** age; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — but *more contemporary* of style and design
**Takes:** *with* + noun — in the 'same period' sense
**Contrast:** *Contemporary* can mean 'of today' or 'of that time': *contemporary accounts of the Civil War* were written during the war, not recently.

Contemporary has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Belonging to the present time; modern.** (современный)

1. The museum focuses on *contemporary* art.
2. The house has a clean, *contemporary* design.
3. Her music is very *contemporary*.

**2. Living or happening at the same time as a particular person or event in the past.** (современный той эпохе, того времени)

4. *Contemporary* newspapers described the fire in detail.
5. Shakespeare was *contemporary* with Cervantes.

---

### contemptible

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈtɛmptəbəl/ &middot; *kuhn-TEMP-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** презренный, достойный презрения
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more contemptible*, *most contemptible*
**Form:** -ible from *contempt*
**Contrast:** *Contemptible* describes what deserves contempt; *contemptuous* describes the person who feels it. *His lie was contemptible; she was contemptuous of him.*

So bad or dishonest that it deserves no respect.

1. Stealing from a charity is *contemptible*.
2. It was the most *contemptible* thing he'd ever done.
3. She called the ad a *contemptible* lie.

---

### contemptuous

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈtɛmptʃuəs/ &middot; *kuhn-TEMP-choo-uhs*
**Русский:** презрительный
**Type:** emotion; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more contemptuous*, *most contemptuous*
**Takes:** *of* + noun
**Form:** -uous from *contempt*
**Contrast:** *Contemptuous* is the person or look that shows contempt; *contemptible* is what deserves it.

Showing that you think someone or something is worthless.

1. She gave him a *contemptuous* look.
2. He was openly *contemptuous* of the new rules.
3. The critics were even more *contemptuous* of the sequel.

---

### contented

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈtɛntɪd/ &middot; *kuhn-TEN-tid*
**Русский:** довольный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more contented*, *most contented*
**Opposite:** *discontented*
**Contrast:** *Content* means the same but normally comes only after a verb (*I'm content*); *contented* also goes before a noun (*a contented sigh*).

Happy and satisfied with what you have.

1. The baby gave a *contented* sigh and fell asleep.
2. They seem *contented* with their quiet life.
3. I've never seen him more *contented*.

---

### contentious

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈtɛnʃəs/ &middot; *kuhn-TEN-shuhs*
**Русский:** спорный; ожесточённый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more contentious*, *most contentious*

Contentious has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Likely to cause disagreement.** (спорный, вызывающий разногласия)

1. Immigration remains a *contentious* issue.
2. The new stadium plan is highly *contentious*.

**2. Full of arguments, or tending to argue.** (ожесточённый, конфликтный)

3. They went through a long, *contentious* divorce.
4. It was the most *contentious* board meeting in years.

---

### contested

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈtɛstɪd/ &middot; *kuhn-TES-tid*
**Русский:** оспариваемый, спорный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more contested*, *most contested*
**Form:** -ed from *contest*

Argued about, or competed for by two or more sides.

1. It was one of the most hotly *contested* elections in state history.
2. The region is still *contested* territory.
3. The facts of the case are not *contested*.

---

### contextual

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈtɛkstʃuəl/ &middot; *kuhn-TEKS-choo-uhl*
**Русский:** контекстный, контекстуальный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *context*

Relating to or depending on the situation something is in.

1. Use *contextual* clues to guess the meaning of a new word.
2. The ads are based on *contextual* information.
3. Whether the joke is offensive is partly *contextual*.

---

### contiguous

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈtɪɡjuəs/ &middot; *kuhn-TIG-yoo-uhs*
**Русский:** смежный, прилегающий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* or *to* + noun
**Contrast:** *The contiguous United States* means the 48 states that border each other, without Alaska and Hawaii.

Touching or next to each other, with no gap.

1. Shipping is free within the *contiguous* United States.
2. The two properties are *contiguous*.
3. The park is *contiguous* with a national forest.

---

### continental

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɑːntəˈnɛntəl/ &middot; *kahn-tuh-NEN-tuhl*
**Русский:** континентальный; европейский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *continent*
**Contrast:** A *continental breakfast* at an American hotel is a light one: bread, pastries, coffee and juice, with no hot food.

Continental has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to a continent, or to the main land of a continent.** (континентальный, материковый)

1. The offer is valid in the *continental* United States.
2. The region has a *continental* climate, with hot summers and cold winters.

**2. Relating to mainland Europe, especially in style.** (европейский (в стиле континентальной Европы))

3. The hotel serves a free *continental* breakfast.
4. The restaurant has a *continental* menu.

---

### contingent

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈtɪndʒənt/ &middot; *kuhn-TIN-juhnt*
**Русский:** зависящий (от); временный, внештатный
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both — the 'depending' sense after a verb, the 'temporary' sense before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *on* or *upon* + noun
**Preposition patterns:** [contingent on](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#contingent-on-contingent-upon) &middot; [contingent upon](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#contingent-on-contingent-upon)
**Contrast:** The noun *a contingent* is a group of people (*a large Canadian contingent*), close to Russian *контингент* in the sense of a group sent somewhere.

Contingent has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Depending on something else happening.** (зависящий от, обусловленный)

1. The job offer is *contingent* on a background check.
2. Our plans are *contingent* upon the weather.

**2. Of workers, hired only for a limited time or for particular tasks.** (временный, внештатный)

3. Many colleges rely on *contingent* faculty.
4. *Contingent* workers often get no health benefits.

---

### continued

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈtɪnjuːd/ &middot; *kuhn-TIN-yood*
**Русский:** продолжающийся, неослабевающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *continue*
**Contrast:** After a verb it is the verb: *To be continued*, *The story is continued on page 6*.

Going on without stopping, or happening again.

1. Thank you for your *continued* support.
2. The *continued* rain caused flooding downtown.
3. His *continued* absence worried his boss.

---

### continuing

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈtɪnjuɪŋ/ &middot; *kuhn-TIN-yoo-ing*
**Русский:** продолжающийся, непрекращающийся
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *continue*

Still happening or existing.

1. The town is dealing with a *continuing* water shortage.
2. Nurses must take *continuing* education courses.
3. It's a *continuing* problem for small businesses.

---

### contorted

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈtɔːrtɪd/ &middot; *kuhn-TOR-tid*
**Русский:** искажённый, искривлённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more contorted*, *most contorted*
**Takes:** *with* + noun — *contorted with pain*
**Form:** -ed from *contort*

Twisted out of its normal shape, or too complicated to follow easily.

1. His face was *contorted* with pain.
2. The yoga teacher held a *contorted* pose.
3. The lawyer's arguments grew more *contorted* as the trial went on.

---

### contraceptive

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɑːntrəˈsɛptɪv/ &middot; *kahn-truh-SEP-tiv*
**Русский:** противозачаточный
**Type:** classifying — medical
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** It is also a noun: *a contraceptive* is a pill or device that prevents pregnancy.

Used to prevent a woman from becoming pregnant.

1. The clinic offers free *contraceptive* pills.
2. Her insurance covers most *contraceptive* methods.
3. The study looked at *contraceptive* use among college students.

---

### contractual

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈtræktʃuəl/ &middot; *kuhn-TRAK-choo-uhl*
**Русский:** договорный, контрактный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *contract*

Stated in or required by a contract.

1. The company has a *contractual* obligation to finish by June.
2. He's in a *contractual* dispute with his record label.
3. The bonus is *contractual*, so they have to pay it.

---

### contradictory

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɑːntrəˈdɪktəri/ &middot; *kahn-truh-DIK-tuh-ree*
**Русский:** противоречивый, противоречащий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more contradictory*, *most contradictory*
**Form:** -ory from *contradict*
**Contrast:** *Contradictory* means the parts say opposite things. For a *противоречивый* person or film that people argue about, say *controversial*.

Containing ideas or statements that cannot all be true.

1. The witnesses gave *contradictory* accounts.
2. The instructions are confusing and *contradictory*.
3. His second statement was even more *contradictory* than the first.

---

### contrary

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːntrɛri/ &middot; *KAHN-trer-ee*
**Русский:** противоположный; упрямый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — but *more contrary* in the 'stubborn' sense
**Takes:** *to* + noun
**Contrast:** *On the contrary* is a fixed phrase that rejects what was just said. In the 'stubborn' sense some speakers stress the second syllable: kuhn-TRER-ee.

Contrary has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Completely different or opposite.** (противоположный, противный (чему-либо))

1. Some experts hold the *contrary* view.
2. *Contrary* to popular belief, bats aren't blind.
3. The results were *contrary* to what we expected.

**2. Deliberately doing or saying the opposite of what others want.** (упрямый, своенравный)

4. He's being *contrary* just to annoy you.
5. My toddler is at a very *contrary* age.

---

### contrasting

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈtræstɪŋ/ &middot; *kuhn-TRAS-ting*
**Русский:** контрастный, противоположный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *contrast*

Very different from each other in a noticeable way.

1. The jacket has *contrasting* stitching.
2. The two candidates offered sharply *contrasting* visions.
3. Paint the trim a *contrasting* color.

---

### contributing

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈtrɪbjətɪŋ/ &middot; *kuhn-TRIB-yuh-ting*
**Русский:** способствующий; внештатный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *contribute*

Contributing has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Helping to cause something.** (способствующий)

1. Fatigue was a *contributing* factor in the crash.
2. Stress can be a *contributing* cause of heart disease.

**2. Writing for a magazine or newspaper regularly without being on its staff.** (внештатный)

3. She's a *contributing* editor at a travel magazine.
4. He became a *contributing* writer for the sports section.

---

### contrite

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈtraɪt/ &middot; *kuhn-TRYT*
**Русский:** раскаивающийся
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more contrite*, *most contrite*

Feeling or showing sorrow for something wrong you have done.

1. He gave a *contrite* apology.
2. The senator looked *contrite* as he spoke to reporters.
3. She seemed more *contrite* the second time.

---

### contrived

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈtraɪvd/ &middot; *kuhn-TRYVD*
**Русский:** надуманный, искусственный
**Type:** evaluative; also participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more contrived*, *most contrived*
**Form:** -ed from *contrive*

Obviously planned or invented, and not natural or believable.

1. The movie's ending felt *contrived*.
2. It was a *contrived* excuse to see her again.
3. The dialogue gets more *contrived* in the second season.

---

### controllable

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈtroʊləbəl/ &middot; *kuhn-TROH-luh-buhl*
**Русский:** управляемый, контролируемый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more controllable*, *most controllable*
**Form:** -able from *control*
**Opposite:** *uncontrollable*

Able to be controlled or managed.

1. Focus on the *controllable* parts of the problem.
2. With medication, her symptoms are *controllable*.
3. The new drone is lighter and more *controllable*.

---

### controlled

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈtroʊld/ &middot; *kuhn-TROHLD*
**Русский:** сдержанный; контролируемый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — the 'regulated' sense mostly before a noun: *a controlled substance*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more controlled*, *most controlled*, in the 'calm' sense
**Form:** -ed from *control*

Controlled has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Calm and not showing strong emotion.** (сдержанный, спокойный)

1. She spoke in a slow, *controlled* voice.
2. His second performance was more *controlled*.

**2. Limited or regulated by rules or laws, or carefully managed.** (контролируемый, регулируемый)

3. He was arrested for possession of a *controlled* substance.
4. The fire department started a *controlled* burn.
5. The scientists ran a *controlled* experiment.

---

### controlling

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈtroʊlɪŋ/ &middot; *kuhn-TROH-ling*
**Русский:** властный, контролирующий; контрольный
**Type:** participial; also quality
**Position:** both — the 'ownership' sense only before a noun: *a controlling interest*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more controlling*, *most controlling*, in the 'bossy' sense
**Form:** -ing from *control*

Controlling has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Trying too hard to control other people's lives.** (властный, контролирующий)

1. Her ex-boyfriend was jealous and *controlling*.
2. My mother became even more *controlling* after my dad left.

**2. Having the power to make decisions for a company or group.** (контрольный)

3. The family still owns a *controlling* interest in the company.
4. He sold his *controlling* stake last year.

---

### controversial

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɑːntrəˈvɜːrʃəl/ &middot; *kahn-truh-VUR-shuhl*
**Русский:** спорный, неоднозначный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more controversial*, *most controversial*
**Form:** -al from *controversy*
**Opposite:** *uncontroversial*
**Contrast:** For statements that contradict each other, use *contradictory*, not *controversial*. *Controversial* means that people argue about it.

Causing a lot of public disagreement.

1. The decision to close the school was very *controversial*.
2. She's one of the most *controversial* figures in Hollywood.
3. He avoids *controversial* topics at dinner.

---

### convenient

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈviːniənt/ &middot; *kuhn-VEE-nee-uhnt*
**Русский:** удобный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more convenient*, *most convenient*
**Takes:** *for* + person or purpose; *to* + place — close to
**Opposite:** *inconvenient*
**Contrast:** *Мне удобно* is *It's convenient for me*, not ✗ *I'm convenient*. For a comfortable chair or shoes, use *comfortable*.

Convenient has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Easy to use or suitable for your plans, so that it saves time or trouble.** (удобный)

1. Is Tuesday *convenient* for you?
2. Online banking is much more *convenient* than going to a branch.
3. That's a very *convenient* excuse.

**2. Near and easy to get to.** (удобно расположенный)

4. The hotel is *convenient* to the airport.
5. We chose the apartment because it's *convenient* for work.

---

### conventional

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈvɛnʃənəl/ &middot; *kuhn-VEN-shuh-nuhl*
**Русский:** традиционный, обычный; неядерный
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both — the 'non-nuclear' sense only before a noun: *conventional weapons*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more conventional*, *most conventional*
**Form:** -al from *convention*
**Opposite:** *unconventional*

Conventional has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Traditional and ordinary, following what most people do or accept.** (традиционный, обычный, общепринятый)

1. *Conventional* wisdom says you should buy a house.
2. Her parents were more *conventional* than she was.
3. The treatment works better than *conventional* medicine.

**2. Of weapons or war, not nuclear.** (обычный, неядерный)

4. The army relies on *conventional* weapons.
5. Experts fear a *conventional* war could turn nuclear.

---

### conversant

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈvɜːrsənt/ &middot; *kuhn-VUR-suhnt*
**Русский:** сведущий, знакомый (с чем-либо)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** predicative only
**Comparison:** more/most — *more conversant*, *most conversant*
**Takes:** *with* or *in* + noun
**Preposition patterns:** [conversant with](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#conversant-with)
**Contrast:** Do not confuse with *conversational* (informal, like talk). In American use *conversant in Spanish* means you can hold a conversation in it.

Knowing about something, or able to use it or talk in it.

1. All staff must be *conversant* with safety procedures.
2. She's fluent in French and *conversant* in Italian.
3. Younger doctors tend to be more *conversant* with the new software.

---

### conversational

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɑːnvərˈseɪʃənəl/ &middot; *kahn-vur-SAY-shuh-nuhl*
**Русский:** разговорный
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more conversational*, *most conversational*
**Form:** -al from *conversation*

Informal and like ordinary talk, or used in conversation.

1. She writes in a relaxed, *conversational* style.
2. I speak *conversational* Spanish.
3. Try to sound more *conversational* in the podcast.

---

### convertible

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈvɜːrtəbəl/ &middot; *kuhn-VUR-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** трансформируемый; конвертируемый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ible from *convert*
**Contrast:** The noun *a convertible* is a car with a roof that folds down (*кабриолет*).

Convertible has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Able to be changed into a different form or used in a different way.** (трансформируемый, раскладной)

1. We bought a *convertible* sofa for the guest room.
2. The crib is *convertible* to a toddler bed.

**2. Of money or investments, able to be exchanged for something else.** (конвертируемый)

3. The currency isn't freely *convertible*.
4. The company issued *convertible* bonds.

---

### convex

**Pronunciation:** /kɑːnˈvɛks/ &middot; *kahn-VEKS*
**Русский:** выпуклый
**Type:** shape
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more convex*, *most convex*
**Opposite:** *concave*

Curving outward, like the outside of a ball.

1. The side mirror is *convex*, so cars look farther away.
2. Use a *convex* lens to make the image larger.
3. The new screen is slightly more *convex* than the old one.

---

### convinced

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈvɪnst/ &middot; *kuhn-VINST*
**Русский:** убеждённый, уверенный
**Type:** participial; also emotion
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more convinced*, *most convinced*
**Takes:** *that*-clause; *of* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *convince*
**Contrast:** The *-ed* form is the person who believes (*I'm convinced*); the *-ing* form is what makes you believe (*a convincing argument*).

Completely sure that something is true.

1. I'm *convinced* he's lying.
2. She wasn't *convinced* of his innocence.
3. The more I read, the more *convinced* I became.

---

### convincing

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈvɪnsɪŋ/ &middot; *kuhn-VIN-sing*
**Русский:** убедительный
**Type:** participial; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more convincing*, *most convincing*
**Form:** -ing from *convince*
**Opposite:** *unconvincing*

Making you believe that something is true, or clearly successful.

1. The lawyer made a *convincing* argument.
2. His excuse wasn't very *convincing*.
3. The Yankees won a *convincing* victory, 9 to 1.

---

### convivial

**Pronunciation:** /kənˈvɪviəl/ &middot; *kuhn-VIV-ee-uhl*
**Русский:** дружелюбный, весёлый, компанейский
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more convivial*, *most convivial*

Friendly, cheerful and good for enjoying company.

1. The bar has a warm, *convivial* atmosphere.
2. Our hosts were *convivial* and generous.
3. The second dinner was far more *convivial* than the first.

---

### cooked

**Pronunciation:** /kʊkt/ &middot; *KUUKT*
**Русский:** приготовленный, варёный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *cook*
**Opposite:** *raw*; *uncooked*

Heated and prepared so that it is ready to eat.

1. Store *cooked* rice in the fridge.
2. Is the chicken *cooked* all the way through?
3. Add the *cooked* pasta to the sauce.

---

### cookie-cutter

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkʊkiˌkʌtər/ &middot; *KUUK-ee-kuht-ur*
**Русский:** шаблонный, однотипный
**Type:** compound — noun + noun used as an adjective; also evaluative
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *cookie* + *cutter*, hyphenated
**Contrast:** The literal *cookie cutter* (no hyphen) is a noun, a metal or plastic shape for cutting dough.

Made to the same pattern as many others, with nothing original about it.

1. The neighborhood is full of *cookie-cutter* houses.
2. I'm tired of *cookie-cutter* superhero movies.
3. We don't offer *cookie-cutter* solutions.

---

### cool

**Pronunciation:** /kuːl/ &middot; *KOOL*
**Русский:** прохладный; спокойный; крутой
**Type:** quality; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *cooler*, *coolest*
**Takes:** *with* + noun — okay with (informal)
**Opposite:** *warm*
**Contrast:** *Cool* is not as cold as *cold*: *прохладный*, not *холодный*. In the 'great' sense it is very informal.

Cool has 5 distinct senses.

**1. Slightly cold, often in a pleasant way.** (прохладный)

1. It's *cooler* in the mountains.
2. Store the potatoes in a *cool*, dry place.
3. The evening air was *cool*.

**2. Calm and not nervous or excited.** (спокойный, невозмутимый)

4. She stayed *cool* under pressure.
5. He kept a *cool* head during the emergency.

**3. Informal: fashionable, attractive or very good.** (крутой, классный)

6. That's a really *cool* jacket.
7. He thinks he's the *coolest* guy in school.
8. Your new place is so *cool*.

**4. Informal: fine, acceptable, or not upset.** (нормальный, не против, в порядке)

9. I'm *cool* with ordering pizza.
10. Sorry about last night. Are we *cool*?

**5. Unfriendly or not enthusiastic.** (прохладный, холодный (об отношении))

11. The plan got a *cool* reception from voters.
12. She was polite but *cool* toward me.

---

### cooperative

**Pronunciation:** /koʊˈɑːpərətɪv/ &middot; *koh-AH-pur-uh-tiv*
**Русский:** готовый к сотрудничеству; совместный; кооперативный
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both — the 'owned by members' sense only before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cooperative*, *most cooperative*
**Form:** co- + *operative*
**Opposite:** *uncooperative*
**Contrast:** The noun *a cooperative* or *a co-op* is a business or apartment building owned by its members.

Cooperative has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Willing to help or do what people ask.** (готовый к сотрудничеству, отзывчивый)

1. The witness was very *cooperative* with police.
2. The kids were more *cooperative* after lunch.

**2. Done by people working together.** (совместный)

3. The project was a *cooperative* effort between two schools.
4. *Cooperative* games teach kids teamwork.

**3. Owned and run by the people who use it or work in it.** (кооперативный)

5. We buy our vegetables at a *cooperative* grocery store.
6. The farmers formed a *cooperative* dairy.

---

### coordinated

**Pronunciation:** /koʊˈɔːrdəˌneɪtɪd/ &middot; *koh-OR-duh-nay-tid*
**Русский:** скоординированный; ловкий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more coordinated*, *most coordinated*
**Form:** -ed from *coordinate*
**Opposite:** *uncoordinated*

Coordinated has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Organized so that different people or parts work together.** (скоординированный, согласованный)

1. Police said it was a *coordinated* attack.
2. We need a more *coordinated* response to the crisis.

**2. Able to control your body's movements well.** (ловкий, с хорошей координацией)

3. I'm not *coordinated* enough to dance.
4. She's the most *coordinated* kid on the soccer team.

**3. Of clothes or colors, chosen to look good together.** (подобранный в тон)

5. The bridesmaids wore *coordinated* dresses.
6. Their outfits were perfectly *coordinated*.

---

### copasetic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkoʊpəˈsɛtɪk/ &middot; *koh-puh-SET-ik*
**Русский:** в полном порядке, отличный (разг.)
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** predicative only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Informal and a little old-fashioned or jokey. Also spelled *copacetic*.

Informal: fine, satisfactory, with no problems.

1. Don't worry, everything's *copasetic*.
2. Things between us are *copasetic* now.
3. As long as the rent gets paid, we're *copasetic*.

---

### copious

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkoʊpiəs/ &middot; *KOH-pee-uhs*
**Русский:** обильный
**Type:** quantity
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more copious*, *most copious*

Large in amount; plentiful.

1. She took *copious* notes during the lecture.
2. The recipe calls for *copious* amounts of garlic.
3. The rainfall this spring was more *copious* than usual.

---

### Coptic

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːptɪk/ &middot; *KAHP-tik*
**Русский:** коптский
**Type:** origin — religion
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Relating to the Copts, the Christian community of Egypt, or their church or language.

1. The *Coptic* Orthodox Church is one of the oldest in the world.
2. They visited a *Coptic* monastery near Cairo.
3. The manuscript is written in *Coptic* script.

---

### copyrighted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːpiˌraɪtɪd/ &middot; *KAH-pee-ry-tid*
**Русский:** защищённый авторским правом
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *copyright*

Legally protected so that others may not copy or use it without permission.

1. You can't post *copyrighted* music without permission.
2. All photos on this site are *copyrighted*.
3. The video was removed for using *copyrighted* material.

---

### coquettish

**Pronunciation:** /koʊˈkɛtɪʃ/ &middot; *koh-KET-ish*
**Русский:** кокетливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more coquettish*, *most coquettish*
**Form:** -ish from *coquette*

Behaving in a playful way to attract romantic attention.

1. She gave him a *coquettish* smile.
2. Her tone was light and *coquettish*.
3. The character is more *coquettish* in the movie than in the book.

---

### coral

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɔːrəl/ &middot; *KOR-uhl*
**Русский:** коралловый (о цвете)
**Type:** color
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Only the color is an adjective. In *a coral reef* or *coral jewelry*, *coral* is a noun used as a modifier.

Pinkish orange in color.

1. She wore a *coral* dress to the wedding.
2. The walls are painted a soft *coral* shade.
3. Her lipstick was bright *coral*.

---

### cordial

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɔːrdʒəl/ &middot; *KOR-juhl*
**Русский:** вежливый, дружелюбный, радушный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cordial*, *most cordial*
**Contrast:** *Cordial* is often weaker than Russian *сердечный*: *cordial relations* are polite and friendly, not necessarily warm. The noun *a cordial* is a sweet liqueur.

Friendly and polite, often in a formal way.

1. The two leaders had a *cordial* meeting.
2. Relations with our neighbors are *cordial* but not close.
3. She was more *cordial* to me than I expected.

---

### cordless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɔːrdləs/ &middot; *KORD-luhs*
**Русский:** беспроводной
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** *cord* + -less

Working on a battery, without an electric cord.

1. I bought a *cordless* drill.
2. Our old home phone was *cordless*.
3. *Cordless* vacuums are easier to carry upstairs.

---

### corn-fed

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɔːrnˌfɛd/ &middot; *KORN-fed*
**Русский:** откормленный кукурузой; простой, деревенский
**Type:** compound — noun + participle
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *corn* + *fed*

Corn-fed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of animals, fed on corn.** (откормленный кукурузой)

1. The restaurant serves *corn-fed* beef.
2. Most chicken in the store is *corn-fed*.

**2. Informal: healthy, simple and typical of the rural Midwest.** (простой, деревенский, пышущий здоровьем)

3. He's a big, *corn-fed* farm boy from Iowa.
4. The ad was full of *corn-fed* small-town values.

---

### cornered

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɔːrnərd/ &middot; *KOR-nurd*
**Русский:** загнанный в угол
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *corner* (verb)

Trapped, with no easy way to escape or get out of a situation.

1. A *cornered* animal can be dangerous.
2. He felt *cornered* by the reporters' questions.
3. When she's *cornered*, she gets defensive.

---

### corny

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɔːrni/ &middot; *KOR-nee*
**Русский:** банальный, избитый, слащавый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *cornier*, *corniest*

Too familiar, silly or sentimental to be taken seriously.

1. My dad loves telling *corny* jokes.
2. I know it sounds *corny*, but you changed my life.
3. That's the *corniest* love song I've ever heard.

---

### coronary

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɔːrəˌnɛri/ &middot; *KOR-uh-ner-ee*
**Русский:** коронарный, сердечный
**Type:** classifying — medical
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** The noun *a coronary* is an informal word for a heart attack: *He almost had a coronary when he saw the bill.*

Relating to the arteries that carry blood to the heart.

1. *Coronary* heart disease is a leading cause of death.
2. The surgeon found a blockage in a *coronary* artery.
3. He was taken straight to the *coronary* care unit.

---

### corporal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɔːrpərəl/ &middot; *KOR-pur-uhl*
**Русский:** телесный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *corporal punishment*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Almost only in *corporal punishment* (hitting as a punishment). *A corporal* is much more often the noun, a low military rank. Do not confuse with *corporate* (of a company).

Relating to the body; used almost only of physical punishment.

1. Many states have banned *corporal* punishment in public schools.
2. The school stopped using *corporal* punishment decades ago.
3. Laws on *corporal* punishment at home differ from state to state.

---

### corporate

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɔːrpərət/ &middot; *KOR-pur-uht*
**Русский:** корпоративный
**Type:** classifying; also evaluative
**Position:** both — sense 1 only before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable — sense 2 informally *more corporate*
**Contrast:** Russian *корпоратив* (an office party) is *a company party* or *an office party* in English, never *a corporate*.

Corporate has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to a large company or to business.** (корпоративный)

1. She left her *corporate* job to open a bakery.
2. The company moved its *corporate* headquarters to Texas.
3. *Corporate* profits rose sharply last year.

**2. Typical of big companies: formal, impersonal and all the same (disapproving).** (казённый, безликий, как в корпорации)

4. The new logo looks too *corporate*.
5. The festival has gotten more *corporate* every year.

---

### corpulent

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɔːrpjələnt/ &middot; *KOR-pyuh-luhnt*
**Русский:** тучный, дородный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more corpulent*, *most corpulent*

Fat, with a large heavy body (formal).

1. A *corpulent* man in a gray suit took the seat next to me.
2. The senator had grown *corpulent* in his later years.
3. He was the *most corpulent* of the three brothers.

---

### correctional

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈrɛkʃənəl/ &middot; *kuh-REK-shuh-nuhl*
**Русский:** исправительный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a correctional facility*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *correction*
**Contrast:** Official American language for prisons: *a correctional facility* is a prison, *a correctional officer* is a prison guard.

Relating to prisons and the punishment of people who have broken the law.

1. He works as a *correctional* officer at the state prison.
2. The *correctional* facility holds about two thousand inmates.
3. She studies the *correctional* system in California.

---

### corrective

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈrɛktɪv/ &middot; *kuh-REK-tiv*
**Русский:** корректирующий, исправляющий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ive from *correct*

Meant to fix a problem or make something right.

1. The company promised to take *corrective* action.
2. She needed *corrective* surgery on her knee.
3. He wears *corrective* lenses when he drives.

---

### corresponding

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɔːrəˈspɑːndɪŋ/ &middot; *kor-uh-SPAHN-ding*
**Русский:** соответствующий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *the corresponding period*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *correspond*
**Contrast:** After a noun, *corresponding to* is the verb: *the number corresponding to your seat*.

Matching or connected with something else; the equivalent one.

1. Sales were up 10 percent from the *corresponding* period last year.
2. Each key opens a *corresponding* lock.
3. Higher prices led to a *corresponding* drop in demand.

---

### corroded

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈroʊdɪd/ &middot; *kuh-ROH-did*
**Русский:** разъеденный коррозией, проржавевший
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more corroded*, *most corroded*
**Form:** -ed from *corrode*

Slowly damaged by rust or chemicals.

1. The battery terminals were badly *corroded*.
2. We replaced the *corroded* pipes under the sink.
3. The pipes were *more corroded* than the plumber expected.

---

### corrosive

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈroʊsɪv/ &middot; *kuh-ROH-siv*
**Русский:** едкий, разъедающий; разрушительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more corrosive*, *most corrosive*
**Form:** -ive from *corrode*

Corrosive has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Able to destroy metal or other material by chemical action.** (едкий, коррозионный)

1. Bleach is *corrosive*, so wear gloves.
2. Salt water is *more corrosive* than fresh water.

**2. Slowly harming or weakening something, such as a relationship or a society.** (разрушительный, губительный)

3. Distrust can have a *corrosive* effect on a marriage.
4. Many voters see big money as a *corrosive* influence on politics.

---

### corrugated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɔːrəɡeɪtɪd/ &middot; *KOR-uh-gay-tid*
**Русский:** гофрированный, рифлёный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *corrugate*

Shaped into a row of folds or ridges, which makes a sheet stronger.

1. The shed has a *corrugated* metal roof.
2. Pack the dishes in *corrugated* cardboard boxes.
3. Rain drummed on the *corrugated* tin above us.

---

### corrupt

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈrʌpt/ &middot; *kuh-RUPT*
**Русский:** коррумпированный, продажный; повреждённый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more corrupt*, *most corrupt*
**Opposite:** *honest*
**Contrast:** *Corrupt* is also a verb: *power corrupts*. For damaged files both *corrupt* and *corrupted* are used, but for people and governments say *corrupt*, not *corrupted*.

Corrupt has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Dishonest, especially using power to get money or favors.** (коррумпированный, продажный)

1. The *corrupt* mayor took bribes from builders.
2. Voters said the whole system was *corrupt*.
3. It was once the *most corrupt* police department in the country.

**2. Of computer data or a file: damaged so that it cannot be read or used.** (повреждённый)

4. The file is *corrupt* and won't open.
5. A *corrupt* driver was causing the crashes.

---

### corrupted

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈrʌptɪd/ &middot; *kuh-RUP-tid*
**Русский:** повреждённый; искажённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *corrupt*
**Contrast:** For dishonest people and governments say *corrupt*: *a corrupt official*, not *a corrupted official*.

Corrupted has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of computer data or a file: damaged by errors so it will not open or work properly.** (повреждённый)

1. The update left me with a *corrupted* hard drive.
2. Try to restore the *corrupted* file from a backup.
3. One of the photos is *corrupted*.

**2. Changed from its original or pure form, usually for the worse.** (искажённый, испорченный)

4. The town's name is a *corrupted* form of a Spanish word.
5. The movie was based on a *corrupted* copy of the script.

---

### cosmetic

**Pronunciation:** /kɑːzˈmɛtɪk/ &middot; *kahz-MET-ik*
**Русский:** косметический; поверхностный
**Type:** classifying; also evaluative
**Position:** both — sense 1 only before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *Cosmetics* (plural noun) are makeup and skin products. Russian *косметический ремонт* is usually *a cosmetic update* or *a refresh* of a room.

Cosmetic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to making the face or body look better.** (косметический)

1. She had *cosmetic* surgery on her nose.
2. The store sells *cosmetic* products and perfume.
3. *Cosmetic* dentistry isn't covered by most insurance plans.

**2. Improving only how something looks, not how it works; not important.** (поверхностный, косметический)

4. The changes to the plan are purely *cosmetic*.
5. The car has some *cosmetic* damage but runs fine.

---

### cosmic

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɑːzmɪk/ &middot; *KAHZ-mik*
**Русский:** космический; вселенский
**Type:** classifying; also evaluative
**Position:** both — sense 1 only before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** For spaceflight say *space*, not *cosmic*: *a spaceship*, *the space program*, *space travel*. *Cosmic* is for the universe itself (*cosmic rays*) or means *huge*.

Cosmic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to the universe or outer space.** (космический)

1. *Cosmic* rays hit the earth all the time.
2. Scientists study *cosmic* dust from comets.

**2. Very great, or seeming to be planned by fate.** (вселенский, грандиозный)

3. The traffic jam reached *cosmic* proportions.
4. It felt like a *cosmic* joke that it rained on our only day off.

---

### cosmopolitan

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɑːzməˈpɑːlɪtən/ &middot; *kahz-muh-PAH-lih-tuhn*
**Русский:** космополитичный, многонациональный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cosmopolitan*, *most cosmopolitan*
**Contrast:** In English *cosmopolitan* is a compliment, with none of the negative Soviet sense of *космополит*. *A Cosmopolitan* is also a cocktail.

Cosmopolitan has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Full of people and influences from many different countries.** (многонациональный, космополитичный)

1. New York is one of the *most cosmopolitan* cities in the world.
2. Miami has a lively, *cosmopolitan* feel.

**2. Having traveled and seen a lot of the world; sophisticated.** (искушённый, повидавший мир)

3. Her *cosmopolitan* friends seemed to have lived everywhere.
4. He's much *more cosmopolitan* than his small-town parents.

---

### cost-effective

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɔːst ɪˈfɛktɪv/ &middot; *kawst ih-FEK-tiv*
**Русский:** экономически выгодный, рентабельный
**Type:** compound — noun + adjective
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cost-effective*, *most cost-effective*

Giving good results for the money spent.

1. Solar panels are a *cost-effective* way to cut your bills.
2. Buying in bulk is usually *more cost-effective*.
3. We need the *most cost-effective* solution, not the fanciest.

---

### cost-efficient

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɔːst ɪˈfɪʃənt/ &middot; *kawst ih-FISH-uhnt*
**Русский:** экономичный, выгодный
**Type:** compound — noun + adjective
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cost-efficient*, *most cost-efficient*

Doing a job well without wasting money.

1. The new engines are *more cost-efficient* than the old ones.
2. We're looking for a *cost-efficient* way to ship packages.
3. Is it really *cost-efficient* to repair this old printer?

---

### Costa Rican

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkoʊstə ˈriːkən/ &middot; *koh-stuh REE-kuhn*
**Русский:** костариканский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *A Costa Rican* is also a person from Costa Rica. Both words take capital letters.

From or relating to Costa Rica.

1. We drank strong *Costa Rican* coffee every morning.
2. Her husband is *Costa Rican*.
3. The *Costa Rican* rainforest is full of birds.

---

### countable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkaʊntəbəl/ &middot; *KOWN-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** исчисляемый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -able from *count*
**Opposite:** *uncountable*
**Contrast:** Many nouns that are countable in Russian are uncountable in English: *advice*, *news*, *information*, *furniture*, *luggage*. Say *a piece of advice*, never *an advice*.

In grammar, of a noun: having a plural form and used with *a* or *an*.

1. *Chair* is a *countable* noun, but *furniture* is not.
2. Use *many* with *countable* nouns and *much* with uncountable ones.
3. In English, *advice* is not *countable*.

---

### counterclockwise

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkaʊntərˈklɑːkwaɪz/ &middot; *kown-tur-KLAHK-wyz*
**Русский:** против часовой стрелки
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun: *a counterclockwise turn*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** counter- + *clockwise*
**Opposite:** *clockwise*
**Contrast:** British English says *anticlockwise*. The word is most often an adverb: *turn the knob counterclockwise*.

Moving in the direction opposite to the hands of a clock.

1. Give the lid a *counterclockwise* turn to open it.
2. The runners move in a *counterclockwise* direction around the track.
3. Hurricanes north of the equator have a *counterclockwise* spin.

---

### counterfeit

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkaʊntərfɪt/ &middot; *KOWN-tur-fit*
**Русский:** поддельный, фальшивый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *genuine*; *real*
**Contrast:** *Counterfeit* is used mostly for money and branded goods; for a signature or document *forged* is more usual. *Counterfeit* is also a noun and a verb.

Made to look exactly like the real thing in order to trick people.

1. Police found thousands of dollars in *counterfeit* bills.
2. The market is full of *counterfeit* designer bags.
3. The bank said the check was *counterfeit*.

---

### counterintuitive

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkaʊntərɪnˈtuːɪtɪv/ &middot; *kown-tur-in-TOO-ih-tiv*
**Русский:** противоречащий интуиции, неочевидный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more counterintuitive*, *most counterintuitive*
**Form:** counter- + *intuitive*

Different from what you would expect or naturally think is true.

1. It seems *counterintuitive*, but hot tea can cool you down.
2. The results of the study were *counterintuitive*.
3. Oddly, the *most counterintuitive* part of the plan worked best.

---

### counterproductive

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkaʊntərprəˈdʌktɪv/ &middot; *kown-tur-pruh-DUK-tiv*
**Русский:** контрпродуктивный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more counterproductive*, *most counterproductive*
**Form:** counter- + *productive*

Having the opposite effect to the one you want.

1. Yelling at kids is often *counterproductive*.
2. Cutting the budget again would be *counterproductive*.
3. Nothing is *more counterproductive* than a meeting about having too many meetings.

---

### counterrevolutionary

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkaʊntərˌrɛvəˈluːʃənɛri/ &middot; *kown-tur-rev-uh-LOO-shuh-ner-ee*
**Русский:** контрреволюционный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** counter- + *revolutionary*
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a counterrevolutionary* is a person who fights a revolution.

Opposed to a revolution and trying to reverse it.

1. The rebels were accused of *counterrevolutionary* activity.
2. The army put down a *counterrevolutionary* uprising.
3. The new regime called every critic *counterrevolutionary*.

---

### counterterrorist

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkaʊntərˈtɛrərɪst/ &middot; *kown-tur-TER-ur-ist*
**Русский:** контртеррористический, антитеррористический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a counterterrorist unit*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** counter- + *terrorist*
**Contrast:** Americans often use the noun *counterterrorism* before another noun instead: *counterterrorism officials*. *A counterterrorist* is also a person.

Meant to prevent or fight terrorism.

1. A *counterterrorist* unit was sent to the airport.
2. The FBI runs *counterterrorist* training for local police.
3. The two countries agreed to share *counterterrorist* intelligence.

---

### countless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkaʊntləs/ &middot; *KOWNT-luhs*
**Русский:** бесчисленный
**Type:** quantity
**Position:** attributive only — *countless times*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *count*
**Contrast:** Only before a plural noun. After a verb say *too many to count*: *the mistakes were too many to count*.

Too many to count; very many.

1. I've told you *countless* times to lock the door.
2. The storm destroyed *countless* homes.
3. She spent *countless* hours practicing the piano.

---

### country-style

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkʌntri ˌstaɪl/ &middot; *KUN-tree styl*
**Русский:** в деревенском стиле
**Type:** compound — noun + noun
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *Country* here means *the countryside*, not *a nation*. *Country-style ribs* are a meaty cut of pork, not a recipe.

Made, cooked or decorated in a simple, traditional rural way.

1. We had *country-style* ribs and cornbread.
2. The kitchen has a big *country-style* wooden table.
3. Breakfast is *country-style*, with biscuits and gravy.

---

### countrywide

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkʌntriˈwaɪd/ &middot; *kun-tree-WYD*
**Русский:** общенациональный, по всей стране
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *country* + *-wide*
**Contrast:** Also an adverb: *the show airs countrywide*. Americans more often say *nationwide*.

Happening or existing in every part of a country.

1. Police launched a *countrywide* search for the missing girl.
2. Within a week the strike was *countrywide*.
3. There is a *countrywide* shortage of nurses.

---

### courageous

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈreɪdʒəs/ &middot; *kuh-RAY-juhs*
**Русский:** смелый, мужественный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more courageous*, *most courageous*
**Form:** -ous from *courage*
**Opposite:** *cowardly*
**Contrast:** The stress moves: KUR-ij, but kuh-RAY-juhs.

Brave; willing to do something dangerous or difficult.

1. It was a *courageous* decision to speak out.
2. The firefighters were incredibly *courageous*.
3. She's the *most courageous* person I know.

---

### court-ordered

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɔːrt ˌɔːrdərd/ &middot; *KORT or-durd*
**Русский:** назначенный судом, по решению суда
**Type:** compound — noun + participle
**Position:** attributive only — *court-ordered therapy*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** After a verb use the passive, with no hyphen: *the test was ordered by the court*.

Required by the decision of a court of law.

1. He had to attend *court-ordered* anger management classes.
2. The *court-ordered* sale of the house happened in May.
3. The company made *court-ordered* payments to its workers.

---

### courteous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɜːrtiəs/ &middot; *KUR-tee-uhs*
**Русский:** вежливый, учтивый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more courteous*, *most courteous*
**Takes:** *to* + person
**Opposite:** *discourteous*; *rude*

Polite and respectful to other people.

1. The hotel staff were friendly and *courteous*.
2. Please be *courteous* to other passengers.
3. The officer was *more courteous* than I expected.

---

### courtly

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɔːrtli/ &middot; *KORT-lee*
**Русский:** галантный, изысканно вежливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more courtly*, *most courtly*
**Form:** -ly from *court*
**Contrast:** An *-ly* adjective, not an adverb: say *in a courtly way*.

Polite, graceful and formal in an old-fashioned way.

1. He greeted her with a *courtly* bow.
2. My grandfather had *courtly* Southern manners.
3. He was the *most courtly* man in the office, always holding doors.

---

### covert

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkoʊvərt; koʊˈvɜːrt/ &middot; *KOH-vurt; koh-VURT*
**Русский:** тайный, скрытый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more covert*, *most covert*
**Opposite:** *overt*

Secret or hidden, especially done by spies or government agents.

1. The CIA ran a *covert* operation in the region.
2. She gave him a *covert* glance across the table.
3. After the arrests, the group became *more covert*.

---

### coveted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkʌvɪtɪd/ &middot; *KUV-ih-tid*
**Русский:** желанный, вожделенный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more coveted*, *most coveted*
**Form:** -ed from *covet*

Wanted very much by many people.

1. She won the *coveted* first prize.
2. Tickets to the final are the *most coveted* in sports.
3. Jobs at the company are highly *coveted*.

---

### covetous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkʌvɪtəs/ &middot; *KUV-ih-tuhs*
**Русский:** завистливый, алчный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more covetous*, *most covetous*
**Takes:** *of* + noun

Wanting very much to have what belongs to someone else (formal).

1. He cast a *covetous* eye on his neighbor's new truck.
2. She was *covetous* of her sister's success.
3. Wealth only made him *more covetous*.

---

### coy

**Pronunciation:** /kɔɪ/ &middot; *KOY*
**Русский:** притворно застенчивый; уклончивый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *coyer*, *coyest*; *more coy*, *most coy*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing* (sense 2)

Coy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Pretending to be shy, often in a playful way to attract someone.** (притворно застенчивый, кокетливый)

1. She gave him a *coy* smile.
2. Don't be *coy*. You know you like him.

**2. Unwilling to give information about something.** (уклончивый, скрытный)

3. The actress is *coy* about her age.
4. The company has been *more coy* about its plans than usual.

---

### cozy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkoʊzi/ &middot; *KOH-zee*
**Русский:** уютный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *cozier*, *coziest*
**Takes:** *with* + person or group (sense 2)
**Contrast:** British spelling is *cosy*; American is *cozy*.

Cozy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Warm, comfortable and pleasant, often because it is small.** (уютный)

1. We found a *cozy* little cafe near the river.
2. The cabin was warm and *cozy*.
3. This is the *coziest* chair in the house.

**2. Too close or friendly, in a way that seems unfair or dishonest (disapproving).** (подозрительно тесный, слишком тёплый)

4. Critics attacked the senator's *cozy* relationship with oil companies.
5. The regulators got a little too *cozy* with the banks.

---

### crabby

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkræbi/ &middot; *KRAB-ee*
**Русский:** ворчливый, раздражительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *crabbier*, *crabbiest*

In a bad mood and easily annoyed (informal).

1. I get *crabby* when I'm hungry.
2. A *crabby* old man yelled at us to get off his lawn.
3. The kids are always *crabbier* after a long car ride.

---

### crack

**Pronunciation:** /kræk/ &middot; *KRAK*
**Русский:** первоклассный, отборный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** attributive only — *a crack shot*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Only before a noun, and mostly in *a crack shot*, *a crack team*, *crack troops*. *Crack* is far more often a noun (a thin break; the drug) or a verb.

Very skillful; expert (informal).

1. He's a *crack* shot with a rifle.
2. A *crack* team of lawyers handled the case.
3. *Crack* troops were sent to guard the border.

---

### cracked

**Pronunciation:** /krækt/ &middot; *KRAKT*
**Русский:** треснувший; надтреснутый; дроблёный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — sense 3 only before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
