**Form:** -ed from *crack*
**Contrast:** *Not all it's cracked up to be* (not as good as people say) comes from the phrasal verb *crack up*, not from this adjective.

Cracked has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Having thin lines of breakage on the surface.** (треснувший, потрескавшийся)

1. I need to replace my *cracked* phone screen.
2. Her lips were dry and *cracked* from the cold.
3. Don't drink from that *cracked* glass.

**2. Of a voice: rough and uneven, as if about to break.** (надтреснутый, срывающийся)

4. He answered in a *cracked* voice.
5. Her voice sounded *cracked* and tired.

**3. Of pepper, grain or nuts: broken into rough pieces.** (дроблёный, крупного помола)

6. Season the steak with salt and *cracked* black pepper.
7. The bread is made with *cracked* wheat.

---

### crafty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkræfti/ &middot; *KRAF-tee*
**Русский:** хитрый; любящий рукоделие
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *craftier*, *craftiest*

Crafty has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Clever at getting what you want, often by tricking people.** (хитрый, пронырливый)

1. The *crafty* old fox got into the henhouse again.
2. He's *craftier* than he looks, so watch him.

**2. Good at making things by hand, or involving handmade crafts (informal).** (рукастый, любящий рукоделие)

3. I'm not very *crafty*, so I bought the decorations.
4. My daughter loves *crafty* projects like making cards.

---

### craggy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkræɡi/ &middot; *KRAG-ee*
**Русский:** скалистый; с резкими чертами
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *craggier*, *craggiest*

Craggy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Steep and rough, with a lot of large rocks.** (скалистый, крутой)

1. They hiked along the *craggy* coastline.
2. The *craggiest* peaks were covered in snow.

**2. Of a man's face: with deep lines, in a strong and attractive way.** (с резкими, грубоватыми чертами)

3. The actor has a *craggy*, handsome face.
4. His face had grown *craggy* with age.

---

### cramped

**Pronunciation:** /kræmpt/ &middot; *KRAMPT*
**Русский:** тесный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cramped*, *most cramped*
**Form:** -ed from *cramp*
**Opposite:** *spacious*; *roomy*

Too small for the people or things in it, so there is not enough room.

1. Four of us shared a *cramped* two-room apartment.
2. The back seat is a little *cramped* for three adults.
3. Airline seats seem *more cramped* every year.

---

### cranky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkræŋki/ &middot; *KRANG-kee*
**Русский:** раздражительный, капризный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *crankier*, *crankiest*
**Contrast:** This is the American meaning. In British English *cranky* more often means *strange* or *eccentric*.

Bad-tempered and easily annoyed, often because you are tired or hungry.

1. The baby gets *cranky* when she misses her nap.
2. My *cranky* neighbor complains about everything.
3. Dad is always *crankier* before his morning coffee.

---

### crappy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkræpi/ &middot; *KRAP-ee*
**Русский:** паршивый, дерьмовый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *crappier*, *crappiest*
**Contrast:** Rude but common in casual American speech; avoid it at work and in writing. Softer choices: *lousy*, *crummy*, *bad*.

Very bad or of poor quality (rude slang).

1. I had a really *crappy* day at work.
2. The hotel room was small and *crappy*.
3. This is the *crappiest* phone I've ever owned.

---

### crass

**Pronunciation:** /kræs/ &middot; *KRAS*
**Русский:** бестактный, грубый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more crass*, *most crass*

Rude and showing no sensitivity or good taste, often about money.

1. It was *crass* to ask how much she paid for the house.
2. The ad was a *crass* attempt to sell toys to toddlers.
3. Of all his jokes that night, that one was the *most crass*.

---

### craven

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkreɪvən/ &middot; *KRAY-vuhn*
**Русский:** трусливый, малодушный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more craven*, *most craven*
**Opposite:** *brave*

Cowardly; lacking courage (formal, disapproving).

1. The senator's *craven* silence angered his voters.
2. Giving in to the threats was a *craven* surrender.
3. I can't think of a *more craven* act than blaming your own staff.

---

### crazed

**Pronunciation:** /kreɪzd/ &middot; *KRAYZD*
**Русский:** обезумевший
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *craze*
**Contrast:** Often in compounds: *power-crazed*, *drug-crazed*, *money-crazed*.

Wild and out of control, like someone who has gone mad.

1. *Crazed* fans pushed toward the stage.
2. He had a *crazed* look in his eyes.
3. She was *crazed* with grief after the accident.

---

### creaky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkriːki/ &middot; *KREE-kee*
**Русский:** скрипучий; дряхлый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *creakier*, *creakiest*

Creaky has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Making a long, high sound when moved or stepped on.** (скрипучий)

1. I tiptoed down the *creaky* stairs.
2. The old door is *creakier* than ever.

**2. Old and not working well.** (дряхлый, устаревший)

3. My knees are getting *creaky*.
4. The city still runs on a *creaky* computer system.

---

### cream-colored

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkriːm ˌkʌlərd/ &middot; *KREEM kuh-lurd*
**Русский:** кремовый
**Type:** color
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + *colored*

Pale yellowish-white, the color of cream.

1. She wore a *cream-colored* sweater.
2. The walls in the living room are *cream-colored*.
3. They chose *cream-colored* tiles for the bathroom.

---

### creamy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkriːmi/ &middot; *KREE-mee*
**Русский:** сливочный, нежный; кремовый
**Type:** quality; also color
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *creamier*, *creamiest*

Creamy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Thick and smooth, or containing a lot of cream.** (сливочный, нежный, густой)

1. The soup is rich and *creamy*.
2. This is the *creamiest* mac and cheese I've ever had.

**2. Pale yellowish-white and smooth-looking.** (кремовый, молочного цвета)

3. She has *creamy* skin and dark hair.
4. The roses are a *creamy* white.

---

### creative

**Pronunciation:** /kriˈeɪtɪv/ &middot; *kree-AY-tiv*
**Русский:** творческий, креативный
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both — senses 2 and 3 only before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more creative*, *most creative*
**Form:** -ive from *create*

Creative has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Good at thinking of new ideas or making original things.** (творческий, изобретательный)

1. She's a very *creative* cook.
2. Kids are often *more creative* than adults.
3. We need some *creative* ideas to raise money.

**2. Involving the making of art, writing or music.** (творческий)

4. He teaches *creative* writing at a community college.
5. She works in the *creative* department of an ad agency.

**3. Using clever but slightly dishonest ways to change the facts (humorous).** («творческий», ловко подтасованный)

6. The company hid its losses with some *creative* accounting.
7. That's a *creative* way to describe getting fired.

---

### credible

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkrɛdəbəl/ &middot; *KRED-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** правдоподобный, заслуживающий доверия
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more credible*, *most credible*
**Opposite:** *not credible*; *implausible*
**Contrast:** The opposite is not *incredible*, which today usually means *amazing*. Say *not credible* or *hard to believe*. Do not confuse with *credulous* (too ready to believe).

Credible has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Able to be believed; convincing.** (правдоподобный, достоверный)

1. The witness gave a *credible* account of the accident.
2. Her story just isn't *credible*.
3. The second report is far *more credible* than the first.

**2. Good enough to be taken seriously, as a real competitor or danger.** (серьёзный, реальный)

4. She's the only *credible* candidate for the job.
5. Police said the bomb threat was *credible*.

---

### creditworthy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkrɛdɪtˌwɜːrði/ &middot; *KRED-it-wur-thee*
**Русский:** кредитоспособный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more creditworthy*, *most creditworthy*
**Form:** compound — *credit* + *-worthy*

Considered safe to lend money to, because likely to pay it back.

1. Banks lend only to *creditworthy* customers.
2. A steady job makes you look *more creditworthy*.
3. After the losses, the company was no longer *creditworthy*.

---

### credulous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkrɛdʒələs/ &middot; *KREJ-uh-luhs*
**Русский:** легковерный, доверчивый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more credulous*, *most credulous*
**Opposite:** *skeptical*
**Contrast:** *Credulous* (too ready to believe) is not *credible* (believable). *Incredulous* means *not able to believe what you see or hear*: *an incredulous look*.

Too ready to believe what people tell you, and so easily tricked.

1. The scam targeted *credulous* retirees.
2. Only the *most credulous* viewers believed the video was real.
3. Don't be so *credulous*; check the facts first.

---

### creepy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkriːpi/ &middot; *KREE-pee*
**Русский:** жуткий; мерзкий, стрёмный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *creepier*, *creepiest*

Creepy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Strange and frightening, so that you feel nervous.** (жуткий, зловещий)

1. The old house is really *creepy* at night.
2. That was the *creepiest* movie I've ever seen.

**2. Unpleasant in a way that makes people uncomfortable, often by showing unwanted interest in them.** (мерзкий, стрёмный)

3. Some *creepy* guy kept staring at her on the train.
4. It's kind of *creepy* how much the app knows about me.

---

### creepy-crawly

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkriːpi ˈkrɔːli/ &middot; *kree-pee-KRAW-lee*
**Русский:** вызывающий мурашки, жутковатый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more creepy-crawly*, *most creepy-crawly*
**Form:** compound — *creepy* + *crawly*
**Contrast:** Also a noun for a bug: *The kids collect creepy-crawlies in a jar*.

Giving you the unpleasant feeling that something is crawling on your skin.

1. The basement has a *creepy-crawly* feel to it.
2. Spiders make me all *creepy-crawly*.
3. She told the kids a *creepy-crawly* story around the fire.

---

### Creole

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkriːoʊl/ &middot; *KREE-ohl*
**Русский:** креольский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *A Creole* is also a person. *A creole*, often with a small letter, is a language that grew out of a mix of languages, such as *Haitian Creole*.

Relating to the Creole people and culture, especially of Louisiana and the Caribbean.

1. The restaurant serves *Creole* dishes like jambalaya and gumbo.
2. The French Quarter is full of old *Creole* townhouses.
3. His family is *Creole*, from southern Louisiana.

---

### crestfallen

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkrɛstˌfɔːlən/ &middot; *KREST-faw-luhn*
**Русский:** удручённый, поникший
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more crestfallen*, *most crestfallen*
**Form:** compound — *crest* + *fallen*

Sad and disappointed because something has gone wrong.

1. He looked *crestfallen* when she said no.
2. The *crestfallen* players walked slowly off the field.
3. She seemed even *more crestfallen* when she saw her grade.

---

### criminal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkrɪmɪnəl/ &middot; *KRIM-ih-nuhl*
**Русский:** уголовный; преступный
**Type:** classifying; also evaluative
**Position:** both — sense 1 only before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *A criminal* is also the noun. English uses *criminal* for both Russian *уголовный* and *криминальный*: *criminal law*, *a criminal record*, *a criminal case*.

Criminal has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to crime or to the laws about crime.** (уголовный, криминальный)

1. He has no *criminal* record.
2. She's a *criminal* defense lawyer.
3. Police opened a *criminal* investigation.

**2. Very wrong or shameful; often used to exaggerate (informal).** (преступный, возмутительный)

4. It's *criminal* that teachers are paid so little.
5. Throwing away all that good food is *criminal*.

---

### crimson

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkrɪmzən/ &middot; *KRIM-zuhn*
**Русский:** малиновый, багровый
**Type:** color
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Crimson has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Deep red with a slight purple color.** (малиновый, тёмно-красный)

1. She wore a *crimson* dress to the party.
2. The maple leaves turn *crimson* in the fall.
3. The school colors are *crimson* and white.

**2. Of a face: red with embarrassment or anger.** (пунцовый, багровый)

4. His face went *crimson* when they laughed.
5. She turned *crimson* and looked away.

---

### crinkled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkrɪŋkəld/ &middot; *KRING-kuhld*
**Русский:** смятый, сморщенный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *crinkle*

Covered in small folds or lines.

1. She smoothed out the *crinkled* receipt.
2. His eyes were *crinkled* with laughter.
3. The *crinkled* old map was hard to read.

---

### crinkly

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkrɪŋkli/ &middot; *KRING-klee*
**Русский:** шуршащий, морщинистый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *crinklier*, *crinkliest*

Having many small folds or lines, often making a soft crackling sound.

1. The chips come in a *crinkly* plastic bag.
2. Grandpa has a warm, *crinkly* smile.
3. The more I folded the map, the *crinklier* it got.

---

### crippled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkrɪpəld/ &middot; *KRIP-uhld*
**Русский:** выведенный из строя; искалеченный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *cripple*
**Contrast:** Used about people, *crippled* is now offensive; say *disabled* or *has a disability*. About things (*a crippled economy*, *a crippled ship*) it is normal.

Crippled has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Badly damaged or weakened, so that it cannot work normally.** (выведенный из строя, подорванный)

1. The storm left the city with a *crippled* power grid.
2. Tugboats towed the *crippled* ship back to port.
3. The strike hit an already *crippled* economy.

**2. Unable to walk or move normally because of injury or illness (now offensive about people).** (искалеченный, увечный)

4. In old novels, *crippled* characters are often objects of pity.
5. We took the *crippled* bird to a wildlife center.

---

### crippling

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkrɪplɪŋ/ &middot; *KRIP-ling*
**Русский:** тяжелейший, разорительный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more crippling*, *most crippling*
**Form:** -ing from *cripple*

So severe that it stops someone or something from working normally.

1. Many graduates face *crippling* student debt.
2. She suffers from *crippling* migraines.
3. It was the *most crippling* drought in decades.

---

### crisp

**Pronunciation:** /krɪsp/ &middot; *KRISP*
**Русский:** хрустящий; свежий; чёткий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *crisper*, *crispest*
**Contrast:** *A crisp* is British for a potato chip; Americans say *chip*. *Crisp* and *crispy* overlap for food, but for weather, clothes, money and pictures use only *crisp*.

Crisp has 4 distinct senses.

**1. Firm and dry, so it breaks with a pleasant sound when you bite it.** (хрустящий, свежий)

1. I love a *crisp* green apple.
2. Cook the bacon until it's *crisp*.

**2. Of weather or air: cold, dry and fresh.** (свежий, бодрящий)

3. It was a *crisp* fall morning.
4. The air gets *crisper* up in the mountains.

**3. Of paper or cloth: fresh, clean and smooth.** (новенький, хрустящий, отглаженный)

5. He wore a *crisp* white shirt.
6. She paid with a *crisp* twenty-dollar bill.

**4. Clear, sharp and quick, with nothing extra.** (чёткий, лаконичный)

7. The new TV has a *crisp* picture.
8. Her answers were short and *crisp*.

---

### crispy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkrɪspi/ &middot; *KRIS-pee*
**Русский:** хрустящий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *crispier*, *crispiest*
**Contrast:** Use *crispy* for cooked food. Fresh fruit and vegetables are usually *crisp*, and so are weather, clothes and bills.

Pleasantly hard and crunchy, especially of fried or baked food.

1. I like my fries extra *crispy*.
2. The chicken has a *crispy* golden crust.
3. Pizza comes out *crispier* in the oven than in the microwave.

---

### critical

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkrɪtɪkəl/ &middot; *KRIT-ih-kuhl*
**Русский:** критический; критичный; решающий
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both — senses 4 and 5 only before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more critical*, *most critical*
**Takes:** *of* + noun (sense 1); *to* or *for* + noun (sense 2)
**Preposition patterns:** [critical of](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#critical-of)
**Contrast:** Not every *critical* is negative: *a critical role* and *critical information* mean *very important*. In American news, *in critical condition* means *in danger of dying*.

Critical has 5 distinct senses.

**1. Saying that someone or something is bad or wrong.** (критический, критично настроенный)

1. Her boss is very *critical* of her work.
2. He made some *critical* comments about the plan.
3. The newspapers were even *more critical* the next day.

**2. Extremely important, because a lot depends on it.** (решающий, крайне важный)

4. The next few days are *critical*.
5. Clean water is *critical* to good health.
6. Timing is the *most critical* part of the plan.

**3. Very serious or dangerous, especially of an illness or situation.** (критический, тяжёлый)

7. The driver is in *critical* condition at the hospital.
8. The shortage of nurses has reached a *critical* point.

**4. Judging something carefully to see what is good or bad about it.** (критический, аналитический)

9. The course teaches *critical* thinking.
10. Read the article with a *critical* eye.

**5. Relating to the opinions of critics about books, movies or art.** (у критиков, критический)

11. The movie was a *critical* success but lost money.
12. Her first novel won *critical* acclaim.

---

### Croatian

**Pronunciation:** /kroʊˈeɪʃən/ &middot; *kroh-AY-shuhn*
**Русский:** хорватский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *A Croatian* is also a person, also called *a Croat*. *Croatian* is also the name of the language.

From or relating to Croatia, its people or its language.

1. We spent a week on the *Croatian* coast.
2. My wife is *Croatian*.
3. He learned a few *Croatian* songs from his grandmother.

---

### crooked

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkrʊkɪd/ &middot; *KRUUK-id*
**Русский:** кривой; нечестный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more crooked*, *most crooked*
**Opposite:** *straight*; *honest*
**Contrast:** The adjective is always two syllables, KRUUK-id, like *naked* and *wicked*. The past tense of the verb (*she crooked her finger*) is one syllable: krukt.

Crooked has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not straight; bent or twisted.** (кривой, изогнутый)

1. Your tie is *crooked*.
2. He gave me a *crooked* smile.
3. The picture looks *more crooked* than before.

**2. Dishonest, especially involved in crime (informal).** (нечестный, продажный)

4. A *crooked* cop was taking money from drug dealers.
5. The whole deal seemed *crooked* to me.

---

### cropped

**Pronunciation:** /krɑːpt/ &middot; *KRAHPT*
**Русский:** коротко остриженный; укороченный; обрезанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *crop*

Cropped has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Of hair or grass: cut very short.** (коротко остриженный)

1. He has closely *cropped* gray hair.
2. Her *cropped* hair made her look younger.

**2. Of clothes: shorter than usual, ending above the waist or the ankle.** (укороченный)

3. She wore a *cropped* jacket over her dress.
4. *Cropped* pants are popular this summer.

**3. Of a picture: with the edges cut off.** (обрезанный, кадрированный)

5. He posted a *cropped* photo that left out his ex.
6. The *cropped* image looks better on the website.

---

### cross

**Pronunciation:** /krɔːs/ &middot; *KRAWS*
**Русский:** сердитый, раздражённый
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb: *she was cross*
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *crosser*, *crossest*; *more cross*
**Takes:** *with* + person; *about* + thing
**Contrast:** More common in British English. Americans understand it and meet it in books, but usually say *mad*, *annoyed* or *upset*. *Cross* is also a noun and a verb.

Annoyed or a little angry.

1. Are you *cross* with me?
2. She was *cross* about the mess in the kitchen.
3. The longer we waited, the *crosser* he got.

---

### cross-cultural

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkrɔːsˈkʌltʃərəl/ &middot; *kraws-KUL-chur-uhl*
**Русский:** межкультурный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *cross* + *cultural*

Involving or comparing two or more different cultures.

1. The company offers *cross-cultural* training for staff moving abroad.
2. She studies *cross-cultural* communication.
3. *Cross-cultural* marriages are more common than they used to be.

---

### cross-eyed

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkrɔːsˌaɪd/ &middot; *KRAWS-yd*
**Русский:** косоглазый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *cross* + *eye* + *-ed*

Having eyes that turn in toward the nose.

1. The *cross-eyed* cat became famous online.
2. She went *cross-eyed* trying to see the tip of her nose.
3. Reading all that fine print made me *cross-eyed*.

---

### cross-sectional

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkrɔːsˈsɛkʃənəl/ &middot; *kraws-SEK-shuh-nuhl*
**Русский:** поперечный; одномоментный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a cross-sectional study*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *cross section*

Cross-sectional has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of research: studying a group of people at one point in time.** (одномоментный, поперечный (об исследовании))

1. The researchers did a *cross-sectional* study of 5,000 adults.
2. *Cross-sectional* data cannot show cause and effect.

**2. Relating to a cross section, the flat surface you see when you cut straight through something.** (поперечный, в разрезе)

3. The pipe has a *cross-sectional* area of two square inches.
4. The textbook shows a *cross-sectional* view of the heart.

---

### cross-town

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkrɔːsˌtaʊn/ &middot; *KRAWS-town*
**Русский:** идущий через весь город
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a cross-town bus*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Americans usually write it as one word, *crosstown*. It is also an adverb: *we drove crosstown*.

Going from one side of a city to the other.

1. Take the *cross-town* bus to the East Side.
2. Traffic made my *cross-town* commute an hour long.
3. The two high schools are *cross-town* rivals.

---

### crotchety

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkrɑːtʃəti/ &middot; *KRAH-chuh-tee*
**Русский:** ворчливый, брюзгливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more crotchety*, *most crotchety*

Easily annoyed and always complaining, especially of an old person.

1. A *crotchety* old man lives next door.
2. Grandpa gets *crotchety* when his back hurts.
3. He grew *more crotchety* with every passing year.

---

### crowded

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkraʊdɪd/ &middot; *KROW-did*
**Русский:** переполненный, людный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more crowded*, *most crowded*
**Takes:** *with* + people or things
**Preposition patterns:** [crowded with](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#crowded-with)
**Form:** -ed from *crowd*
**Opposite:** *empty*; *deserted*

Crowded has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Full of people, with little room to move.** (переполненный, людный)

1. The subway is always *crowded* at rush hour.
2. The beach was *crowded* with tourists.
3. Downtown is *more crowded* on weekends.

**2. Having too many things or events in a small space or time.** (загруженный, заставленный)

4. I have a very *crowded* schedule this week.
5. The market for electric cars is getting *crowded*.

---

### crowning

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkraʊnɪŋ/ &middot; *KROW-ning*
**Русский:** высший, венчающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *her crowning achievement*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *crown*

Being the best or most important part of something.

1. The bridge was the engineer's *crowning* achievement.
2. Winning the title was the *crowning* moment of his career.
3. The *crowning* touch was a single cherry on top.

---

### cruddy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkrʌdi/ &middot; *KRUD-ee*
**Русский:** паршивый, грязный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *cruddier*, *cruddiest*

Dirty, unpleasant or of poor quality (informal, milder than *crappy*).

1. I'm stuck with a *cruddy* old laptop.
2. I feel *cruddy*, so I'm staying home.
3. It was the *cruddiest* motel on the highway.

---

### crude

**Pronunciation:** /kruːd/ &middot; *KROOD*
**Русский:** примитивный; грубый; сырой
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — sense 3 only before a noun
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *cruder*, *crudest*
**Contrast:** Russian *сырой* about food is *raw*, not *crude*: *raw fish*. *Crude* alone is also a noun for oil: *the price of crude*.

Crude has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Simple and made without much skill or detail; rough, not exact.** (примитивный, грубый, приблизительный)

1. He built a *crude* shelter out of branches.
2. This is only a *crude* estimate.
3. The first computer models were even *cruder*.

**2. Rude and offensive, especially about sex or the body.** (грубый, вульгарный)

4. He made a *crude* joke about her dress.
5. His language was pretty *crude*.

**3. Of oil and other materials: in a natural state, before being processed.** (сырой, неочищенный)

6. The price of *crude* oil went up again.
7. The plant turns *crude* sugar into white sugar.

---

### crumbly

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkrʌmbli/ &middot; *KRUM-blee*
**Русский:** рассыпчатый, крошащийся
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *crumblier*, *crumbliest*

Easily breaking into small pieces.

1. The cake was dry and *crumbly*.
2. Add some *crumbly* feta cheese to the salad.
3. The old bricks get *crumblier* every winter.

---

### crummy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkrʌmi/ &middot; *KRUM-ee*
**Русский:** паршивый, дрянной
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *crummier*, *crummiest*

Bad, cheap or of poor quality (informal).

1. We stayed in a *crummy* motel by the highway.
2. The weather has been *crummy* all week.
3. That was the *crummiest* job I ever had.

---

### crumpled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkrʌmpəld/ &middot; *KRUM-puhld*
**Русский:** смятый, скомканный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *crumple*

Pressed or crushed into folds, so it is no longer flat or smooth.

1. He pulled a *crumpled* dollar bill out of his pocket.
2. Her dress was *crumpled* after the long flight.
3. The front of the car was *crumpled* like paper.

---

### crushed

**Pronunciation:** /krʌʃt/ &middot; *KRUSHT*
**Русский:** измельчённый; подавленный
**Type:** participial; also emotion
**Position:** both — sense 2 mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *crush*
**Contrast:** *Have a crush on someone* (be attracted to them) uses the noun *crush*, not this adjective.

Crushed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Pressed or broken into very small pieces.** (измельчённый, дроблёный, толчёный)

1. I'd like a soda with *crushed* ice.
2. Add two cloves of *crushed* garlic.
3. The path is covered with *crushed* stone.

**2. Extremely sad and disappointed.** (подавленный, убитый)

4. She was *crushed* when she didn't get the part.
5. He looked *crushed* after the loss.

---

### crushing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkrʌʃɪŋ/ &middot; *KRUSH-ing*
**Русский:** сокрушительный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more crushing*, *most crushing*
**Form:** -ing from *crush*

Very severe; causing great damage, defeat or disappointment.

1. The team suffered a *crushing* defeat.
2. Losing her job was a *crushing* blow.
3. It was the *most crushing* loss in the team's history.

---

### cryptic

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkrɪptɪk/ &middot; *KRIP-tik*
**Русский:** загадочный, зашифрованный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cryptic*, *most cryptic*

Having a hidden or unclear meaning; hard to understand.

1. He left a *cryptic* note on the fridge.
2. Her answer was short and *cryptic*.
3. His texts are getting *more cryptic* every day.

---

### cryptographic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkrɪptəˈɡræfɪk/ &middot; *krip-tuh-GRAF-ik*
**Русский:** криптографический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a cryptographic key*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ic from *cryptography*

Relating to writing or breaking secret codes, especially to protect computer data.

1. Every message is protected with a *cryptographic* key.
2. The bank updated its *cryptographic* software.
3. Researchers found a flaw in the *cryptographic* protocol.

---

### crystal clear

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkrɪstəl ˈklɪr/ &middot; *kris-tuhl KLIR*
**Русский:** кристально чистый; предельно ясный
**Type:** compound — noun + adjective
**Position:** both — hyphenated before a noun: *crystal-clear water*
**Comparison:** not gradable

Crystal clear has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of water, air, glass or sound: completely clear.** (кристально чистый, прозрачный)

1. We swam in *crystal-clear* water.
2. The lake is *crystal clear* in the morning.
3. The new speakers give *crystal-clear* sound.

**2. Very easy to understand; completely obvious.** (предельно ясный)

4. Let me make this *crystal clear*: no phones in class.
5. The message from voters was *crystal clear*.

---

### crystallized

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkrɪstəlaɪzd/ &middot; *KRIS-tuh-lyzd*
**Русский:** кристаллизованный; засахаренный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *crystallize*
**Contrast:** British spelling is *crystallised*. For fruit Americans more often say *candied*: *candied ginger*.

Crystallized has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Turned into crystals.** (кристаллизованный, засахарившийся)

1. Warm the jar to soften *crystallized* honey.
2. *Crystallized* salt covered the rocks by the shore.

**2. Of fruit: cooked in sugar and covered with sugar crystals.** (засахаренный)

3. The cake was topped with *crystallized* ginger.
4. She makes *crystallized* orange peel every December.

---

### Cuban

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkjuːbən/ &middot; *KYOO-buhn*
**Русский:** кубинский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *A Cuban* is also a person from Cuba, and in Florida it can mean a pressed ham-and-pork sandwich.

From or relating to Cuba.

1. We ate *Cuban* sandwiches in Miami.
2. His parents are *Cuban*.
3. She loves old *Cuban* music.

---

### cubic

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkjuːbɪk/ &middot; *KYOO-bik*
**Русский:** кубический
**Type:** classifying; also shape
**Position:** attributive only — *cubic feet*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ic from *cube*

Cubic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Used in measuring volume: equal to a cube whose sides are the given length.** (кубический)

1. The fridge has 20 *cubic* feet of space.
2. We ordered ten *cubic* yards of mulch.
3. The engine is 1,500 *cubic* centimeters.

**2. Shaped like a cube.** (кубический, кубической формы)

4. The museum is a large *cubic* building of glass.
5. Salt forms tiny *cubic* crystals.

---

### cuddly

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkʌdli/ &middot; *KUD-lee*
**Русский:** мягкий, приятный на ощупь, милый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *cuddlier*, *cuddliest*

Soft, warm and pleasant to hug.

1. The baby hugged her *cuddly* teddy bear.
2. Puppies are so *cuddly*.
3. Our new cat is much *cuddlier* than the old one.

---

### culinary

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkʌlɪnɛri; ˈkjuːlɪnɛri/ &middot; *KUL-ih-ner-ee; KYOO-lih-ner-ee*
**Русский:** кулинарный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *culinary skills*
**Comparison:** not gradable

Relating to cooking.

1. She went to *culinary* school in New York.
2. He's proud of his *culinary* skills.
3. New Orleans is famous for its *culinary* traditions.

---

### culpable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkʌlpəbəl/ &middot; *KUL-puh-buhl*
**Русский:** виновный, заслуживающий порицания
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more culpable*, *most culpable*
**Takes:** *for* + noun or *-ing*
**Opposite:** *blameless*

Deserving blame for something wrong (formal).

1. Both drivers were partly *culpable* for the accident.
2. The report found the company *culpable* for the spill.
3. In my view, the managers were *more culpable* than the workers.

---

### cultivated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkʌltɪveɪtɪd/ &middot; *KUL-tih-vay-tid*
**Русский:** возделанный; культурный
**Type:** participial; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — sense 2: *more cultivated*, *most cultivated*
**Form:** -ed from *cultivate*
**Contrast:** For an educated, well-mannered person, Russian *культурный* is *cultured* or *cultivated*, never *cultural*.

Cultivated has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of land or plants: used for growing crops, or grown by farmers rather than wild.** (возделанный; культурный (о растениях))

1. Only a small part of the valley is *cultivated* land.
2. *Cultivated* mushrooms are cheaper than wild ones.

**2. Well educated, with good taste and manners.** (культурный, образованный)

3. She's a *cultivated* woman who loves opera.
4. His voice sounded *more cultivated* than his clothes suggested.

---

### cultural

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkʌltʃərəl/ &middot; *KUL-chur-uhl*
**Русский:** культурный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -al from *culture*
**Contrast:** *Cultural* never describes a person's manners. *Культурный человек* is *a cultured person* or *a well-mannered person*; *a cultural person* is wrong.

Cultural has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to the customs, beliefs and way of life of a group of people.** (культурный)

1. Moving abroad meant getting used to *cultural* differences.
2. The festival celebrates the city's *cultural* diversity.
3. Some of the problems are *cultural*, not economic.

**2. Relating to art, music, literature and theater.** (культурный, культурно-художественный)

4. Chicago has a rich *cultural* life.
5. The new *cultural* center has a theater and a gallery.

---

### cultured

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkʌltʃərd/ &middot; *KUL-churd*
**Русский:** культурный, образованный; искусственно выращенный
**Type:** quality; also participial
**Position:** both — sense 2 only before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — sense 1: *more cultured*, *most cultured*
**Contrast:** Not *cultural*: *a cultured person*, but *cultural events*.

Cultured has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Well educated and interested in art, music and literature.** (культурный, образованный)

1. He's a *cultured* man who reads in three languages.
2. A year in Paris made her feel *more cultured*.

**2. Grown or made using living cells or bacteria under controlled conditions.** (искусственно выращенный; кисломолочный)

3. She wore a string of *cultured* pearls.
4. The recipe calls for *cultured* buttermilk.

---

### cum laude

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkʊm ˈlaʊdeɪ/ &middot; *kuum-LOW-day*
**Русский:** с отличием (о дипломе)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — *a cum laude graduate*; after a verb it is an adverb: *she graduated cum laude*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** Latin — 'with praise'; stronger: *magna cum laude*, *summa cum laude*
**Contrast:** Keep the Latin phrase in two words and never add *-er* or *very*. The higher honors are *magna cum laude* and *summa cum laude*.

Of a degree or a graduate: awarded with honors.

1. He's a *cum laude* graduate of Ohio State.
2. *Cum laude* honors take a 3.5 average here.
3. She hung her *cum laude* diploma over the desk.

---

### cumbersome

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkʌmbərsəm/ &middot; *KUM-bur-suhm*
**Русский:** громоздкий; обременительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cumbersome*, *most cumbersome*

Cumbersome has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Large and heavy, and so hard to carry or use.** (громоздкий, неуклюжий)

1. The old cameras were *cumbersome* to carry.
2. We finally got rid of that *cumbersome* sofa.

**2. Slow and complicated.** (громоздкий, обременительный)

3. The application process is *cumbersome*.
4. The new rules are even *more cumbersome* than the old ones.

---

### cumulative

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkjuːmjələtɪv; ˈkjuːmjəˌleɪtɪv/ &middot; *KYOO-myuh-luh-tiv; KYOO-myuh-lay-tiv*
**Русский:** совокупный, накопительный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Increasing over time as each new amount is added.

1. The *cumulative* effect of small changes can be huge.
2. Her *cumulative* GPA is 3.8.
3. The damage to his hearing was *cumulative*.

---

### cunning

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkʌnɪŋ/ &middot; *KUN-ing*
**Русский:** хитрый, коварный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cunning*, *most cunning*
**Contrast:** *Cunning* is not simple praise: it means clever in a sly, often dishonest way. For clever in a good sense say *smart* or *clever*. In older American books *cunning* can also mean *cute* (*a cunning little hat*).

Clever at getting what you want, especially by tricking people.

1. The villain in the movie is *cunning* and cruel.
2. They came up with a *cunning* plan to avoid paying taxes.
3. The fox is supposed to be the *most cunning* animal in the forest.

---

### curable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkjʊrəbəl/ &middot; *KYUUR-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** излечимый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more curable*, *most curable*
**Form:** -able from *cure*
**Opposite:** *incurable*

Able to be cured.

1. The disease is *curable* if it's caught early.
2. Doctors now treat it as a *curable* condition.
3. Skin cancer is *more curable* than many other cancers.

---

### curative

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkjʊrətɪv/ &middot; *KYUUR-uh-tiv*
**Русский:** лечебный, целебный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ive from *cure*

Able to cure an illness, or meant to.

1. Some people believe in the *curative* powers of honey.
2. The town was famous for its *curative* hot springs.
3. The treatment is not *curative*, but it eases the pain.

---

### curdled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɜːrdəld/ &middot; *KUR-duhld*
**Русский:** свернувшийся, скисший
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *curdle*

Of milk or a sauce, separated into lumps, usually because it has gone sour or been overheated.

1. The milk in the fridge was *curdled*.
2. Don't boil the sauce or you'll end up with a *curdled* mess.
3. The creamer came out *curdled*, so I threw it away.

---

### cured

**Pronunciation:** /kjʊrd/ &middot; *KYUURD*
**Русский:** вяленый, копченый; вылеченный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *of* + noun (sense 2: *cured of his fear*)
**Form:** -ed from *cure*

Cured has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of meat or fish, preserved by salting, smoking or drying.** (вяленый, копченый, соленый)

1. The deli sells Italian *cured* meats.
2. She served *cured* salmon on rye bread.
3. Bacon is *cured* pork belly.

**2. Healed of an illness, or free of a problem.** (вылеченный, излечившийся)

4. After six months of treatment, she was completely *cured*.
5. I thought I was *cured* of my fear of flying, but I wasn't.

---

### curled

**Pronunciation:** /kɜːrld/ &middot; *KURLD*
**Русский:** завитой, скрученный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *curl*
**Contrast:** *Curly* hair grows that way; *curled* hair has been styled with an iron or rollers.

Twisted into rings or curves, or bent into a round shape.

1. She had long, *curled* hair for the wedding.
2. The cat lay *curled* up by the fire.
3. The old photo had *curled* edges.

---

### curmudgeonly

**Pronunciation:** /kərˈmʌdʒənli/ &middot; *kur-MUJ-uhn-lee*
**Русский:** ворчливый, брюзгливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more curmudgeonly*, *most curmudgeonly*
**Form:** -ly from *curmudgeon* (a grumpy old person)
**Contrast:** It ends in -ly but is an adjective, not an adverb.

Bad-tempered and easily annoyed, especially in an old person.

1. Our *curmudgeonly* neighbor yells at kids who step on his lawn.
2. He's *curmudgeonly*, but he has a good heart.
3. My grandpa got *more curmudgeonly* every year.

---

### current

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɜːrənt/ &middot; *KUR-uhnt*
**Русский:** текущий, нынешний; действующий
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable — except sense 2: *more current*, *most current*
**Takes:** *on* or *with* + noun (sense 2: *current on your payments*)
**Contrast:** *Currently* is the adverb (*She currently lives in Denver*). *Current* is also a noun: *an electric current*, *an ocean current*. Russian *актуальный* is often *relevant* or *topical*, not *current*.

Current has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Happening or existing now.** (текущий, нынешний, современный)

1. What's your *current* address?
2. The *current* mayor took office in January.
3. Prices are high in the *current* economy.

**2. Up to date, or still valid.** (действующий, актуальный, без задолженности)

4. You need a *current* passport to fly.
5. Are you *current* on your rent?
6. This map is the *most current* one we have.

---

### cursed

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɜːrsɪd; kɜːrst/ &middot; *KUR-sid; KURST*
**Русский:** проклятый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun (sense 2)
**Contrast:** Before a noun it is often KUR-sid (*a cursed house*); in *cursed with* it is KURST. *Cursed* is also the past of the verb *curse* (*He cursed loudly*).

Cursed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Under a curse, so that bad things keep happening.** (проклятый, заколдованный)

1. The locals say the old house is *cursed*.
2. Some fans believe the team is *cursed*.
3. Nobody wanted to buy the *cursed* ring.

**2. Suffering from something bad that you cannot get rid of.** (страдающий (от), обремененный)

4. She was *cursed* with terrible luck.
5. He's *cursed* with a bad back.

---

### cursive

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɜːrsɪv/ &middot; *KUR-siv*
**Русский:** рукописный, слитный (о почерке)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** A false friend: *cursive* is joined-up handwriting, not *курсив*. Slanted print is *italics*. *Cursive* is also a noun: *Kids don't learn cursive anymore*.

Of handwriting, with the letters joined together.

1. Grandma's letters were written in beautiful *cursive* script.
2. She signed her name in *cursive* letters.
3. Many schools stopped teaching *cursive* handwriting.

---

### cursory

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɜːrsəri/ &middot; *KUR-suh-ree*
**Русский:** беглый, поверхностный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cursory*, *most cursory*
**Opposite:** *thorough*

Done quickly, without much attention to detail.

1. He gave the report only a *cursory* glance.
2. After a *cursory* search, the police left.
3. The inspection was *cursory* at best.

---

### curt

**Pronunciation:** /kɜːrt/ &middot; *KURT*
**Русский:** резкий, отрывистый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more curt*, *most curt*

Rudely short in speech or manner.

1. She gave a *curt* reply and hung up.
2. He was *curt* with the waiter.
3. His emails got *more curt* as the deadline approached.

---

### curvaceous

**Pronunciation:** /kɜːrˈveɪʃəs/ &middot; *kur-VAY-shuhs*
**Русский:** с пышными формами, фигуристый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more curvaceous*, *most curvaceous*
**Form:** from *curve*

Having attractive curves, usually said of a woman's body, sometimes of a car or other object.

1. The actress was known for her *curvaceous* figure.
2. The car's *curvaceous* body turned heads.
3. The new model is *more curvaceous* than the old boxy one.

---

### curvy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɜːrvi/ &middot; *KUR-vee*
**Русский:** извилистый; фигуристый
**Type:** shape; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *curvier*, *curviest*
**Form:** -y from *curve*

Curvy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having many curves or bends.** (извилистый, изогнутый)

1. We drove along a *curvy* mountain road.
2. The chair has a *curvy* back.
3. This is the *curviest* stretch of the highway.

**2. Of a woman, having a full figure with noticeable hips and breasts.** (фигуристая, с пышными формами)

4. The store sells jeans for *curvy* women.
5. She's *curvier* than her sister.

---

### cushioned

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkʊʃənd/ &middot; *KUUSH-uhnd*
**Русский:** мягкий, с мягкой обивкой
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cushioned*, *most cushioned*
**Form:** -ed from *cushion*

Soft and padded, so it protects you from hard contact.

1. The chairs have *cushioned* seats.
2. We sat on a *cushioned* bench by the window.
3. These running shoes are *more cushioned* than my old ones.

---

### cushy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkʊʃi/ &middot; *KUUSH-ee*
**Русский:** непыльный, легкий, удобный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *cushier*, *cushiest*

Easy and comfortable, needing little effort; informal.

1. He has a *cushy* job at his uncle's firm.
2. Life in the suburbs was pretty *cushy*.
3. She landed the *cushiest* assignment in the office.

---

### custodial

**Pronunciation:** /kʌˈstoʊdiəl/ &middot; *kuh-STOH-dee-uhl*
**Русский:** опекунский; связанный с содержанием под стражей; хозяйственный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ial from *custody* and *custodian*
**Contrast:** In American English *custodian* is also the polite word for a janitor, so *custodial staff* are the people who clean a school or office.

Custodial has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Having legal custody of a child.** (имеющий право опеки, опекунский)

1. The *custodial* parent decides where the child goes to school.
2. She was granted *custodial* rights after the divorce.

**2. Involving being held in prison or by the police.** (связанный с содержанием под стражей)

3. The confession came during a *custodial* interrogation.
4. Several deaths in *custodial* settings are under investigation.

**3. Relating to cleaning and taking care of a building.** (хозяйственный, связанный с уборкой)

5. The school hired more *custodial* staff.
6. *Custodial* services are included in the rent.

---

### custom

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkʌstəm/ &middot; *KUS-tuhm*
**Русский:** сделанный на заказ, индивидуальный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** The noun *custom* means a tradition (*обычай*), and *customs* is the check at the border (*таможня*). The adjective *custom* is American; British English prefers *bespoke* or *made-to-measure*.

Made or done to a particular customer's order.

1. They have a *custom* kitchen with walnut cabinets.
2. The shop does *custom* paint jobs.
3. You can order *custom* T-shirts with your team's logo.

---

### custom-built

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkʌstəmˈbɪlt/ &middot; *kus-tuhm-BILT*
**Русский:** сделанный на заказ
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *custom* + participle *built*

Built to a particular customer's order.

1. They live in a *custom-built* house by the lake.
2. His guitar was *custom-built* in Nashville.
3. The company makes *custom-built* computers for gamers.

---

### custom-made

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkʌstəmˈmeɪd/ &middot; *kus-tuhm-MAYD*
**Русский:** сделанный (сшитый) на заказ
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *custom* + participle *made*
**Opposite:** *ready-made*; *off-the-rack*

Made to a particular customer's order or measurements.

1. He wore a *custom-made* suit to the wedding.
2. The boots were *custom-made* for her.
3. We ordered *custom-made* curtains for the big windows.

---

### customary

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkʌstəmɛri/ &middot; *KUS-tuh-mair-ee*
**Русский:** обычный, принятый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more customary*, *most customary*
**Takes:** *it is customary to* + infinitive; *for* someone *to* do
**Form:** -ary from *custom*

Usual, according to custom or habit.

1. It is *customary* to tip 15 to 20 percent in restaurants.
2. He took his *customary* seat at the end of the table.
3. In some cultures, it's *more customary* to bow than to shake hands.

---

### cut

**Pronunciation:** /kʌt/ &middot; *KUT*
**Русский:** срезанный; граненый; мускулистый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — except sense 3, informally *more cut*
**Form:** past participle of *cut*
**Contrast:** *He cut his hand* is the verb. In the muscle sense *cut* is informal American slang, like *ripped*.

Cut has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Removed from the plant by cutting.** (срезанный, скошенный)

1. A vase of fresh *cut* flowers stood on the table.
2. The yard smelled of freshly *cut* grass.

**2. Shaped by cutting, like glass, a gem or a piece of clothing.** (граненый, ограненный, скроенный)

3. The chandelier was made of *cut* crystal.
4. He wore a well-*cut* gray suit.
5. She wore a beautifully *cut* diamond.

**3. Having muscles that show clearly, with very little fat.** (мускулистый, рельефный)

6. He's been lifting weights, and he's really *cut*.
7. She got even *more cut* before the competition.

---

### cut-and-dried

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkʌt ən ˈdraɪd/ &middot; *kut-uhn-DRYD*
**Русский:** ясный, однозначный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cut-and-dried*, *most cut-and-dried*
**Form:** compound — *cut* + *and* + *dried*
**Contrast:** Mostly used with a negative: *It's not that cut-and-dried*. After a verb it is often written without hyphens: *The case is cut and dried*.

Simple and clear, with no room for doubt or argument.

1. The case isn't as *cut-and-dried* as it looks.
2. There's no *cut-and-dried* answer to that question.
3. For the judge, it was all pretty *cut-and-dried*.

---

### cut-rate

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkʌtˈreɪt/ &middot; *kut-RAYT*
**Русский:** дешевый, по сниженной цене
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *cut* + *rate* (price)

Sold at a reduced price, and often of low quality.

1. They sell *cut-rate* airline tickets online.
2. He bought a *cut-rate* phone that broke in a week.
3. Don't trust a *cut-rate* contractor with your roof.

---

### cute

**Pronunciation:** /kjuːt/ &middot; *KYOOT*
**Русский:** милый, симпатичный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *cuter*, *cutest*
**Contrast:** Said of an adult in American English, *cute* usually means *attractive* (*a cute guy*), not only sweet like a baby. Said with an edge (*Don't get cute with me*), it means too clever or cheeky.

Cute has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Pretty and charming, especially a baby, a small animal or a small thing.** (милый, хорошенький)

1. What a *cute* puppy!
2. That's the *cutest* baby I've ever seen.
3. Your little house is so *cute*.

**2. Sexually attractive.** (симпатичный, привлекательный)

4. There's a *cute* guy in my chemistry class.
5. Do you think he's *cuter* than his brother?

**3. Clever in a way that is annoying or not quite honest.** (хитрый, дерзкий)

6. Don't get *cute* with me — just answer the question.
7. That was a *cute* trick, but it won't work twice.

---

### cutthroat

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkʌtθroʊt/ &middot; *KUT-throht*
**Русский:** беспощадный, жестокий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cutthroat*, *most cutthroat*
**Form:** compound — *cut* + *throat*

Ready to do anything to win, with no concern for others.

1. They faced *cutthroat* competition from the big chains.
2. The restaurant business is *cutthroat*.
3. Wall Street was the *most cutthroat* place I've ever worked.

---

### cutting

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkʌtɪŋ/ &middot; *KUT-ing*
**Русский:** язвительный; пронизывающий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cutting*, *most cutting*
**Form:** -ing from *cut*
**Contrast:** *A cutting* is also a noun: a piece cut from a plant to grow a new one. Don't confuse it with *cutting-edge*.

Cutting has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Unkind and meant to hurt someone's feelings.** (язвительный, колкий)

1. She made a *cutting* remark about his weight.
2. His review of the film was *cutting*.
3. It was the *most cutting* thing anyone had ever said to her.

**2. Of wind or cold, sharp and unpleasant.** (пронизывающий, резкий)

4. A *cutting* wind blew off the lake.
5. The cold was *cutting* that morning.

---

### cutting-edge

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkʌtɪŋ ˈɛdʒ/ &middot; *kut-ing-EJ*
**Русский:** передовой, новейший
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cutting-edge*, *most cutting-edge*
**Form:** compound — *cutting* + *edge*
**Opposite:** *outdated*
**Contrast:** Hyphenated as an adjective (*cutting-edge research*). *At the cutting edge of* is the noun phrase, with no hyphen.

The newest and most advanced.

1. The lab uses *cutting-edge* technology.
2. Their design is really *cutting-edge*.
3. It's the *most cutting-edge* phone on the market.

---

### cyclical

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪklɪkəl; ˈsaɪklɪkəl/ &middot; *SIK-lih-kuhl; SY-klih-kuhl*
**Русский:** циклический, цикличный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cyclical*, *most cyclical*
**Form:** -ical from *cycle*

Happening in cycles, repeating the same pattern again and again.

1. The housing market is *cyclical*.
2. Some historians see a *cyclical* pattern in history.
3. Some industries are *more cyclical* than others.

---

### cylindrical

**Pronunciation:** /səˈlɪndrɪkəl/ &middot; *suh-LIN-drih-kuhl*
**Русский:** цилиндрический
**Type:** shape
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ical from *cylinder*

Shaped like a cylinder, long and round.

1. The oil is stored in *cylindrical* tanks.
2. A soda can has a *cylindrical* shape.
3. The old water tower is *cylindrical*.

---

### cynical

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪnɪkəl/ &middot; *SIN-ih-kuhl*
**Русский:** циничный, недоверчивый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more cynical*, *most cynical*
**Takes:** *about* + noun
**Contrast:** Partly a false friend. English *cynical* mostly means *distrustful, expecting the worst of people*. Russian *циничный* often suggests shameless or crude; for that, say *crude* or *shameless*.

Cynical has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Believing that people act only out of selfishness, and distrusting their motives.** (циничный, недоверчивый, скептический)

1. Many voters are *cynical* about politicians.
2. After years in the news business, he became *more cynical*.
3. Don't be so *cynical* — some people really do want to help.

**2. Done for selfish reasons, with no concern for what is right.** (циничный, расчетливый)

4. It was a *cynical* attempt to win votes.
5. The company made a *cynical* decision to hide the risks.

---

### Cypriot

**Pronunciation:** /ˈsɪpriət/ &middot; *SIP-ree-uht*
**Русский:** кипрский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *A Cypriot* is also a person from Cyprus. The country is *Cyprus*.

From or relating to Cyprus.

1. Halloumi is a traditional *Cypriot* cheese.
2. Her mother is *Cypriot*.
3. The *Cypriot* government announced new rules for tourists.

---

### Cyrillic

**Pronunciation:** /səˈrɪlɪk/ &middot; *suh-RIL-ik*
**Русский:** кириллический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Always with a capital *C*. It is also a noun: *Can you read Cyrillic?*

Relating to the alphabet used for Russian, Ukrainian, Bulgarian, Serbian and other languages.

1. Russian is written in the *Cyrillic* alphabet.
2. The sign was in *Cyrillic* letters, so I couldn't read it.
3. My keyboard has both Latin and *Cyrillic* characters.

---

### czarist

**Pronunciation:** /ˈzɑːrɪst/ &middot; *ZAHR-ist*
**Русский:** царский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ist from *czar*
**Contrast:** Also spelled *tsarist*. It means *of the czars*, not *magnificent*: for *царский прием* say *a lavish welcome*.

Relating to the czars or their government in Russia before 1917.

1. The family fled *czarist* Russia in 1905.
2. Under *czarist* rule, most peasants could not read.
3. The museum has a collection of *czarist* coins.

---

### Czech

**Pronunciation:** /tʃɛk/ &middot; *CHEK*
**Русский:** чешский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Pronounced like *check*. The language is *Czech*, the people are *Czechs*, and the country is the *Czech Republic* or *Czechia*.

From or relating to the Czech Republic, its people or its language.

1. Prague is famous for *Czech* beer.
2. My grandparents were *Czech*.
3. She's taking a *Czech* class at the community college.

---

### Czechoslovakian

**Pronunciation:** /ˌtʃɛkəsloʊˈvɑːkiən/ &middot; *chek-uh-sloh-VAH-kee-uhn*
**Русский:** чехословацкий
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Historical: Czechoslovakia split into the Czech Republic and Slovakia in 1993. *Czechoslovak* means the same.

From or relating to the former country of Czechoslovakia.

1. He played on the *Czechoslovakian* national hockey team in the 1980s.
2. The film is a *Czechoslovakian* classic from the 1960s.
3. Her passport was still *Czechoslovakian* when she came to the U.S.
